### [1] [Nucleon-sigma terms at $m_π=222 ~\rm MeV$ with a variational analysis from lattice QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05989)
*Lorenzo Barca,Gunnar Bali,Sara Collins,Marcel Rodekamp*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper investigates excited-state contamination in lattice QCD calculations of nucleon sigma terms, focusing on suppression of $N\pi\pi$ states at physical pion mass ($m_\pi = 222~\text{MeV}$) using a variational basis including $N$ and $N\sigma$ operators.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Resolving the persistent tension between lattice QCD and phenomenological determinations of nucleon sigma terms—critical for nucleon mass decomposition and scalar-coupled BSM physics—by addressing uncontrolled excited-state contamination, particularly $N\sigma$ (and now $N\pi\pi$) states.

Method: Variational analysis with a small operator basis ($N$ and $N\sigma$) applied to lattice QCD ensembles at $m_\pi = 222~\text{MeV}$; examines overlap of the $N\sigma$ operator with $N\pi\pi$ states arising from $\sigma \to \pi\pi$ decay.

Result: Preliminary evidence that the same $N$–$N\sigma$ variational basis effective at $m_\pi = 429~\text{MeV}$ also suppresses $N\pi\pi$ contamination at $m_\pi = 222~\text{MeV}$, suggesting robustness of the method near the physical point.

Conclusion: The variational approach using $N$ and $N\sigma$ operators remains viable for controlling multi-hadron excited-state contamination even when the $\sigma$ resonance becomes unstable, supporting more reliable lattice extractions of sigma terms at near-physical quark masses.

Abstract: Nucleon sigma terms are important for the decomposition of the nucleon mass and for searches for new physics beyond the Standard Model involving scalar interactions. A persistent tension between lattice QCD and phenomenological determinations may be due to uncontrolled excited-state contamination in lattice QCD analyses. In previous work at $m_π=429~\rm MeV$, we showed that this contamination is dominated by $Nσ$ states and can be strongly suppressed with a variational analysis using $N$ and $Nσ$ operators. In this talk, we present preliminary results at $m_π=222~\rm MeV$, where the $σ$ becomes unstable and decays into $ππ$. We investigate whether the same small variational basis can remove the expected $Nππ$ contamination through the overlap of the $Nσ$ operator with these states.

</details>


### [2] [ggpeps: A package for Gauged Gaussian PEPS](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06486)
*Ariel Kelman,Itay Gomelski,Jonathan Elyovich,Erez Zohar,Patrick Emonts*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: Introduces ggpeps, a Python package implementing Gauged Gaussian Projected Entangled Pair States (GGPEPS) for simulating lattice gauge theories using tensor network methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable efficient numerical simulation of non-perturbative regimes of quantum field theories—particularly lattice gauge theories—where traditional analytical or Monte Carlo methods face limitations.

Method: Development and implementation of the GGPEPS tensor network ansatz, incorporating gauge invariance and Gaussian constraints, packaged as an open-source Python library with documentation and usage examples.

Result: A functional, documented Python package (ggpeps) enabling variational simulations of lattice gauge theories via gauged Gaussian PEPS, supporting systematic study of low-energy dynamics and entanglement structure.

Conclusion: GGPEPS provides a scalable, gauge-invariant tensor network framework for non-perturbative QFT studies; the ggpeps package lowers the barrier to adoption and facilitates reproducible, algorithm-driven exploration of lattice gauge models.

Abstract: Lattice gauge theories are a low-energy regularization of quantum field theories. They are the basis for the development of numerical algorithms that provide insight into the non-perturbative regime of quantum field theories which is difficult to achieve through other methods. We present a python package, ggpeps, implementing gauged Gaussian projected entangled pair states (GGPEPS), specialized to study lattice gauge theories. We give a an overview of the considered systems, describe the GGPEPS ansatz and provide a usage guide for the ggpeps package.

</details>


### [3] [Sector-Resolved Flow Sampling for Topologically Frozen Lattice Gauge Theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07781)
*Ankur Singha,Jacob Kauffmann,Karl Jansen,Jacob Finkenrath,Vipul Arora,Shinichi Nakajima*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: Introduces a mixture of sector-resolved samplers (MSRS) using generative models and bijective topological shifts to overcome topological freezing in lattice gauge theory, validated in 2D compact U(1) theory.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Topological freezing hinders sampling in nonperturbative gauge theories near the continuum limit, causing Markov chains to get stuck in fixed topological charge sectors and biasing observables like susceptibility.

Method: Proposes MSRS: (i) trains a generative model (e.g., normalizing flow) in a reference topological sector; (ii) applies a deterministic, bijective topological shift map to generate samples in other sectors; (iii) computes domain-restricted partition functions to infer correct sector weights.

Result: In 2D compact U(1) theory, MSRS reproduces exact topological-charge distribution, yields unbiased topological susceptibility where HMC freezes and overrelaxation fails, and outperforms prior flow-based samplers by orders of magnitude.

Conclusion: Explicit resolution of topological sectors via generative modeling—leveraging exact density evaluation and bijective shifts—is a robust and scalable strategy to circumvent topological barriers in lattice QCD and related theories.

Abstract: Topological fluctuations are essential to nonperturbative gauge theories but become increasingly difficult to sample toward the continuum limit, where Markov chains can freeze in sectors of fixed topological charge. We introduce a generative sampler, a mixture of sector-resolved samplers (MSRS) that explicitly resolves these sectors and exploits a key advantage of generative models, the ability to directly evaluate the domain-restricted partition function and thereby determine the relative weights of disconnected sectors. We train a generative model in a reference topological sector combined with a bijective topological shift that deterministically maps its samples to other sectors. We demonstrate the method in two-dimensional compact U (1) lattice gauge theory, where it reproduces the topological-charge distribution and yields an unbiased susceptibility in a regime where hybrid Monte Carlo is frozen and overrelaxation gives inaccurate estimates. Our approach also outperforms existing flow-based samplers by orders of magnitude. These results demonstrate that explicit sector resolution provides a promising route to overcoming topological barriers in lattice gauge theory.

</details>


### [4] [Gauge field digitization in the Hamiltonian limit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07886)
*Attila Pasztor,David Pesznyak*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper investigates digitization errors when approximating the continuous U(1) gauge group with finite Z(N) subgroups in quantum simulations of (2+1)D gauge theories, using anisotropic Euclidean lattices to approach the Hamiltonian limit; it finds that freezing persists and Z(N) deviates significantly from U(1) even outside the frozen regime—unlike on isotropic lattices—providing classical benchmarks for quantifying systematic digitization errors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To control digitization errors arising from replacing continuous U(1) gauge fields with finite Z(N) subgroups in quantum simulations of gauge theories at finite density or in real time, especially in the Hamiltonian (continuous-time) limit.

Method: Analytical derivation of renormalization-group trajectories for spatial and temporal gauge couplings on anisotropic Euclidean lattices approaching the Hamiltonian limit; validation via classical Monte Carlo simulations and exact diagonalization of small-system Hamiltonians.

Result: Temporal coupling scales logarithmically for Z(N) versus power-law for U(1); freezing transition persists in the Hamiltonian limit; Z(N) theories exhibit substantial deviations from U(1) even outside the frozen regime—contrasting isotropic-lattice behavior where Z(N) closely approximates U(1) at weak coupling.

Conclusion: Finite-subgroup digitization introduces non-negligible, persistent systematic errors in the Hamiltonian limit, necessitating careful error quantification; anisotropic lattice analysis provides a classical benchmark for assessing such errors in quantum simulations of gauge theories.

Abstract: Quantum computers can circumvent the numerical sign problem in gauge theories at finite density or in real time. Quantum simulations of gauge theories require a finite-dimensional representation of continuous gauge fields. Replacing a continuous gauge group by a finite subgroup can substantially reduce the required quantum resources, but introduces digitization errors that must be controlled in the Hamiltonian, or continuous-time, limit. Previous studies, using the isotropic Euclidean lattices showed that the freezing transition of the discrete subgroup can make it a bad approximation for the continuous group at large Euclidean couplings. Here, we study the digitization of U(1) by its Z($N$) subgroups in 2+1 dimensions using anisotropic Euclidean lattices. We derive the trajectories of the spatial and temporal gauge couplings along which the Hamiltonian limit is approached at fixed Hamiltonian coupling. While the temporal coupling exhibits power-law scaling in the continuous U(1) theory, it grows only logarithmically for finite Z($N$). Using classical lattice simulations and exact diagonalization, we verify that these trajectories reproduce the corresponding Hamiltonian theories. We find that the freezing transition persists in the Hamiltonian limit of discrete gauge groups and that finite-$N$ theories can differ substantially from U(1) even outside the frozen regime, in contrast to the behavior on isotropic Euclidean lattices, where for small couplings, the discrete group provides a very accurate approximation of the continuous group. Our results provide a classical benchmark for quantifying the systematic errors due to gauge-field digitization in quantum simulations.

</details>

### [5] [Energy dissipation in linear viscoelasticity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05465)
*Juan Luis Gonzalez-Santander,Francesco Mainardi*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes and compares the asymptotic behavior of specific dissipation $ Q^{-1}(\omega) $ across several linear viscoelastic models — Becker, Lomnitz, Lambert, generalized Becker, and extended Jeffreys–Lomnitz — deriving novel closed-form expressions and establishing their limiting behaviors (especially as $ \omega \to 0^+ $ and $ \omega \to +\infty $), with consistent convergence to the Maxwell model’s dissipation at high frequencies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically compare and unify the frequency-domain dissipation characteristics of classical and extended linear viscoelastic models, particularly clarifying their low- and high-frequency asymptotics and relationships to the Maxwell model.

Method: Analytical derivation of asymptotic expansions and closed-form expressions for $ Q^{-1}(\omega) $ using Laplace transforms and fractional calculus; rational-parameter specialization for the generalized Becker model; piecewise analysis for the extended Jeffreys–Lomnitz model over different ranges of the exponent $ \\alpha $.

Result: New closed-form formulas for $ Q_\\nu^{-1}(\\omega) $ (generalized Becker, $ \\nu \\in (0,1] $ rational) and $ Q_\\alpha^{-1}(\\omega) $ (extended Jeffreys–Lomnitz, for $ \\alpha \\in (0,1] $ and $ \\alpha \\in (-\\infty,1] $); asymptotic equivalence of all models’ $ Q^{-1}(\\omega) $ to the Maxwell model as $ \\omega \\to +\\infty $; explicit $ \\omega \\to 0^+ $ behavior for Becker and Lomnitz models.

Conclusion: The Maxwell model emerges universally as the high-frequency limit of the considered viscoelastic models; the generalized Becker and extended Jeffreys–Lomnitz models provide flexible, analytically tractable interpolations between Maxwell, Becker, and Lomnitz behaviors, with rational- or real-parameter tuning enabling precise control over dissipation spectra.

Abstract: We compare the specific dissipation $Q^{-1}\left( ω\right) $ for the Becker, Lomnitz, and Lambert models in linear viscoelasticity. Graphically, the specific dissipation of these models behaves similarly as $ω\rightarrow 0^{+}$ and $ω\rightarrow +\infty$. Furthermore, we obtain analytic formulas for the asymptotic behavior of $Q^{-1}\left( ω\right) $ in the Becker and Lomnitz models as $ω\rightarrow 0^{+}$ and $ω\rightarrow +\infty$, where the latter limit coincides with the specific dissipation of the Maxwell model. We also derive a novel closed-form expression for the specific dissipation in the generalized Becker model, $Q_{ν}^{-1}\left( ω\right) $, when the parameter $ν\in \left( 0,1\right] $ is rational. When $ν\rightarrow 0$, we recover the specific dissipation of the Maxwell model, whereas when $ν=1$, we recover that of the original Becker model. In addition, we derive two new closed-form expressions for the specific dissipation for the extended Jeffreys--Lomnitz model $Q_{α}^{-1}\left( ω\right) $, the first being valid for $α\in \left( 0,1\right] $, and the second for $α\in \left( -\infty ,1\right] $. When $α\rightarrow 0$, we recover the specific dissipation of the Lomnitz model, whereas when $α=1$, we recover that of the Maxwell model. Finally, we conclude that the asymptotic behavior of $Q_{α}^{-1}\left( ω\right) $ as $ω\rightarrow +\infty $ coincides with the specific dissipation of the Maxwell model. Keywords:

</details>


### [6] [Information Loss under Gauge Reduction of Discrete Electromagnetic Fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05473)
*Jean-Pierre Magnot*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper decomposes relative entropy in Whitney-discretized electromagnetism under gauge reduction, showing exact splitting into physical marginal and nonnegative conditional gauge contributions via Schur complements and correlations; it proves the physical divergence is the minimum over Gaussian auxiliary extensions and provides an information-geometric interpretation of gauge reduction without ascribing physical meaning to gauge variables.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide an information-geometric understanding of gauge reduction in discrete electromagnetism—specifically, how relative entropy behaves when eliminating unphysical gauge degrees of freedom—without endowing gauge variables with physical significance.

Method: Uses discrete Hodge decomposition on Whitney-complex discretizations to separate gauge (exact), harmonic, and coexact components; analyzes Gaussian distributions on the auxiliary potential space; applies matrix algebra (Schur complements, conditional expectations) to decompose relative entropy and Fisher information; derives minimization properties via convexity of KL divergence.

Result: Relative entropy splits exactly into physical marginal entropy plus a nonnegative gauge contribution expressible via Schur complements, conditional means, and physical–gauge correlations; equality holds iff physical–gauge correlations vanish; physical divergence equals the minimum relative entropy over all Gaussian auxiliary extensions; infinitesimal Fisher information admits analogous decomposition; a two-variable model demonstrates non-uniqueness of auxiliary potentials yielding identical physical statistics.

Conclusion: Gauge reduction in discrete electromagnetism admits a rigorous, intrinsic information-geometric characterization: physical relative entropy is the minimal achievable divergence, and the gauge contribution quantifies statistical redundancy—not physical content—of the potential representation.

Abstract: We study relative entropy under gauge reduction in Whitney-discretized electromagnetism. The discrete Hodge decomposition separates exact gauge directions from harmonic and coexact physical degrees of freedom. For Gaussian distributions on the auxiliary potential space, relative entropy splits exactly into the relative entropy of the physical marginals and a nonnegative conditional gauge contribution. We express this contribution through Schur complements, conditional means, and physical--gauge correlations, and characterize equality between auxiliary and physical relative entropies. The physical divergence is also shown to be the minimum over all Gaussian auxiliary extensions. At the infinitesimal level, Fisher information admits an analogous decomposition. A two-variable model illustrates how statistically distinct auxiliary potentials may represent identical physical statistics. The construction provides an information-geometric interpretation of discrete gauge reduction without assigning physical significance to the eliminated gauge variables.

</details>


### [7] [Distance Energies and Negative Type of Flat Tori](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05622)
*Ye Zhou*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies distance energies on flat tori, establishes a Fourier signature of the cut locus, proves Haar measure is never a local maximizer for positive distance powers, shows all d≥2 flat tori have zero supremal negative type and generalized roundness, and solves the global maximization problem for orthogonal rectangular and regular hexagonal tori with phase transitions in extremal measures depending on α.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand extremal probability measures for distance energies on flat tori, characterize geometric-analytic properties (e.g., cut locus) via Fourier analysis, and determine how metric geometry influences energy maximization—especially in relation to negative type and generalized roundness.

Method: Fourier analysis on flat tori; asymptotic analysis of Fourier coefficients of ρ_Λ^α near facet normals of the dual lattice; variational arguments and symmetry-based classification of extremal measures; explicit computation of distance energies for structured measures (two-torsion, cyclic subgroups, diametral pairs).

Result: 1) Quantitative Fourier signature linking cut locus geometry to positivity and asymptotics of ρ_Λ^α’s Fourier coefficients; 2) Haar measure fails to be a local maximizer for any α > 0; 3) All d ≥ 2 flat tori have supremal negative type = generalized roundness = 0; 4) Complete classification of global maximizers: phase transition at α = 2 for orthogonal rectangular tori; uniform measures on order-3 cyclic subgroup are unique maximizers for all α ≥ 1 on the hexagonal torus.

Conclusion: The geometry of the cut locus governs extremal behavior of distance energies via Fourier signatures; Haar measure is universally suboptimal for positive α; metric rigidity (via negative type/roundness) vanishes in dimension ≥2; and optimal measures exhibit rich, lattice-dependent structure with sharp transitions governed by α.

Abstract: Let \(T_Λ=\mathbb R^d/Λ\), \(d\geq2\), be a flat torus with quotient metric \(ρ_Λ\), and consider the distance energies \(I_α(μ)=\iint ρ_Λ(x,y)^α\,dμ(x)\,dμ(y)\) of Borel probability measures \(μ\). We prove a quantitative Fourier signature of the cut locus: for every Voronoi facet and every \(α>0\), there is a sequence of dual-lattice frequencies approaching the facet normal along which the Fourier coefficients of \(ρ_Λ^α\) are positive, with an explicit leading asymptotic determined by the facet. As direct consequences, Haar measure is not a local maximizer for any positive distance power, even among smooth densities, and every flat torus of dimension at least two has supremal negative type and generalized roundness zero. We then solve the global maximization problem for two classes of flat tori. On an orthogonal rectangular torus, the maximizers undergo a transition at \(α=2\): for \(1\leqα<2\) they are the translated uniform measures on the two-torsion subgroup; at \(α=2\) all balanced couplings on translates of that subgroup are extremal; and for \(α>2\) only equally weighted diametral pairs remain. On the regular hexagonal torus, the maximizers are precisely the uniform measures on translates of a distinguished cyclic subgroup of order three for every \(α\geq1\).

</details>


### [8] [An Algebraic Proof of Gauge-Fixing Independence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05872)
*Jiuhe Liu*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves algebraically that perturbative asymptotic expansions in a finite-dimensional non-Abelian gauge theory model are independent of the choice of gauge-fixing condition, provided transversality conditions hold.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish rigorous gauge-fixing independence of perturbative expansions in non-Abelian gauge theories—a foundational issue for consistency of BRST quantization and physical observables.

Method: Algebraic approach using BRST formalism and perturbation theory in a finite-dimensional setting; proof relies on transversality conditions and smooth deformations of gauge-fixing functions.

Result: Normalized expectation values of observables are invariant under smooth deformations of gauge-fixing conditions satisfying transversality assumptions.

Conclusion: Gauge-fixing independence holds at the level of perturbative asymptotic expansions in this algebraic finite-dimensional model, supporting the robustness of BRST-based quantization schemes.

Abstract: This paper provides an algebraic proof of the gauge-fixing independence of perturbative asymptotic expansions in a finite-dimensional model of non-Abelian gauge theory. We develop an algebraic framework using the BRST formalism and perturbation theory. Our main theorem shows that under appropriate transversality conditions, normalized expectation values of observables remain unchanged under smooth deformations of the gauge-fixing conditions that preserve these assumptions.

</details>


### [9] [Renormalization Group on Wiener Space: Spectral Theory and Universality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06183)
*André L. P. Considera,Alexei A. Mailybaev*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a renormalization group (RG) formalism on Wiener space, linearizes the RG operator around the Wiener measure, constructs its spectral theory using Hida distributions, identifies universal correction terms to Donsker’s invariance principle via Wick polynomials of white noise, and recovers the irrelevant/marginal/relevant classification from spectral data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a rigorous RG framework for stochastic processes—specifically Brownian motion—and uncover universal structure beyond convergence in Donsker's theorem, including leading corrections and perturbative classifications akin to quantum field theory.

Method: Introduces an RG operator R acting on path-space probability measures; linearizes it around Wiener measure to obtain operator L; constructs generalized eigenvectors as Hida distributions (not ordinary measures); uses white noise analysis (Hida calculus) to define spectral theory; identifies explicit eigenpairs involving Wick-ordered integrals of white noise; applies spectral decomposition to analyze universality of corrections to Brownian scaling.

Result: Rigorous construction of L's spectrum in Hida space; spectral gap and diagonal-concentration property; explicit eigenfamily {U_n} with eigenvalues λ_n = 2^{1−n/2}; universal hierarchy of corrections to Donsker limit governed by (λ_n, U_n); formal extension to Gibbs perturbations yielding standard QFT-type relevance classification from spectral exponents.

Conclusion: The Wiener measure is a hyperbolic RG fixed point with discrete, computable spectrum in the Hida space; this spectral structure encodes both fine-scale universality in functional central limit theorems and a mathematically grounded perturbative classification mirroring quantum field theory.

Abstract: We develop a rigorous renormalization group (RG) formalism on Wiener space. We introduce an RG operator $\mathcal R$ acting on probability measures over continuous paths, whose fixed point is the Wiener measure. We linearize $\mathcal{R}$ around the Brownian fixed point and analyze the spectral structure of the resulting operator $\mathscr{L}$. Its eigenvectors are too singular to be realized as honest measures on path space, and we therefore develop a generalized spectral theory within the framework of white noise analysis (Hida calculus). The eigenvectors of $\mathscr{L}$ are realized as Hida distributions, satisfying $\mathscr{L} U=λU$ in the weak sense. This analysis yields structural results such as a spectral gap and a diagonal-concentration property of the eigenvectors. Moreover, we identify a family $\{\mathfrak{U}_n\}_{n \geq 0}$ of eigenvectors with eigenvalues $λ_n = 2^{1-n/2}$, consisting of Wick polynomials of white noise formally expressed as $\mathfrak{U}_n = \int_0^1 {:}\dot W(t)^n{:}\, dt$, and rigorously constructed as Hida distributions. We then use this spectral structure to uncover a finer, second layer of universality in the Donsker invariance principle: not only is the convergence of random walks towards the Brownian scaling limit universal, but the entire hierarchy of leading corrections to the Brownian limit is universal as well, governed by the eigenpairs $(λ_n, \mathfrak{U}_n)$. We also show, at a formal level, that the framework developed here extends to Gibbs-type perturbations of the Brownian fixed point, in the spirit of self-interacting quantum field theories. In particular, we recover the standard irrelevant/marginal/relevant classification, usually obtained in the physics literature by power-counting, solely from the spectral analysis of $\mathscr{L}$.

</details>


### [10] [Condensation and Collapse in the Mean-Field Limit of Rotating 2D Bose Gases with Two-Body and Three-Body Interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06466)
*Deke Li,Yuan Li,Qingxuan Wang*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies the mean-field collapse regime of N interacting bosons in a rotating 2D harmonic trap with attractive two-body and repulsive three-body interactions. It proves that, as the two-body coupling approaches criticality and three-body coupling vanishes, the many-body ground state energy and wavefunction asymptotically match those of a rotating cubic-quintic NLS functional — with universal blow-up governed by the Gagliardo-Nirenberg optimizer and precise energy expansion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously justify the emergence of a rotating cubic-quintic nonlinear Schrödinger (NLS) description for dilute, rotating Bose gases with competing two- and three-body interactions in the collapse regime, where standard mean-field theory breaks down due to singular behavior.

Method: Rigorous many-body analysis combining variational methods, quantitative energy estimates, coherent states, and dimensional reduction techniques; derivation and analysis of the effective rotating cubic-quintic NLS functional; use of the Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality to characterize universal blow-up profiles; matching asymptotics between many-body and mean-field limits under critical scaling.

Result: Proof that the quantum ground state energy converges to the NLS energy with identical leading-order asymptotic expansion in the collapse regime; demonstration of complete Bose-Einstein condensation onto the universal blow-up profile (Gagliardo-Nirenberg optimizer); explicit energy formula E^NLS = (1 − ζ/4 + o(1)) Q_pot ℓ_n² with ζ ≥ 0 determined by relative rates of a_n → a_* and b_n → 0.

Conclusion: The rotating cubic-quintic NLS functional provides a mathematically rigorous and asymptotically exact effective description of the many-body system in the collapse regime, confirming universality of blow-up and establishing BEC onto a scale-invariant profile despite rotational effects and interaction competition.

Abstract: We consider a system of $N$ interacting bosons in a rotating harmonic trap in $\mathbb{R}^2$, where the two-body interaction is attractive and scaled as $N^{2α}U(N^αx)$ with $0<α<1/12$, and the three-body interaction is repulsive and scaled as $N^{4β}W(N^βx,N^βy)$ with $0<β<1/24$. In the mean-field limit, the ground state energy is effectively described by a rotating cubic-quintic nonlinear Schrödinger functional. We analyze the collapse regime where the two-body coupling $a$ approaches the critical value $a_*$ and the three-body coupling $b$ tends to zero. The NLS ground states blow up with a universal profile given by the optimizer of the Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality, and the energy satisfies $E^{\mathrm{NLS}} = (1-ζ/4+o(1))\mathcal{Q}{\rm{pot}}\ell_n^2$ with $ζ\geqslant0$ determined by the relative rates of $a_n\to a_*$ and $b_n\searrow0$. From the many-body theory, we rigorously justify this effective description: the quantum ground state energy converges to the NLS energy with the same asymptotic expansion in the collapse regime, and the many-body ground states exhibit complete Bose-Einstein condensation onto the universal blow-up profile.

</details>


### [11] [Novel computational formulas and relations for special numbers and functions: approach to analysis of Debye functions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06620)
*Yilmaz Simsek*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a new family of special functions related to Debye functions, derives novel identities involving Debye functions, multiple Hurwitz–Lerch zeta functions, and Apostol-Bernoulli numbers of higher order using generating functions; establishes connections to various classical number sequences and distributions; and provides recurrence relations, Maclaurin expansions, approximations, and applications in mathematical physics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a new family of special functions generalizing and unifying Debye functions and linking them systematically to higher-order Apostol-type numbers, Hurwitz–Lerch zeta functions, and classical combinatorial and probabilistic objects (e.g., negative binomial distribution, Frobenius–Euler and Bernoulli numbers, Stirling numbers).

Method: Generating function method; analytic continuation and functional equations; series manipulations; derivation of recurrence relations and Maclaurin expansions; integral representations; comparison with known special functions and number-theoretic objects.

Result: New special functions unifying Debye functions; novel identities connecting Debye functions with multiple Hurwitz–Lerch zeta functions and Apostol-Bernoulli numbers of higher order at negative integers; integral and special-value representations linking to negative binomial moment generating functions, Apostol-type numbers, Frobenius–Euler, Bernoulli, and Stirling numbers; recurrence relations, Maclaurin series, and approximation formulas.

Conclusion: The introduced function family provides a unifying framework bridging Debye functions, analytic number theory (via Hurwitz–Lerch zeta and Apostol-type numbers), combinatorics, and probability; results extend classical theory and offer new tools for applications in mathematical physics.

Abstract: The first goal of this paper is to give a new family of special function related to the Debye functions. By using these function and generating function method, we derive many novel formulas and relations involving the Debye functions, multiple Hurwitz Lerch zeta function which interpolate the Apostol-Bernoulli numbers of higher order at negative integers. Proof method of theorems in this paper are different from those of theory of analytic numbers. The second goal of this paper is to show that special values and integral presentations of this function are closely related to unification of the Debye functions, the moment generating function for the negative binom distribution, the generating functions for the Apostol-type numbers of higher-order, and the Frobenius-Euler numbers, the Bernoulli numbers, and the Stirling numbers etc. Moreover, we give recurrence relations, Maclaurin's series expansion, and approximation formula for unification of the Debye functions. Finally, we give further remarks, observations and applications in mathematical physics on the results of this paper.

</details>


### [12] [Commutative Factorization of Nonlinear Second-Order Differential Equations: Theory and Applications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06932)
*Gabriel Gonzalez*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper generalizes commutative factorization for second-order nonlinear ODEs by incorporating Riccati-Bernoulli equations and Bäcklund transformations to systematically construct particular and general solutions, validated on physically relevant models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the commutative factorization framework to a broader class of second-order nonlinear ODEs—beyond its original scope—by integrating Riccati-Bernoulli equations and Bäcklund transformations for systematic solution construction.

Method: Generalized commutative factorization using Riccati-Bernoulli equations and Bäcklund transformations; applied to multiple physically motivated nonlinear ODEs including those from classical mechanics and nonlinear oscillatory systems.

Result: A systematic method for obtaining both particular and general solutions of second-order nonlinear ODEs; successful application to several physically relevant models confirms efficacy.

Conclusion: Commutative factorization, enhanced with Riccati-Bernoulli and Bäcklund techniques, is a powerful and broadly applicable analytical tool for solving second-order nonlinear ODEs.

Abstract: This work presents a generalization of the commutative factorization framework for a broad class of second-order nonlinear ordinary differential equations. The main objective was to extend the commutative factorization procedure in order to use the Riccati-Bernoulli equation and the Bäcklund transformation. This provides a systematic route for constructing both particular and general solutions of the original second-order nonlinear equation. The theoretical development is illustrated through several nonlinear models of physical and mathematical interest, including equations arising in classical mechanics and nonlinear oscillatory systems. The results demonstrate that commutative factorization constitutes an effective analytical tool for solving nonlinear differential equations.

</details>


### [13] [A Note on Extended States on the Bethe Lattice](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06987)
*Vojkan Jaksic,Yoram Last,Simone Warzel*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper provides a new proof for the existence of absolutely continuous spectrum in the weakly disordered Anderson model on the Bethe lattice, using a cyclicity criterion and Hellinger affinity; it also extends the framework to Z^d via a Schur complement formulation of a matrix spectral measure.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a new, conceptually transparent proof of absolutely continuous spectrum for the weakly disordered Anderson model on the Bethe lattice—bypassing traditional multiscale or diagrammatic methods—and to formulate a quantitative generalization applicable to Z^d.

Method: Applies a general cyclicity criterion for Anderson-type Hamiltonians; reduces spectral continuity to non-mutual singularity of spectral measures from two independent rooted trees; uses Hellinger affinity as a detectability tool; employs harmonic analysis and compactness for weak-disorder estimates; extends the criterion to Z^d via Schur complement of the Poisson transform of a 2×2 matrix-valued spectral measure.

Result: Rigorous proof of absolutely continuous spectrum for weak disorder on the Bethe lattice; derivation of a quantitative cyclicity condition for Z^d expressed through the Schur complement of a matrix spectral measure's Poisson transform.

Conclusion: Cyclicity—linked to non-singularity of spectral measures and quantified by Hellinger affinity—provides a robust, unifying framework for absolute continuity in tree and lattice geometries, with potential for broader applicability to random operators.

Abstract: We give a new proof of the existence of absolutely continuous spectrum for the weakly disordered Anderson model on the Bethe lattice. The argument follows a general cyclicity criterion for Anderson-type Hamiltonians and reduces the problem to showing that the spectral measures of two independent copies of the rooted tree are not mutually singular. This is detected by their Hellinger affinity, and the required weak-disorder estimate follows from a short harmonic and compactness argument. We also formulate an analogous quantitative cyclicity criterion for the Anderson model on $\mathbb{Z}^d$, expressed through the Schur complement of the Poisson transform of a $2\times2$ matrix spectral measure.

</details>


### [14] [Renormalization group bounds on Araki relative entropy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07277)
*Edoardo D'Angelo*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a multiscale Bakry-Émery criterion to bound the Araki relative entropy for perturbations of equilibrium states in relativistic quantum field theory (QFT), leveraging stochastic positivity, Feynman–Kac representations, abstract Wiener space structure of the Euclidean free field, and a Polchinski-type equation; it applies the criterion to prove the bound for the ultraviolet-finite Lorentzian massive Sine-Gordon model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain rigorous, operator-algebraic bounds on the Araki relative entropy for physically relevant interacting QFT models—particularly where standard perturbative or lattice methods fail—by bridging probabilistic methods (stochastic analysis, Gaussian measures) with algebraic QFT.

Method: Combines stochastic positivity to map relativistic bosonic field perturbations to Euclidean massive free field perturbations; uses Feynman–Kac formula to express Araki relative entropy probabilistically; exploits abstract Wiener space structure to formulate a Polchinski equation; extends Bauerschmidt–Bodineau–Dagallier’s multiscale analysis to derive a probabilistic (hence operator-algebraic) bound via a multiscale Bakry–Émery curvature criterion.

Result: Derives a general multiscale Bakry–Émery criterion yielding an upper bound on Araki relative entropy for affiliated perturbations; proves that the Lorentzian massive Sine-Gordon model in its UV-finite regime satisfies this bound.

Conclusion: The multiscale Bakry–Émery framework successfully bridges probabilistic renormalization group methods and algebraic QFT, enabling non-perturbative, operator-algebraic control of relative entropy—a key quantity for quantum information and stability in QFT.

Abstract: This paper derives a multiscale Bakry-Émery criterion for an upper bound on the Araki relative entropy of perturbations of an equilibrium state in relativistic QFT. Using stochastic positivity, affiliated perturbations of the relativistic bosonic field can be put in correspondence with perturbations of the massive Euclidean free field. This gives a Feynman-Kac formula, which in turns is used to derive a probabilistic expression for the Araki relative entropy. The description of the massive Euclidean free field as an abstract Wiener space provides the infinite-dimensional framework to derive a Polchinski equation. Extending the methods of Bauerschmidt, Bodineau, and Dagallier to this context yields a probabilistic bound on the Araki relative entropy, which can be formulated entirely in operator-algebraic terms. Using the criterion, it is shown that the Lorentzian massive Sine-Gordon model in the ultraviolet finite regime satisfies the relative entropy bound.

</details>


### [15] [Big Crunch on Julia and Anti-Julia Sets in the Integrable Limit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07308)
*Masatomi Iizawa,Satoru Saito,Yasuhito Narita,Yasuyuki Maeda,Hiromitsu Harada,Akira Shudo*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces the 'anti-Julia set'—the closure of divergences of periodic equations—and studies its interplay with the classical Julia set under a deformation parameter $a$ that interpolates between integrable ($a \to 0$) and chaotic regimes; it reveals a topological annihilation ('Big Crunch') at the integrable limit, analytically characterizes asymptotic behavior near $a=0$, and numerically visualizes the transition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain why Julia sets—hallmarks of chaos—vanish in integrable systems, by identifying a dual structure (anti-Julia set) and analyzing their coevolution across an integrability-breaking deformation.

Method: Analytical construction of pre-Julia/pre-anti-Julia sets via periodic equation divergences; asymptotic analysis of set boundaries near the integrable limit $a \to 0$; numerical visualization of set evolution under a complex map parametrized by real $a \in (0,1)$.

Result: For $0 < a \leq 1/2$, Julia and anti-Julia sets coincide as closed sets while their pre-sets remain disjoint; at $a \to 0$, they undergo critical collision and annihilation ('Big Crunch'); for $1/2 < a < 1$, the Julia set lies within the boundary of the pre-anti-Julia set; asymptotics of pre-anti-Julia set near $a=0$ are derived.

Conclusion: The disappearance of Julia sets in integrable systems is explained by the mutual annihilation of dual fractal structures—the Julia and anti-Julia sets—at the integrable limit, revealing a topological phase transition governed by parameter-driven set convergence and collapse.

Abstract: The Julia set is defined by the closure of repelling periodic points in chaotic systems. Why does this structure not appear in integrable systems? In this paper, we address this question by demonstrating the existence of the closure of divergences of the periodic equations, which we designate as the "anti-Julia set." We also call the sets before taking the closures the pre-Julia and pre-anti-Julia sets, respectively. We illustrate the transition mechanism by considering a complex map that interpolates between integrable and non-integrable dynamics, by introducing a real deformation parameter $a$. For $0<a\le 1/2$, we show that the Julia set and the anti-Julia set coincide in the complex plane, although the pre-Julia and pre-anti-Julia sets remain completely disjoint. At the integrable limit $a\to 0$, these two dual structures undergo a critical collision and subsequent annihilation, reminiscent of a cosmological Big Crunch. When $1/2<a<1$, on the other hand, the boundary of the pre-anti-Julia set includes the Julia set. We analytically characterize these phenomena, focusing in particular on the asymptotic behavior of the pre-anti-Julia set as it approaches the integrable limit, and provide numerical visualizations that elucidate the underlying mechanisms of this Big Crunch phenomenon.

</details>


### [16] [Towards Heterogeneous Exascale CFD with a Single Fortran Code Base: OpenMP Target Acceleration of the High-Order Unstructured Solver UCNS3D](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07387)
*Panagiotis Tsoutsanis,Michal Uminski,Christopher Day,David Henty*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents a single-source OpenMP target modernization of UCNS3D, a high-order unstructured finite-volume CFD solver, enabling portable, production-scale CPU/GPU acceleration while preserving numerical fidelity, Fortran code base, and workflows.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To avoid code fragmentation and reproducibility loss from rewriting mature high-order CFD solvers for accelerators, and to enable portable, standards-based heterogeneous acceleration without sacrificing numerical correctness or software sustainability.

Method: Single-source OpenMP target offloading applied to the full explicit time-advancement path of UCNS3D; techniques include persistent target data regions, flat runtime arrays, compile-time temporary storage sizing, explicit local algebra, target-callable routines, and GPU-aware MPI with device-resident buffers.

Result: Verification shows machine-precision agreement between CPU and GPU dissipation histories (LUMI, ARCHER2); CPU path speeds up 1.27–1.67×; GPU offload achieves same-node speed-ups of 2.71–4.05× and 84–101% strong-scaling efficiency on Taylor-Green vortex, LM1021, and NASA CRM benchmarks.

Conclusion: Standards-based OpenMP target provides portable, production-ready acceleration for high-order unstructured CFD—maintaining numerical fidelity, improving CPU performance, and ensuring software sustainability.

Abstract: Heterogeneous exascale systems are reshaping computational fluid dynamics, yet rewriting mature high-order solvers for accelerators can fragment code bases and weaken reproducibility. We present a single-source OpenMP target modernisation of UCNS3D, a high-order unstructured finite-volume solver for compressible flows. The common CPU/GPU implementation preserves its numerical formulation, Fortran code base, and workflows. Rather than porting isolated kernels, we enable the complete explicit time-advancement path: high-order reconstruction, gradient evaluation, inviscid and viscous fluxes, boundary treatment, halo exchange, and solution update. The implementation uses persistent target data regions, flat run-time arrays, compile-time sizing of temporary storage, explicit local algebra, target-callable routines, and GPU-aware MPI with device-resident buffers. These choices address the irregular stencils, complex data structures, and substantial temporary storage of high-order unstructured CFD. Verification using the compressible Taylor-Green vortex shows CPU and GPU dissipation histories agreeing to machine precision on LUMI and with published reference data. A single-node run on the ARCHER2 GPU platform provides an independent portability check. End-to-end performance is evaluated on fully populated nodes using Taylor-Green vortex, LM1021 sonic-boom, and NASA high-lift CRM benchmarks. Relative to the previous production implementation, the refactored CPU path is 1.27-1.67 times faster, while GPU offload delivers same-node speed-ups of 2.71-4.05 and 84-101 percent strong-scaling efficiency. The results show that standards-based OpenMP provides portable, production-scale acceleration without sacrificing numerical fidelity, CPU performance, or software sustainability.

</details>


### [17] [Möbius Invariant Dimers and Miquel Dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07485)
*Niklas C. Affolter,Luis Mühlhoff*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a Möbius-invariant correspondence between circle patterns and the dimer model, extending prior Euclidean-invariant results; it proves Miquel dynamics preserve the partition function, links convex t-embeddings to positive face weights, reveals a hidden PO(3,3) symmetry via Lorentz lift, and applies the framework to isoradial graphs, Doyle spirals, punctured tori, and isothermic patterns.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the known Euclidean-invariant correspondence between circle patterns and the dimer model to one respecting the natural conformal symmetry group PO(3,1) of circle patterns, and to uncover deeper geometric and algebraic structures (e.g., hidden PO(3,3) symmetry) governing integrability and positivity.

Method: Constructs a Möbius-invariant mapping from circle patterns to dimer models using t-embeddings and their Lorentz lifts; analyzes Miquel dynamics via discrete holomorphicity and cluster algebra-type transformations; derives positivity conditions from convexity of t-embeddings in hyperbolic space; leverages conformal geometry and representation theory of orthogonal groups.

Result: A PO(3,1)-invariant dimer correspondence for circle patterns; proof that Miquel dynamics preserve the dimer partition function; convexity of the t-embedding implies positivity of face weights; discovery of an extended symmetry group PO(3,3) arising from the Lorentz lift; unified treatment of isoradial graphs, Doyle spirals, once-punctured torus embeddings, and isothermic patterns under this framework.

Conclusion: The Möbius-invariant dimer correspondence provides a unifying geometric framework for integrable circle pattern dynamics, revealing deeper symmetries (PO(3,3)) and positivity conditions tied to convex t-embeddings; it generalizes and enriches both discrete complex analysis and statistical mechanics models on planar graphs.

Abstract: As is already known, there is a correspondence between circle patterns and the dimer model, which is invariant under Euclidean transformations. We establish a new correspondence that is invariant under the group of Möbius transformations PO(3,1), which is the natural symmetry group of circle patterns. We show that Miquel dynamics preserve the partition function, and that convexity of the t-embedding guarantees positivity of the face weights. We also show that the correspondence has a somewhat hidden symmetry group PO(3,3), which relates to the Lorentz lift of the t-embedding. Finally, we consider isoradial graphs, Doyle spirals, the once-punctured torus and isothermic circle patterns.

</details>


### [18] [Entropic repulsion to the middle layer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07578)
*Max Mihailescu,Ron Peled*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies discrete gradient Gibbs measures for height functions with even, convex interaction potentials W on Z^d, constrained to bounded integer intervals [-S,S]. It establishes uniqueness of the Gibbs measure (with exponential decay of correlations) in various dimensional and temperature regimes, proves magnetization bounds implying delocalization, and extends results to continuous and half-integer-valued height functions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how entropic repulsion affects gradient Gibbs measures with bounded height constraints and general convex interactions, resolving open questions from prior work (e.g., Bricmont–El Mellouki–Fröhlich, 1986) and extending uniqueness and delocalization results beyond the quadratic case.

Method: Probabilistic analysis of gradient Gibbs measures using coupling, contour arguments, and entropy estimates; adaptation of Pirogov–Sinai theory for low-temperature regime; construction of counterexamples for non-uniqueness via reflection positivity and layering arguments; extension to continuous and half-integer settings via approximation and symmetry arguments.

Result: Uniqueness of Gibbs measure with exponential decay of correlations in: (a) d=2 at all T; (b) d≥3 for W with non-increasing second derivative (e.g., |x|^p, p∈[1,2]); (c) d≥3 at low/high T. Delocalization shown via magnetization bound |m| < 1/2 for all d, W, T. Counterexamples for non-uniqueness constructed. Extensions to [-1,1]-valued and half-integer-valued heights proved.

Conclusion: Entropic repulsion enforces delocalization and often uniqueness for bounded-gradient height functions under general convex interactions; however, non-uniqueness is possible in high dimensions and specific W, showing sharp dependence on interaction structure and dimension—resolving longstanding questions and significantly generalizing prior quadratic results.

Abstract: We consider $\nabla\varphi$ height functions with even and convex interaction energy $W$ on the lattice $\mathbb{Z}^d$, which are restricted to take values in the set $\{-S, \ldots, S\}$ for some integer $S \ge 1$. We study the effect of entropic repulsion, which tends to push the spin values to the middle layer.
  We prove that the model has a unique Gibbs measure, with exponential decay of correlations, in the cases: (a) Dimension $d=2$ at all temperatures. (b) Dimensions $d\ge3$ at all temperatures, for a wide class of $W$ with non-increasing second derivative, including the family $W(x)=|x|^p$ for $p\in[1,2]$. (c) Dimensions $d\ge 3$ at both low and high temperatures, $T\in (0,\frac{W(1)d}{4(\ln d+\ln 8)})\cup(d W(2S),\infty]$, with the normalization $W(0)=0$. At low temperatures, our proof provides an alternative to Pirogov--Sinai methods.
  Conversely, we exhibit a class of even and convex interaction energies $W$ which, in high dimensions and suitable temperature regimes, have multiple Gibbs measures.
  Though uniqueness may fail, we show that the magnetization of every Gibbs measure lies in $(-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})$. This implies the delocalization of the model restricted to take values in $\{0,1,\ldots\}$ (i.e., conditioned to lie above a floor) for all dimensions, any even and convex $W$, and all temperatures.
  Our methods extend to additional setups: We prove that height functions taking values in the real interval $[-1,1]$ always have a unique Gibbs measure, a result previously proved only for the quadratic interaction. For height functions taking values in $\{-S+\frac{1}{2}, \ldots, S-\frac{1}{2}\}$, $S \ge1 $ integer, we prove that the magnetization of every Gibbs measure lies in $(-1,1)$.
  The special case $W(x)=x^2$ of our results addresses questions left open in the work of Bricmont--El Mellouki--Fröhlich (1986).

</details>


### [19] [Classification of Conformally Covariant 2-Tensors from the Kulkarni--Nomizu Product in Dimension 4](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07750)
*Yury N. Berdinsky*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: Classifies all natural, conformally covariant, symmetric (0,2)-tensors of weight -2 and order ≤4 in 4D; shows they form a 2D space spanned by the Bach and Eastwood-Singer tensors; proofs include formal verification in Lean 4 and exact computational validation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically characterize all natural, conformally covariant, symmetric (0,2)-tensors of fixed weight and low differential order in dimension 4—motivated by geometric analysis, conformal geometry, and the search for canonical curvature obstructions.

Method: Algebraic classification using invariant theory for natural tensors; explicit construction and linear independence analysis of candidates built from metric, Schouten tensor, covariant derivatives, and Kulkarni-Nomizu product; formal proof in Lean 4 (Mathlib) for algebraic core; exact rational arithmetic computation for constraint matrix (8×11) and divergence-free condition.

Result: The space of such tensors is precisely 2-dimensional; basis consists of the Bach tensor (order 2) and the Eastwood-Singer tensor (order 4); both tensors are shown to be the unique (up to scale) natural, conformally covariant, symmetric (0,2)-tensors of weight -2 and order ≤4 in 4D.

Conclusion: No other independent natural, conformally covariant, symmetric (0,2)-tensors of weight -2 and order ≤4 exist in dimension 4 beyond the Bach and Eastwood-Singer tensors; the classification is complete and rigorously verified via formal and computational methods.

Abstract: We classify all natural, conformally covariant, symmetric (0,2)-tensors of conformal weight -2 and differential order less than or equal to 4 in dimension 4, built from the metric, the Schouten tensor, covariant derivatives, and the Kulkarni-Nomizu product. We prove that the space of such tensors is exactly 2-dimensional, spanned by the Bach tensor (order 2) and the Eastwood-Singer tensor (order 4). The algebraic core is formally verified using Lean 4 with Mathlib. The 8x11 constraint matrix and divergence-free condition are verified computationally with exact rational arithmetic. Both English and Russian versions provided; Russian version available as ancillary.

</details>


### [20] [Equation of Motion for Open Thermodynamic Systems and Its Dynamical Equivalence with Mechanical and Electrical Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07810)
*E Aydiner*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives an explicit equation of motion for open thermodynamic systems directly from the first law via dimensional reduction, revealing its equivalence to a driven damped harmonic oscillator and a driven RLC circuit; it establishes universal dynamical structure across thermodynamic, mechanical, and electrical systems and constructs a consistent Lagrangian formulation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge the gap between the compact, energy-conservation form of the first law of thermodynamics and an explicit, time-dependent equation of motion—unlike mechanical and electrical systems, no such direct dynamical formulation existed for thermodynamics.

Method: Dimensional reduction applied to the first law for open thermodynamic systems; derivation of an equation of motion; dimensional analysis of effective coefficients; systematic cross-domain comparison (mechanical, electrical, thermodynamic); construction of a thermodynamic Lagrangian and verification of correspondence with mechanical/electrical Lagrangians.

Result: An explicit second-order ODE governing thermodynamic state evolution, structurally identical to that of a driven damped harmonic oscillator and a driven RLC circuit; demonstration of universal dynamical isomorphism across domains; a well-defined thermodynamic Lagrangian consistent with variational principles and analogous to those in mechanics and circuit theory.

Conclusion: The first law of thermodynamics admits a direct, rigorous derivation of an equation of motion for open systems; thermodynamic dynamics are not fundamentally distinct but share a universal dynamical framework with mechanical and electrical systems—enabling unified modeling, control, and interpretation across physics domains.

Abstract: Changes in the internal energy of a thermodynamic system are fundamentally described by the first law of thermodynamics. However, the first law has a highly compact form that does not explicitly reveal the dynamics of the system or provide a corresponding equation of motion. While equations of motion can be written explicitly for mechanical and electrical systems, an analogous dynamical formulation for thermodynamic systems has, to our knowledge, not been established directly from the first law. In this work, we show that, through dimensional reduction, the first law can be transformed from its compact thermodynamic form into an explicit equation of motion for an open thermodynamic system. Remarkably, the resulting equation reveals that the dynamics of the thermodynamic system is equivalent to that of a driven damped harmonic oscillator and a driven RLC electrical circuit. Detailed dimensional analyses of the derived equations and effective coefficients demonstrate the internal consistency of the resulting formulation. We further perform a systematic comparison of the mechanical, electrical, and thermodynamic systems and show that, despite their distinct physical origins, they share the same universal dynamical structure. Finally, we construct a Lagrangian formulation of the thermodynamic system and establish its correspondence with the Lagrangian descriptions of the mechanical and electrical systems. These results demonstrate that an explicit equation of motion can be generated directly from the first law of thermodynamics and reveal a common dynamical framework underlying thermodynamic, mechanical, and electrical systems.

</details>


### [21] [Energy diffusion in a pinned harmonic chain with free boundary condition and interactions of increasing range](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08138)
*Yipeng Lu,Wei Wang*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves convergence of empirical energy measures to the heat equation's weak solution for a 2D pinned harmonic chain with diverging-range interaction and rotational noise under specific scaling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how macroscopic diffusive behavior (e.g., heat conduction) emerges from microscopic stochastic lattice dynamics with long-range interactions.

Method: Rigorous probabilistic analysis using hydrodynamic limits, scaling limits, and measure-valued weak solutions; employs joint scaling where interaction range diverges microscopically but vanishes macroscopically.

Result: The expected empirical energy measures converge to the unique measure-valued weak solution of the heat equation.

Conclusion: Diffusive energy transport is robust even in the presence of diverging interaction range and conservative noise, confirming universality of the heat equation as the macroscopic limit.

Abstract: We consider a finite two-dimensional pinned harmonic chain with an interaction range that diverges with the system size and with conservative rotational noise. Under a joint scaling in which the interaction range diverges microscopically but vanishes macroscopically, we prove that the expected em- pirical energy measures converge to the unique measure-valued weak solution of the heat equation.

</details>


### [22] [Asymptotics of Ground States for Helium-Like and Bosonic Atoms in a Two-Cluster Region](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08257)
*Yukimi Goto*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the asymptotic behavior of ground-state wavefunctions for N-particle atomic systems in a two-cluster configuration, establishing precise decay rates and error bounds for the wavefunction's factorization as one particle escapes to infinity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the mathematical structure of multi-electron atomic systems in the limit where one electron separates far from the rest (two-cluster region), particularly near and at the ionization threshold — a regime critical for spectral theory and scattering analysis.

Method: Rigorous asymptotic analysis of Schrödinger eigenfunctions using spectral theory, weighted resolvent estimates, and careful control of Coulomb singularities; employs cluster decomposition, scaling arguments, and logarithmic corrections to derive uniform pointwise and L² error bounds.

Result: Proves that the N-particle ground state ψ factorizes asymptotically as ψ ≈ φ ⊗ √ρ with explicit remainder estimates: O(|x_N|^{-2+α} ln|x_N|) uniformly for |x_i| ≤ |x_N|^α, O(|x_N|^{-2}(ln|x_N|)^2) in the logarithmic region |x_i| ≤ R₀ ln|x_N|, and an O(|x_N|^{-2}) bound on the squared L² error — valid both below and at the essential spectrum threshold.

Conclusion: The asymptotic factorization of the many-body ground state holds with quantified, near-optimal decay rates even at the ionization threshold, confirming the physical picture of asymptotic decoupling and providing foundational estimates for rigorous scattering and resonance theory in atomic systems.

Abstract: We study the asymptotic behavior of positive ground states of $N$-particle atomic Coulomb Hamiltonians in a two-cluster region. For the $N$-particle ground state $ψ$, its one-particle density $ρ$, and $(N-1)$-particle ground state $φ$, we show that for $0<α<1$, as $|x_N|\to\infty$, $ψ(x_1,\dots,x_N)/\sqrt{ρ(x_N)}= φ(x_1,\dots,x_{N-1})+O(|x_N|^{-2+α} \ln |x_N|)$ uniformly in the region $|x_i|\le |x_N|^α$. In the region $|x_i|\le R_0\ln |x_N|$, the convergence rate is $O(|x_N|^{-2} (\ln |x_N|)^2)$. We also establish an $O(|x_N|^{-2})$ bound on the squared $L^2$ error. These results hold both below the essential spectrum and at threshold.

</details>


### [23] [Arithmetic triangular structures in the transfer-matrix of finite Kronig-Penney models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08523)
*Lidia Aceto,Pietro Antonio Grassi,Helmuth Robert Malonek*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives closed-form expressions for transmission and reflection amplitudes in the finite Kronig-Penney model using Chebyshev polynomials of the second kind, revealing novel structural links among quantum scattering, discrete convolution, and hypercomplex combinatorics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously derive closed-form expressions for transfer-matrix entries and combinatorial coefficients in the finite Kronig-Penney model with Dirac delta potentials—previously identified but not analytically established.

Method: Expressing the Nth power of the unit-cell transfer matrix via Chebyshev polynomials of the second kind to obtain exact analytical forms of global transmission and reflection amplitudes.

Result: Explicit closed-form representations of transmission and reflection amplitudes; discovery of a structural correspondence between quantum scattering, discrete convolutional patterns, and hypercomplex combinatorial structures.

Conclusion: The Chebyshev-based analytical framework fully characterizes the transfer-matrix structure of the finite Kronig-Penney model and uncovers deep mathematical connections across scattering theory, discrete mathematics, and hypercomplex algebra.

Abstract: This work provides a complete analytical characterization of the transfer- matrix structure associated with the finite Kronig-Penney model consisting of one-dimensional arrays of Dirac delta potentials recently introduced by Figueroa et al. (2025). Although their study identified the emergence of specific transfer-matrix entries and related combinatorial coefficients, a rig- orous derivation of the corresponding closed-form expressions has not yet been established. By expressing the N th power of the unit-cell transfer ma- trix in terms of Chebyshev polynomials of the second kind, we obtain explicit closed-form representations for the global transmission and reflection ampli- tudes. The proposed formulation reveals a previously unrecognized structural correspondence between multiple quantum scattering processes, discrete con- volutional patterns, and hypercomplex combinatorial structures.

</details>


### [24] [The phase coordinates transformation in Moyal noncommutative framework 2-dimensional harmonic oscillator and Painlevé second equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08525)
*Irfan Mahmood*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces new coordinate transformations linking canonical to purely noncommuting phase-space coordinates within the Moyal noncommutative framework, yielding a 16-component traceless antisymmetric tensor and enabling construction of noncommutative analogs of the 2D harmonic oscillator and Painlevé II equation, with implications for superintegrability, symmetry enhancement, and special polynomial generation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the physical applicability of Moyal noncommutative geometry beyond standard Heisenberg-type noncommutativity by incorporating simultaneous space-space and space-momentum noncommutativity, and to explore its consequences for integrable systems and symmetry structures.

Method: Derivation of novel canonical-to-noncommutative phase-space transformations consistent with Moyal brackets; construction of a 16-component traceless antisymmetric tensor encoding the noncommutative deformation parameters; application to formulate noncommutative versions of the 2D harmonic oscillator and Painlevé II equation; analysis of resulting deformed Hamiltonians, symmetries, and polynomial solutions (e.g., Yablonskii–Vorobev polynomials).

Result: A consistent Moyal-based noncommutative phase-space formalism incorporating both position-position and position-momentum noncommutativity; explicit noncommutative analogs of the 2D harmonic oscillator exhibiting superintegrability and deformed Painlevé II dynamics; emergence of extra symmetries in deformed Hamiltonians; successful generation of the first Yablonskii–Vorobev polynomial via noncommutative Painlevé II coordinate transformations.

Conclusion: The proposed transformations provide a physically meaningful extension of Moyal noncommutativity into full phase space, enriching integrable system theory by revealing new symmetry structures, preserving superintegrability in deformed settings, and establishing concrete links between noncommutative geometry and Painlevé transcendents and their associated polynomials.

Abstract: This work aims to explore the physical application of Moyal noncom- mutative formalism with the the presentation of new transformations which connect the old canonical coordinates to purly noncommuting phase coordi- nates through the parameters describe the Moyal noncommutative deforma- tion. These transformations are consistent with the Moyal noncommunica- tive brackets and build sixteen components traceless anti-symmetric tensor. This formalism incorporates space-space and space-momentum noncommu- taivity rather then noncommutativity (Heisenberg quantum commutation ) only for the canonical coordinates. The transformations imply to construct noncommutative analogs of 2-dimensional harmonic oscillator with
  its superintegrability and Painlevé second equation. The associated deformed Hamiltonians additionally involve some extra symmetries and reveal a deep physical understanding of about the additional symmetries with noncommunicative structure. The Painlevé second noncommutative coordinates transformation are also efficiently applied to generate the first Yablonskii Vorobev polynomial with Painlevé second parameter transformation.

</details>


### [25] [On spatial decoherence in many-body systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08910)
*Stefano Marcantoni,Marco Merkli*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes quantum decoherence in a many-body system coupled to a bosonic reservoir at infinite coupling strength, identifying an exact fine-grained timescale (τ = λt) where decoherence is resolved; it constructs a controlled approximate effective evolution map—generically Markovian but non-semigroup—and rigorously explains spatial localization of macroscopic quantum objects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how strong system-reservoir coupling leads to instantaneous spatial decoherence in many-body quantum systems, and to resolve the temporal structure of this process beyond the infinite-coupling limit.

Method: Mathematical analysis of the limit λ → ∞ with time rescaling t ∝ λ⁻ᵃ; identification of critical scaling α = 1 (fine-grained scale τ = λt); construction of an approximate effective evolution map with rigorous error bounds using operator-theoretic and asymptotic techniques.

Result: Decoherence is instantaneous for 0 ≤ α < 1 and trivial for α > 1; at α = 1, the dynamics is nontrivial and exactly resolvable; the effective evolution is Markovian but violates semigroup composition (i.e., lacks complete positivity or time-homogeneity in standard Lindbladian form); rigorous derivation of position-space localization for macroscopic objects.

Conclusion: Strong coupling induces instantaneous spatial decoherence generically, but its dynamical resolution reveals a non-semigroup Markovian effective evolution on the fine-grained scale τ = λt; this provides a mathematically rigorous foundation for environmental-induced localization of macroscopic quantum systems.

Abstract: We study the dynamics of a many-body quantum system strongly interacting with a bosonic reservoir. The coupling is given by a potential operator for the system and it is linear in the field. We show that in the limit of infinite coupling strength the system undergoes instantaneous spatial decoherence. To resolve the decoherence process in time we consider large coupling strengths $λ$ and short times scaling as $t\propto λ^{-α}$, with $α\ge0$, in the limit $λ\rightarrow\infty$. On short time scales $α>1$ the dynamics is trivial while on longer ones $0\leα<1$ the decoherence is instantaneous. We show that the decoherence process is resolved exactly for $α=1$, defining the fine-grained time scale $τ=λt$. We construct an approximate effective evolution map of the many-body system with controlled error estimates. Generically, the effective dynamics is markovian, but not given by a dynamical semigroup. As a physical application we give a rigorous description of the phenomenon of localization of macroscopic quantum objects in position space.

</details>


### [26] [The inverse problem of time-dependent density functional theory on the torus](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08984)
*Asbjørn Bækgaard Lauritsen,Mathieu Lewin,Jakob Oldenburg*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: Proves existence and uniqueness of an external potential that reproduces a given sufficiently regular one-particle density in the many-body Schrödinger equation—foundational for time-dependent density functional theory (TDDFT).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish rigorous mathematical foundations for time-dependent density functional theory, specifically the existence and uniqueness of the mapping from time-dependent densities to external potentials.

Method: Mathematical analysis of the many-body Schrödinger equation; proof of existence and uniqueness of an external potential generating a prescribed one-particle density, under sufficient regularity conditions and including Coulomb interaction and extended nuclei.

Result: For any sufficiently regular one-particle density, there exists a unique external potential such that the many-body Schrödinger solution yields exactly that density; this justifies exact non-interacting reproduction of interacting-system time-dependent densities.

Conclusion: The Hohenberg–Kohn-type uniqueness theorem is extended to the time-dependent setting with physical interactions, solidifying the theoretical basis of TDDFT.

Abstract: We prove that, for any given sufficiently regular one-particle density, there exists a unique external potential for which the solution to the many-body Schrödinger equation has this prescribed density. This implies in particular that one can exactly reproduce the time-dependent density of an interacting system using non-interacting electrons, which is at the heart of time-dependent density functional theory. Our result covers the Coulomb interaction and densities arising from extended nuclei.

</details>
