### [27] [Minimal spontaneous baryogenesis from flavor](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05605)
*Martin A. Mojahed,Alfredo Stanzione*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes that the Froggatt-Nielsen mechanism—extended with a global horizontal U(1)_H symmetry broken before inflation—generates an axion-like particle whose misalignment-driven coherent oscillation induces spontaneous CPT violation; combined with B−L-violating Weinberg operator (neutrino mass generation), it produces the observed baryon asymmetry of the Universe (BAU), thereby unifying flavor structure, neutrino masses, and baryogenesis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the Standard Model flavor puzzle while simultaneously explaining the origin of the baryon asymmetry of the Universe (BAU) and light neutrino masses within a unified framework—specifically, by leveraging the Froggatt-Nielsen (FN) mechanism's horizontal U(1)_H symmetry and its cosmological consequences.

Method: The authors analyze the cosmological evolution of a pseudo-Nambu-Goldstone boson (axion-like particle) arising from spontaneous breaking of a global U(1)_H symmetry in the minimal FN setup. They incorporate spontaneous CPT violation induced by coherent axion oscillations and couple it to B−L-violating dynamics via the dimension-five Weinberg operator. A complete set of Boltzmann-like evolution equations for SM particle asymmetries is solved numerically, including effects of baryonic isocurvature and entropy dilution from axion decays.

Result: The mechanism robustly generates a baryon asymmetry matching or exceeding the observed BAU (η_B ≈ 6 × 10⁻¹⁰); successful reproduction requires axion oscillation temperatures T_osc > 10¹¹ GeV and decay constants f_a ∈ [10¹³–10¹⁴, >10¹⁷] GeV. Isocurvature and dilution constraints are satisfied in this parameter space.

Conclusion: The minimal Froggatt-Nielsen mechanism—with a global horizontal U(1)_H broken pre-inflation—can simultaneously explain fermion flavor hierarchies, light neutrino masses, and the baryon asymmetry of the Universe through axion-driven spontaneous CPT violation and leptogenesis-like dynamics; the framework is generalizable to other global horizontal symmetries and axion initial conditions.

Abstract: In its minimal realization, the Froggatt-Nielsen (FN) mechanism addresses the Standard Model (SM) flavor puzzle via the spontaneous breaking of a horizontal $U(1)_H$ symmetry. If $U(1)_H$ is global and broken before the end of inflation, it gives rise to a pseudo-Nambu-Goldstone boson (axion-like particle) with a homogeneous initial misalignment, whose subsequent coherent evolution sources spontaneous $CPT$ violation in the SM sector. We show that in the presence of $B-L$-violating processes $-$ parametrized here model-independently via the dimension-five Weinberg operator responsible for light neutrino masses $-$ the minimal FN setup can generate the baryon asymmetry of the Universe (BAU), linking the origin of flavor directly to the BAU and neutrino masses. Solving a complete set of evolution equations for SM asymmetries, we demonstrate that this mechanism can readily yield a baryon asymmetry comparable to the observed value, or even exceeding it by several orders of magnitude. Accounting for baryonic isocurvature and entropy dilution from axion decays, we find that the observed BAU is successfully reproduced for axion oscillation temperatures above $10^{11}$ GeV and axion decay constants ranging from $10^{13\cdots 14}$ GeV to above $10^{17}$ GeV. Finally, while we focus on a global $U(1)_H$ symmetry and axion motion from standard misalignment, our framework is readily extendable to other global horizontal symmetries and arbitrary axion motion.

</details>


### [28] [The Colors of Jet Quenching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05609)
*Hannah Bossi,Maxence Larose,Yacine Mehtar-Tani*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses jet nuclear modification factors and energy-energy correlators to extract quark/gluon quenching factors and medium resolution scale; it finds Casimir scaling violation under coherent energy loss, but restoration when including color decoherence via antenna energy loss—supporting partial jet substructure resolution in the QGP.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether the quark-gluon plasma (QGP) resolves internal jet structure—i.e., induces color decoherence—by testing Casimir scaling of parton energy loss, a key signature distinguishing coherent vs. decoherent energy loss mechanisms.

Method: Data-driven extraction combining inclusive jet R_AA (nuclear modification factor) and energy-energy correlators (EEC) in the small-angle region; comparison of two theoretical frameworks: (1) coherent energy loss (null hypothesis), and (2) antenna-based energy loss incorporating color decoherence.

Result: Coherent energy loss fails to reproduce data and violates Casimir scaling; antenna energy loss with decoherence successfully describes both R_AA and EEC data and restores Casimir scaling.

Conclusion: The restoration of Casimir scaling under antenna energy loss provides evidence that the QGP partially resolves jet substructure via color decoherence—a hallmark of medium-induced jet fragmentation modification in heavy-ion collisions.

Abstract: We combine inclusive jet nuclear modification factors with energy-energy correlators to perform a data-driven extraction of the quark and gluon quenching factors and the medium resolution scale from the small-angle region. Taking coherent energy loss as a null hypothesis, we find jet quenching factors incompatible with Casimir scaling. Incorporating color decoherence through antenna energy loss improves the description of the data and restores Casimir scaling, providing evidence that the quark-gluon plasma partially resolves the internal structure of jets in heavy-ion collisions.

</details>


### [29] [Energy-energy correlators and color decoherence from a generating functional](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05614)
*Yacine Mehtar-Tani*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a generating-functional approach using jet calculus at leading-logarithmic accuracy to model jets in quark-gluon plasma, yielding coupled evolution equations for the jet nuclear modification factor and energy-energy correlators (EECs); it treats both incoherent and coherent-to-incoherent transitions (in the large-$N_c$ limit), and demonstrates how combining EECs with $R_{AA}$ provides complementary constraints on jet energy loss magnitude, flavor dependence, and medium-induced color decoherence scale.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To simultaneously constrain jet energy loss magnitude and flavor dependence, and the medium resolution angle governing color decoherence, by going beyond single-observable analyses like $R_{AA}$ alone.

Method: Jet calculus at leading-logarithmic accuracy; generating-functional formalism for the hard-collinear sector; derivation of coupled evolution equations for the jet nuclear modification factor ($R_{AA}$) and energy-energy correlators (EECs); treatment of fully incoherent regime and coherent-to-incoherent transition in the large-$N_c$ limit.

Result: New coupled evolution equations for $R_{AA}$ and EECs; explicit formulation of EEC evolution across coherence regimes; demonstration that joint analysis of $R_{AA}$ and EECs gives complementary sensitivity to global suppression and internal jet structure modifications.

Conclusion: The framework enables simultaneous, differential constraints on jet quenching parameters—specifically energy loss magnitude/flavor dependence and the medium resolution angle—by exploiting orthogonal information encoded in integrated suppression ($R_{AA}$) and intra-jet correlations (EECs).

Abstract: Using jet calculus at leading-logarithmic accuracy, we develop a generating-functional approach to describe the hard-collinear sector of jets propagating through the quark-gluon plasma. This framework yields coupled evolution equations for the jet nuclear modification factor and energy-energy correlators (EECs). We first construct the EEC evolution in the fully incoherent regime, and then derive evolution equations that account for the transition from the coherent to the incoherent limit in the large-$N_c$ limit. Furthermore, we show that combining the EEC with the jet nuclear modification factor provides complementary sensitivity to the overall jet suppression and to medium-induced modifications of the jet's internal structure. This framework therefore offers a way to constrain the magnitude and flavor dependence of jet energy loss, together with the medium resolution angle governing color decoherence.

</details>

Main category: hep-ph

Abstract: We propose the use of sphere-and-plate Casimir force measurement setups to detect ultralight vector dark matter. We demonstrate that sub-picometer signals can be parametrically amplified to be detectable. We present a novel improvement that allows the continuous tuning of the natural frequency of the system, opening new research opportunities at the submicron scale. We show that the improved setup can determine the dark matter mass with high accuracy, significantly expand the detectable mass range, and achieve the best sensitivity to date in the $8\times10^{-14}\sim1\times10^{-12}{\rm~ eV}$ mass range.

</details>

Abstract: We compile and recompute a subset of the landscape of constraints for macroscopic dark matter (macros), explicitly including the dependence on the fraction of dark matter in macros $f_{\rm dm}$. This allows us to map these constraints to scenarios with extended mass functions for the macros, which is a generic outcome for many of the assembly processes relevant to macro formation. We compute a number of examples of constraints for extended mass functions, finding that in many cases, wider mass functions can be severely constrained by just the interactions of the tails of the distributions. Finally, we present the code and data required for computing these limits and generating these plots in a publicly accessible repository.

</details>

Abstract: We perform a quantitative analysis of the $B^{0}$ meson spectroscopic parameters in a hot and dense medium. Moreover, these temperatures move toward lower values as the baryon chemical potential increases. On the other hand, for various fixed temperatures, both the mass and decay constant exhibit a slight initial increase with increasing baryon chemical potential, followed by a subsequent decrease, eventually vanishing at sufficiently high chemical potentials. The vanishing points for the decay constant is determined to be in baryon chemical potential interval $(0.23-0.83)~\mathrm{GeV}$. For the mass, the decrease at $T=0$ is much weaker than at finite temperatures; excluding this case, the vanishing points for the mass are determined to be in baryon chemical potential interval $(0.38-1.43)~\mathrm{GeV}$ for different fixed temperatures. It is further observed that the baryon chemical potential values corresponding to the vanishing of both the mass and decay constant move to lower values as the temperature increases. In the $T \to 0$ and $μ_B \to 0$ limit ...

### [33] [Agnostic search for a vector-like top partner at a lepton collider via the recoil mass technique](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05689)
*Higinio Valle,Cristina Oropeza Barrera,Oscar Ochoa-Valeriano*

TL;DR: This paper studies single vector-like top partner (T) production at a 3 TeV e+e− collider using a recoil-mass-based, decay- and mass-agnostic selection; it quantifies discovery reach in the (m_T, κ_T) plane under ideal and realistic conditions (including -80% e− polarization and ISR), finding >3σ significance for low m_T when κ_T ≳ 0.3.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe vector-like top partners—key candidates in BSM physics addressing the hierarchy problem—via model-independent, high-sensitivity searches at future high-energy e+e− colliders, circumventing assumptions about decay modes or mass.

Method: Uses fully simulated events processed through a common reconstruction chain; employs recoil mass as core observable for a decay- and mass-agnostic selection; validates baseline without beam polarization or ISR; then evaluates impact of realistic conditions: -80% e− polarization and initial-state radiation; performs statistical significance scan over m_T and coupling κ_T.

Result: Under baseline conditions, statistical significance exceeds 3σ for lowest benchmark m_T when κ_T ≳ 0.3; full evaluation maps discovery reach across (m_T, κ_T) parameter space including ISR and polarization effects.

Conclusion: Recoil-mass-driven, agnostic selection enables robust sensitivity to single T production at 3 TeV e+e− colliders; realistic beam conditions moderately affect reach but do not preclude discovery for moderate couplings and light-to-intermediate masses.

Abstract: A study of the single production of a vector-like top partner $T$ at a $\sqrt{s}=3$ TeV $e^{+}e^{-}$ collider is presented, using a decay-mode and mass-agnostic event selection built around the recoil mass observable. The analysis relies on fully simulated samples processed through a common reconstruction and event selection chain. The baseline selection is validated without beam polarization or initial-state radiation (ISR). The impact of more realistic running conditions, including ISR together with an $e^{-}$ beam polarization of $-80\%$, is then evaluated and the discovery reach is mapped as a function of the top partner mass $m_{T}$ and coupling strength $κ_{T}$. In the baseline scan, the estimated statistical significance rises above $3σ$ for the lowest benchmark masses once $κ_{T}$ exceeds approximately 0.3.

</details>


### [34] [Fermion mass hierarchy and flavor signals from a $D_4$ dihedral symmetry within a 2HDM-Tx with four-zero textures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05773)
*M. A. Arroyo-Ureña,E. Díaz,J. L. Díaz-Cruz,H. Hernández-Arellano*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper introduces an extended Two-Higgs Doublet Model (2HDM-Tx) with a D₄ symmetry enforcing a four-zero texture in fermion mass matrices via non-equivalent Yukawa matrices; it suppresses FCNCs, explains fermion mass hierarchy, includes a dark matter candidate, and accommodates 95 GeV and 152 GeV LHC resonance excesses while satisfying Higgs and LFV constraints; it also assesses HL-LHC sensitivity to h → τμ.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address flavor-changing neutral currents (FCNCs), fermion mass hierarchy, and dark matter within a theoretically consistent framework, while explaining anomalous LHC resonance excesses at 95 GeV and 152 GeV.

Method: Constructs a Texturized 2HDM with D₄ discrete symmetry imposing a Hermitian four-zero texture on fermion mass matrices using non-equivalent Yukawa matrices; performs phenomenological analysis including collider constraints (LHC Higgs data, resonance fits), LFV bounds (e.g., μ → eγ), and dark matter relic density/direct detection compatibility; computes HL-LHC sensitivity to h → τμ.

Result: The model simultaneously fits the 95 GeV and 152 GeV neutral resonance excesses, satisfies all current LHC Higgs signal strength and coupling constraints, evades LFV limits (e.g., BR(μ → eγ) < 4.2×10⁻¹³), predicts viable scalar spectra with constrained parameter space, and yields observable h → τμ rates at HL-LHC for certain benchmark points.

Conclusion: The 2HDM-Tx with D₄-induced non-equivalent Yukawa textures provides a unified explanation of multiple anomalies and hierarchies while remaining testable via h → τμ at HL-LHC; it demonstrates that structural fermion mass textures can concurrently address flavor, collider, and dark matter phenomenology.

Abstract: We propose an extended Texturized Two-Higgs Doublet Model (2HDM-Tx) where a discrete $D_4$ symmetry predicts a four-zero texture in the fermion mass matrices. In contrast to the standard ansatz where the underlying Yukawa matrices are assumed to be structurally identical, we introduce a novel configuration of non-equivalent matrices that complementarily generate a specific Hermitian four-zero mass structure. This framework naturally suppresses flavor-changing neutral currents and provides a structural rationale for the fermion mass hierarchy, while in addition including a dark matter candidate. As a concrete phenomenological application, we show that the model simultaneously accommodates the persistent neutral resonance excesses at $95\text{ GeV}$ and $152\text{ GeV}$, satisfies current \text{LHC} Higgs data, and is consistent with the stringent upper bounds from lepton flavor violation processes. These combined constraints severely restrict the parameter space of the scalar phenomenology. Finally, we study the prospects for the discovery of the lepton-flavor violating process $h \to τμ$ at the High-Luminosity LHC.

</details>


### [35] [Deconfining Phase Transition under Real Rotation: A Matrix Model Study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05865)
*Qianqian Du,Jing He,Yun Guo,Mei Huang,Enke Wang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs a matrix model to study how real rotation affects the deconfining phase transition of a pure gluon plasma in a finite cylindrical volume; it finds that rotation induces radial inhomogeneity, lowers the critical temperature $T_c$ (especially near the axis), and its $Ω$-dependence hinges on the assumed gluon effective mass—monotonic decrease for constant mass, non-monotonic for $Ω$-dependent mass—and confirms finite-volume suppression of $T_c$, while noting unresolved discrepancies with lattice and phenomenological results.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguities in how real rigid rotation modifies the deconfining phase transition of pure Yang-Mills plasma, particularly under finite-volume confinement and within relativistic kinematic constraints ($\mathcal{R} \Omega < 1$), and to reconcile conflicting predictions from lattice QCD and phenomenological models.

Method: Constructs a matrix model incorporating both perturbative (one-loop effective potential in background field) and non-perturbative (gluon effective mass correction) contributions; solves for the Polyakov loop expectation value to locate the deconfinement transition; analyzes radial dependence and $\Omega$-scaling of $T_c$ under two effective mass assumptions (constant vs. $\Omega$-dependent); benchmarks against $\Omega = 0$ finite-volume limit and existing lattice/phenomenological data.

Result: Real rotation induces radial inhomogeneity in $T_c$, consistent with Tolman-Ehrenfest law; $T_c$ decreases radially outward; for constant gluon mass, $T_c$ monotonically decreases with $\Omega$; for $\Omega$-dependent mass, $T_c$ exhibits non-monotonic behavior at small $\Omega$; finite volume alone suppresses $T_c$ relative to infinite-volume limit; quantitative disagreement persists across current theoretical and numerical approaches.

Conclusion: The deconfining transition in rotating gluon plasma is sensitive to both rotational kinematics and the infrared structure (effective mass) of gauge fields; no universal $\Omega$-dependence of $T_c$ emerges without specifying the mass model; finite-size and rotation effects are entangled; definitive conclusions require improved first-principles input—especially on the gluon thermal mass in rotating frames—and higher-precision lattice simulations at physical $\mathcal{R}\Omega$.

Abstract: We construct a matrix model to study the deconfining phase transition for a pure gluon plasma that is confined in a cylinder of radius ${\cal R}$ and rotating rigidly at a real-valued angular velocity $Ω$, satisfying $\mathcal{R} Ω<1$. The deconfining phase transition arises due to the competition between two terms that constitute the matrix model. The perturbative term comes from the one-loop effective potential computed in the presence of a background field, while the non-perturbative term represents a correction to the perturbative contribution which is brought about by taking into account an effective mass of the gauge fields. Our results show that real rotation induces a radial inhomogeneity of the system and the deconfining temperature $T_c$ drops away from the rotation axis which is consistent with the Tolman-Ehrenfest law. As for the $Ω$-dependence of $T_c$, it relies on our assumptions of the gluon effective mass. For a constant mass, $T_c$ is found to always decrease with increasing $Ω$. A non-monotonic behavior of $T_c$ shows up when a $Ω$-dependent mass is considered, leading to a qualitative change in the region of small angular velocity. In addition, by setting $Ω=0$ to eliminate rotational effects, we also demonstrate that the finite-volume effect reduces the deconfining temperature relative to the infinite-volume limit. Comparisons between our results and those from various lattice simulations and phenomenological models suggest that controversy remains over how the deconfining phase transition is modified by real rotation and further work is required to reach a definite conclusion.

</details>


### [36] [Decipher the nature of glueball candidate $X(2370)$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05908)
*Sheng-Qi Zhang,Bing-Dong Wan,Cong-Feng Qiao*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates the internal structure of the X(2370) resonance—assessed as a pseudoscalar glueball candidate—by analyzing three-pseudoscalar decay ratios and proposing two resonance-resolved ratios to distinguish among compact tetraquark, hybrid, and trigluon glueball interpretations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Flavor-singletness of X(2370) is insufficient to uniquely identify its constituent structure (e.g., trigluon glueball vs. hybrid vs. tetraquark); decay pattern analysis is needed to resolve this ambiguity.

Method: The authors compute and compare theoretical three-pseudoscalar decay ratios—including resonance-resolved ratios a₀(1450)π/[K₀*(1430)K̄ + c.c.] and φφ/[b₁(1235)ρ]—across competing structural models (trigluon glueball, hybrid, compact tetraquark).

Result: Current data favor a flavor-singlet hybrid interpretation for X(2370), though a trigluon glueball remains compatible; crucially, the two proposed ratios exhibit parametrically separated predictions across models, enabling experimental discrimination.

Conclusion: The constituent nature of X(2370) is not settled by flavor singletness alone; the proposed decay ratios provide model-discriminating observables that turn structural ambiguity into a testable experimental question.

Abstract: Recent evidence for an approximately flavor-singlet assignment has strengthened the case for $X(2370)$ as a pseudoscalar glueball candidate. However, flavor singletness alone does not uniquely determine its constituent structure. Using calculated three-pseudoscalar decay ratios, we find that current data favor a flavor-singlet hybrid while remaining compatible with a trigluon glueball. More importantly, we identify two resonance-resolved ratios, $a_0(1450)π/[K_0^*(1430)\bar K+\mathrm{c.c.}]$ and $φφ/[b_1(1235)ρ]$, whose predicted hierarchies place the compact-tetraquark, hybrid, and trigluon-glueball realizations in parametrically separated regions. These observables convert the constituent ambiguity of $X(2370)$ into a directly testable experimental question.

</details>


### [37] [Mass Generation for Axion Like Particles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06030)

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reviews the current status of axions and axion-like particles, emphasizing how exotic axion models extend the traditional parameter space and addressing challenges in explaining their mass origins while exploring links to other Standard Model puzzles.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the Strong CP problem via the QCD axion and explore how exotic axion models broaden the viable parameter space and potentially connect to other unresolved issues in the Standard Model.

Method: Literature review and conceptual analysis of axion models, focusing on mass generation mechanisms and theoretical consistency with the Standard Model.

Result: Demonstrates that exotic axion models significantly expand the traditional axion parameter space and suggest nontrivial connections between axion mass origins and other Standard Model problems.

Conclusion: Exotic axion models provide a richer theoretical framework for solving the Strong CP problem and may serve as bridges to deeper physics beyond the Standard Model.

Abstract: The most widely accepted solution to the Strong CP problem is the QCD axion, whose traditional parameter space is relatively constrained. However, recent studies on exotic axion models have shown that this space can be significantly extended, opening up a wide range of previously unexplored possibilities for model building. I first review the current status of axions and axion-like particles, then focus on the challenges related to the origin of their masses and explore potential connections to other unresolved issues within the Standard Model.

</details>


### [38] [Improved analyses of the electroweak phase transition and its phenomenology in the Georgi-Machacek model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06139)
*Cheng-Wei Chiang,Kazuki Enomoto*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies the electroweak phase transition (EWPT) in the Georgi-Machacek model—a beyond-Standard-Model scenario with isospin-triplet scalars—using a thermally resummed one-loop effective potential and RG-improved couplings/fields; it identifies viable parameter regions via Bayesian analysis constrained by theory and experiment, and shows that strong first-order EWPT points are testable via Higgs decays, di-Higgs production, and stochastic gravitational waves at next-generation experiments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether the Georgi-Machacek model can realize a strong first-order electroweak phase transition—potentially enabling electroweak baryogenesis—and to assess its experimental detectability given theoretical consistency and current collider and precision constraints.

Method: Computes the finite-temperature effective potential at one-loop with thermal resummation; includes custodial symmetry breaking via renormalization group evolution of couplings and classical fields; performs Bayesian parameter inference incorporating theoretical bounds (e.g., perturbativity, vacuum stability) and experimental constraints (e.g., Higgs signal strengths, diboson searches, electroweak precision observables).

Result: Identifies physically allowed parameter regions supporting a strong first-order EWPT; finds that such scenarios predict enhanced h → Zγ/γγ, observable di-Higgs production at HL-LHC/FCC, and stochastic gravitational wave spectra potentially detectable by LISA or BBO.

Conclusion: The Georgi-Machacek model remains a viable and testable framework for a strong first-order electroweak phase transition; its phenomenological signatures span collider physics and gravitational-wave astronomy, making it highly accessible to next-generation experiments.

Abstract: The Georgi-Machacek model is a promising new physics scenario with isospin-triplet scalar fields, whose effects can qualitatively change the nature of the electroweak phase transition compared with the Standard Model. We examine the electroweak phase transition in this model by using the thermally resummed one-loop effective potential. We also incorporate effects of custodial symmetry breaking due to radiative corrections by evolving the couplings and classical fields according to the renormalization group equations. We identify viable parameter regions by Bayesian analysis using theoretical requirements and the latest experimental bounds. We then investigate the electroweak phase transition in the resulting parameter regions and discuss its phenomenological implications, such as Higgs boson decays into neutral gauge bosons, di-Higgs production at high-energy colliders, and stochastic gravitational waves associated with a first-order electroweak phase transition. We find that parameter points for the strong first-order electroweak phase transition can be verified by these observables at next-generation experiments.

Unlike matter-induced perturbations, the Planck-scale correction is constant along the neutrino trajectory. Consequently, its effect on the transition probability remains bounded and independent of the baseline at the amplitude level, while the associated modification of the effective mass-squared splitting produces a phase mismatch that increases linearly with propagation distance. Our results indicate that the accumulated phase drift provides a particularly promising observable for probing Planck-scale mass corrections and for distinguishing a possible quantum-gravity contribution from conventional matter-induced effects.

Abstract: Hadronic decay channels can provide direct information on the momentum-space structure of confined quark systems. The BS solutions yield nearby bare $3\,^3S_1$ and $2\,^3D_1$ states at 4051 and 4110 MeV, respectively, whose opposite displacements from the physical vector states motivate an effective two-state level-repulsion description. By comparing the matched open-charm channels $D\bar D$, $D\bar D^*+{\rm c.c.}$, $D_s\bar D_s$, and $D_s\bar D_s^*+{\rm c.c.}$ with the same leading $P$-wave threshold behavior, we show that channel-dependent overlap kernels act as momentum-space nodal filters. Result: They identify that near-threshold endothermic BDM scattering can naturally confine the signal between sharp kinematic recoil endpoints—suppressing low- and high-energy sidebands—and present benchmark spectra consistent with the observed 248-keV candidate; they also outline testable predictions for other targets (e.g., argon, germanium) and large-volume liquid-scintillator experiments.

Conclusion: Light boosted dark matter offers a viable, theoretically motivated alternative to heavy halo DM for explaining the LZ anomaly, with distinctive spectral features and falsifiable signatures across multiple detection channels.

Abstract: The LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) Collaboration has reported a nuclear-recoil candidate at $E_R=248\pm23_{\rm stat}\pm23_{\rm sys}$ keV, with a maximum local significance of $3.4σ$ and a global significance of $2.6σ$. A prominent interpretation invokes heavy halo dark matter near an inelastic threshold and therefore depends sensitively on the poorly constrained high-speed tail of the Galactic velocity distribution. In this Letter, we propose a qualitatively different possibility based on light boosted dark matter (BDM), whose incident energy is determined primarily by the dark-sector mass spectrum. We consider multi-component scenarios in which the boosted state scatters elastically or inelastically off xenon nuclei. For elastic scattering, pseudoscalar-mediated momentum dependence suppresses low-energy recoils. Near-threshold endothermic scattering of a nearly monochromatic BDM flux can instead confine the signal between kinematically determined recoil endpoints, suppressing events in both the low- and high-energy sidebands. The upscattered state may furthermore decay invisibly within the dark sector, preserving a single-nuclear-recoil signature without requiring it to be detector-stable. We present representative benchmark spectra and discuss complementary tests using other target nuclei and large-volume liquid-scintillator experiments.

</details>


### [50] [GUT-induced FCC signatures of the LUX-ZEPLIN event](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06750)
*Wojciech Kotlarski,Kamila Kowalska,Enrico Maria Sessolo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes that the LUX-ZEPLIN large-recoil event may be explained by inelastic scattering of pseudo-Dirac Higgsino dark matter, and shows that a GUT-inspired SU(6) model links the required small mass splitting δm to a 10-TeV-scale Z′ boson detectable at FCC-hh via dilepton resonances.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the anomalous large-recoil event observed by LUX-ZEPLIN using pseudo-Dirac dark matter (e.g., MSSM Higgsino), and to assess whether the implied mass splitting δm can point to testable new physics—specifically, accessible heavy states—at future colliders.

Method: Constructs a concrete grand unification model based on SU(6) where the pseudo-Dirac nature and mass splitting δm of Higgsino-like DM arise from GUT-scale symmetry breaking; computes the resulting Z′ mass from δm scaling; derives the dilepton production cross section and branching ratio; compares signal significance with FCC-hh sensitivity projections for Z′ → ℓ⁺ℓ⁻.

Result: The experimentally preferred δm ≈ 100 keV implies a Z′ boson mass of O(10) TeV; its dilepton resonance signal yields a discovery significance >5σ with ~100 fb⁻¹ at FCC-hh, well within early-run reach.

Conclusion: Pseudo-Dirac DM interpretations of direct detection anomalies can yield falsifiable, collider-accessible predictions; the SU(6) GUT scenario provides a theoretically motivated and experimentally testable link between sub-eV-scale DM splittings and multi-TeV Z′ bosons.

Abstract: The recent observation of an event with large recoil energy at LUX-ZEPLIN has found a possible explanation in terms of the inelastic scattering of a pseudo-Dirac particle like the Higgsino of the MSSM. For pseudo-Dirac dark matter, the mass splitting $δm$ is correlated with the mass of some heavier states. While rough dimensional considerations seem to indicate mass ranges of the heavy particles hopelessly out of reach even at future colliders, we point out that this is not the case in GUT-induced scenarios. Working with a specific model based on SU(6), we show that the experimentally favored $δm$ implies the presence of a $Z'$ gauge boson with a mass in the 10s of TeV. We derive the predicted $Z'$ signal in the dilepton channel and we confront it with the sensitivity of the FCC-$hh$ to $Z'$ resonances. We show that the $Z'$ falls squarely in reach of the early discovery potential of the FCC-$hh$.

</details>


### [51] [Inelastic $B-L$ scalar dark matter and the LUX-ZEPLIN event](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06909)
*Nobuchika Okada,Osamu Seto*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes an inelastic scalar dark matter model within a gauged $U(1)_{B-L}$ framework that explains the 2.6σ high-energy recoil excess observed by LZ via resonant annihilation and predicts a DM-nucleon scattering cross section consistent with the signal; it is testable via $Z'$ resonance searches at future colliders.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the 2.6σ high-energy nuclear recoil excess reported by the LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) experiment using a theoretically motivated dark matter framework that simultaneously satisfies thermal relic abundance constraints.

Method: Analyzes an inelastic scalar dark matter model embedded in a gauged $U(1)_{B-L}$ extension of the Standard Model; imposes thermal relic density constraint requiring resonant annihilation ($m_S \simeq m_{Z'}/2$) and determines viable parameter space for $g_{B-L} \sim 0.5$ and TeV-scale $m_S$, then computes the spin-independent inelastic DM-nucleon cross section $\sigma_\mathrm{SI}$.

Result: Predicts $\sigma_\mathrm{SI} \sim 10^{-45}~\mathrm{cm^2}$, which matches the LZ excess; identifies required mass relation $m_S \simeq m_{Z'}/2$ and coupling $g_{B-L} \sim 0.5$ to satisfy Planck-measured relic density.

Conclusion: The inelastic scalar DM in gauged $U(1)_{B-L}$ provides a consistent, minimal explanation of the LZ anomaly while remaining testable—specifically, through direct $Z'$ resonance searches at high-energy colliders.

Abstract: We show that our previously proposed inelastic scalar dark matter (DM) model in gauged $U(1)_{B-L}$ symmetry naturally accounts for the $2.6σ$ high-energy recoil event recently reported by the LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) collaboration. To satisfy the thermal relic abundance, TeV-scale scalar DM $S$ with $m_S \simeq m_{Z'}/2$ for resonant annihilation and the gauge coupling constant $g_{B-L} \sim 0.5$ are required, where $m_{Z^\prime}$ is the mass of $U(1)_{B-L}$ gauge boson $Z^{\prime}$. The resulting predicted DM-nucleon inelastic cross section of $σ_\mathrm{SI} \sim 10^{-45}$ cm${}^2$ is in excellent agreement with the LZ event. This scenario can be decisively tested for future collider searches for $Z^\prime$ boson resonance.

</details>


### [52] [LZ Nuclear-Recoil Excess from Boosted Light Magnetic Dipole-dipole Dark Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06756)
*Jin-Han Liang,Zuowei Liu,Van Que Tran,Yongheng Xu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper proposes that a boosted dark matter velocity distribution can better explain the LZ nuclear-recoil excess at ~248 keV, resolving tension with null results at lower energies and allowing lighter (GeV-scale) dark matter candidates; this is demonstrated model-independently and realized in a concrete annihilation-to-boosted-dark-sector model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the tension between the LZ-reported 2.6σ nuclear-recoil excess near 248 keV and the absence of an excess at lower recoil energies under standard halo model assumptions — particularly for TeV-scale magnetic dipole-dipole interacting DM, which overpredicts low-energy events.

Method: Model-independent spectral fitting of the LZ recoil spectrum using a boosted DM velocity distribution; followed by construction and analysis of a concrete particle physics model where halo DM annihilates into on-shell mediators that decay into boosted dark-sector particles.

Result: A boosted velocity distribution significantly improves the fit to the LZ spectrum, alleviates the low-energy tension, and enables viable GeV-scale DM explanations; the proposed annihilation-based model successfully realizes such boosted kinematics and remains consistent with observational constraints.

Conclusion: Boosted dark-sector particles constitute a physically motivated and phenomenologically viable interpretation of the LZ excess, broadening the allowed DM mass range and offering testable implications for direct detection and dark-sector phenomenology.

Abstract: The LZ collaboration has reported a nuclear-recoil excess near 248 keV with a global significance of $2.6σ$. Although halo dark matter with a magnetic dipole-dipole interaction and a TeV-scale mass provides the best fit to the excess among the interactions considered by LZ, it predicts a considerable number of events at lower recoil energies, where no excess is observed. We show that a boosted velocity distribution can alleviate this tension and provide a better fit to the LZ recoil spectrum. Moreover, the boost opens up the possibility of explaining the excess with much lighter dark matter, with masses down to the GeV scale. We demonstrate these features first in a model-independent analysis and then realize them in a concrete dark matter model, in which halo dark matter annihilates into on-shell mediators that subsequently decay into boosted dark-sector particles. Our results demonstrate that boosted dark sector particles provide a viable interpretation of the LZ excess.

</details>


### [53] [LZ nuclear recoil event from inelastic singlet-doublet scalar dark matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07451)
*Disha Bandyopadhyay,Debasish Borah,Pankaj Borah*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores whether singlet-doublet scalar dark matter can explain a recent high-energy nuclear recoil event observed by the LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) experiment, while satisfying relic abundance and direct detection constraints; it identifies viable parameter space enabling inelastic up-scattering consistent with the LZ signal, and extends beyond pure scalar doublet DM by incorporating singlet-doublet mixing and Z₂-odd right-handed neutrinos for neutrino mass generation and leptogenesis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the anomalous high-energy nuclear recoil event reported by LZ within a theoretically consistent dark matter framework that satisfies cosmological and experimental constraints.

Method: Analyzes the singlet-doublet scalar DM model—comprising a scalar doublet and a real scalar singlet, both odd under an unbroken Z₂ symmetry—computing relic abundance, elastic and inelastic scattering cross sections, and compatibility with LZ direct detection limits and recoil spectrum.

Result: Identifies a broad region of parameter space where the model simultaneously satisfies correct thermal relic density, evades current direct detection bounds on elastic scattering, and predicts inelastic up-scattering rates and recoil energies compatible with the LZ event; shows enhanced viability relative to pure scalar doublet DM due to singlet-doublet mixing; incorporates Z₂-odd right-handed neutrinos enabling Type-I seesaw and leptogenesis.

Conclusion: The singlet-doublet scalar DM framework provides a viable and phenomenologically rich explanation for the LZ anomaly, extending conventional scalar DM scenarios and linking dark matter physics to neutrino mass generation and baryogenesis.

Abstract: We study the possibility of explaining the recently reported high-energy nuclear recoil event by the LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) collaboration within the framework of singlet-doublet scalar dark matter (DM). Considering DM to be the CP even mass eigenstate formed out of a scalar doublet and a real scalar singlet, both being odd under an unbroken $Z_2$ symmetry, we find the parameter space of the model consistent with correct relic abundance and direct-detection limits on elastic scattering rates. A large part of the parameter space can also lead to inelastic up-scattering of DM with a rate and recoil energy consistent with the recent LZ event. Depending upon the singlet-doublet mixing, the model allows a much wider range of currently allowed parameter space compared to the pure scalar doublet DM limit. Presence of $Z_2$-odd right-handed neutrinos also leads to other interesting phenomenology related to the origin of light neutrino masses and leptogenesis.

</details>


### [54] [Solar Capture Tests of Inelastic Dark Matter after the LZ High-Recoil Event](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06760)
*Mattia Di Mauro,Halim Shaikh*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper examines Solar-capture constraints on three endothermic dark matter (DM) models proposed to explain a 248 keV nuclear-recoil candidate observed by LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ). It finds the canonical thermal Higgsino is ruled out by IceCube limits, while pseudo-Dirac and neutron-philic O₄ models remain viable due to suppressed annihilation rates and capture efficiencies well below IceCube’s bb̄ sensitivity threshold.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether solar capture and annihilation of endothermic DM—proposed to explain the LZ 248 keV nuclear-recoil event—can be constrained by existing IceCube neutrino limits, particularly for non-standard thermal relics.

Method: Computes solar capture rates (C⊙) and annihilation rates (ΓA) for three endothermic DM benchmarks: (i) thermal Higgsino, (ii) pseudo-Dirac fermion with off-diagonal vector interactions, and (iii) neutron-philic spin-dependent scattering via effective operator O₄. Includes finite-temperature effects, two-state kinetics, thermally assisted re-excitation (χ₁→χ₂), and spin-response uncertainties; compares ΓA to IceCube’s bb̄ neutrino flux limit.

Result: Thermal Higgsino with Δ ≈ 0.38 MeV is excluded (Δ < 0.51–0.56 MeV required by IceCube). Pseudo-Dirac model yields C⊙ = 1.48×10²⁰ s⁻¹ but ΓA,max ≈ 4.1×10¹⁴ s⁻¹ — ~1.8×10⁵ below IceCube bb̄ limit. Neutron-philic O₄ at mχ = 1 TeV, δ = 300 keV gives C⊙ = (1.77–9.72)×10¹⁷ s⁻¹; even upper-bound ΓA remains ~154× below limit; aggressive stress test still yields ~19× suppression.

Conclusion: Solar capture + IceCube annihilation constraints robustly exclude the canonical thermal Higgsino explanation of the LZ candidate, but do not generically rule out other endothermic DM scenarios—particularly pseudo-Dirac and neutron-philic O₄ models—due to kinematically and thermally suppressed annihilation.

Abstract: The LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) Collaboration has reported a $248$ keV nuclear-recoil candidate for which endothermic dark matter (DM) gives some of the largest local significances. We study Solar-capture constraints on three simple interpretations: a thermal Higgsino, a thermal pseudo-Dirac fermion with off-diagonal vector interactions, and neutron-philic endothermic spin-dependent scattering through $\mathcal{O}_4$. The canonical full-density thermal Higgsino is excluded: its LZ-preferred splitting near $0.38,\mathrm{MeV}$ lies below the IceCube requirement, $δ\gtrsim0.51$--$0.56,\mathrm{MeV}$. For the pseudo-Dirac benchmark we find $C_\odot=1.48\times10^{20},\mathrm{s}^{-1}$, but two-state kinetics strongly suppresses annihilation. Including thermally assisted $χ_1\toχ_2$ re-excitation gives a maximum $Γ_A\simeq4.1\times10^{14},\mathrm{s}^{-1}$, about $1.8\times10^5$ below the adopted IceCube $b\bar b$ limit. For neutron-philic $\mathcal{O}_4$ scattering at $m_χ=1$ TeV and $δ=300$ keV, finite-temperature capture gives $C_\odot=3.98\times10^{17},\mathrm{s}^{-1}$, with spin-response range $(1.77$--$9.72)\times10^{17},\mathrm{s}^{-1}$. Even in equilibrium, the upper edge remains about a factor $154$ below the adopted $b\bar b$ limit, while an aggressive no-finite-$q$ suppression plus upper-spin stress test still leaves a factor about $19$. The $b\bar b$ mapping is used as a representative hadronic template for the two non-Higgsino benchmarks. Solar capture therefore excludes the thermal-Higgsino interpretation but does not generically exclude endothermic explanations of the LZ candidate.

</details>


### [55] [Inelastic Singlet-Doublet Fermion Dark Matter in light of the 248 keV LZ event](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07800)
*Debasish Borah,Sujit Kumar Sahoo,Narendra Sahu,Shashwat Sharma*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes an inelastic dark matter model—singlet-doublet fermion DM with a scalar triplet—to explain LZ's single high-energy dark matter event and absence of lower-energy events, linking DM phenomenology to neutrino mass generation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) collaboration’s observation of a single dark matter–nucleus scattering event at ~248 keV recoil energy and the lack of events at lower energies, which suggests inelastic scattering rather than standard elastic WIMP interactions.

Method: The authors construct and analyze an extended singlet-doublet fermion dark matter model where the DM consists of two pseudo-Dirac states; the lighter state is Majorana, forbidding tree-level Z-mediated elastic scattering, while inelastic scattering proceeds via Z exchange. A Z₂-even scalar triplet is added to generate the pseudo-Dirac mass splitting and realize Type-II seesaw neutrino masses.

Result: The model naturally accommodates the LZ signal as an inelastic scattering event, suppresses low-energy elastic rates, and simultaneously explains neutrino masses without conflicting with direct detection bounds or electroweak precision constraints.

Conclusion: The singlet-doublet fermion DM model augmented with a scalar triplet provides a consistent, testable framework that reconciles the anomalous LZ event with neutrino mass generation and evades current direct detection limits.

Abstract: Recently, the LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) collaboration reported the observation of a single dark matter (DM)-nucleus scattering event at a nuclear recoil energy of $248\pm23_{\rm stat}\pm23_{\rm sys}$ keV, corresponding to an exposure of 2.84 tonne-year. The absence of events at lower nuclear recoil energies in the predicted spectrum is naturally explained if the underlying process is inelastic DM-nucleus scattering. Motivated by this, we investigate the singlet-doublet fermion DM model, in which the DM consists of two pseudo-Dirac states: the Majorana nature of the lighter state forbids tree level $Z$-mediated elastic scattering identically, while the same states enable inelastic DM-nucleus scattering via $Z$ exchange, with any residual elastic scattering proceeding only through a suppressed Higgs-mediated channel. We further extend the model with a $Z_2$-even scalar triplet, which is responsible both for generating the pseudo-Dirac splitting and for realizing Majorana neutrino masses via the Type-II seesaw mechanism.

</details>


### [56] [Revisiting two-body charmed anti-charmed baryonic $B$ decays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08053)
*Chun-Khiang Chua*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper revisits two-body charmed-anticharmed baryonic B̄ → B_c B̄_c decays using the topological amplitude approach, incorporating both b→c c̄ q and previously neglected b→u ū q contributions; it shows that b→u ū q effects—CKM-suppressed but enhanced by ~20× in ΔS=0 vs. ΔS=−1 transitions—can significantly increase predicted rates for several specific decays (e.g., B⁻→Ξ_c⁰ Ξ̄_c⁻), and their experimental measurement would clarify the role of these subleading weak transitions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reassess the role of b→uūq contributions in B̄ → B_c B̄_c decays, which were previously neglected despite potential enhancement in ΔS=0 transitions due to favorable CKM factor ratios (λ⁻² ≈ 20), and to reconcile theory with emerging data suggesting non-negligible contributions.

Method: Topological amplitude approach with updated SU(3)_F decomposition for all allowed flavor representations (3̄_f, 3_f, 6_f, 6̄_f) of B_c B̄_c final states; inclusion of both b→c c̄ q and b→u ū q tree-level amplitudes; CKM scaling analysis comparing ΔS=0 and ΔS=−1 transitions.

Result: b→uūq contributions are enhanced ~20× in ΔS=0 relative to ΔS=−1 transitions; current data on B̄→B_c(3̄_f)B̄_c(3_f) decays allow or slightly prefer non-negligible b→uūq amplitudes; predicted branching fractions for B⁻→Ξ_c⁰ Ξ̄_c⁻, B̄_s⁰→Λ_c⁺ Ξ̄_c⁻, and B̄⁰→Ξ_c⁺ Ξ̄_c⁻ increase substantially when b→uūq is included.

Conclusion: b→uūq contributions are phenomenologically important in ΔS=0 charmed-anticharmed baryonic B decays; their inclusion improves consistency with data and motivates dedicated experimental measurements to probe subleading weak dynamics and test SU(3)_F symmetry in baryonic decays.

Abstract: The study of two-body charmed anti-charmed baryonic $\overline B\to {\bf B}_c \overline {\bf B}_c$ decays using the topological amplitude approach is revisited. We include the $b\to u\bar u q$ contributions, in addition to the $b\to c\bar c q$ contributions, to these decays. The topological decomposition of $\overline B\to {\bf B}_c(\bf {\bar 3_f}) \overline {\bf B}_c(\bf { 3_f})$, ${\bf B}_c(\bf 6_f) \overline {\bf B}_c(\bf { 3_f})$, ${\bf B}_c(\bf {\bar 3_f}) \overline {\bf B}_c(\bf {\bar 6_f})$ and ${\bf B}_c(\bf 6_f) \overline {\bf B}_c(\bf {\bar 6_f})$ decay amplitudes are updated accordingly. Although the $b\to u\bar u q$ contributions are CKM suppressed in $ΔS=-1$ transitions, their effects are significantly amplified in $ΔS=0$ transitions, as the relative size of the CKM factors in $b\to u\bar u q$ and $b\to c\bar c q$ contributions in $ΔS=0$ transitions is enlarged by $λ^{-2}$, roughly a factor of 20, from the one in $ΔS=-1$ transitions. Present data in $\overline B\to {\bf B}_c(\bf {\bar 3_f}) \overline {\bf B}_c(\bf { 3_f})$ decay rates allow or even slightly prefer non-negligible $b\to u\bar u q$ contributions. These $b\to u\bar u q$ contributions can allow much larger rates for $B^-\to Ξ_c^0 \bar Ξ_c^{-}$, $\overline B{}_s^0\to Λ_c^+ \bar Ξ_c^{-}$ and $\overline B{}^0\to Ξ_c^+ \bar Ξ_c^{-}$ decays than those only have $b\to c\bar c d$ contributions. Measuring these decays will be interesting and useful in clarifying the role of $b\to u\bar u q$ contributions in two-body charmed anti-charmed baryonic $\overline B\to {\bf B}_c \overline {\bf B}_c$ decays.

</details>


### [57] [Reggeization of quarks from next-to-eikonal high-energy QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06833)
*Tolga Altinoluk,Guillaume Beuf,Jules Favrel,Michael Fucilla*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs a Wilson-line-based formulation of quark Reggeization in QCD, deriving an interpolating operator for the Reggeized quark and its nonlinear rapidity evolution; it recovers Regge-pole behavior in the dilute limit, signature degeneracy in the planar limit, extends to massive quarks, and shows eigenstate stability up to NLL accuracy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a first-principles, gauge-invariant Wilson-line formulation of quark Reggeization—complementing the well-established gluon Reggeization—and resolve open questions about operator structure, signature dependence, and universality across kinematic regimes and quark masses.

Method: Uses the background-field method to derive rapidity evolution of a next-to-eikonal operator built from a semi-infinite Wilson line and a background-quark insertion; identifies an interpolating operator for the Reggeized quark; analyzes signature components (positive/negative), dilute vs. planar limits, and gluon-to-quark transition operators; extends to massive quarks via coordinate-space kernel and Fourier analysis; employs operator-mixing and signature symmetry arguments for eigenstate stability.

Result: Derives a gauge-invariant Reggeized-quark interpolating operator; obtains its nonlinear rapidity evolution equation; confirms Regge-pole evolution for positive-signature component in dilute regime; reveals quark–gluon mixing in negative-signature (Regge-cut) sector; recovers signature-degenerate Reggeization in planar limit; demonstrates universality across different Wilson-line constructions; provides massive-quark evolution kernel matching known Regge trajectory; proves eigenstate property holds up to next-to-leading logarithmic (NLL) accuracy.

Conclusion: Quark Reggeization admits a consistent, Wilson-line-based formulation rooted in QCD’s gauge structure and rapidity evolution; the Reggeized quark emerges as a well-defined, universal, and evolution-stable composite operator—valid in both massless and massive cases—with its dynamics fully encoded in signature-resolved rapidity evolution.

Abstract: We develop a comprehensive Wilson-line formulation of quark Reggeization in QCD. Starting from a next-to-eikonal operator built from a semi-infinite Wilson line and a background-quark insertion, we identify an interpolating operator for the Reggeized quark and derive its nonlinear rapidity evolution using the background-field method. In the dilute regime, its positive-signature component exhibits Regge-pole evolution governed by the quark Regge trajectory, whereas the negative-signature sector displays mixing between quark and gluon degrees of freedom, in accordance with its known Regge-cut structure. In the planar limit, this mixing is suppressed, and Reggeization emerges without an explicit signature projection, recovering signature degeneracy. We illustrate the universality of the construction by extracting the same Reggeized-quark operator from a more general Wilson-line operator describing a gluon-to-quark transition. Furthermore, we extend the formalism to massive quarks, deriving a coordinate-space evolution kernel whose Fourier transform reproduces the massive-quark Regge trajectory. Finally, from the leading operator-mixing structure of the evolution equation and signature arguments, we show that the Reggeized-quark interpolating operator is expected to remain an eigenstate of the rapidity evolution up to next-to-leading logarithmic accuracy.

</details>


### [58] [Quark mass functions in Minkowski space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06839)
*Elmar P. Biernat,Franz Gross,M. T. Peña,Alfred Stadler*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses Covariant Spectator Theory with a lattice-fitted dressed-gluon propagator to compute quark mass functions and wave-function renormalization for u/d, s, c, and b quarks in both spacelike and timelike Minkowski regions; free parameters are constrained by fits to two independent lattice datasets, yielding constituent-mass-range predictions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a relativistic, covariant description of quark propagation across spacelike and timelike momenta, bridging nonperturbative lattice QCD results with phenomenological quark models.

Method: Covariant Spectator Theory (CST) with a model dressed-gluon propagator constrained by spacelike lattice QCD data; quark self-energy parameters fitted to lattice quark mass functions, incorporating a consistency constraint on the wave-function renormalization Z(p²).

Result: Dressed quark mass functions and Z(p²) computed for u/d, s, c, and b quarks over full Minkowski momentum range; fitted parameters yield mass predictions consistent with standard constituent quark model values.

Conclusion: CST, augmented by lattice-informed gluon and self-energy inputs, successfully reproduces nonperturbative quark properties across spacelike and timelike domains, supporting its use for hadron structure calculations beyond Euclidean lattice frameworks.

Abstract: Using the Covariant Spectator Theory (CST), we calculate the dressed quark mass function and wave-function renormalization for the five quark flavors from up/down to bottom in both the spacelike and timelike regions of Minkowski space. The calculation employs a model dressed-gluon propagator fitted to lattice data in the spacelike region. The remaining free parameters of the quark self-energy are determined by fits to lattice results for the quark mass function including a constraint built into the wave-function renormalization. We perform these fits using lattice data from two different groups and find that the resulting mass predictions are within the range of the typical constituent masses used in quark models.

</details>


### [59] [Inclusive electron-nucleus cross section models from domain adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08463)
*Krzysztof M. Graczyk,Beata E. Kowal,Rwik Dharmapal Banerjee,Jose Luis Bonilla,Hemant Prasad,Jan T. Sobczyk*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper applies transfer learning to build data-driven models of electron-nucleus cross sections, fine-tuning deep neural networks pretrained on carbon-12 data for six other nuclei; performance gains vary by target, depend critically on data quality/coverage, and layer-wise analysis reveals nucleus-specific adaptation depth requirements.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve predictive accuracy and robustness of data-driven models for inclusive electron-nucleus cross sections across multiple nuclear targets—especially where experimental data is sparse—by leveraging knowledge from a well-measured nucleus (12C) via transfer learning.

Method: Ensemble of deep neural networks pretrained on 12C inclusive electron-nucleus cross section data; fine-tuned separately for 3He, 6Li, 16O, 27Al, 40Ca, and 56Fe; systematic ablation studies on number of fine-tuned layers, training data fraction/selection, and kinematic domain overlap; comparison with phenomenological F1F2 model.

Result: All fine-tuned models show improved performance over baseline (especially for light/heavy nuclei beyond oxygen); 16O requires only shallow fine-tuning, while 3He, 40Ca, and 56Fe need deeper adaptation; 6Li exhibits lowest robustness due to limited data; 27Al shows high sensitivity to few constraining measurements; adapted models extrapolate reasonably to kinematic regions outside 12C training coverage, remaining consistent with uncertainties.

Conclusion: Transfer learning is effective for cross-section modeling across nuclei, but optimal adaptation strategy is nucleus- and data-dependent; success hinges on data quantity, kinematic coverage, and measurement precision—not just architecture—and offers a viable alternative or complement to phenomenological models like F1F2.

Abstract: We apply transfer learning (TL) to construct data-driven models of inclusive electron-nucleus cross sections. Starting from an ensemble of deep neural networks pretrained on \(^{12}\)C data, we fine-tune the models separately for \(^{3}\)He, \(^{6}\)Li, \(^{16}\)O, \(^{27}\)Al, \(^{40}\)Ca, and \(^{56}\)Fe. The resulting models improve for all targets, marginally so for oxygen, where the carbon baseline is already adequate, although their predictive robustness depends on the amount, coverage, and precision of the available target data. We systematically study how model performance depends on the number of fine-tuned layers, on the fraction and selection of the training data, and on the overlap between the source and target kinematic domains. The layer-wise analysis shows that oxygen requires only shallow adaptation, whereas helium, calcium, and iron require substantially deeper fine-tuning. Lithium represents the least robust case because of its limited dataset, while aluminum demonstrates a strong sensitivity to a small subset of highly constraining measurements. For selected kinematic configurations outside the coverage of the carbon training data, the adapted models remain consistent with the measurements within their estimated uncertainties. Finally, we compare the resulting predictions with those of the phenomenological F1F2 model.

</details>


### [60] [Geometric Phases in Two-Level Mixing: From Neutral Mesons to Holonomic Qubits and Topological Majorana Modes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06857)
*Swarup Sangiri,Utpal Sarkar,A Taraphder*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper unifies geometric, qubit, fermionic, and topological perspectives of two-level Hermitian Hamiltonians—starting from neutral meson mixing—by analyzing Bloch-sphere geometry, CP-like geometric phases, Bargmann invariants, Majorana bilinears, and momentum-space topology in the Kitaev chain.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To uncover a unified geometric framework linking neutral meson oscillations (via Hermitian two-level mixing Hamiltonians) with quantum information (qubit), fermionic (Majorana/Bogoliubov), and topological (winding/Zak phase) descriptions.

Method: Mapped the mixing Hamiltonian to a qubit on the Bloch sphere; computed geometric (Berry/Zak) phases and third-order Bargmann invariants; interpreted the complex mixing phase as a CP-like geometric parameter; expressed dynamics via R_z gates and Majorana bilinears; extended analysis to momentum-dependent Bogoliubov-de Gennes Hamiltonian of the Kitaev chain.

Result: Established that the mixing phase governs azimuthal orientation and CP-conjugation symmetry on the Bloch sphere; Bargmann invariants yield a discrete phase consistent with continuous geometric construction; cyclic evolution is equivalent to an R_z gate; momentum-space winding in the Kitaev chain maps to topological regimes and quantized Zak phase; Bargmann phase converges to global phase in continuum limit.

Conclusion: A coherent geometric language bridges CP-like structure in particle physics, single-qubit operations, fermionic parity representations, and 1D topological superconductivity—demonstrating deep structural unity across two-level Hermitian systems.

Abstract: We investigate the geometric structure of a Hermitian two-level mixing Hamiltonian motivated by neutral meson oscillations and its connections with qubit, fermionic, and topological descriptions. Mapping the Hamiltonian to an effective qubit representation, we analyze the geometric phase associated with cyclic parameter evolution on the Bloch sphere. Without identifying the complex mixing phase with a physical CP-violation observable, we interpret it as a CP-like geometric parameter controlling the azimuthal orientation of the Hamiltonian vector and its reversal under phase conjugation. Third-order Bargmann invariants constructed from the eigenstates yield an associated discrete phase whose dependence on the mixing parameters is examined alongside the continuous geometric construction. The cyclic evolution is also represented as a single-qubit $R_z$ phase operation and expressed through Majorana bilinears. Extending the construction to the momentum-dependent Bogoliubov-de Gennes Hamiltonian of the Kitaev chain, we relate momentum-space winding to the topological regimes and the quantized Berry (Zak) phase in the real-parameter model, while the corresponding Bargmann construction approaches the global phase in the continuum limit. Together, these results provide a geometric perspective connecting phase structure, qubit operations, fermionic representations, and momentum-space topology within related two-level Hamiltonians.

</details>


### [61] [A Vector-Like Lepton Interpretation of the High-Energy Nuclear Recoil Candidate in LUX-ZEPLIN](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08993)
*Fatemeh Elahi,Pedro Schwaller*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: LZ observed a high-energy nuclear recoil candidate; the paper proposes spin-dependent dark matter scattering via operator O4 as an explanation, realized in singlet-doublet Majorana DM with a Higgs blind spot to suppress SI interactions; the model fits both the LZ event rate and thermal relic abundance, and is testable via solar neutrino searches (e.g., IceCube).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The unusually high-energy (248 keV) nuclear-recoil candidate observed by LZ cannot be easily explained by standard spin-independent WIMP-nucleon interactions due to strong suppression of xenon response at such energies; thus, alternative DM interaction mechanisms with harder recoil spectra — particularly spin-dependent — are motivated.

Method: The authors analyze elastic spin-dependent scattering mediated by the nonrelativistic operator O4 = S_DM · S_N, embedded in a singlet-doublet Majorana dark matter model; they identify a Higgs blind spot where Higgs-mediated spin-independent interactions are suppressed while Z-mediated spin-dependent interactions remain sizable; they compute recoil spectra, event rates in LZ’s high-energy window, thermal relic density, and solar capture rates leading to neutrino fluxes testable by IceCube.

Result: A viable region along the Higgs blind spot simultaneously reproduces the observed LZ high-recoil event rate (~O(1) event), the correct thermal relic abundance, and predicts detectable solar neutrino signals; current IceCube limits exclude part of this parameter space, but a compatible region remains open.

Conclusion: The LZ high-energy recoil candidate can be consistently explained by spin-dependent scattering of singlet-doublet Majorana dark matter at a Higgs blind spot; this scenario is falsifiable through near-future xenon-based exposures and improved solar-neutrino experiments.

Abstract: The LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) experiment has recently reported a nuclear-recoil candidate at $E_R=248\pm23_{\rm stat}\pm23_{\rm sys}\,{\rm keV}$ in an exposure of $2.84\,{\rm tonne\,yr}$. At this unusually high recoil energy, the spin-independent (SI) xenon response is strongly suppressed, motivating dark matter interactions with a harder recoil spectrum. We show that elastic spin-dependent (SD) scattering through the nonrelativistic operator $\mathcal O_4=\mathbf S_{\rm DM}\cdot\mathbf S_N$ provides a simple realization of such a spectrum. We consider singlet-doublet Majorana dark matter, for which a diagonal $Z$ coupling generates the required SD interaction. The accompanying Higgs-mediated SI interaction would generically produce too many low-energy recoils, but can be suppressed along a Higgs blind spot while retaining a nonzero $Z$ coupling. We find a region of the Higgs blind spot that simultaneously predicts an $\mathcal O(1)$ event rate in the LZ high-recoil window and reproduces the observed thermal relic abundance. The same SD interaction leads to solar capture, allowing this region to be tested independently with solar-neutrino searches. Current IceCube limits already probe part of the LZ-compatible thermal parameter space, while a viable region remains. Additional xenon exposure and improved solar-neutrino searches can therefore provide complementary tests of this interpretation.

</details>


### [62] [Neutron disappearance and the LZ nuclear recoil event](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09037)
*Mohammad Aghaie,Alessandro Strumia*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The LZ experiment observed an anomalous 248 keV nuclear recoil in xenon, prompting a hypothesis of bound neutron disappearance—either via spontaneous decay or dark-matter-induced reaction—with distinct spectral and target-dependent signatures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the anomalous 248 keV nuclear recoil observed by the LZ experiment, potentially inconsistent with standard background models, by proposing bound neutron disappearance as a novel nuclear process.

Method: Theoretical modeling of nuclear recoil spectra from bound neutron disappearance (spontaneous or dark-matter-triggered), including kinematic constraints, target-material dependence (xenon vs. oxygen), and estimates of 'quiet' event fractions (i.e., events without associated secondary nuclear activity).

Result: For large-energy-release scenarios, the recoil spectrum is broad near 248 keV and only ~10% of xenon events are 'quiet'; for near-threshold dark-matter-induced disappearance, the process can be kinematically allowed in xenon but forbidden in oxygen, raising the quiet fraction to ~30% and producing line-like recoil features.

Conclusion: Bound neutron disappearance—especially in a near-threshold dark-matter-mediated channel—is a viable, testable explanation for the LZ anomaly, with distinctive experimental signatures distinguishable from conventional backgrounds.

Abstract: The LZ experiment reported a nuclear recoil with anomalous energy $E_R=248$ keV, around the typical neutron motion in xenon. This motivates interpreting the event as the disappearance of a bound neutron, either through spontaneous decay or a dark-matter-induced reaction. If the energy released is large, the predicted recoil spectrum is broad around the observed value; only $\sim10\%$ of xenon events avoid extra nuclear activity; the disappearance rate is target-independent and mildly constrained by oxygen experiments. In the special case of near-threshold DM-induced neutron disappearance, it can be open in xenon and closed in oxygen, the quiet fraction rises to $\sim30\%$, the recoil spectrum turns into lines.

</details>


### [63] [Bottomonium suppression with a machine-learning-informed Debye mass](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09047)
*Ajaharul Islam,Shibo Chen,Fu-Peng Li,Long-Gang Pang,Guang-You Qin*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses machine learning to refine the Debye mass in the complex KSU heavy-quark potential, then applies it within the QTraj framework to compute bottomonium suppression observables in Pb-Pb collisions at 5.02 TeV, achieving better agreement with ALICE/ATLAS/CMS data—especially for Υ(2S) and Υ(3S).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve modeling of in-medium QCD effects on bottomonium suppression by leveraging data-driven (lattice-informed) machine learning for the Debye mass, addressing limitations of the original KSU potential.

Method: Trains an ML model on lattice-QCD inputs to obtain a complex-valued, temperature-dependent Debye mass; embeds it into the KSU heavy-quark potential; solves the real-time Schrödinger equation using the quantum trajectories (QTraj) method to simulate bottomonium evolution in QGP; computes R_AA and double ratios for Υ(1S), Υ(2S), Υ(3S).

Result: ML-informed Debye mass yields significantly improved agreement with experimental nuclear modification factors and double ratios from ALICE, ATLAS, and CMS—particularly for excited states (Υ(2S), Υ(3S))—compared to the original KSU model.

Conclusion: Machine learning, when constrained by lattice-QCD data, enhances the physical fidelity of in-medium heavy-quark potentials and provides a robust pathway toward quantitatively predictive models of quarkonium suppression in heavy-ion collisions.

Abstract: Motivated by recent progress in data-driven approaches, we introduce a machine-learning (ML)-informed Debye mass, extracted from lattice-informed inputs, exclusively in the complex-valued heavy-quark Kent State University (KSU) potential. The resulting complex potential is used to solve the real-time Schrödinger equation within the quantum trajectories (QTraj) framework for the evolution of bottomonium in the quark-gluon plasma. We then compute the nuclear modification factors and double ratios for bottomonium $Υ(1S)$, $Υ(2S)$, and $Υ(3S)$ states in Pb-Pb collisions at $\sqrt{s_{NN}} = 5.02$ TeV. We compare our ML-induced results with those from the original KSU model and with experimental measurements from ALICE, ATLAS, and CMS collaborations. We find that the machine-learned Debye mass leads to improved agreement with data, particularly for excited states, highlighting the utility of machine learning in modeling in-medium QCD effects.

</details>


### [64] [Electromagnetic structure of strange vector mesons in nuclear medium](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06938)
*Parada T. P. Hutauruk,Terry Mart,Kazuo Tsushima*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies in-medium modifications of the electromagnetic form factors (charge, magnetic, quadrupole) of the $K^{*+}$ vector meson in symmetric nuclear matter using the Schwinger proper-time NJL model; all form factors are suppressed with increasing density, and the in-medium charge radius at normal density is found to be 0.74 fm.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how the internal electromagnetic structure of strange vector mesons—specifically $K^{*+}$—is modified inside nuclear medium, complementing studies on non-strange mesons like $\rho$ and probing quark-level medium effects.

Method: Schwinger proper-time regularization within the Nambu-Jona-Lasinio (NJL) model, applied to compute in-medium charge ($G_C^*$), magnetic ($G_M^*$), and quadrupole ($G_Q^*$) form factors of $K^{*+}$ in zero-temperature symmetric nuclear matter; form factors and charge radius extracted from $Q^2$-dependence near $Q^2 = 0$.

Result: All three form factors ($G_C^*, G_M^*, G_Q^*$) are suppressed with increasing nuclear density; the in-medium charge radius of $K^{*+}$ at normal nuclear density is $r_{K^{*+}}^* = 0.74~\mathrm{fm}$, slightly smaller than $r_{\rho^+}^* \simeq 0.75~\mathrm{fm}$.

Conclusion: The $K^{*+}$ meson exhibits significant in-medium structural modifications at the quark level, evidenced by density-dependent suppression of its electromagnetic form factors; the NJL framework successfully unifies description of medium effects and meson structure, suggesting strangeness-dependent differences in medium response between $K^{*+}$ and $\rho^+$.

Abstract: We investigate the in-medium modifications of the charge (electric) $G_C^{*}(Q^2)$, magnetic $G_M^{*}(Q^2)$, and quadrupole $G_Q^{*}(Q^2)$ form factors of the positively charged vector meson $K^{*+}$ in symmetric nuclear matter at zero temperature within the Schwinger proper-time Nambu-Jona-Lasinio (NJL) model. In this framework, both the nuclear medium effects and the electromagnetic structure of the $K^{*+}$ meson are described consistently in the NJL model at the quark level. We find that the charge, magnetic, and quadrupole form factors are suppressed with increasing nuclear density, indicating substantial in-medium modifications of the strange vector meson's internal structure. We further obtain a charge radius of $r_{K^{*+}}^{*}=0.74~\mathrm{fm}$ at normal nuclear density, which is slightly smaller than the corresponding value for the $ρ^{+}$ meson, $r_{ρ^{+}}^{*}\simeq0.75~\mathrm{fm}$.

</details>


### [65] [Exploring the $P$-wave bottom-strange molecular pentaquarks with the complex scaling method](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07068)
*Yuhang Zheng,Qing-Fu Song,Xiaonu Xiong*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper systematically investigates P-wave bottom-strange hadronic systems (Σ_b K, Λ_b K*, etc.) in the positive-parity sector, predicting several narrow and broad molecular resonance candidates—some above, some below thresholds—with varying isospin and spin-parity quantum numbers, aiming to guide LHCb searches for bottom-strange pentaquarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend prior work on negative-parity bottom-strange systems and comprehensively explore the positive-parity sector for possible molecular pentaquark states, motivated by recent experimental interest in hidden- and open-flavor exotic hadrons at LHCb.

Method: Coupled-channel effective field theory analysis using heavy hadron chiral perturbation theory; solving the Lippmann–Schwinger equation with regularized contact interactions; scanning cutoff dependence to assess robustness of resonance predictions.

Result: Predictions include: (i) a narrow 1/2(1/2^+) resonance above Σ_b K threshold; (ii) a molecular 1/2(3/2^+) candidate above Λ_b K* threshold; (iii) compact 3/2(1/2^+) and 3/2(3/2^+) resonances in Σ_b K*; (iv) a cutoff-sensitive 1/2(1/2^+) resonance in the antikaon sector; (v) a broad subthreshold 1/2(3/2^+) molecular candidate in Σ_b K̄*.

Conclusion: The positive-parity bottom-strange systems are rich in resonance structures, many exhibiting molecular characteristics; several predictions are experimentally testable at LHCb, particularly those near accessible thresholds and with favorable quantum numbers.

Abstract: In this work, we perform a systematic investigation of the $P$-wave $Σ_b K/Λ_b K^{*}/Σ_b K^{*}$ and $Σ_b\bar{K}/Λ_b\bar{K}^{*}/Σ_b\bar{K}^{*}$ systems for all possible $I(J^P)$ combinations. In contrast to our earlier study of the negative-parity sector [Eur. Phys. J. C \textbf{85}, 1026 (2025)], the positive-parity sector is dominated by resonances. For the coupled-channel $I(J^P)=1/2(1/2^+)$ $Σ_b K/Λ_b K^*/Σ_b K^*$ system, we predict a narrow resonance above the $Σ_b K$ threshold. In the $I(J^P)=1/2(3/2^+)$ $Λ_b K^*/Σ_b K^*$ system, a molecular resonance candidate is found above the $Λ_b K^*$ threshold. For the higher-isospin $I=3/2$ sector, resonances are identified in the $Σ_b K^*$ subsystem for both $J^P=1/2^+$ and $3/2^+$ cases, though both states exhibit relatively compact sizes. We also extend our analysis to the $Σ_b\bar{K}/Λ_b\bar{K}^{*}/Σ_b\bar{K}^{*}$ systems, where a cutoff-sensitive resonance is found in the $I(J^P)=1/2(1/2^+)$ configuration. A broad molecular resonance candidate is found below the $Σ_b\bar{K}^*$ threshold in the $I(J^P)=1/2(3/2^+)$ system. We hope that these predictions provide useful guidance for future experimental searches for bottom-strange molecular pentaquarks by LHCb.

</details>


### [66] [Majoron-driven spontaneous leptogenesis in type-II seesaw model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07130)
*Eung Jin Chun,Tae Hyun Jung,Jin Sun*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies spontaneous leptogenesis in the type-II seesaw model with a rotating Majoron background, deriving Boltzmann equations to identify freeze-out/freeze-in regimes; lepton/Higgs asymmetries depend mainly on triplet branching fractions—not mass—and scale as the square root of the smaller branching fraction when one decay channel is suppressed; the Majoron background induces no CP-asymmetric decay in the massless limit, making spontaneous leptogenesis a minimal, robust alternative to thermal type-II leptogenesis without extra triplets or explicit CP violation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
