Motivation: To realize spontaneous leptogenesis minimally within the type-II seesaw framework, avoiding ad hoc CP-violating phases or additional scalar fields required by conventional thermal leptogenesis.

Method: Derivation and analysis of the full set of Boltzmann equations for asymmetric particle densities; analytical and numerical identification of freeze-out and freeze-in regimes; examination of CP asymmetry induced by a rotating Majoron background in the massless final-state limit.

Result: Lepton and Higgs asymmetries are determined primarily by scalar triplet branching fractions and are nearly insensitive to triplet mass; asymmetry scales as sqrt(smaller branching fraction) under suppression of either decay channel; no CP-asymmetric decay arises from the Majoron background in the massless limit.

Conclusion: Spontaneous leptogenesis in the type-II seesaw with a rotating Majoron is a simple, robust mechanism that generates baryon asymmetry without requiring additional scalar triplets or explicit CP violation—distinguishing it from thermal type-II leptogenesis.

Abstract: We investigate a minimal realization of spontaneous leptogenesis in the type-II seesaw model, where the lepton and Higgs asymmetries are generated as decay and inverse-decay processes drive the plasma toward the displaced equilibrium dictated by a rotating Majoron background. We derive the complete set of Boltzmann equations governing the evolution of asymmetric particle densities and analytically and numerically identify the freeze-out and freeze-in regimes. The resulting asymmetries depend primarily on the triplet branching fractions and are nearly independent of the triplet mass. The asymmetry scales as the square root of the smaller branching fraction when either decay mode into leptons or Higgs fields is suppressed, reflecting the symmetry structure; the lepton number is conserved once either coupling is turned off. We also show that the Majoron background does not induce CP-asymmetric decay of the scalar triplet in the massless final state limit, and thus its impact is negligible. Our results establish spontaneous leptogenesis as a simple and robust alternative to thermal type-II leptogenesis that requires neither additional scalar triplets nor explicit CP-violating interactions.

</details>


### [67] [A Dark-Dimension Origin of Geometric Inelastic Dark Matter: The LUX-ZEPLIN High-Recoil Event and Multi-Target Tests](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07138)
*Waqas Ahmed,George K. Leontaris*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a pseudo-Dirac electroweak-doublet dark matter model in a 5D Dark-Dimension framework to explain a single high-energy (248 keV) nuclear recoil event observed by LZ; it attributes the required ~360 keV mass splitting to exponential suppression from extra-dimensional geometry, predicts a neutron-scattering cross section of ~1.86×10⁻³⁹ cm², and identifies viable parameter space near (1.35 TeV, 366 keV), testable via multi-target (Xe, W, CaWO₄) direct detection.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the single 248 keV nuclear recoil event reported by LZ's extended analysis—unexplained by standard elastic WIMP models—via endothermic (inelastic) dark matter scattering, requiring a small mass splitting δ ~ O(100) keV between pseudo-Dirac components.

Method: Constructs a five-dimensional 'Dark-Dimension' model with SM and vectorlike electroweak doublets localized on the visible brane; dark-number violation mediated by a neutral bulk field induces exponentially suppressed mass splitting δ = δ_UV e^(−2πMR); computes inelastic scattering rates off nuclei (Xe, W, CaWO₄) using effective neutral-current interactions; performs two-bin Poisson likelihood analysis over LZ's high-recoil window (200–3000 keV).

Result: Identifies best-fit point at (m_χ, δ) ≈ (1.35 TeV, 366 keV); thermally motivated (1.1 TeV, 360 keV) lies only Δχ² ≈ 0.21 higher; predicts σ_χn^Z ≈ 1.86×10⁻³⁹ cm²; yields 3.15×10⁻² events kg⁻¹ yr⁻¹ in CaWO₄ (95–150 keV tungsten recoils); model is excluded for light targets (e.g., Si, Ge) but remains viable for Xe and W.

Conclusion: The geometric origin of the pseudo-Dirac mass splitting in a 5D Dark-Dimension framework naturally generates the ~360 keV scale needed for inelastic scattering to explain the LZ high-recoil event; the model is testable via target-dependent spectral signatures—especially contrasting responses of xenon, tungsten, and calcium tungstate—and thus offers a falsifiable link between extra-dimensional geometry and direct-detection phenomenology.

Abstract: The extended nuclear-recoil analysis of LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) has reported one event compatible with a recoil energy near $248$ keV, motivating endothermic dark-matter scenarios with support at large recoil energy. We study a pseudo-Dirac electroweak-doublet dark-matter model embedded in a five-dimensional Dark-Dimension framework. The Standard Model and the vectorlike electroweak doublets are localized on the visible brane, while dark-number violation is communicated through a neutral bulk state. The resulting mass splitting is exponentially suppressed, $δ=δ_{\rm UV}e^{-2πMR}$, providing a geometric origin for the few-hundred-keV scale relevant to inelastic scattering. The neutral weak current is dominantly off diagonal, fixing the neutron-scale normalization at $σ_{χn}^{Z}\simeq1.86\times10^{-39}\,\mathrm{cm^2}$. Using a two-bin Poisson diagnostic of the extended LZ recoil window, we find a shallow minimum near $(m_χ,δ)\simeq(1.35~\TeV,366~\keV)$, while the thermally motivated point $(1.1~\TeV,360~\keV)$ lies only $Δχ^2\simeq0.21$ higher. The same benchmark is kinematically inaccessible to light targets but remains open for xenon and tungsten, providing a characteristic multi-target test of the model. For CaWO$_4$ we obtain an integrated tungsten rate of $3.15\times10^{-2}$ events kg$^{-1}$ yr$^{-1}$ in the $95$--$150$ keV interval. These results connect the geometric origin of the pseudo-Dirac splitting with high-recoil direct-detection phenomenology.

</details>


### [68] [Probing Neutral Triple Gauge Couplings at e- p colliders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07168)
*Xue-Jia Cheng,Chong-Xing Yue,Ji-Chong Yang,Si-Tong Liu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates neutral Triple Gauge Couplings (nTGCs) within the SMEFT framework using $e^{-}p$ collisions at the FCC-he, deriving improved constraints on dimension-8 operator coefficients and demonstrating competitive sensitivity relative to LHC and CEPC.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe new physics beyond the Standard Model in a model-independent way via nTGCs using the SMEFT framework, leveraging the unique capabilities of $e^{-}p$ colliders like FCC-he.

Method: Analyzes six distinct processes at FCC-he; models signals and backgrounds; applies tailored event selection strategies; derives expected constraints—individual and combined—on dimension-8 SMEFT operator coefficients.

Result: Obtains projected constraints on dimension-8 nTGC-related operator coefficients; shows FCC-he sensitivity comparable to current LHC limits and superior to CEPC projections.

Conclusion: FCC-he offers a highly competitive platform for nTGC studies within SMEFT, complementing and in some cases surpassing existing and planned lepton-hadron and lepton-antilepton colliders.

Abstract: The Standard Model Effective Field Theory~(SMEFT) has attracted much attention as a model-independent way for probing new physical signals. In the SMEFT framework, $e^{-}p$ colliders can be used to study new interactions of gauge bosons, such as neutral Triple Gauge Couplings~(nTGCs). We study the signals and backgrounds of six different processes at the Future Circular Collider-hadron electron~(FCC-he) and propose different event selection strategies. The expected constraints on the coefficients of the dimension-8 operators for each process, as well as the combined constraints are obtained. The results show that the sensitivity of $e^{-}p$ colliders to nTGCs is similar to the current Large Hadron Collider~(LHC) experiments and is more competitive than that of Circular Electron Positron Collider~(CEPC) experiments.

</details>


### [69] [Hadronic heavy neutral lepton decays to the limit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07189)
*Jonathan L. Schubert,Babette Döbrich*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies the hadronic decay structure of Heavy Neutral Leptons (HNLs) for multi-body final states, proposing a mass-based mapping between hadronic and partonic descriptions; it highlights implications for HNL lifetime and identifies novel multi-hadron experimental signatures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address multiple Standard Model shortcomings via Heavy Neutral Leptons (HNLs), and to better model their hadronic decays—particularly multi-body final states—where conventional parton–hadron correspondence breaks down.

Method: Develops a hadronic description of HNL decays into multi-hadron final states, and introduces an invariant-mass-dependent mapping to connect it smoothly to the underlying partonic picture.

Result: Demonstrates that multi-hadron decay channels significantly affect the total HNL lifetime and yield distinctive, experimentally accessible signatures.

Conclusion: A consistent hadronic treatment of multi-body HNL decays, anchored by invariant mass, is essential for accurate lifetime predictions and for identifying viable search strategies at current and future experiments.

Abstract: Heavy Neutral Leptons are a class of hypothetical particle, motivated by simultaneously solving multiple of the standing issues of the Standard Model. In this work we investigate the decay structure of this type of particle in the hadronic picture for multi-body final states. We propose to link the resulting hadronic description to a partonic description in a seamless transition based on the hadron system's invariant mass. Beyond the impact on the total HNL lifetime, we will show that the multi-hadron channels emerging in this description can constitute interesting experimental signatures.

</details>


### [70] [Chiral Doubling, Renormalization Group Fixed Points, and Dense Matter Equations of State](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07190)
*Chihiro Sasaki*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a top-down fixed-point-extrapolation framework in effective field theory to unify descriptions of hadronic spectroscopy, dense baryonic matter, and compact star physics—linking vacuum meson spectra, neutron star mass constraints, and quark-hadron transitions via renormalization group fixed points and controlled symmetry breaking.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify disparate nuclear and astrophysical phenomena—hadron spectroscopy, neutron star structure, and deconfinement—under a single, theoretically robust EFT framework rooted in RG fixed points rather than phenomenological model-building.

Method: Formulates an effective Lagrangian directly at RG fixed points; introduces minimal symmetry breaking to extrapolate to physical regimes; incorporates light vector meson loops for vacuum spectra; couples chiral-invariant nucleon mass m0 with walking vector couplings for dense matter; extends to quark-hadron hybrid description for deconfinement analysis.

Result: Reproduces heavy-light meson parity doubling in vacuum; satisfies 2M⊙ neutron star mass constraint while respecting gravitational-wave bounds; predicts sequential deconfinement, stable hybrid star cores, and characteristic baryon number fluctuations—all governed by underlying RG fixed-point structure.

Conclusion: RG fixed points serve as organizing principles for hadronic dynamics across energy and density scales—from QCD vacuum to neutron star interiors—and provide a predictive, unified EFT framework beyond conventional chiral or mean-field approaches.

Abstract: We review a top-down fixed-point-extrapolation paradigm as a unified effective field theory framework for hadronic spectroscopy, dense baryonic matter, and compact star physics. By formulating our effective Lagrangian directly at renormalization group (RG) fixed points and introducing minimal symmetry breaking, we extrapolate the theory back to physical environments. In the vacuum, this framework naturally reproduces the heavy-light meson parity-doubling spectrum via light vector meson loops. In dense matter, the interplay between the chiral-invariant nucleon mass m0 and walking vector couplings reconciles gravitational-wave constraints with $2M_\odot$ massive neutron stars. Generalization to a quark-hadron hybrid approach further illuminates sequential deconfinement, core stability, and baryon number fluctuations, demonstrating how underlying RG fixed points dictate hadronic dynamics from vacuum to neutron star interiors.

</details>


### [71] [Pseudo-Dirac Inelastic Dark Matter in the Leptophobic $U(1)_B$ Model: Confronting the LUX-ZEPLIN High-Recoil Event with Collider Searches](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07225)
*Xin-Yu Du,Wenjie Huang,Keping Xie*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a baryon-number-gauged dark matter model to explain the 248 keV nuclear-recoil candidate observed by LZ as an endothermic transition, predicting specific mass–coupling points testable at LHC Run-3 and HL-LHC via dijet searches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To interpret the 248 keV nuclear-recoil excess in LZ data as an endothermic dark matter transition without ad hoc assumptions—specifically, avoiding manual imposition of a stable dark state and purely off-diagonal coherent current.

Method: Constructs a minimal gauged baryon-number model with chiral dark fermions χ_L and χ_R carrying fractional baryon charges (−3/2, −9/2); uses Majorana mass generation to induce residual parity P = (−1)^{6B} for stability and ensures vector-current dominance via the same mechanism; matches LZ signal kinematics (m_χ = 1 TeV, δ = 300 keV), relic density (leading-order), and LHC dijet constraints to solve for (M_{Z_B}, g_B).

Result: A unique perturbative solution (M_{Z_B} ≈ 1.44 TeV, g_B ≈ 0.233) satisfies all three constraints simultaneously; it lies just below current LHC dijet trigger sensitivity but is testable with Run-3 data (√s = 13.6 TeV); for δ = 200 keV, predicts M_{Z_B} ≈ 2.4–3.0 TeV and g_B ≈ 0.15–0.34, within HL-LHC dijet reach.

Conclusion: Gauging baryon number naturally provides both dark matter stability and the required off-diagonal current structure; the model makes falsifiable, parameter-space-constrained predictions for near-future LHC analyses, turning the LZ anomaly into a concrete target for collider verification.

Abstract: Interpreting the $248$~keV nuclear-recoil candidate reported by LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) as an endothermic transition needs two ingredients usually put in by hand: a stable dark state, and a coherent current that is purely off diagonal. Gauging baryon number supplies both. For the minimal dark charges $B(χ_L)=-3/2$ and $B(χ_R)=-9/2$, the scalars that generate the dark masses leave an exact residual parity $P=(-1)^{6B}$ stabilizing the lightest state, while the same Majorana masses make the vector current transition dominated as an identity rather than a choice. At $m_χ=1$~TeV and $δ=300$~keV the model is over-determined: the observed LZ interval, a leading-order relic estimate and the LHC dijet limit meet at the single perturbatively acceptable point $(M_{Z_B},g_B)\simeq(1.44~\mathrm{TeV},0.233)$. That point is allowed by currently existing searches but sits just below the trigger-level dijet boundary, so it is alive and not yet decided--and a re-run of that analysis on the Run-3 data at $\sqrt s=13.6$~TeV, already on tape, will exclude it or find it. Nor is the interpretation confined to that splitting: at $δ=200$~keV, the same construction predicts a heavier mediator at comparable coupling, $M_{Z_B}\simeq2.4$-$3.0$~TeV with $g_B\simeq0.15$-$0.34$, a range the HL-LHC dijet program will probe.

</details>


### [72] [Vector-meson properties in a data-driven AdS/QCD model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07374)
*Xun Chen,Floriana Giannuzzi,Stefano Nicotri*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies ρ, ψ, and Υ mesons using an AdS/QCD holographic framework to compute vacuum properties (masses, decay constants, muon g−2 contributions) and in-medium behavior (thermal spectral functions, mass shifts, broadening, melting) and derives electrical conductivity via the Kubo formula.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify vacuum phenomenology and finite-temperature/density dynamics of heavy and light vector mesons within a consistent holographic QCD framework, bridging hadronic structure, thermal QCD, and transport properties.

Method: Holographic AdS/QCD approach: reconstructs flavor-specific gauge kinetic functions from a thermodynamic model; computes mass spectrum, decay constants, and hadronic vacuum polarization (HVP) for (g−2)μ; evaluates thermal spectral functions using finite-temperature black-brane backgrounds; applies Kubo formula to extract electrical conductivity σ(𝑇,𝜇).

Result: Quantitative predictions for vector-meson masses and decay constants matching experimental values; HVP contributions to muon anomalous magnetic moment; temperature- and chemical-potential-dependent spectral functions showing resonance suppression, broadening, and dissociation; finite electrical conductivity σ(𝑇,𝜇) reflecting charge transport in the QGP-like medium.

Conclusion: The AdS/QCD framework successfully unifies vacuum and in-medium vector-meson physics, providing a first-principles–inspired description of spectral evolution and electromagnetic transport in hot/dense QCD matter.

Abstract: We investigate the properties of $ρ$, $ψ$, and $Υ$ mesons within a holographic AdS/QCD framework, capturing both their vacuum phenomenology and in-medium dynamics. By reconstructing flavor-specific gauge kinetic functions within a model originally developed to study thermodynamic functions, we compute the vector-meson mass spectrum, the decay constants and the hadronic vacuum polarization contributions to the anomalous magnetic moment of the muon at zero temperature. Furthermore, we evaluate the thermal spectral functions to analyze mass shifts, resonance broadening, and the melting of vector bound states in a hot and dense medium. Finally, using the Kubo formula, we extract the electrical conductivity of the plasma as a function of temperature and chemical potential.

</details>


### [73] [Connecting Regge factorization and Collins-Soper evolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07424)
*Andrea Simonelli*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a connection between Regge factorization and Collins-Soper-Sterman factorization by linking the Collins-Soper kernel to the Regge trajectory, enabling unified treatment of rapidity divergences and extending k_T-factorization beyond leading-log accuracy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify the description of rapidity divergences in high-energy scattering and transverse-momentum-dependent (TMD) factorization, bridging conceptual and technical gaps between Regge and TMD frameworks.

Method: Analytical field-theoretic analysis relating the Collins-Soper evolution kernel — governing rapidity renormalization in TMD factorization — to the Regge trajectory function that controls high-energy (s-channel) asymptotics of scattering amplitudes.

Result: A direct correspondence is identified: the Collins-Soper kernel is shown to be governed by the same analytic structure as the Regge trajectory; this allows consistent resummation across high-energy (Regge) and low-q_T (TMD) regimes.

Conclusion: The equivalence provides a coherent theoretical foundation for joint resummation of large logarithms in both energy and transverse momentum, and paves the way for next-to-leading-logarithmic (and beyond) extensions of k_T-factorization.

Abstract: The relationship between Regge factorization of amplitudes and the Collins-Soper-Sterman factorization of hadronic cross sections is explored. Seeking a unified description of rapidity divergences in high-energy scattering and transverse-momentum-dependent factorization, a connection emerges between the Collins-Soper kernel and the Regge trajectory. This provides a framework for bridging high-energy and low-transverse-momentum resummation and for future extensions of $k_T$-factorization beyond leading-logarithmic accuracy.

</details>


### [74] [Baryon number freeze-out in the Standard Model, precisely](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07467)
*Cristina Benso,Dietrich Bödeker,Kohei Kamada,Kyohei Mukaida,Laura Sagunski,Philipp Schicho,Kai Schmitz*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper improves the calculation of baryon asymmetry generation via weak sphaleron transitions during the electroweak crossover by solving a temperature-dependent Boltzmann equation, incorporating higher-order corrections to the partition function and Higgs VEV, yielding precise sphaleron conversion factors for B−L and flavored lepton charges.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard equilibrium calculations assume constant temperature and neglect the dynamical evolution of the Higgs expectation value and sphaleron rate across the electroweak crossover; this oversimplification may bias predictions of baryon asymmetry from flavored lepton asymmetries, especially when B−L = 0.

Method: The authors derive and solve a Boltzmann equation for baryon number evolution across the electroweak crossover, including higher-order corrections to the grand canonical partition function and the perturbative Higgs vacuum expectation value, while consistently tracking temperature-dependent sphaleron rates and Higgs dynamics.

Result: Two sphaleron conversion factors are obtained: C_sph = 0.3328(5) for B−L conservation, and F_sph = 0.0279(19) for flavored lepton charges weighted by charged-lepton Yukawa couplings—both with quantified uncertainties.

Conclusion: Accounting for the full temperature dependence and higher-order corrections significantly refines the sphaleron conversion efficiency, enabling more accurate predictions of baryogenesis from flavored leptogenesis in the Standard Model plasma during the electroweak crossover.

Abstract: Weak sphaleron transitions turn a lepton asymmetry of the Standard Model plasma in the early Universe into a baryon asymmetry, conserving baryon-minus-lepton number $B-L$ and its individual flavored charges. A baryon asymmetry can thus also arise from flavored lepton asymmetries with vanishing $B-L$. Standard equilibrium calculations in the symmetric and broken phases are performed at constant temperature and hence neglect the fact that both the Higgs expectation value and the sphaleron rate vary as functions of temperature across the electroweak crossover. We derive a Boltzmann equation for the baryon number evolution across the crossover and calculate the freeze-out abundance including higher-order corrections to both the grand canonical partition function and the perturbative Higgs expectation value. This yields two sphaleron conversion factors: $C_\text{sph} = 0.3328(5)$ for $B-L$ and $\mathcal{F}_\text{sph} = 0.0279(19)$ for the flavored charges weighted by the charged-lepton Yukawa couplings.

</details>


### [75] [Gravitational Wave in an $A_4 \otimes Z_4$ Neutrino Model with Generalised CP](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07661)
*Pulakesh Borah,Mrinal Kumar Das*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes an extension of the Standard Model with A4 × Z4 flavor symmetry and generalized CP symmetry, using flavon fields to explain lepton mixing; it predicts a maximal Dirac CP phase (270°), effective Majorana mass ~15 meV, and a testable stochastic gravitational-wave background correlated with θ₁₃.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To simultaneously explain the observed lepton mixing pattern—including nonzero reactor angle θ₁₃—and generate testable cosmological signatures (e.g., gravitational waves) via symmetry-breaking dynamics, while linking neutrino oscillation parameters to GW observables.

Method: Introduces two A4-triplet flavons and one A4-singlet flavon coupled via complex quartic interaction; spontaneous breaking of A4 × Z4 and generalized CP generates leptonic mixing and biases domain-wall formation; global fit to neutrino oscillation data constrains parameters; gravitational-wave spectrum computed from domain-wall decay dynamics.

Result: Reproduces all measured neutrino mixing angles and mass splittings; predicts δCP = 270°, ⟨mββ⟩ ≈ 15 meV; derives stochastic GW background with peak frequency ∝ sinθ₁₃ and amplitude ∝ sin⁻⁴θ₁₃, detectable by PTAs and LISA.

Conclusion: The model unifies neutrino phenomenology and early-universe cosmology: it explains θ₁₃ and CP violation in leptonic sector, yields falsifiable 0νββ and GW predictions, and establishes a quantitative, observable link between low-energy neutrino parameters and high-energy gravitational-wave signals.

Abstract: We extend the Standard Model with an $A_4 \times Z_4$ flavor symmetry and generalized CP (GCP) symmetry, realized through two flavon triplets that spontaneously break $A_4$ and generate the observed lepton mixing pattern. A new $A_4$-singlet flavon couples to one of these triplets through a complex quartic interaction that breaks GCP and fixes the reactor mixing angle; this same coupling biases the domain walls formed when that triplet breaks $A_4$. A global fit to oscillation data reproduces all mixing angles and mass splittings, predicting a maximal Dirac CP phase of $270^\circ$ and an effective Majorana mass near $15\text{ meV}$, testable by upcoming neutrinoless double-beta-decay ($0νββ$) experiments. The same coupling generates a stochastic gravitational-wave background peaking in the pulsar-timing-array and LISA bands, with peak frequency and amplitude predicted to scale as $\sinθ_{13}$ and $\sin^{-4}θ_{13}$, a falsifiable link between neutrino oscillation and gravitational-wave (GW) observations.

</details>


### [76] [Boosted dark particles and the LZ nuclear recoil event](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07742)
*Kristjan Kannike,Martti Raidal,Alessandro Strumia*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The LZ experiment observed one high-energy nuclear recoil event (248 keV); standard halo dark matter explanations are disfavored, prompting consideration of elastic scattering by a 1–74 GeV dark sector particle with momentum-dependent interactions—specifically a pseudoscalar-pseudoscalar nucleon operator—which evades constraints from SNO and predicts distinct experimental signatures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the single anomalous 248 keV nuclear recoil event observed by LZ without violating existing constraints from other experiments (e.g., SNO, argon-based detectors) or contradicting expectations from standard galactic halo DM models.

Method: Theoretical analysis of elastic scattering kinematics and interaction operators; exclusion of sub-GeV masses using SNO deuteron break-up limits; evaluation of momentum-transfer dependence of scattering rates; comparison with spin-independent, inelastic, and light DM scenarios.

Result: A 1–74 GeV dark sector particle undergoing elastic scattering via a pseudoscalar-pseudoscalar nucleon operator can explain the LZ event while avoiding lower-energy excesses; this scenario predicts no leading signal in argon, <1% annual modulation, and observable recoils only in spinful light nuclei—distinct from inelastic halo DM.

Conclusion: The anomalous LZ event may arise from elastic scattering of a moderately heavy (1–74 GeV), momentum-coupled dark sector particle, not conventional or inelastic DM; the favored interaction operator implies testable, experiment-specific phenomenology.

Abstract: The LZ experiment reported one anomalous event with nuclear recoil energy of 248 keV. Galactic halo dark matter (DM) cannot produce such a large energy unless heavier than 74 GeV, and heavy DM with spin-independent interactions would have produced unseen recoils at lower energy. Inelastic DM has been proposed as a way out. We consider, instead, an elastic collision of a dark sector particle with a momentum of at least 123~MeV and mass in the range 1-74 GeV as deuteron break-up in SNO disfavors sub-GeV masses. The absence of accompanying lower-energy recoils favours interactions whose rate grows with the momentum transfer, such as a pseudoscalar-pseudoscalar nucleon operator. This implies no leading-order signal in argon, an annual modulation below 1 %, and recoils in spinful light nuclei where inelastic halo DM gives nothing.

</details>


### [77] [Not so good $ν$s for Higgsino dark matter as LZ excess: stringent limits from Super-Kamiokande and IceCube](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07807)
*Debajit Bose,Akash Kumar Saha,Deep Jyoti Das,Rinchen Sherpa,Abhijeet Singh,Durba Ghosh,Jaya Doliya,Subhadip Bouri,Biprajit Mondal,Ranjini Mondol,Nirmal Raj,Ranjan Laha*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper analyzes whether the single high-energy nuclear recoil event observed by LZ can be explained by inelastic scattering of Higgsino dark matter, and uses neutrino flux constraints from Super-Kamiokande and IceCube to exclude small inter-state mass splittings (<557 keV), disfavoring the Higgsino interpretation under standard halo assumptions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the viability of interpreting the LZ 250-keV nuclear recoil event as inelastic scattering of supersymmetric Higgsino dark matter, particularly by assessing consistency with solar capture and annihilation signatures in neutrino telescopes.

Method: Uses atmospheric neutrino flux measurements from Super-Kamiokande and IceCube to constrain the parameter space of thermal and non-thermal Higgsino dark matter, focusing on inter-state mass splitting; models solar capture via scattering on heavy elements and subsequent annihilation to W+W− and ZZ, yielding detectable neutrino fluxes.

Result: Excludes inter-state mass splittings ≲ 557 keV for both thermal and non-thermal Higgsinos, thereby disfavoring the Higgsino explanation of the LZ event under standard halo velocity assumptions.

Conclusion: The Higgsino-like interpretation of the LZ event is strongly disfavored by existing neutrino telescope data, unless non-standard astrophysical or particle physics assumptions (e.g., altered halo model or enhanced capture) are invoked.

Abstract: The LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) collaboration has recently reported a single nuclear recoil event at a high recoil energy of about 250 keV. This has been interpreted as inelastic scattering of dark matter that is a supersymmetric Higgsino with a mass splitting between the neutral states of a few 100 keV. Such dark matter may be captured at high recoil in the Sun through scattering on heavy elements in it, and annihilate to $W^+W^-$ and $ZZ$, in turn giving rise to a neutrino flux detectable on Earth. Using measurements of atmospheric electron- and muon-neutrino fluxes by Super-Kamiokande and IceCube, we constrain thermal and non-thermal Higgsino dark matter, excluding inter-state mass splittings $\lesssim 557$ keV. This disfavors Higgsino-like interpretations of the LZ event for standard halo velocities.

</details>


### [78] [Higgsino Dark Matter Interpretation of the LZ High-Recoil Event in the GNMSSM with TeV-Scale Gauginos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07811)
*Subhadip Bisal,Junjie Cao,Fei Li*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores endothermic dark matter scattering in the GNMSSM to explain the LUX-ZEPLIN 248-keV nuclear recoil event, using Higgsino–Singlino mixing to achieve sub-MeV neutralino mass splittings with only multi-TeV gauginos—avoiding the need for ultraheavy gauginos—while satisfying relic density and experimental constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the anomalous ~248 keV nuclear recoil event observed by LUX-ZEPLIN via endothermic (inelastic) dark matter scattering, which requires a small neutralino mass splitting (~hundreds of keV) incompatible with conventional MSSM thermal Higgsino scenarios demanding ultraheavy gauginos (~10^7 GeV).

Method: Analyzes the General Next-to-Minimal Supersymmetric Standard Model (GNMSSM), where Higgsino–Singlino mixing enables fine-tuned cancellation of gaugino-induced neutralino mass splitting; computes relic abundance (including slepton coannihilation), direct detection (Z-mediated inelastic scattering), and consistency with Higgs, flavor, and LHC constraints; constructs six benchmark points with 0.66–1.11 TeV DM mass, 333–350 keV splittings, and 2–5 TeV gauginos; performs extended-likelihood fit to LZ data under Standard Halo Model.

Result: Six viable GNMSSM benchmarks reproduce Ωh² ≈ 0.12, satisfy all current collider, Higgs, flavor, and direct-detection bounds, and yield Δχ² < 1 relative to LZ best-fit point; demonstrate that sub-MeV splittings are achievable without ultraheavy gauginos due to singlino–higgsino mixing.

Conclusion: The GNMSSM provides a theoretically consistent and phenomenologically viable framework for interpreting the LZ high-recoil event via endothermic scattering, circumventing the need for implausibly heavy gauginos in the MSSM; however, solar capture and neutrino telescope limits remain unassessed and are critical for full viability.

Abstract: The nuclear recoil event at approximately 248 keV reported by the LUX-ZEPLIN collaboration motivates an investigation of endothermic dark matter scattering. We study this within the General Next-to-Minimal Supersymmetric Standard Model (GNMSSM), with Higgsino-dominated neutralino DM undergoing the $Z$-mediated transition $\widetildeχ_1^0N\to\widetildeχ_2^0N$. In the conventional thermal Higgsino limit of the MSSM, the observed relic abundance selects a mass near 1.1 TeV, while a neutralino splitting of a few hundred keV typically requires gaugino masses of order $10^7$ GeV. In the GNMSSM, Higgsino-Singlino mixing introduces an additional contribution to the splitting that can cancel the gaugino-induced contribution, allowing sub-MeV splitting with multi-TeV gauginos. This mixing also modifies the inelastic scattering coupling and annihilation rates, while coannihilation with sleptons provides freedom in obtaining the observed relic abundance. We present six benchmark points with dark-matter masses of 0.66-1.11 TeV, neutralino splittings of 333-350 keV, and gaugino masses of 2-5 TeV. These points reproduce the observed relic abundance and satisfy direct-detection, Higgs, flavor, and collider constraints. Within the Standard Halo Model and extended-likelihood analysis, all six points yield $Δχ^2<1$ relative to the best fit. Our results show how the GNMSSM can accommodate the LZ high-recoil event without an ultraheavy gaugino sector. A quantitative assessment of solar-capture and neutrino-telescope constraints remains necessary for establishing viability.

</details>


### [79] [Quantum-Geometric Meissner Effect in Magnetized Color Superconductors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07835)
*Kazuya Mameda,Noriyuki Sogabe*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper identifies a quantum-geometric Meissner effect in magnetized two-flavor color-superconducting (2SC) quark matter, where Landau quantization suppresses the conventional Fermi-surface contribution and shifts dominance to the quantum metric of the lowest Landau level (LLL); the resulting transverse Meissner mass scales with the pairing gap—not the chemical potential—and its unconventional scaling is topologically protected by the LLL’s Chern number, with implications for magnetar kHz quasi-periodic oscillations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how strong magnetic fields modify the Meissner effect in color-superconducting quark matter—particularly whether quantum geometric effects, rather than standard Fermi-surface physics, govern electromagnetic response in extreme conditions relevant to magnetars.

Method: Analytical derivation of the transverse Meissner mass in magnetized 2SC quark matter using quantum kinetic theory and Berry-phase geometry; explicit computation of the quantum metric and Chern number of Landau levels, focusing on the lowest Landau level (LLL) in the strong-field limit.

Result: The transverse Meissner mass is governed by the quantum metric of the LLL in the strong-field regime, leading to gap-dominated (Δ) scaling m_γ^⊥ ∝ Δ, replacing the conventional μ-scaling; this scaling is topologically enforced by the unit Chern number of the LLL.

Conclusion: Quantum geometry—not just quasiparticle dispersion—controls the Meissner response in highly magnetized color superconductors; the resulting gap-scaled Meissner mass may lower magnetic field screening thresholds and influence magnetoelastic oscillations in magnetars.

Abstract: We find a quantum-geometric Meissner response in magnetized two-flavor color-superconducting (2SC) quark matter. Landau quantization quenches the transverse quasiparticle dispersion, suppressing the conventional Fermi-surface contribution and giving rise to a Meissner response governed by the quantum geometry of the Landau levels. In the strong-field regime, the response becomes dominated by the quantum metric of the lowest Landau level (LLL), and the leading scaling of the transverse Meissner mass is consequently set by the pairing gap, in contrast to the chemical potential scaling of conventional color superconductors. This unconventional scaling has a topological origin, as the LLL quantum metric is constrained by its Chern number. The reduced transverse Meissner mass provides potential implications for kHz quasi-periodic oscillations in magnetars.

</details>


### [80] [Initial momentum anisotropies in the kT-factorization of the CGC I: Gradient Expansion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07847)
*Oscar Garcia-Montero*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper revisits gluon production in the dilute-dilute CGC limit and shows that standard k⊥-factorization is only the zeroth-order term in a systematic gradient expansion in transverse source coordinates, organized as powers of 1/(QsR). Higher orders introduce spatial derivatives of unintegrated distributions, generating scalar/tensor structures encoding local energy-momentum tensor moments and initial-state momentum anisotropies—missed by leading k⊥-factorization. Real-time Glasma computation confirms this and adds further suppressed interference terms (e.g., chromo-EM interference, longitudinal energy flux), all locally expressible in terms of the same unintegrated distributions. These corrections are phenomenologically relevant for dilute, fluctuation-dominated systems like light-ion collisions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To go beyond the leading k⊥-factorization approximation in the Color Glass Condensate framework, which neglects spatial gradients and initial-state momentum-space anisotropies arising from transverse density variations — especially important in dilute or fluctuation-dominated collision systems where QsR is not large.

Method: Systematic gradient expansion in transverse coordinates of the two colliding color sources, organized in powers of 1/(QsR); derivation of extended k⊥-factorized formula retaining spatial dependence and kinetic moments; independent verification via real-time classical Glasma field computation in the future light cone, including eikonal and coherent amplitude–conjugate interference effects.

Result: A hierarchy of corrections to k⊥-factorization: zeroth order recovers standard factorization; higher orders introduce transverse derivatives of unintegrated gluon distributions, yielding scalar and tensor structures corresponding to local energy-momentum tensor components (e.g., pressure anisotropy, longitudinal energy flux, chromo-electric–magnetic interference); all terms are local operators on the same unintegrated distributions and scale with gradients of transverse density profiles.

Conclusion: Standard k⊥-factorization is insufficient to capture pre-hydrodynamic momentum anisotropies and energy-momentum structure in the initial state; the gradient-expanded formalism provides a systematically improvable, locally factorized framework that can be directly incorporated into existing saturation-based initial-state models — enhancing fidelity without sacrificing computational tractability, especially for light-ion or peripheral collisions.

Abstract: We revisit single-inclusive gluon production in the dilute--dilute limit of the Color Glass Condensate and show that $k_\perp$-factorization is only the zeroth order of a systematic gradient expansion in the transverse positions of the two colliding sources, organized in powers of $1/(Q_sR)$, with $Q_s$ the saturation scale and $R$ the size over which the sources vary. Collecting terms order by order builds a ladder of scalar and tensor structures from the local distributions and their transverse derivatives, yielding an extended $k_\perp$-factorized formula that retains the spatial dependence of the sources and, through kinetic moments of the spectrum, the energy-momentum tensor. Repeating the expansion from a real-time computation of the classical Glasma fields in the future light cone reproduces the momentum-space result in the eikonal limit, but additionally retains coherent interference between amplitude and conjugate. This adds terms to the ladder (flow responses, chromo-electric--magnetic interference, and a longitudinal energy flux) further suppressed by powers of the large outgoing momentum $|\pp|\gg Q_s$. The hierarchy thus reveals momentum-space anisotropies present in the initial state before any hydrodynamic evolution, which leading $k_\perp$-factorization misses by construction. Since each order is a local operator on the same unintegrated distributions, these corrections can be added directly to existing saturation-based initial-state models, giving a dynamically generated initial momentum anisotropy and a more faithful early-time energy-momentum tensor without abandoning the tractable factorized form. Set by gradients of the transverse density profiles, they grow towards more dilute, fluctuation-dominated systems, e.g. light-ion collisions. Phenomenological consequences will be developed in a companion paper.

</details>


### [81] [Recent applications of the gradient flow](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07940)
*R. Harlander,A. Shindler*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reviews how gradient flow serves as a gauge- and O(4)-invariant UV regulator in QCD, enabling consistent matching between lattice non-perturbative results and perturbative OPE-based analyses; it establishes exact flow-time dependence of Wilson coefficient conversion within a fixed OPE basis, and surveys applications to PDFs, heavy-meson observables, quark masses, and gradient-flow-defined α_s, plus a brief review of Ricci flow in quantum gravity for non-Gaussian fixed-point studies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge non-perturbative lattice QCD with perturbative operator-product expansion (OPE) frameworks using gradient flow as a theoretically clean, symmetry-preserving UV regulator, and to extend analogous geometric flow concepts to quantum gravity.

Method: Analytical perturbative QCD within the gradient-flow formalism; systematic OPE construction with flowed operators; matching of flowed and unflowed matrix elements; renormalization-group analysis of flow-time dependence; extension to Ricci flow in Euclidean quantum gravity via functional renormalization group (FRG) or perturbative beta-function calculations.

Result: Proof that flow-time dependence of Wilson coefficient conversion is exact order-by-order in perturbation theory within a given OPE basis; successful applications to parton distribution functions (via pseudo-PDFs), B-meson decay constants, RI/MOM-scheme quark masses, and scale-setting via gradient-flow coupling; demonstration of Ricci flow’s utility in identifying non-Gaussian fixed points in asymptotically safe gravity.

Conclusion: Gradient flow provides a rigorous, symmetry-respecting framework for high-precision QCD phenomenology by unifying lattice and perturbative methods; its conceptual extension to Ricci flow offers promising tools for exploring quantum gravity fixed points.

Abstract: The gradient flow provides a gauge- and O(4)-invariant ultraviolet regulator for QCD. This opens new opportunities for combining non-perturbative lattice calculations with perturbative results, in particular in the context of an operator-product expansion (OPE). We review the concepts behind this and argue that, within the operator basis of a given OPE, the flow-time dependence of the conversion between flowed and regular Wilson coefficients is exact at each perturbative order. Recent applications to parton distribution functions, heavy-meson observables, quark masses, and gradient-flow definitions of the strong coupling are discussed. Finally, the perturbative formulation of the Ricci flow in quantum gravity and its application to the study of non-Gaussian fixed points is reviewed.

</details>


### [82] [Near-Threshold Dynamics of \c{hi}c1(3872) and T+cc(3875) States via Effective Range Expansion and Monte Carlo Uncertainty Propagation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08289)
*A. A. Atangana Likéné,A. Franck Rothen,J. M. Ema'a Ema'a,P. Pradyun Hebbar,Saralees Nadarajah,Tobias Golling,G. H. Ben-Bolie*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses effective-range expansion (ERE) and compositeness relations to analyze the near-threshold tetraquark candidates χc1(3872) and Tcc⁺(3875), finding large negative scattering lengths and >95% molecular composition—strongly supporting their predominantly hadronic-molecule nature.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine the internal structure (compact tetraquark vs. hadronic molecule) of the exotic near-threshold states χc1(3872) and Tcc⁺(3875), leveraging their proximity to meson-meson thresholds and sensitivity of ERE to compositeness.

Method: Two-channel effective-range expansion combined with resonance compositeness relations; scattering lengths, effective ranges, and molecular compositeness coefficients extracted from experimental masses/widths via Monte Carlo uncertainty propagation (N=50,000); supplemented by ERE+CDD-pole analysis to test for bare-state contributions.

Result: Large negative scattering lengths (a = −8.49⁺⁰.⁹³₋₁.₀₅ fm for χc1(3872); a = −14.57⁺¹.⁷₀₋₁.₆₆ fm for Tcc⁺) and molecular compositeness X₂ > 0.95; no physically viable bare-state pole consistent with data and natural background found.

Conclusion: Both χc1(3872) and Tcc⁺(3875) are overwhelmingly molecular (loosely bound hadronic molecules), not compact tetraquarks; the ERE+compositeness framework is robust and reliable for near-threshold exotic hadron structure analysis.

Abstract: We investigate the near-threshold structure of the exotic tetraquark candidates $χ_{c1}(3872)$ and $T_{cc}^+(3875)$ using the effective-range expansion (ERE) and resonance compositeness relations. From the experimental masses and widths, we extract scattering lengths, effective ranges, and molecular compositeness coefficients within a two-channel framework, with uncertainties propagated through $N=50000$ Monte Carlo samples. We find large negative scattering lengths, $a=-8.49^{+0.93}_{-1.05}$ fm for $χ_{c1}(3872)$ and $a=-14.57^{+1.70}_{-1.66}$ fm for $T_{cc}^+$, and dominant molecular components $X_2>0.95$. The results are robust against variations of compositeness, experimental correlations, and the loop-function subtraction constant. An explicit ERE+CDD-pole analysis finds no physically reasonable bare-state contribution consistent with the observed poles and a natural background. Our results provide strong evidence for predominantly molecular structures of both states and demonstrate the robustness of the ERE approach to near-threshold exotic hadrons.

</details>


### [83] [Top-quark flavor-changing neutral currents and charged-lepton flavor violation in a generational three-Higgs-doublet model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08350)
*Hao-Ran Ma,Ti-Bin Hou,Yu-Ju Peng,Jin-Lei Yang,Tai-Fu Feng*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies flavor-changing and flavor-violating processes—top-quark FCNC decays, charged-lepton flavor violation (CLFV), and coherent μ-e conversion—in the CP-conserving generational three-Higgs-doublet model (G3HDM), incorporating tree-level and one-loop contributions. After applying stringent theoretical and experimental constraints (vacuum stability, perturbative unitarity, Higgs data, EW precision, B-physics), it finds Br(t→ch₁) ≤ 1.04×10⁻⁵, Br(h₁→μτ) ≤ 4.83×10⁻⁴, and μ-e conversion rates up to ~5×10⁻¹⁵ (Al) and ~1×10⁻¹⁴ (Au), with the Al rate potentially observable in next-gen experiments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe new physics beyond the Standard Model through highly suppressed flavor-violating observables—specifically, top-quark FCNC decays, CLFV, and coherent μ-e conversion—in a theoretically consistent and phenomenologically viable extension: the CP-conserving generational three-Higgs-doublet model (G3HDM).

Method: A comprehensive scan of the G3HDM parameter space (4839 points) subject to theoretical constraints (vacuum stability, perturbative unitarity) and experimental bounds (Higgs mass & signal strengths, electroweak precision observables, UTfit 2025 neutral-meson mixing, B→Xₛγ, Bₛ→μ⁺μ⁻); calculation of tree-level and one-loop contributions to t→ch₁, h₁→μτ, and coherent μ-e conversion; identification of dominant mechanisms via flavor-changing neutral-Higgs couplings.

Result: Br(t→ch₁) ≤ 1.04×10⁻⁵, Br(h₁→μτ) ≤ 4.83×10⁻⁴, CR(μAl→eAl) ≈ 4.95×10⁻¹⁵, CR(μAu→eAu) ≈ 1.05×10⁻¹⁴; tree-level flavor-changing neutral-Higgs couplings dominate and enhance t→ch₁ and h₁→μτ; μ-e conversion in aluminum lies within reach of upcoming experiments (e.g., Mu2e, COMET).

Conclusion: The G3HDM can generate observable signals in top FCNC, CLFV, and μ-e conversion while satisfying all current constraints; the predicted μ-e conversion rate in aluminum is testable in next-generation experiments, making it a promising probe of this model.

Abstract: In this work, we systematically investigate top-quark flavor-changing neutral-current decays, charged-lepton flavor-violating processes, and coherent $μ-e$ conversion in the CP-conserving generational three-Higgs-doublet model (G3HDM), including the relevant tree-level and one-loop contributions. The 4839-point sample passes conservative sufficient vacuum-stability conditions, necessary perturbative-unitarity preselection cuts, Higgs-mass and signal-strength constraints, electroweak precision data, the UTfit 2025 neutral-meson-mixing ranges, $\overline{B}\to X_sγ$, and $B_s\toμ^+μ^-$. In the scanned region, $\mathrm{Br}(t\to c h_1)\leq 1.04\times10^{-5}$ and $\mathrm{Br}(h_1\toμτ)\leq 4.83\times10^{-4}$, while the maximal conversion rates are $\mathrm{CR}(μ\mathrm{Al}\to e\mathrm{Al})\approx 4.95\times10^{-15}$ and $\mathrm{CR}(μ\mathrm{Au}\to e\mathrm{Au})\approx 1.05\times10^{-14}$. The dominant collider signals are mainly driven by tree-level flavor-changing neutral-Higgs couplings, which determine the relative strengths of these processes and induce the pronounced enhancement of $t\to c h_1$ and $h_1\toμτ$. Meanwhile, the upper range of the predicted $μ-e$ conversion rate in aluminum lies within the sensitivity reach of next-generation $μ-e$ conversion experiments.

</details>


### [84] [Light cone distributions of $P$-wave quarkonia](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08393)
*Yang Li,Tianyang Hu,Xianghui Cao,Meijian Li*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes leading-twist light-cone distribution amplitudes (LCDAs) of P-wave quarkonia using basis light-front quantization (BLFQ), revealing relativistic corrections—especially 'W'-shaped structures in axial-vector mesons—beyond the non-relativistic limit, crucial for hard exclusive process predictions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Renewed interest in light-cone distributions of P-wave quarkonia motivates a first-principles, relativistic treatment to go beyond the non-relativistic approximation and provide accurate non-perturbative inputs for high-energy collider phenomenology.

Method: Basis Light-Front Quantization (BLFQ) — a non-perturbative Hamiltonian light-front approach — is employed to compute leading-twist distribution amplitudes of P-wave quarkonia (e.g., χ_c, h_c, χ_b), explicitly incorporating relativistic dynamics and Fock-space mixing (e.g., S/D-wave admixtures).

Result: The computed LCDAs show macroscopic agreement with non-relativistic expectations but feature distinct relativistic signatures: prominent 'W'-shaped structures in axial-vector (h_c, h_b) distribution amplitudes, arising from relativistically induced S- and D-wave components; such structures vanish in the strict non-relativistic limit.

Conclusion: Relativistic frameworks like BLFQ are indispensable for quantitatively describing P-wave quarkonium structure; the observed 'W'-shaped LCDAs constitute novel, non-perturbative predictions essential for precision QCD analyses of exclusive quarkonium production at colliders (e.g., LHC, EIC).

Abstract: Motivated by renewed interest in the light-cone distributions of $P$-wave quarkonia, we investigate the leading-twist distribution amplitudes of these states within the basis light-front quantization (BLFQ) formalism. While our extracted distributions exhibit macroscopic shapes consistent with expectations from the non-relativistic limit, we find that relativistic effects introduce critical structural features. Most notably, we observe novel ``W"-shaped structures in the distribution amplitudes of the axial vector mesons, which arise from relativistically induced $S/D$ partial waves and cannot be explained by non-relativistic dynamics. These findings provide essential non-perturbative inputs for analyzing hard exclusive processes and highlight the importance of relativistic frameworks for understanding heavy quarkonium production and structure at modern high-energy colliders.

</details>


### [85] [Time evolution of scalar condensate decay](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08420)
*Ayuki Kamada,Kodai Sakurai*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates how the parametric-resonance decay rate of a coherently oscillating scalar field converges to the asymptotic Feynman-diagrammatic result over time, revealing band-dependent convergence behavior in the narrow-resonance regime and contrasting bosonic vs fermionic quantum analogs (Rabi models) to highlight the role of quantum statistics (Bose enhancement vs Pauli blocking).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the time-scale and mechanism by which the parametric-resonance description of particle production from an oscillating scalar field converges to the standard Feynman-diagrammatic decay rate—especially given their known agreement at late times—and to clarify the roles of instability band structure and quantum statistics.

Method: Numerical time evolution of the phase-space density of daughter particles to track convergence of resonance bands; analytical and numerical analysis of Bosonic and Fermionic Rabi models as simplified quantum analogs of preheating dynamics.

Result: In the narrow-resonance regime, the lowest-order instability band converges to the Feynman result within ~10–100 scalar oscillation periods, independent of coupling; higher bands show coupling-dependent convergence. The Bosonic Rabi model reproduces exact agreement at all times, while the Fermionic Rabi model disagrees asymptotically—indicating Bose enhancement enables convergence, whereas Pauli blocking prevents it.

Conclusion: The total decay width, dominated by the lowest instability band in perturbation theory, approaches the Feynman-diagrammatic result universally (coupling-independently), and this convergence is intrinsically tied to bosonic quantum statistics—highlighting a fundamental distinction between bosonic and fermionic particle production in coherent-field decays.

Abstract: A scalar field, which oscillates coherently over the space and decays through production of daughter particles, plays an important role in cosmology. It was recently shown that the parametric-resonance and Feynman-diagrammatic approaches give the same decay rate for a Bosonic daughter particle at a large time duration. We study how the parametric-resonance result approaches to this asymptotic value, by numerically following the time evolution of the phase density of the daughter particle. We see a difference among different instability bands in the narrow-resonance regime. The lowest-order instability band approaches to the Feynman-diagrammatic result in tens of scalar oscillation periods, while the high-order instability bands approach dependently also on a coupling between the scalar and daughter particles. This would infer that the total decay width, to which the lowest-order instability band contributes most dominantly in the perturbation theory, approaches to the Feynman-diagrammatic result also independently of the coupling. To confirm it, we consider a simpler and analytically solvable quantum mechanical model: Rabi models. In the Bosonic Rabi model, the parametric-resonance and Feynman-diagrammatic results agree with each other for all the time. On the other hand, in the Fermionic Rabi model, they disagree at a large time duration. This difference would be attributed to Bose enhancement vs Pauli blocking.

</details>


### [86] [How to choose a good rational basis for elliptic Feynman integrals?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08875)
*Ekta Chaubey,Vasily Sotnikov*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates whether a universal, minimal relation exists between rational and elliptic bases when decoupling differential equation blocks requires complete elliptic integrals—extending prior work (arXiv:2504.20897) on elliptic leading singularities—and finds that the ε-dependence of off-diagonal blocks organizes into a universal structure preserved under decoupling transformations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine if a universal and minimal basis transformation exists for multi-loop scattering amplitudes when decoupling differential equation blocks necessitates elliptic (rather than purely algebraic) functions—specifically complete elliptic integrals.

Method: Extends the elliptic leading singularity basis construction from arXiv:2504.20897 to analyze off-diagonal blocks of rational differential equations; examines how ε dependence transforms under elliptic decoupling maps to iterated integrals.

Result: The ε dependence of off-diagonal blocks in the rational differential equations exhibits a universal structure that remains invariant under the elliptic decoupling transformation used to express solutions in terms of iterated integrals.

Conclusion: A universal, minimal relation analogous to the algebraic case does emerge in the elliptic setting—not for the basis change itself, but for the ε-organized structure of off-diagonal couplings—supporting the elliptic leading singularity basis as a natural framework for elliptic amplitudes.

Abstract: Representing multi-loop scattering amplitudes as linear combinations of multivalued transcendental functions with process-dependent rational coefficients has long been understood to be advantageous. In general, these transcendental functions satisfy a system of differential equations with coupled homogeneous blocks. When these coupled blocks can be removed through algebraic basis transformations, the relation between the rational and algebraic bases is universal and minimal. Here, we ask whether an analogous universal and minimal relation exists when decoupling requires transformations involving complete elliptic integrals. We elaborate on the method proposed in ref. arXiv:2504.20897, in which we suggested that a basis constructed using an elliptic generalization of leading singularities may provide an answer to this question. We extend this analysis to the off-diagonal blocks of the rational differential equations satisfied by the bases obtained through this construction. We find that the $ε$ dependence of these blocks can be organized into a universal structure that is preserved under the decoupling transformation used to express the solutions in terms of iterated integrals.

</details>


### [87] [Relativistic spatial distributions of transverse angular momentum](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08902)
*Cédric Lorcé,Asmita Mukherjee,Ravi Singh,Ho-Yeon Won*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends prior work on transverse angular momentum distributions by analyzing transverse boost distributions and studying how both angular momentum and boost densities depend on the choice of relativistic center (mass, energy, spin); it derives 2D transverse spatial distributions for spin-1/2 targets, examines their Lorentz boost evolution, and connects instant-form and light-front descriptions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The definition of generalized angular momentum and boost densities is pivot-dependent; understanding their behavior relative to different relativistic centers (e.g., center of mass, energy, spin) is essential for a consistent relativistic description of hadron structure and for bridging instant-form and light-front frameworks.

Method: Analytical derivation of 2D transverse spatial distributions of total angular momentum and boost densities for spin-1/2 targets; systematic comparison across canonical (spin) center and alternative relativistic centers (mass, energy); analysis of longitudinal Lorentz boost dependence; construction of light-front counterparts and mapping to instant-form densities.

Result: Explicit expressions for transverse 2D distributions of angular momentum and boost relative to multiple relativistic centers; demonstration of distinct spatial patterns and their nontrivial evolution under longitudinal boosts; establishment of a rigorous correspondence between instant-form and light-front transverse density representations.

Conclusion: The spatial structure of transverse angular momentum and boost is intrinsically tied to the choice of relativistic center; no single center universally simplifies all observables—physical interpretation requires careful specification of pivot; the light-front and instant-form pictures are reconcilable via proper kinematic matching, enabling unified access to partonic and spatial information.

Abstract: In our previous work [C. Lorcé et al., Phys. Lett. B 868 (2025) 139792], we investigated the 2D spatial distributions of transverse total angular momentum, including orbital angular momentum and intrinsic spin, relative to the canonical center (or center of spin). In the present work, we extend this analysis in two directions. First, we study the corresponding transverse boost distributions relative to the canonical center. Second, since the definition of generalized angular momentum density depends crucially on the choice of pivot, we analyze how the spatial distributions of transverse total angular momentum and boost are modified when they are defined relative to different relativistic centers, viz.~the relativistic centers of mass, energy, and spin. Considering spin-1/2 targets, we derive the corresponding 2D spatial distributions in the transverse plane, and further investigate how the spatial patterns evolve under longitudinal Lorentz boosts. Additionally, we provide the corresponding light-front distributions in the transverse plane, establishing a clear connection between the instant-form and light-front descriptions of transverse angular momentum and boost.

</details>


### [88] [Nonperturbative functional renormalization group for Higgs-singlet models with physics-informed neural networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08470)
*Norimi Yokozaki*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a physics-informed neural network (PINN)-based nonperturbative functional renormalization group (FRG) method in LPA' to compute the finite-temperature effective potential for a $Z_2$-symmetric real singlet extension of the Standard Model, avoiding polynomial truncations; it demonstrates mesh-free, continuous reconstruction of the potential, validates against perturbation theory and grid solvers, and identifies soft consistency constraints as essential—and still imperfectly tuned—for physical solution selection.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome limitations of polynomial truncations in conventional FRG approaches for computing the scale- and field-dependent effective potential—especially near phase transitions—while enabling accurate, nonperturbative, and mesh-free treatment of the electroweak phase transition in a BSM scalar singlet model.

Method: Develops a hybrid ansatz combining tree-level contributions with a physics-informed neural network (PINN) to represent the scale-dependent effective potential within the LPA' approximation of the Wetterich equation; implements multi-domain RG flow in both scale and field space with derivative-matching boundary conditions; incorporates gauge/Yukawa effects via perturbative two-loop running couplings and includes anomalous dimensions; enforces a soft consistency constraint aligned with resummed perturbation theory to guide solution selection.

Result: Achieves continuous, mesh-free reconstruction of the finite-temperature effective potential across field and scale domains; successfully computes 1D slices at benchmark temperatures and a 2D reconstruction at T = 100 GeV yielding a two-step bounce action; validates PINN solutions against both resummed perturbation theory and a grid-based relaxation solver; finds the soft consistency constraint necessary—not optional—for selecting physically sensible solutions.

Conclusion: PINNs offer a viable, flexible framework for nonperturbative FRG computations beyond polynomial truncations, but residual dependence on hand-tuned soft constraints (weights and analytic thermal targets) reveals an unresolved challenge in fully objective, first-principles solution selection.

Abstract: We develop a nonperturbative functional renormalization group framework within the LPA' to solve the Wetterich flow equation for the $Z_2$-symmetric real singlet extension of the Standard Model at zero and finite temperature, without a polynomial expansion of the scale-dependent effective potential, using a physics-informed neural network (PINN) representation. In contrast to conventional truncations based on low-order field expansions, our hybrid tree-level--plus--neural-network ansatz yields a continuous, mesh-free description of the effective potential over the full relevant field and scale range. As a proof of concept, we apply the framework to the finite-temperature effective potential relevant to the electroweak phase transition in this model: one-dimensional field-space slices at two benchmark temperatures, and a two-dimensional reconstruction at $T=100$ GeV from which a two-step bounce action is extracted. The renormalization group flow is implemented in a multi-domain setup in scale and field space with derivative matching conditions that ensure smoothness and numerical stability. Gauge and Yukawa sectors are incorporated via independently computed perturbative two-loop running couplings, and anomalous dimensions are included in the flow at the LPA' level. We benchmark the network against resummed perturbation theory and against a grid-based relaxation solver of the same equation. Finally, we introduce a soft consistency constraint that keeps the solution close, in sign and order of magnitude, to perturbation theory across the field-space domain; we find it necessary, rather than merely helpful, for selecting a physically sensible solution of the flow equation, and find that the converged result retains a residual dependence on this guidance -- through hand-tuned weights and an analytic thermal target -- which we identify as the central open problem.

</details>


### [89] [Gravitational transverse momentum dependent distributions for the gluons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08499)
*Kauship Saha,Dipansu Mishra,Dipankar Chakrabarti,Asmita Mukherjee*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies the momentum-space structure of the gauge-invariant gluon energy-momentum tensor (EMT) for spin-0 and spin-1/2 hadrons using light-front formalism, introducing gluon gravitational TMDs and linking them to conventional twist-2/twist-3 gluon TMDs; it further shows how EMT components encode gluon kinetic four-momentum and hadronic mechanical properties (pressure, shear).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the internal mechanical structure and momentum distribution of gluons inside hadrons via a gauge-invariant, relativistic framework.

Method: Light-front quantization; parameterization of the nonlocal gluon EMT in terms of gravitational transverse-momentum-dependent distributions (gravitational TMDs); derivation of relations between gravitational TMDs and standard twist-2/twist-3 gluon TMDs.

Result: Gravitational TMDs are established as fundamental objects encoding both gluonic kinetic four-momentum and spatially resolved mechanical properties (e.g., pressure and shear force distributions) inside hadrons.

Conclusion: The gauge-invariant gluon EMT admits a physically transparent decomposition in momentum space via gravitational TMDs, enabling quantitative access to gluon dynamics and hadron mechanical structure beyond partonic distributions.

Abstract: We investigate the momentum-space structure of the gauge-invariant gluon energy-momentum tensor (EMT) for spin-$0$ and spin-$\tfrac{1}{2}$ hadrons in the light-front formalism. We parameterize the nonlocal gluon EMT in terms of so-called gluon gravitational transverse-momentum-dependent distributions (gravitational TMDs) and derive their connections to twist-2 and twist-3 gluon TMDs. Moreover, we demonstrate that particular components of the gluon EMT encode information about the average kinetic four-momentum of gluons and the internal mechanical properties of hadrons-such as pressure and shear distributions.

</details>


### [90] [Two-Loop Anomalous Dimension for Non-Global and Clustering Logarithms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09104)
*Thomas Becher,Jürg Haag,Nicolas Schalch*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the two-loop anomalous dimension for resumming subleading non-global and clustering logarithms using effective field theory, identifies issues with the MS-bar scheme for parton shower implementation, proposes modified dipole subtraction schemes for frame-independent RG evolution, and validates scheme independence numerically in the leading-color limit within the Marzini framework.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable consistent resummation of subleading non-global and clustering logarithms—crucial for precision QCD predictions at colliders—while overcoming scheme-dependent complications in parton shower matching and frame dependence.

Method: Effective field theory (SCET) formalism; derivation of the two-loop anomalous dimension matrix; comparison of renormalization schemes (MS-bar vs. modified dipole subtraction schemes); implementation in the leading-color limit within the Marzini parton shower framework; numerical validation using gap-between-jets cross sections.

Result: A complete two-loop anomalous dimension for subleading non-global and clustering logarithms; identification of MS-bar’s inadequacy due to dimensional regularization artifacts in parton shower setup; construction of viable, frame-independent modified dipole subtraction schemes; numerical confirmation of scheme independence for physical observables.

Conclusion: Modified dipole subtraction schemes are essential for consistent, frame-independent resummation of subleading non-global and clustering logarithms in SCET; the derived two-loop anomalous dimension enables improved theoretical predictions for jet substructure observables.

Abstract: We derive the two-loop anomalous dimension relevant for the resummation of subleading non-global and, for the first time, also clustering logarithms within the framework of effective field theory. Special emphasis is put on the choice of the renormalization scheme. We show that the modified minimal subtraction scheme is problematic since it requires extra dimensions to set up the parton shower to perform the resummation. We discuss a set of modified dipole subtraction schemes which are free from this complication and can be used to obtain a frame-independent result for the renormalization-group evolution. We implement the leading-color limit of our result in the Marzili framework and numerically verify the scheme independence for gap-between-jets cross sections.

</details>


### [91] [A distance-independent constraint on the axion-electron coupling from RGB stars](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08504)
*Thomas Levasseur,Oscar Straniero,Andrea Caputo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper improves constraints on the axion-electron coupling (gae) using a distance-independent differential observable—the bolometric magnitude difference between the tip of the red giant branch (TRGB) and the RGB bump (RGBB)—in three metal-rich globular clusters. It derives a 95% C.L. upper limit of g13 ≡ gae/10⁻¹³ ≤ 1.49, with enhanced robustness against systematics; additionally, stellar evolution arguments exclude g13 > 7.5, surpassing current direct experimental limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome systematic uncertainties (distance, extinction, bolometric correction zero-point) plaguing traditional TRGB-based axion bounds, and to obtain more robust constraints on the axion-electron coupling gae relevant for the strong CP problem and dark matter.

Method: Uses the bolometric magnitude difference ΔM_bol(TRGB−RGBB) as a distance- and extinction-insensitive observable; analyzes V/I-band photometry from NGC 104, NGC 362, and NGC 5904; validates stellar models against observed RGBB luminosity; performs maximum likelihood analysis with Monte Carlo uncertainty propagation.

Result: Combined analysis yields maximum likelihood at g13 = 0.8 and 95% C.L. upper limit g13 ≤ 1.49; complementary stellar evolution argument rules out g13 > 7.5 (i.e., gae > 7.5 × 10⁻¹³ GeV⁻¹), exceeding sensitivity of XENONnT.

Conclusion: The differential TRGB–RGBB method provides significantly more robust axion-electron coupling constraints than absolute TRGB luminosity alone; its application to larger globular cluster samples will further tighten bounds on gae and probe BSM physics.

Abstract: Interest in axions and axion-like particles has resurged, driven by their role in solving the strong CP problem and their appeal as dark matter candidates. The luminosity of the tip of the red giant branch (TRGB) offers a key observable for probing these particle properties. This work aims to improve existing bounds on the axion-electron coupling by adopting a differential observable that is less sensitive to distance, interstellar extinction, the zero-point of bolometric corrections (BCs), and other systematic uncertainties. We use the bolometric magnitude difference between the TRGB and the RGB bump (RGBB) as a distance-independent constraint, applied to three globular clusters of intermediate-to-high metallicity: NGC 104 (47 Tuc), NGC 362, and NGC 5904 (M5). Using photometric catalogs of RGB stars, we determine V- and I-band magnitudes of both features and convert them to bolometric values. After validating that our stellar models reproduce the observed RGBB luminosity, we perform a maximum likelihood analysis with Monte Carlo simulations to propagate uncertainties and derive new bounds on the coupling. A combined analysis of the three clusters yields a maximum likelihood at g13 = gae/10^-13 = 0.8 and a 95% C.L. upper limit of 1.49. Although slightly less stringent than recent bounds from larger multi-cluster samples, this limit is substantially more robust. Under reasonable mass-loss assumptions, g13 > 7.5 is ruled out, as it predicts the disappearance of the HB and AGB phases routinely observed in globular clusters, a limit more than an order of magnitude stronger than current direct experimental bounds such as XENONnT. We demonstrate the effectiveness of this differential, distance-independent method for constraining physics beyond the Standard Model. Applying it to a wider sample of globular clusters would further refine the constraint on gae.

</details>


### [92] [Hot QCD: transport coefficients in strong and weak coupling regimes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08521)
*Gabriele Parisi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes shear viscosity η and spatial heavy-quark diffusion coefficient D_s in a Quasi-Particle Model (QPM) using new 3+1-flavor lattice QCD data; it employs tree-level scattering matrices, Fokker–Planck approximation for D_s, dual-component Chapman–Enskog formalism for η/s, and compares results with Green–Kubo/Boltzmann methods and strong/weak coupling limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve theoretical predictions of transport coefficients in the quark-gluon plasma (QGP) by incorporating modern N_f=3+1 lattice QCD input into a quasi-particle framework, enabling more realistic modeling of gluon mass and interactions beyond ideal gas assumptions.

Method: Quasi-Particle Model with massive gluons; tree-level Feynman diagram-derived scattering matrices for qq, qg, gg interactions; Fokker–Planck approximation to solve relativistic Boltzmann equation for charm/bottom diffusion; dual-component Chapman–Enskog expansion for η/s in quark–gluon mixture; comparison with Green–Kubo calculations from stochastic relativistic Boltzmann solver.

Result: Temperature-dependent η and D_s computed for N_f=3 and N_f=3+1 QGP; η/s ratio obtained analytically via Chapman–Enskog; quantitative agreement/disagreement between QPM and Green–Kubo results established; ratio (2πT D_s)/(4πη/s) analyzed across temperature to probe strong vs. weak coupling behavior.

Conclusion: The QPM with N_f=3+1 lQCD constraints provides a consistent and predictive framework for heavy-quark diffusion and shear viscosity; deviations from weak-coupling expectations highlight non-perturbative dynamics near T_c, supporting intermediate coupling in the QGP.

Abstract: We evaluate the transport coefficients, namely the shear viscosity $η$ and the spatial diffusion coefficient of heavy quarks $D_s$, within a Quasi-Particle Model (QPM). In particular, for the first time this has been done by exploiting novel lQCD data at $N_f=3+1$. In such a framework, in which the gluons are massive, the interactions among quarks and gluons have been evaluated via the scattering matrices evaluated from tree level Feynman diagrams. As far as the evaluation of $D_s$ is concerned, the Fokker-Planck approximation for the relativistic Boltzmann equation has been employed for the diffusion of both charm and bottom quarks in either a $N_f=3$ and a $N_f=3+1$ bulk. We have developed a dual-component Chapman-Enskog formalism for the ratio $η/s$ (being $s$ the entropy density) of a mixture of quarks and gluons. The temperature dependence of the shear viscosity is then compared to the results from a Green-Kubo approach, based on the numerical resolution of the relativistic Boltzmann equation with a stochastic implementation of the collision integral.
