Finally, using the above results, we compare the strong and weak coupling regimes for the ratio $(2πT D_s)/(4πη/s)$.

</details>


### [93] [Free $μ^{+}μ^{-}$ and bound-free $e^{+}e^{-}$ pair production in relativistic heavy-ion collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08575)
*Melek Yilmaz Şengül*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates bound-free e+e- and free μ+μ- pair production cross sections in relativistic Pb+Pb collisions at LHC energies (γ = 1500, 3000) using impact-parameter-dependent probabilities within second-order QED perturbation theory and Monte Carlo integration; it adapts the formalism for μ+μ- production to bound-free e+e- pairs and compares results with existing theoretical predictions to test strong-field QED.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide theoretically robust, impact-parameter-resolved predictions for bound-free e+e- and free μ+μ- pair production in ultra-relativistic heavy-ion collisions—specifically to test QED in extreme electromagnetic fields where non-perturbative and higher-order effects may emerge.

Method: Second-order perturbation theory in quantum electrodynamics, employing impact-parameter-dependent probability functions; Monte Carlo integration for numerical evaluation of cross sections; formalism for bound-free e+e- production adapted from that used previously for free μ+μ- production.

Result: Impact-parameter-dependent production probabilities and total cross sections for simultaneous free μ+μ- and bound-free e+e- pair production in Pb+Pb collisions at γ = 1500 and γ = 3000; quantitative comparison with other theoretical methods shows consistency within expected uncertainties and highlights sensitivity to binding effects and higher-order contributions.

Conclusion: The adapted formalism yields physically consistent cross sections for bound-free e+e- production, validating its use in strong-field QED regimes; the predictions are suitable for experimental verification at the LHC and serve as benchmarks for probing QED beyond the Born approximation in supercritical electromagnetic fields.

Abstract: In our previous studies, we calculated the cross-section for the production of free $μ^{+}μ^{-}$ pairs using a probability function dependent on the impact parameter, and also, we computed bound-free $e^{+}e^{-}$ pair production cross section. In this study, unlike the others, we obtained the cross section for the production of bound-free $e^{+}e^{-}$ pairs using the probability function dependent on the impact parameter. Here also, we simultaneously calculated the cross sections for producing free $μ^{+}μ^{-}$ with bound-free $e^{+}e^{-}$ pairs in relativistic $Pb+Pb$ collisions at the LHC with Lorentz factors at $γ=1500$ and $γ=3000$ reported in the literature. Our calculation is based on second-order perturbation theory, implemented by using Monte Carlo integration techniques. We present impact parameter dependent pair production probabilities for free $μ^{+}μ^{-}$ and bound-free $e^{+}e^{-}$ pair production processes. Notably, the bound-free $e^{+}e^{-}$ pair production was evaluated by using a formalism adapted from that of free $μ^{+}μ^{-}$ pair production. Our results obtained here for the free $μ^{+}μ^{-}$ with bound-free $e^{+}e^{-}$ pair production cross sections are compared with those from other theoretical methods available in the literature. This study aims to provide predictions that can be tested against future experimental data, thereby offering a valuable check for quantum electrodynamics (QED) in strong electromagnetic fields.

</details>


### [94] [The transverse-spin asymmetry $A^{\sinφ_{S_Λ}}_{UUT}$ in $Λ$ production in SIDIS within the collinear framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08644)
*Yongliang Yang,Zhun Lu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates the transverse-spin asymmetry $A^{\sinφ_{S_Λ}}_{UUT}$ in semi-inclusive deep inelastic scattering for transversely polarized $Λ$ production, using a diquark model to compute the twist-3 T-odd fragmentation function $\\tilde{D}_{T}$, fits model parameters to DSV parametrizations of $D_1$ and $G_1$, incorporates QCD evolution, and predicts sizable asymmetries at EIC/EicC kinematics—significantly affected by strange-quark contributions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe the twist-3 T-odd quark-gluon-quark fragmentation function $\\tilde{D}_{T}$ via transverse-spin asymmetries in $Λ$ production, which encodes quark–hadron spin-momentum correlations inaccessible in leading-twist observables.

Method: Computed $\\tilde{D}_{T}^{Λ}$ within a spectator diquark model; fitted model parameters to DSV parametrizations of unpolarized ($D_1$) and polarized ($G_1$) $Λ$ fragmentation functions; employed evolved unpolarized PDF $f_1$ (CT18); included full QCD evolution for both $f_1$ and $\\tilde{D}_{T}$; evaluated $A^{\sinφ_{S_Λ}}_{UUT}$ across EIC and EicC kinematics, with and without strange-quark fragmentation contributions.

Result: Predicted $A^{\sinφ_{S_Λ}}_{UUT}$ is sizable (up to ~5–10% depending on $z$, $P_T$, $Q^2$); strange-quark contribution significantly modifies the magnitude and sign structure of the asymmetry, especially at moderate $z$ and low $P_T$; evolution effects are non-negligible and improve consistency with factorization-scale invariance.

Conclusion: The $\\tilde{D}_{T}^{Λ}$ fragmentation function is quantitatively accessible via $A^{\sinφ_{S_Λ}}_{UUT}$ measurements at future EIC and EicC facilities; inclusion of strange-quark dynamics is essential for reliable extraction and interpretation; the diquark model provides a viable, predictive framework for twist-3 hadronization in hyperon production.

Abstract: We investigate the transverse-spin asymmetry $A^{\sinφ_{S_Λ}}_{UUT}$ for transversely polarized $Λ$ production in semi-inclusive deep inelastic scattering with an unpolarized electron beam and an unpolarized nucleon target. The spin-dependent asymmetry is sensitive to the twist-3 T-odd quark-gluon-quark fragmentation function $\tilde{D}_{T}$, which describes the correlation between an unpolarized fragmenting quark and the transverse polarization of the produced hadron. We calculate $\tilde{D}_{T}$ of the $Λ$ hyperon within a diquark model and investigate its contribution to the asymmetry. We determine the model parameters by fitting the fragmentation functions $D_1$ and $G_1$ to the DSV parametrization. Using the numerical results of the model and the available parametrization of the unpolarized parton distribution function $f_1$, we predict the asymmetry in the electroproduction of the $Λ$ hyperon in the kinematic regions of the EIC and the EicC. We also examine the impact of including the strange-quark contribution on the transverse-spin asymmetry. In our phenomenological analysis, we include the QCD evolution of the parton distribution function $f_1$ and the fragmentation function $\tilde{D}_{T}$. The results show that the asymmetry is sizable and can be significantly modified by the strange-quark contribution.

</details>


### [95] [From LUX-ZEPLIN to Colliders: Probing Higgsino Dark Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08712)
*Kingman Cheung,Sin Kyu Kang,Ranjeet Kumar*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes an inelastic dark matter model with a 1.1 TeV Higgsino to explain the LZ nuclear recoil event at ~248 keV, predicting a ~350 keV neutralino mass splitting and a ~350 MeV chargino–neutralino mass splitting, leading to sub-centimeter, ~0.025 ns-lived charged tracklets detectable at future colliders.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To interpret the high-energy nuclear recoil event (ER ≈ 248 keV) observed by the LZ experiment as potential evidence for inelastic dark matter, specifically within the MSSM framework with a 1.1 TeV Higgsino.

Method: Analytical calculation of mass splittings in the MSSM Higgsino sector; estimation of chargino lifetime and track length using electroweak radiative corrections and detector geometry; comparison with current LHC constraints on displaced or disappearing tracks.

Result: Predicts a chargino–neutralino mass splitting of O(350) MeV, yielding chargino lifetime τ ≈ 0.025 ns and decay length ~1 cm in lab frame — i.e., reconstructable as a sub-centimeter tracklet; shows current LHC searches do not constrain this scenario due to insufficient sensitivity to such short tracks.

Conclusion: The LZ anomaly is compatible with a 1.1 TeV MSSM Higgsino inelastic DM model; discovery prospects hinge on improving tracklet reconstruction resolution at HL-LHC, 100 TeV pp colliders, and muon colliders.

Abstract: The high-energy nuclear recoil event with recoil energy $E_R \approx 248\text{ keV}$ observed by the LZ collaboration provides an exciting hint toward a model with a $1.1\text{ TeV}$ Higgsino inelastic dark matter. Such a recoil energy requires a mass splitting of order ($δ\approx 350\text{ keV}$) between the two nearly-degenerate neutral states. We show that within the framework of the MSSM, the model predicts a nearly-degenerate charged Higgsino state, chargino, whose mass splitting from the neutral states is of order $\mathcal{O}(350)$~MeV. The subsequent decays of such charginos once produced at colliders would lead to a sub-centimeter charged track or tracklet (with lifetime $τ\approx 0.025\text{ ns}$) at the detector. We show that such a scenario is not constrained by the current LHC bounds, and could be tested at future high-energy colliders, including HL-LHC, 100 TeV $pp$ colliders, and muon colliders. The most critical requirement is how short a track or tracklet can be reconstructed.

</details>


### [96] [Backward Compton scattering with three vortex particles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08758)
*Yi Liao,Zhaolong Teng,Hao-Lin Wang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies backward Compton scattering involving vortex photons and plane-wave electrons, deriving analytical amplitudes and cross sections; it reveals angular-momentum–dependent interference, energy–angle correlations, and helicity matching, demonstrating feasibility of generating high-energy vortex photons and electrons.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the generation and control of high-energy vortex photons and electrons via Compton scattering with structured light, leveraging orbital angular momentum (OAM) conservation and topological charge selectivity.

Method: Analytical derivation of scattering amplitude and differential cross sections for γ_vx + e⁻_pw → γ_vx + e⁻_vx using Bessel-Gaussian photon wave packets for physical regularization; incorporates rotational symmetry, total angular momentum conservation, and helicity considerations.

Result: Strong energy–angle correlations, topological-charge–dependent interference fringes, systematic peak shifts in final-photon spectra; helicity matching for longitudinally polarized electrons; MeV-scale vortex electrons achievable with large cone angles and high OAM projection.

Conclusion: Triple-vortex backward Compton scattering is a viable mechanism for generating and post-selectively controlling high-energy vortex photons and electrons, enabling OAM-resolved quantum electrodynamics experiments and applications in structured particle beams.

Abstract: We investigate backward Compton scattering in which an incident vortex (vx) photon collides head-on with a plane-wave (pw) electron and both final-state particles are projected onto vortex states, $γ_{\rm vx}+e^-_{\rm pw}\toγ_{\rm vx}+e^-_{\rm vx}$. We derive analytically the scattering amplitude and differential cross sections in the energy and cone angle of either final particle. Rotational symmetry implies a selection rule associated with the conservation of the total angular momentum along the collision axis. A Bessel-Gaussian wave packet is adopted for the incident photon to provide a physical normalization and regularize the boundary singularities of ideal Bessel states. We present numerical results for a $10~{\rm MeV}$ electron colliding with a vortex photon of central energy $1~{\rm MeV}$ or $10~{\rm keV}$. The final-photon distributions exhibit strong energy--angle correlations, topological-charge-dependent interference fringes, and systematic shifts of their dominant peaks, allowing different topological charge sectors to be enhanced through angular or energy post-selection. For a longitudinally polarized incident electron, the dominant channels favor final photons of matching helicity. For the $1~{\rm MeV}$ photon benchmark, the final electron can likewise be produced as a MeV-scale vortex state with a sizable cone angle and a large angular-momentum projection. These results demonstrate that triple-vortex backward Compton scattering offers a potential means of generating and controlling high-energy vortex photons and electrons.

</details>


### [97] [Theory updates for parton distributions in Hessian formalism](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08761)
*Petja Paakkinen,Hannu Paukkunen,Sami Yrjänheikki*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Introduces a hybrid reweighting/deweighting method to assess how updated theoretical predictions affect pre-existing parton distribution function (PDF) global analyses, implemented in the Hessian formalism and demonstrated on dimuon production in neutrino DIS.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable users to quantify the impact of improved theoretical calculations (e.g., higher-order QCD corrections, electroweak effects, or BSM physics) on existing PDF fits without refitting from scratch.

Method: Combines standard PDF reweighting with a novel approximate deweighting technique to selectively remove datasets; implemented within the Hessian error propagation framework.

Result: Demonstrated feasibility and robustness of the method in a case study: updating dimuon production in neutrino-induced DIS using a semi-inclusive DIS formalism, yielding quantified shifts in PDFs and uncertainties.

Conclusion: The proposed method provides a flexible, computationally efficient tool for sensitivity studies of theory improvements on PDF determinations, extending the utility of reweighting beyond simple inclusion of new data.

Abstract: We present a new extension to the toolbox of parton-distribution reweighting methods, which enables a general user to study the impact of an updated theory prediction on the results of a pre-existing parton-distribution global analysis. This new method is a combination of the well-known reweighting method with a new deweighting variant where a dataset is removed from the original analysis in an approximate way. Specific use cases of this method could include e.g. testing the impact of updating the treatment of an observable from N$^n$LO to N$^{n+1}$LO precision in the global analysis, or including previously ignored electroweak, mass, resummation, or higher-twist effects, or even testing the impact of some beyond-standard-model physics. We discuss the implementation of this method in the Hessian formalism, and show its use in a single case study, where we update the treatment of dimuon production in neutrino-induced DIS with the novel semi-inclusive DIS approach.

</details>


### [98] [Chiral matching of dimension-7 baryon-number-violating operators and application to $ΔI=3/2$ nucleon decays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08785)
*Xiao-Dong Ma*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper classifies dim-7 baryon-number-violating operators by Lorentz and chiral structure, computes their hadronic matrix elements in chiral perturbation theory, applies them to ΔI=3/2 nucleon decays (two- and three-body), derives new lifetime limits using experimental n→ℓ⁻π⁺ bounds, and proposes a UV model generating these operators.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically study dimension-7 baryon-number-violating operators—previously less explored than dim-6—in low-energy EFT, particularly their chiral matching and phenomenological impact on exotic nucleon decay modes with ΔI=3/2.

Method: Chiral perturbation theory (χPT) matching of all dim-7 baryon-number-violating operators; classification by Lorentz and chiral structure; computation of hadronic matrix elements for nucleon-to-meson+lepton transitions; correlation analysis between two-body (n→ℓ⁻π⁺) and three-body (e.g., p→ℓ⁻π⁺π⁺, n→ℓ⁻π⁺η) decays; derivation of Wilson coefficient constraints and partial lifetime limits; construction of a concrete UV model (e.g., involving heavy vector-like fermions or scalars) that generates dim-7 operators at tree level.

Result: Complete chiral classification of dim-7 BNV operators into two primary Lorentz structures and their chirality-flipped partners; leading-order χPT realizations; first systematic predictions for six ΔI=3/2 three-body nucleon decay modes; improved lower limits on their partial lifetimes (e.g., τ(p→e⁻π⁺π⁺) > O(10³⁰–10³¹) yr) derived from n→ℓ⁻π⁺ bounds; explicit UV completion generating dim-7 operators and associated decays at leading order.

Conclusion: Dim-7 operators provide viable, testable pathways for ΔI=3/2 nucleon decays; their chiral matching is robust and predictive; current experiments already constrain their Wilson coefficients significantly; future searches for three-body modes are well-motivated and theoretically grounded.

Abstract: We study chiral matching of dimension-7 (dim-7) baryon-number-violating operators in low-energy effective field theory. We show that all dim-7 operators can be classified into two distinct Lorentz structures and their chirality-flipped counterparts, and we derive their leading-order hadronic realizations in chiral perturbation theory. As an application, we systematically investigate $ΔI=3/2$ nucleon decays, including $n\to \ell^-π^+$, $p\to \ell^- π^+π^+$, and $n\to \ell^- π^+ (π^0, η)$, with $\ell=e,~μ$. Using existing experimental bounds on $n\to \ell^-π^+$, we set stringent constraints on the relevant Wilson coefficients and derive improved lower limits on the partial lifetimes of the six three-body modes from their correlations with the two-body modes. We further present a concrete ultraviolet model that generates the dim-7 operators and the associated $ΔI=3/2$ nucleon decays at leading order. Our results pave the way for exploring dim-7 contributions to nucleon decays and motivate future searches for these exotic modes.

</details>


### [99] [Neutrino mass, scalar dark matter, and collider signatures in a radiative doublet-triplet model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08808)
*Isaac Bamwidhi,Mohamed Belfkir,Mohamed Amin Loualidi,Salah Nasri*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a radiative neutrino mass model (T4-3-i-C1) featuring an inert doublet, real triplet scalar, Majorana fermion triplet, and Dirac singlet; it yields a rank-two neutrino mass matrix (one massless neutrino), provides a CP-even scalar dark matter candidate, satisfies relic density via annihilation/coannihilation, and predicts testable signals in beta decay, 0νββ, cosmology, and colliders (prompt/displaced/long-lived tracks).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a viable radiative neutrino mass model based on the T4-3-i topology that simultaneously explains neutrino masses, dark matter, and yields testable phenomenology while avoiding tree-level contributions via an exact Z2 symmetry.

Method: Model building within the T4-3-i-C1 realization of the Weinberg operator's one-loop completion; imposition of an exact Z2 symmetry to forbid tree-level type-III seesaw; comprehensive analysis of theoretical constraints (perturbativity, vacuum stability, unitarity), experimental bounds (electroweak precision, Higgs signal strengths, direct DM detection, LHC searches), and computation of neutrino observables (mass ordering, mββ, mβ, Σmi), DM relic density (including coannihilation), and collider signatures from the electroweak triplet sector.

Result: Viable parameter space exists for both normal and inverted neutrino mass orderings; inverted ordering predicts larger mββ, mβ, and Σmi — enhancing detectability; DM relic abundance is reproduced via annihilation and coannihilation of the neutral CP-even scalar; the fermion triplet induces prompt, displaced, or long-lived charged/neutral tracks at colliders.

Conclusion: The T4-3-i-C1 model successfully unifies radiative neutrino mass generation, scalar dark matter, and rich collider phenomenology; its inverted ordering prediction is particularly testable with upcoming experiments in neutrinoless double beta decay, beta decay, cosmology, and high-energy colliders.

Abstract: We explore a radiative neutrino mass model based on the topology T4-3-i, which is a one-loop completion of the Weinberg operator. We focus on its T4-3-i-C1 realization, containing an inert scalar doublet, a real scalar triplet, a Majorana fermion triplet, and a Dirac singlet fermion. An exact $Z_2$ symmetry forbids the tree-level type-III term and stabilizes the lightest $Z_2$-odd particle, leaving the one-loop topology as the leading source of neutrino mass. The model accomodate a neutral {\it CP}-even scalar dark matter candidate and predicts a rank two neutrino mass matrix, and thus one neutrino is massless. A combined analysis incorporating theoretical and experimental constraints reveals viable solutions to both neutrino mass orderings, with the inverted ordering predicting larger values of $m_{ββ}$, $m_β$, and $\sum_i m_i$, thereby enhancing its testability through future beta-decay, neutrinoless double beta decay, and cosmological probes. Within the same viable parameter region, the observed dark matter relic abundance is accounted for by annihilation and coannihilation processes, while the electroweak triplet sector gives rise to prompt, displaced, or long-lived particle signatures, offering complementary collider probes to dark matter and low-energy searches.

</details>


### [100] [Axion Hair and Pulsar Electrodynamics: modelling, discharge dynamics, and particle-in-cell simulations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08840)
*Samuel J. Witte,Andrea Caputo,Stefan Stelzl,Alexander Chernoglazov,Alexander A. Philippov,Surjeet Rajendran*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops the theoretical and numerical framework for how static axion field gradients ('axion hair') from dense nuclear matter modify pulsar electrodynamics—particularly in the polar gap—via analytic force-free analysis and 1D PIC simulations, revealing hemisphere- and current-dependent enhancement of particle acceleration or screening.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide the extended theoretical and numerical foundation supporting new constraints on light QCD axions and CP-violating axion-nucleon couplings derived from axion hair effects on old rotation-powered pulsars' near-field electrodynamics.

Method: Self-consistent computation of axion hair field profiles sourced by dense nuclear matter; analytic treatment of axion-gradient-modified electrodynamics in polar gaps using force-free split-monopole and perturbative dipole configurations; dedicated 1D particle-in-cell (PIC) simulations capturing leading-order kinetic behavior near the neutron star surface.

Result: Axion hair gradients induce spatially asymmetric modifications to polar gap electrodynamics: they either enhance particle acceleration or enhance charge screening, with the dominant effect flipping between northern/southern hemispheres and between out-flowing vs. return current field lines.

Conclusion: Axion hair is not merely a passive background field but actively reshapes pulsar magnetospheric structure and pair production dynamics in a geometrically and topologically nontrivial way—enabling novel astrophysical probes of ultralight axions and CP-violating nucleon couplings.

Abstract: In a companion paper, we demonstrated that static axion field gradients sourced by dense nuclear matter (\emph{axion hair}) can dominate the near-field electrodynamics of old rotation-powered pulsars, leading to new constraints on light QCD axions and on CP-violating axion-nucleon interactions. This article provides the extended theoretical and numerical framework underlying those results. We begin by providing a detailed description of the sourcing of axion hair from dense nuclear matter, computing self-consistent field profiles for each interaction across the relevant parameter space. We then study the modification to the electrodynamics induced in the polar gap region by these axion gradients; this is done at the analytic level by studying the modification induced by axion field gradients on the effective discharge parameter (computed in the force-free limit of the split monopole magnetic field configuration, and looking at leading deviations from the force-free limit for dipolar field configurations), and numerically by developing dedicated 1D particle-in-cell simulations which capture the leading order dynamical behavior near the star. Our results demonstrate that axion hair serves to either enhance acceleration, or enhance screening, where the relevant effect changes between the northern and southern hemispheres of the star, and between the field lines which support out-flowing and return currents.

</details>


### [101] [ALP-mediated inelastic dark matter and the LUX-ZEPLIN high-recoil candidate event LZ230616](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08893)
*Guan-Wen Yuan,Bo Zhang,Wen-Yu Cao,Lei Feng,Ruizhi Yang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores whether the LZ230616 high-energy nuclear recoil event (248 keV) observed by the LUX-ZEPLIN experiment could arise from inelastic scattering of two Majorana dark matter particles mediated by a 0.3 GeV axionlike particle coupling to gluons; it identifies viable dark matter mass (~0.35 TeV) and mass splitting (~330 keV) parameters, but notes strong sensitivity to Galactic halo velocity cutoff and requirement of large couplings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To interpret the anomalous high-energy nuclear recoil event LZ230616 in terms of a specific dark matter model—inelastic scattering between Majorana DM states mediated by a gluon-coupled axionlike particle—beyond standard elastic WIMP scenarios.

Method: Constructed an inelastic scattering model with independent dark-sector and gluonic couplings; incorporated momentum-dependent nucleon form factors and xenon nuclear responses; used approximate single-event likelihood analysis to fit event energy, accounting for kinematic constraints and Galactic halo velocity distribution.

Result: Identified a narrow viable parameter region at ma = 0.3 GeV, mχ ≃ 0.35 TeV, δ ≃ 330 keV yielding a spectrum peaked near 248 keV; however, this requires large coupling product and is highly sensitive to the Galactic halo speed cutoff (e.g., vmax).

Conclusion: The LZ230616 event is *kinematically compatible* with this inelastic axion-mediated Majorana DM scenario, but viability hinges on untested astrophysical (halo cutoff) and particle-physics (coupling strength, thermal relic consistency, mediator constraints) assumptions—motivating further cross-checks.

Abstract: The LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) Collaboration has reported a high-energy candidate event LZ230616 with a reconstructed nuclear recoil energy $E_R=248\pm23_{\rm stat}\pm23_{\rm sys}~{\rm keV}$. We investigate a possible interpretation in terms of inelastic scattering between two Majorana dark matter states mediated by an axionlike particle coupled to gluons. The positive mass splitting suppresses low-energy recoils, while the momentum dependence of the interaction reshapes the high-energy spectrum. We treat the dark-sector and gluonic couplings independently and retain the momentum-dependent nucleon form factors and xenon nuclear responses. Using an approximate single-event likelihood, we find that, for $m_a=0.3~{\rm GeV}$, a narrow spectrum near the candidate energy arises at $m_χ\simeq0.35~{\rm TeV}$ and $δ\simeq330~{\rm keV}$, although this configuration requires a large coupling product and is highly sensitive to the Galactic halo speed cutoff. Our analysis establishes the kinematic and coupling requirements for subsequent tests using the thermal relic abundance and laboratory constraints on the mediator.

</details>


### [102] [Probing the Inert Scalar Sector of the Inert Doublet Model via Vector-Boson Fusion at a Muon Collider](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08918)
*Abdesslam Arhrib,Es-said Ghourmin,Ayoub Hmissou,Larbi Rahili,Bassim Taki*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies how a future high-energy muon collider (3–20 TeV) can probe the inert scalar sector of the Inert Doublet Model (IDM) via vector boson fusion–driven 2→4 processes, including charged/neutral inert scalar production; it finds significantly enhanced sensitivity at 10 and 20 TeV after theoretical, DM, and collider constraints, confirmed by detector-level Monte Carlo analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the inert scalar sector of the IDM — particularly the lightest inert neutral scalar H as a dark matter candidate — using the unique kinematic advantages of high-energy muon colliders, where vector boson fusion (VBF) cross sections grow with energy, unlike conventional hadron or electron colliders.

Method: Analyzes electroweak 2→4 processes: μ⁺μ⁻ → H⁺H⁻νν̄ (WW fusion), μ⁺μ⁻ → HH⁺μ⁻ν / AH⁺μ⁻ν (WZ/Wγ fusion); computes subprocess amplitudes (WW→H⁺H⁻, WV→H⁺Sᵢ), accounts for destructive interference; applies theoretical constraints (unitarity, stability, perturbativity), DM relic density & direct detection limits, and LHC constraints; performs full detector-level Monte Carlo simulation with cut-based signal/background discrimination at √s = 3, 10, 20 TeV.

Result: Signal cross sections rise sharply with energy due to VBF enhancement; 10 and 20 TeV muon colliders achieve strong sensitivity to inert scalars — especially H⁺H⁻ and associated channels — surpassing current LHC reach; background suppression is effective with kinematic cuts exploiting forward muons and large missing energy.

Conclusion: High-energy muon colliders (particularly at 10 and 20 TeV) are uniquely powerful facilities to test the IDM inert sector, offering complementary and superior reach compared to existing experiments, especially for scenarios where inert scalars evade LHC detection due to soft final states or low production rates.

Abstract: We investigate the sensitivity of a future high-energy muon collider (MuCs) to the inert scalar sector of the Inert Doublet Model (IDM) through a comprehensive set of $2\to4$ electroweak gauge-boson fusion processes. The scalar spectrum consists of two CP-even neutral scalars: $h$ and $H$, one CP-odd neutral scalar $A$, and a pair of charged scalars $H^\pm$. We assume the neutral scalar $H$ to be the lightest inert state and the dark matter (DM) candidate, while the observed $125$~GeV Higgs boson is identified with the scalar of the Standard Model-like doublet. We consider charged Higgs pair production: $μ^+μ^-\to H^+H^-ν\barν$ induced by WW fusion, as well as the associated channels: $μ^+μ^-\to HH^\pmμ^\mpν$ and $μ^+μ^-\to AH^\pmμ^\mpν$, induced by $WZ$ and $Wγ$ fusion. Owing to their Vector Boson Fusion (VBF) origin, the corresponding cross sections increase significantly with collider energy, making them particularly well suited for exploration at a high-energy MuCs. We first study the $2\to 2$ subprocesses $WW\to H^+ H^-$ and $WV\to H^+ S_i$, $V=Z, γ$ and
  $S_i=H$ or $A$ and establish the destructive interference among the contributing diagrams. After imposing theoretical consistency requirements and current experimental constraints, including those from DM and collider searches, we evaluate the corresponding signal rates at $\sqrt{s}=3$, $10$, and $20$~TeV. We further perform a detector-level Monte Carlo analysis combined with a cut-based signal-background selection. Our results demonstrate that the sensitivity to inert scalar production increases significantly with collision energy, establishing the 10 and 20~TeV MuCs stages as powerful probes of the IDM inert scalar sector.

</details>


### [103] [Final-state effects on the transverse-momentum spectra of charged hadrons in $p+Pb$ and $Pb+Pb$ collisions at $\sqrt {s_{\rm{NN}}}~=~5.02$~TeV using the modified Tsallis distribution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08997)
*Kapil Saraswat,Deependra Singh Rawat,Manoj Kumar Singh,Prashanta Kumar Khandai,Damini Singh,Venktesh Singh*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes charged-hadron $p_{\rm{T}}$ spectra in $p+p$, $p+Pb$, and $Pb+Pb$ collisions at 5.02 TeV using a modified Tsallis parametrization that jointly models transverse flow (low–intermediate $p_{\rm{T}}$) and medium-induced parton energy loss (high $p_{\rm{T}}$); it extracts the energy-loss exponent $\alpha$ and reveals its centrality- and system-size dependence, quantifying the interplay between flow and energy loss.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To disentangle and quantify the concurrent effects of transverse collective flow and medium-induced parton energy loss on charged-hadron production in small ($p+Pb$) and large ($Pb+Pb$) collision systems at LHC energies.

Method: Phenomenological fitting of ATLAS $p_{\rm{T}}$ spectra using a modified Tsallis distribution incorporating both radial flow velocity and a power-law energy-loss term parameterized by exponent $\alpha$; fits performed across centrality classes in $p+Pb$ and $Pb+Pb$.

Result: The modified Tsallis model consistently describes spectra over the full $p_{\rm{T}}$ range; extracted $\alpha$ values are 0.59–0.73 in $p+Pb$ and 0.32–0.59 in $Pb+Pb$, decreasing with centrality—indicating stronger energy loss suppression and enhanced flow in more central collisions.

Conclusion: Transverse collective flow and medium-induced parton energy loss coexist and are quantitatively separable via this parametrization; the systematic centrality and system-size dependence of $\alpha$ reflects evolving medium properties—from weakly interacting to strongly coupled QGP-like behavior.

Abstract: The ATLAS Collaboration has reported measurements of the transverse momentum ($p_{\rm{T}}$) spectra of charged hadrons in proton-proton ($p+p$), proton-lead ($p+Pb$), and lead-lead ($Pb+Pb$) collisions at a nucleon-nucleon center-of-mass energy of $\sqrt{s_{\rm{NN}}}~=$~5.02~TeV within the rapidity interval $-2.5<y<2.0$. In the present work, we investigate medium effects on charged-hadron production in $p+Pb$ and $Pb+Pb$ collisions using a phenomenological modified Tsallis parametrization that accounts for transverse collective flow in the low-to-intermediate $p_{\rm{T}}$ region and medium-induced parton energy loss at high $p_{\rm{T}}$. The modified parametrization provides a consistent description of the measured spectra over the full investigated $p_{\rm{T}}$ range across different centrality classes. The extracted energy-loss exponent $α$, which characterizes the energy dependence of parton energy loss, is found to lie in the range 0.59$-$0.73 for $p+Pb$ collisions and 0.32$-$0.59 for $Pb+Pb$ collisions. In addition, the extracted parameters exhibit a clear centrality dependence, reflecting enhanced collective effects in central collisions. These results provide quantitative insight into the interplay between transverse collective flow and medium-induced parton energy loss in charged-hadron production at LHC energies.

</details>


### [104] [Endothermic dark matter with a light dark photon and the LUX--ZEPLIN high-energy nuclear-recoil candidate](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09015)
*Pengxuan Zhu,Giovani Dalla Valle Garcia,Xuan-Gong Wang,Anthony W. Thomas,Martin J. White*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores whether the single nuclear-recoil event observed by LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) can be explained by endothermic inelastic dark matter coupled to a kinetically mixed dark photon, while satisfying relic abundance and cosmological constraints; it identifies a preferred TeV-scale DM parameter region, highlights tension from long-lived excited-state population, and proposes a dimension-five transition dipole as a solution enabling testability in accelerator experiments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the single 248-keV nuclear-recoil candidate from LZ within a theoretically consistent dark matter framework that simultaneously reproduces the observed relic density (Ωh² ≈ 0.12) and satisfies cosmological and experimental constraints.

Method: Global Bayesian scan over five parameters of an endothermic inelastic DM model with kinetic mixing to a dark photon; combines LZ high-energy nuclear-recoil likelihood (energy-only recast) with Planck-based relic-density likelihood; assesses cosmological viability of the excited-state population (χ₂), including energy-injection bounds and potential exothermic scattering; introduces and evaluates a dimension-five transition dipole operator for χ₂ depletion.

Result: Identifies a preferred parameter region: DM mass ~TeV, mass splitting δ ≈ few hundred keV (near kinematic threshold for LZ signal), dark photon mass ~GeV; predicts exactly 1 accepted event at 248 keV and Ωh² = 0.120; finds δ < 2mₑ, suppressing e⁺e⁻ decay and yielding a long-lived χ₂ subject to strong CMB/BBN energy-injection limits; shows transition dipole enables efficient χ₂ depletion without affecting relic density or LZ signal.

Conclusion: The LZ candidate is compatible with endothermic inelastic DM + dark photon, but minimal implementation faces cosmological tension from χ₂ longevity; introducing a transition dipole resolves this while preserving phenomenology, and the resulting light-dark-photon scenario remains falsifiable via upcoming intensity-frontier experiments (LHCb, Belle II, SHiP).

Abstract: The LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) experiment has reported a single nuclear-recoil candidate at $E_{\rm nr}=248\pm23_{\rm stat}\pm23_{\rm sys}\,{\rm keV}$. We investigate whether this event can be explained by endothermic inelastic dark matter coupled to a kinetically mixed dark photon, while reproducing the observed dark-matter relic abundance. Performing a global scan of the five model parameters, combining an energy-only recast of the LZ high-energy likelihood with a relic-density likelihood, we find a preferred region with TeV-scale dark matter masses, mass splittings of a few hundred keV, and a GeV-scale dark photon. The high recoil energy requires the splitting to lie close to the kinematic threshold, so that the signal is supplied by the high-velocity tail of the halo, while the secluded annihilation mechanism fixes the dark gauge coupling, largely independently of the kinetic mixing. The benchmark point predicts $1$ accepted event at the candidate energy with $Ωh^2=0.120$. The preferred splittings are below the $e^+e^-$ threshold, closing the fastest decay channels and leaving a long-lived excited state. Its surviving population is subject to stringent cosmological constraints from energy injection and can also produce an additional exothermic scattering signal, making the late-time abundance an important consistency condition for the minimal model. A dimension-five transition dipole provides a simple way to efficiently deplete $χ_2$ without modifying either the relic abundance or the endothermic LZ signal. The corresponding light-dark-photon scenario remains testable in accelerator searches, including future LHCb, Belle II, and SHiP experiments.

</details>


### [105] [For Whom the Xenon Recoils: Magnetic Inelastic Dark Baryons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09107)
*Pouya Asadi,Austin Batz,Patrick J. Fox,Samuel D. Homiller,Graham D. Kribs*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Proposes a dark baryon dark matter model with inelastic scattering via transition magnetic dipole, explaining LZ's single event; predicts correlated photon signals (E = δ) enabling directional detection and distinguishing it from other iDM models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Explain the single nuclear recoil event observed by the LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) experiment using a theoretically motivated dark matter candidate that avoids constraints from elastic scattering searches.

Method: Constructs strongly-coupled dark sector models where dark baryons interact with nuclei via a transition magnetic dipole operator, suppressing elastic scattering via accidental symmetries; computes nuclear response using spin-dependent structure factors; fits parameter space (mass m_χ ~ 1–50 TeV, splitting δ ~ 100–500 keV) to LZ data under varied velocity distributions.

Result: Identifies viable parameter space consistent with LZ’s event; predicts simultaneous monochromatic photon emission (E = δ) per scattering, enabling novel directional detection; finds suppressed solar capture/annihilation (weaker indirect constraints) and distinctive collider signatures from dark mesons.

Conclusion: A theoretically robust dark baryon iDM scenario with transition magnetic dipole mediation successfully accommodates the LZ anomaly, offers falsifiable signatures (coincident photons, directional sensitivity), and evades existing experimental bounds due to symmetry-suppressed elastic scattering.

Abstract: We develop a theory of a dark baryon dark matter candidate that interacts with nuclei dominantly through inelastic scattering mediated by a transition magnetic dipole operator. Models are presented that can elegantly explain both the scattering rate and the size of the inelastic splitting to be consistent with the one event observed at LZ\@. Elastic scattering is suppressed due to accidental symmetries within the strongly-coupled sector. The nuclear response is dominated by a spin-dependent structure factor, thus we find a large range of masses, $1 \; {\rm TeV} \lesssim m_χ\lesssim 50 \; {\rm TeV}$, and mass splitting of $100 \; {\rm keV} \lesssim δ\lesssim 500 \; {\rm keV}$ can fit the data for a variety of possible dark matter velocity distributions. The models predict that some nuclear scattering events will be accompanied by a simultaneous photon signal with energy $δ$, which not only distinguishes this model from other inelastic dark matter explanations but also enables directional detection in conventional direct detection experiments. We briefly comment on the dark matter abundance, indirect detection signals (including the models' significantly weaker constraints from dark matter annihilation in the Sun), and the associated collider signals that arise from the meson sector of the theory.

</details>


### [106] [Complex Scalar Dark Matter with a Vector-Like Quark and Lepton: Precision, Flavor, and HL-LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09120)
*Lipika Kolay,Rusa Mandal,Manimala Mitra,Dipankar Pradhan,Subham Saha*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a minimal SM extension with Z3-stabilized complex scalar dark matter, a down-type vector-like quark (VLQ), and a charged vector-like lepton (VLL); it performs a comprehensive one-loop analysis of flavor, electroweak precision, DM, and collider observables, identifying viable parameter regions above ~1.0 TeV (CSDM) and ~1.5 TeV (VLQ), with LHC/HL-LHC exclusion and discovery reach quantified.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a minimal, phenomenologically viable dark matter model that simultaneously explains relic abundance via semi-/co-annihilation, satisfies stringent flavor and electroweak constraints, and yields testable signatures across multiple experimental frontiers.

Method: Comprehensive one-loop effective field theory analysis including neutral meson mixing (K0, B0, Bs0), rare decays (e.g., B→Xsγ, K→πνν), charged lepton flavor violation (μ→eγ, τ→μγ), anomalous magnetic moments (ae, aμ, aτ), Z-pole observables, direct/indirect DM detection, and LHC VLQ/VLL production cross sections and decay topologies.

Result: Neutral meson mixing and rare decays dominantly constrain VLQs; CLFV tightly bounds VLL Yukawas; direct detection requires λΦH ≲ 5×10⁻³ and yd ≲ 0.05 for TeV-scale VLQs; viable parameter space exists for CSDM > ~1.0 TeV and VLQ > ~1.5 TeV; LHC excludes VLQs up to ~1.6 TeV and VLLs up to ~0.38 TeV (2σ); HL-LHC enables 3σ discovery in extended regions.

Conclusion: The model is viable only in a highly constrained high-mass regime, demonstrating strong complementarity among flavor, DM, and collider probes—no single experiment suffices, but their combination robustly tests the framework.

Abstract: We investigate a minimal extension of the SM consisting of a $\mathbb{Z}_3$-stabilized complex scalar dark matter (CSDM) candidate, a down-type vector-like quark (VLQ), and a charged vector-like lepton (VLL). The additional vector-like fermions not only enable the CSDM to reproduce the observed relic abundance beyond the Higgs-resonance region through semi-annihilation and co-annihilation processes, but also induce correlated signatures across flavor, electroweak precision, dark matter, and collider observables. We perform a comprehensive one-loop analysis of neutral meson mixing, rare meson decays, charged lepton flavor violation, anomalous magnetic moments of charged leptons, and $Z$-pole observables. We find that neutral meson mixing and rare meson decays provide the dominant constraints on the VLQ sector, while charged lepton flavor-violating processes strongly restrict the VLL Yukawa couplings. Current direct-detection limits require Higgs-DM coupling $λ_{ΦH} \lesssim5\times10^{-3}$ and the VLQ Yukawa coupling $\mathtt{y}_d \lesssim 0.05$ for TeV-scale VLQ masses, whereas present indirect-detection searches impose no additional constraints. Combining all flavor, electroweak precision, dark matter, and collider constraints, we identify viable parameter regions with CSDM masses above approximately $1.0~\rm TeV$ and VLQ masses above about $1.5~\rm TeV$. We also find that the LHC can exclude VLQs (VLLs) with masses up to approximately $1.6~(0.38)$ TeV, depending on the CSDM mass, at the $2σ$ confidence level. The HL-LHC can further probe an extended region of the parameter space, with discovery prospects at the $3σ$ level. Finally, we demonstrate the complementarity of flavor, dark matter, and collider searches in probing this framework.

</details>


### [107] [A Warped Extra Dimensional Candidate for the LZ 248 keV Event](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09136)
*Vincent S. H. Lee,Lisa Randall*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes an inelastic dark matter (iDM) model within a Randall-Sundrum warped extra-dimensional framework to explain the single 248 keV nuclear-recoil event observed by LZ, evading solar capture and IceCube neutrino constraints that exclude standard thermal Higgsino iDM.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile the LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) single low-energy nuclear-recoil event with viable thermal dark matter, while resolving tensions faced by conventional inelastic Higgsino DM — namely excessive solar capture and neutrino flux violating IceCube limits, and mismatched recoil energy expectations.

Method: Constructs a concrete iDM candidate in a Randall-Sundrum (RS) model: a vector-like fermion on the TeV brane coupled to a bulk fermion; incorporates UV-brane-originated Majorana mass for the left-handed bulk component and TeV-scale dark Kaluza-Klein Z′ gauge boson mediation; computes mass splitting δ ~ 300 keV, DM mass mχ ~ TeV, and coupling ratios consistent with LZ signal and astrophysical bounds.

Result: Demonstrates that the proposed RS-based iDM model yields δ ≈ 300 keV, naturally suppresses solar capture (due to suppressed nucleon couplings and kinematics), avoids IceCube neutrino overproduction, and predicts recoils dominantly near 248 keV — matching the LZ observation while satisfying direct detection (XENONnT, PandaX), indirect detection (IceCube), and collider constraints.

Conclusion: Standard thermal Higgsino iDM is excluded by multi-experiment consistency requirements, but broader iDM frameworks — particularly those arising from warped extra dimensions with hierarchical mass generation — remain viable and can quantitatively explain the anomalous LZ event without fine-tuning.

Abstract: The recent result by the LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) experiment of a single nuclear-recoil event at $248\,\mathrm{keV}$ suggests an interpretation in terms of inelastic scattering between dark matter (DM) and the xenon target. The widely considered thermal Higgsino DM with $m_χ\sim$~TeV and mass splitting $δ\sim 350$~keV is essentially excluded as it would be captured efficiently by the Sun, with a resulting neutrino flux from its annihilation in excess of the IceCube limit, and expected recoils above the LZ nominal search window, where none were seen. However we argue that although these tensions apply to a Higgsino with constrained cross section, they need not apply to the broader set of inelastic candidates that alternative solutions to the hierarchy problem might suggest. We show that a new massive gauge boson, $Z'$ with a candidate DM sector and Majorana mass splitting of order $\sim 10^{-7}$ of the Dirac mass, evades current constraints. We propose an inelastic dark matter (iDM) candidate that can arise naturally in a Randall-Sundrum warped extra-dimensional model. The DM candidate involves a vector-like fermion confined to the TeV brane and a bulk field. The brane Lagrangian contains Dirac mass terms that are also naturally exponentially suppressed. The left-handed component of the bulk field has a Majorana mass with origin on the UV brane, while the interaction with Standard Model nucleons is mediated by a massive dark Kaluza-Klein (KK) gauge boson whose mass is also automatically in the right range. We propose a concrete model of thermal DM with $m_χ\sim$~TeV in an RS geometry with warp factor $πkR\simeq 31.7$ and the $Z'$ a KK mode with natural TeV-ish scale mass in which the ratio of neutrino to quark coupling is also natural, giving a mass splitting of $δ\sim 300$~keV, and show how it could explain the LZ signal while being consistent with existing constraints.

</details>


### [108] [Mixing-suppressed inelastic dark matter: a minimal model for the LZ 248 keV event](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09138)
*Seung J. Lee,Taewook Youn*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Proposes a minimal Majorana singlet-vector-like-doublet dark matter model to explain LZ's single 248-keV nuclear recoil event, using singlet-doublet mixing to suppress both direct detection rate and solar capture/annihilation while enabling coannihilation; predicts mass splitting δ ≈ 331–341 keV near 730–733 GeV for consistency with relic density and spectrum.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the anomalous single nuclear-recoil event at ~248 keV observed by the LZ experiment, while reconciling it with constraints from IceCube, solar capture, relic abundance, and spectral shape—avoiding overproduction of events expected from electroweak-strength Z-mediated transitions.

Method: Constructs a minimal effective model with a Majorana singlet and vector-like doublet (both SU(2)_L doublets) featuring small mass splitting δ; employs singlet-doublet mixing to suppress scattering rate without distorting recoil spectrum; incorporates nonthermal cooling via suppressed Higgs-mediated annihilation due to small δ; computes solar capture, annihilation, coannihilation, and direct detection rates at tree level; fits to LZ event count, relic density Ωh², and IceCube neutrino limits.

Result: Identifies viable two-dimensional parameter space (mass M ~ 730–733 GeV, splitting δ ~ 331–341 keV) that simultaneously satisfies: (i) one expected LZ event, (ii) correct thermal relic abundance, (iii) evasion of IceCube limits under nonthermal cooling, and (iv) consistency with recoil spectral shape; distinguishes this from thermalized scenarios (δ ≈ 300–301 keV) which are excluded.

Conclusion: The singlet-vector-like-doublet framework with controlled mixing and small mass splitting provides a consistent, minimal explanation for the LZ anomaly, evading multiple astrophysical and cosmological constraints; suggests testable signatures in next-generation direct detection and neutrino telescopes, and admits UV completions such as gauged U(1)_N or R-symmetric SUSY.

Abstract: We construct a minimal Majorana singlet-vector-like-doublet model for the single nuclear-recoil-like event reported by LZ near 248 keV. At the splitting inferred from the recoil energy, an electroweak-strength $Z$ transition predicts thousands of events; singlet-doublet mixing suppresses the rate without moving the recoil spectrum. Fixed-coupling interpretations instead require a larger splitting in the extreme halo tail, but solar gravitational acceleration removes this suppression, while the larger splitting shifts recoils toward LZ's empty high-energy sideband. In our model the same mixing suppresses solar capture and annihilation, while the small mass gap required for coannihilation weakens Higgs-mediated cooling, allowing the captured population to remain extended and out of equilibrium. Imposing the relic abundance and normalizing the rate to one event leaves a two-dimensional mass-splitting parameter space. A thermalized population gives the conservative IceCube limit $δ=$300-301 keV; with our nonthermal cooling ansatz and elastic scattering treated at tree level, the nominal limit is $δ=$331-341 keV near the 730-733 GeV relic-density endpoint, which is also favored by the recoil spectrum. We outline a gauged $U(1)_N$ origin for the parity and splitting and a candidate $R$-symmetric supersymmetric embedding.

</details>


<div id='hep-th'></div>

# hep-th [[Back]](#toc)

### [109] [Cosmology, Cluster Algebras, and $\boldsymbol{u}$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05606)
*Daniel Glazer,Austin Joyce*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes a correspondence between kinematic variables in de Sitter cosmology and cluster algebra variables (via u-variables), showing that n-site chain and cycle Feynman graphs map to A_{2n−2} and B_{2n−1} cluster algebras respectively; cluster compatibility then constrains symbol entries and enables bootstrapping—wavefunctions and correlators share cluster structure for chains, but only the wavefunction satisfies compatibility for cycles.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To uncover underlying mathematical structures governing cosmological observables—specifically, to explain why certain symbol alphabets appear in wavefunctions and correlators of conformally coupled scalars in de Sitter space, and to leverage cluster algebra constraints for bootstrapping.

Method: Mapping kinematic u-variables from cosmological Feynman graphs (chains and cycles) to cluster algebra u-variables; identifying associated Dynkin types (A_{2n−2}, B_{2n−1}); imposing cluster compatibility (i.e., adjacency conditions on symbol letters) to constrain and bootstrap the symbol of wavefunctions and correlators.

Result: For n-site chain graphs: wavefunction and correlator share the same A_{2n−2} cluster structure and both satisfy cluster compatibility. For n-site cycle graphs: only the wavefunction satisfies B_{2n−1} cluster compatibility; the correlator fails this condition, implying the wavefunction is uniquely selected by the cluster structure.

Conclusion: Cluster algebras provide a powerful organizing principle for de Sitter wavefunctions—encoding kinematic constraints, enabling symbol bootstrapping, and distinguishing physical roles of wavefunctions versus correlators depending on graph topology.

Abstract: We explore the cluster algebra structures present in the wavefunction and correlators of conformally coupled scalar fields in de Sitter space. To connect cosmological kinematics to cluster variables, we utilize the so-called $u$-variables that appear on both sides. Since $u$-variables parameterize a rigid space, it is natural to identify the ones that appear from cosmological graphs and those that arise from cluster algebras. This provides a mapping between kinematic variables and cluster variables. For $n$-site chain Feynman graphs, this gives a coordinate-invariant relation to $A_{2n-2}$-type cluster algebras, while in the case of $n$-site cycle graph momentum integrands, the relevant cluster algebra is $B_{2n-1}$, as found previously. Since kinematic variables are mapped to nonlinear combinations of cluster variables, cluster compatibility imposes strong conditions on possible symbol entries. We use this notion of compatibility to bootstrap the symbol. In the chain case, both the cosmological wavefunction and products of lower-point functions satisfy cluster compatibility, with the correlator a particular combination, so that the wavefunction and correlator share the same cluster structure. In the cycle case, however, the wavefunction appears to be uniquely selected by the cluster structure, and the correlator does not display the same compatibility as the wavefunction.

</details>


### [110] [Matrix Holography on an Optical Lattice](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05613)
*Robin Löwenberg,Julian Sonner*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Proposes an analog quantum-simulation protocol using atomic ensembles and time-averaged Floquet dynamics to efficiently realize four-body potentials in bosonic BFSS-like matrix quantum mechanics, achieving polynomial (O(N³)) scaling in control frequency and enabling strong-coupling holographic physics simulation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the exponential circuit-depth overhead of digital gate-based quantum simulations of large-N matrix quantum mechanics (e.g., BFSS), and enable experimental access to holographic strong-coupling regimes.

Method: Analog quantum simulation via a lattice of atomic ensembles under time-averaged Floquet driving to engineer effective four-body interactions; control complexity is kept constant per Floquet cycle, with scaling bottleneck in required frequency bandwidth.

Result: Achieves polynomial O(N³) scaling in control frequency bandwidth with matrix size N; enables tunable coupling beyond O(1), permitting simulation of strong-coupling holographic phases.

Conclusion: Provides a scalable, experimentally feasible analog route to large-N matrix quantum mechanics, circumventing digital circuit-depth limitations and opening access to non-perturbative, holographically relevant physics.

Abstract: We propose an analog quantum-simulation protocol based on a lattice of atomic ensembles and time-averaged Floquet dynamics to construct the four-body potentials that typically appear in bosonic BFSS-like matrix quantum mechanics. In contrast to gate-based digital implementations requiring deep circuits, our approach generates the target model through a fixed set of control stages per Floquet cycle, keeping the number of consecutive unitaries constant with respect to the matrix size $N$. The principal scaling cost in our construction is given by the frequency range required to control the growing lattice. We show that this cost grows at most as $\mathcal{O}(N^3)$, giving a polynomial scaling route to large$-N$, attractive to experimental implementation. Additionally, we demonstrate that the coupling parameter can be tuned beyond $\mathcal{O}(1)$, enabling the simulation of strong-coupling physics necessary for holographic phases.

</details>


### [111] [Equation of State of a Strongly Coupled Perfect Fluid with Spin from Holography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05630)
*Andres Anabalon,Horatiu Nastase*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper uses holography to derive the equation of state for a strongly coupled relativistic perfect fluid with finite spin in 2+1 dimensions, dual to a spinning AdS₄ black hole; it identifies a critical rotation threshold for thermodynamic stability and reveals an exotic rotating fluid dual to a non-spinning black hole on a second boundary.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a holographic equation of state for strongly coupled relativistic fluids with intrinsic angular momentum (finite spin), and to explore how black hole rotation governs thermodynamic stability and exotic fluid behavior in lower-dimensional boundary theories.

Method: Holographic duality (AdS/CFT) applied to a rotating black hole in asymptotically AdS₄ spacetime; thermodynamic quantities (temperature, angular velocity, entropy) are extracted from horizon properties (Hawking temperature, angular velocity, Bekenstein-Hawking entropy) and mapped to boundary fluid variables; local thermodynamic stability is analyzed via standard criteria (e.g., specific heat, compressibility, rotation-dependent Hessian conditions).

Result: Derivation of the equation of state for a 2+1D strongly coupled relativistic fluid with finite spin; identification of a critical rotation bound ω < 4πT / 2.77239 for thermodynamic stability; discovery of an 'exotic' rotating fluid dual to a non-rotating black hole on a second boundary, implying novel spin-thermodynamics correspondence.

Conclusion: Black hole rotation in the bulk directly controls intrinsic angular momentum and thermodynamic stability of boundary fluids; the holographic framework yields both conventional and exotic equations of state, offering new insights into spinful strongly coupled matter—e.g., quark-gluon plasma—and establishing a concrete link between gravitational angular momentum and fluid thermodynamics.

Abstract: We derive the equation of state of a strongly coupled relativistic perfect fluid with finite spin in $2+1$ dimensions using holography. The dual gravitational description is provided by a spinning black hole in $AdS_4$, whose Hawking temperature, angular velocity, and Bekenstein-Hawking entropy determine the thermodynamic properties of the boundary fluid. We obtain the equation of state and analyze its local thermodynamic stability, finding a critical rotation above which the fluid becomes thermodynamically unstable providing a bound for the rotation of a strongly coupled quark gluon plasma at temperature $T$ and angular velocity $ω$ given by $\frac{4πT}ω> 2.77239$. Remarkably, the same gravitational solution contains a second black hole associated with a second boundary. Although this black hole is non-spinning, its dual fluid rotates and possesses a distinct, ``exotic'' equation of state. Our results provide a holographic equation of state for strongly coupled matter with finite spin and establish a direct connection between black-hole rotation, intrinsic angular momentum, and the thermodynamic stability of relativistic fluids.

</details>


### [112] [Birth and Death in Two-color ABJM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05653)
*Marco S. Bianchi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper exactly solves the leading quantum deformation of the two-point metric for half-BPS operators in U(2)×U(2) ABJM theory at finite rank, revealing a Jacobi operator structure on two-row Young diagrams, linked to Racah polynomials, conditioned Plancherel measure, and a restricted SU(2) Casimir.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To exactly compute quantum corrections to the two-point function of half-BPS operators in ABJM theory at finite rank — a regime where standard large-N approximations fail and exact integrability-based structures emerge.

Method: Uses the Schur basis (coupling-independent frame), computes tree-normalized two-loop correction, applies ground-state transform to map the operator to a reversible birth–death process, identifies spectral properties via orthogonal polynomials (Racah), and connects to SU(2) representation theory via a restricted Casimir.

Result: The two-loop metric deformation is an exactly solvable Jacobi operator on the lattice of two-row Young diagrams; its eigenfunctions are Racah polynomials; its stationary distribution is Plancherel measure conditioned on ≤2 rows; its continuum limit yields radial Ornstein–Uhlenbeck dynamics; and it coincides with a restriction of the SU(2) Casimir acting on a two-spin system.

Conclusion: The quantum-deformed two-point metric in finite-rank ABJM exhibits hidden integrability and algebraic structure: it is governed by discrete orthogonal polynomials, stochastic duality, and representation-theoretic constraints — unifying combinatorics, quantum field theory, and stochastic processes.

Abstract: We solve exactly the leading quantum deformation of the two-point metric of half-BPS operators in $U(2)\times U(2)$ ABJM theory at finite rank. In the natural coupling-independent Schur frame, its tree-normalized two-loop correction is a Jacobi operator on the chain of two-row Young diagrams. A ground-state transform maps it to a reversible birth--death process whose stationary distribution is the Plancherel measure conditioned on diagrams with at most two rows. Its eigenmodes are Racah polynomials, its large-$n$ limit approaches radial Ornstein--Uhlenbeck dynamics, and an equivalent two-spin description identifies the Jacobi operator with a restricted $SU(2)$ Casimir.

</details>


### [113] [Searching for Bell-CHSH-Violating Bose Operators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05685)
*T. Newton,S. P. Sorella*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs explicit bounded wedge-local observables for Bell inequalities in a relativistic free Bose field using a spectral-fibre method, proving their locality, self-adjointness, unitarity, and anticommutation; it demonstrates constructive Bell violation—enhanced by reflection branches—and confirms the translation-cell result as a rigorous lower bound.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish rigorously that Bell nonlocality can be exposed by simple, bounded, local observables in relativistic quantum field theory—not just abstractly, but constructively and measurably—thereby bridging algebraic QFT with operational Bell tests.

Method: Exact spectral-fibre decomposition of the free Bose field; construction of wedge-local observables as measurable fibre involutions; operator-algebraic verification of wedge locality, self-adjointness, unitarity, and exact anticommutation; derivation of Bell violations via translation-cell and reflection-included observables with controlled error analysis.

Result: Explicit bounded wedge-local observables satisfying all required algebraic and physical properties; finite, controlled Bell violation from translation-cell observables; significantly larger violation when reflection branches are included; rigorous proof that the translation-cell violation is a strict lower bound, not an artifact.

Conclusion: Bell nonlocality is constructively and operationally accessible in relativistic QFT using elementary bounded local observables; the spectral-fibre framework provides both conceptual clarity and quantitative control, confirming that locality and nonlocality coexist consistently within the algebraic structure of the theory.

Abstract: We construct explicit bounded wedge-local observables for Bell inequalities in a relativistic free Bose field using an exact spectral-fibre approach. The construction turns the relevant canonical pair into measurable fibre involutions and provides an operator-algebraic proof of wedge locality, self-adjointness, unitarity, and exact anticommutation. Translation-cell observables yield an explicit Bell violation with a controlled infinite-tail error, while inclusion of reflection branches produces a substantially larger violation. These results demonstrate that simple bounded local observables can expose Bell nonlocality constructively in relativistic quantum field theory and that the translation-cell result is not artifact but a strictly valid lower bound.

</details>


### [114] [Landau and cluster structures of one-loop amplitudes in ${\mathcal N}=4$ SYM in dimensional regularization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05716)
*Athanasia-Konstantina Angelopoulou,Ruth Britto,Matteo Parisi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper unifies generalized unitarity, Landau analysis, and cluster adjacency in one-loop planar N=4 SYM amplitudes, showing how symbol structure—especially weight-two—is governed by leading singularities, Landau loci, and cluster algebras (Fl_{2,4;n} and Gr(4,n)), with geometric interpretations in the amplituhedron and novel algebraic patterns in four-mass letters.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify the interplay among three powerful frameworks—generalized unitarity (cut-based), Landau analysis (singularity structure), and cluster adjacency (algebraic/combinatorial)—in constraining scattering amplitudes, using one-loop planar N=4 SYM as a controlled, all-multiplicity laboratory.

Method: Analyzes the weight-two symbol of dimensional-regularized one-loop planar N=4 SYM amplitudes; decomposes it into LS-driven, algebraic four-mass, and residual sectors; derives and proves two-mass triangle relations among box coefficients via global residue theorem and amplituhedral geometry (triple-cut projection); verifies cluster adjacency in Fl_{2,4;n}; establishes and proves a stronger Gr(4,n)-adjacency conjecture for NMHV; examines Sklyanin-bracket structure of four-mass letters.

Result: 1) Symbol decomposes cleanly into LS/Landau-organized, algebraic four-mass, and residual parts; 2) Two-mass triangle relations are proven and geometrically interpreted via amplituhedron dissections; 3) Full rational symbol (including IR-divergent part) obeys cluster adjacency in Fl_{2,4;n} for all n, helicity; 4) Stronger Gr(4,n) adjacency is proven for NMHV and conjectured generally; 5) Four-mass letters exhibit emergent Sklyanin-bracket pattern.

Conclusion: Generalized unitarity, Landau analysis, and cluster adjacency are not independent constraints but deeply intertwined: Landau loci govern symbol ordering, leading singularities fix coefficients, and cluster algebras encode both infrared safety and rational structure—unified geometrically in the amplituhedron and algebraically in flag and Grassmannian cluster algebras.

Abstract: Generalized unitarity, Landau analysis, and cluster adjacency encode complementary aspects of scattering amplitudes. We use one-loop planar $\mathcal N=4$ SYM amplitudes in dimensional regularization, at arbitrary multiplicity and helicity, to make their interface explicit. The weight-two symbol decomposes into an LS part, in which maximal-cut leading singularities furnish the coefficients and Landau loci associated with nested cuts organize the ordered symbol entries, an algebraic four-mass sector, and residual terms. Cancellations of certain letters contributed by individual box integrals, as well as further simplifications, are explained by the two-mass triangle relations among box coefficients. We prove these relations using a BCFW-like application of the global residue theorem and show that they can be understood geometrically as different dissections of the same region in the tree amplituhedron obtained by projecting the loop geometry of a triple cut. We then prove that the full rational symbol, including its infrared-divergent part, obeys cluster adjacency in the flag cluster algebra $\mathrm{Fl}_{2,4;n}$ for all multiplicities and helicities. Within the sector depending only on momentum-twistor four-brackets, we conjecture a stronger cluster property in $\operatorname{Gr}(4,n)$ for all helicities and prove it for NMHV amplitudes: the amplitude admits a representation in which every pole of each coefficient is compatible with both symbol entries. Finally, we observe that the algebraic four-mass letters, although non-rational, exhibit a suggestive Sklyanin-bracket pattern.

</details>


### [115] [Non-critical M-Theory and the Resolved Conifold: Refinement and Nonperturbative Completion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06075)
*Fengjun Xu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends the Hořava--Keeler correspondence to the refined setting, showing that the grand potential of stationary rotating non-critical M-theory reproduces the refined Gopakumar–Vafa expansion; it matches known nonperturbative conifold completions and provides a spectral interpretation via the M-theory resolvent and spectrum.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the Hořava–Keeler correspondence to the refined topological string regime and provide a microscopic, spectral explanation for nonperturbative completions of the conifold free energy.

Method: Analyzes the grand potential of stationary rotating non-critical M-theory at finite temperature; identifies its relation to the refined Gopakumar–Vafa expansion; compares with Hattab–Palti and Chuang’s nonperturbative conifold completions; derives the Schwinger integrand and integration cycle from the M-theory spectrum and resolvent.

Result: Exact match between M-theory grand potential and refined conifold completions; spectral origin of both integrand and contour; full determination of nonconstant BPS and cubic local contributions; remaining polynomial ambiguity and constant-map sector require external normalization.

Conclusion: The resolved conifold’s single primitive spinless multiplet enables this precise identification; extension to general local Calabi–Yau requires additional charge- and spin-dependent microscopic data from the dual M-theory spectrum.

Abstract: We extend the Hořava--Keeler correspondence between finite-temperature non-critical M-theory and the resolved-conifold A-model to the refined theory. The grand potential of the stationary rotating non-critical M-theory vacuum reproduces the refined Gopakumar--Vafa expansion, with the angular chemical potential deforming the $Ω$-background away from the self-dual locus $\eps_1=-\eps_2$. At finite thermal radius, we establish an exact match with the nonperturbative conifold completions proposed by Hattab--Palti and by Chuang in the unrefined and refined cases, respectively. Both the one-particle Schwinger integrand and its integration cycle arise from the non-critical M-theory spectrum and resolvent, providing a microscopic spectral realization of these completions. The correspondence fixes the nonconstant BPS sector and the cubic local contribution, while the remaining polynomial ambiguity and the modulus-independent constant-map sector require separate normalization. We explain why the single primitive spinless multiplet of the resolved conifold makes this identification possible, and discuss what additional charge- and spin-dependent microscopic data would be needed for an extension to more general local Calabi--Yau geometries.

</details>


### [116] [The Wilson-line-dressed charged sector of scalar QED: superselection and the infraparticle](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06224)
*Gordon W. Semenoff,Conor Waterfield*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper develops a gauge- and Lorentz-invariant perturbative framework in scalar QED to analyze finite-time/distance evolution of Wilson-line-dressed charged states; it establishes 'cloud orthogonality' between non-parallel dressings (with an angle-dependent, one-loop-exact infrared exponent), superselection by dressing orientation, infrared-finite yet infraparticle-like 2-point functions (no mass-shell pole, only a branch cut), and a robust proper-time power-law spreading governed by a one-loop-exact scaling exponent.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To render structural features of the charged sector—previously accessible only via non-perturbative or algebraic methods—explicit, computable, and perturbatively controlled using a manifestly gauge- and Lorentz-invariant infrared regulator.

Method: Constructs a manifestly gauge- and Lorentz-invariant perturbative calculus using Wilson-line-dressed scalar operators in scalar QED; employs Osterwalder-Schrader reflection and Euclidean gluing to express dressed-state overlaps as Euclidean 2-point functions; uses a Stueckelberg photon mass as a BRST-, gauge-, and Lorentz-invariant infrared cutoff; performs renormalized perturbation theory to compute overlaps and 2-point functions in closed form.

Result: 1) Non-parallel Wilson-line-dressed states are 'cloud orthogonal': their overlap vanishes as a power of the IR regulator, with exponent determined solely by the angle between lines—a one-loop-exact infrared analogue of the cusp anomalous dimension. 2) Charged states are superselected by dressing orientation. 3) Within a superselection sector, the 2-point function is IR-finite but exhibits infraparticle behavior: no mass-shell pole, only a branch cut edge; asymptotic charge spreading follows a proper-time power law with a one-loop-exact, dressing- and velocity-dependent exponent. 4) This scaling law persists over a wide proper-time window despite extreme IR sensitivity of cloud orthogonality and infraparticle structure.

Conclusion: The charged sector of scalar QED admits a fully perturbative, manifestly invariant characterization revealing deep infrared structure: dressing orientation induces superselection, cloud orthogonality encodes angular IR sensitivity, and infraparticle dynamics yield robust, calculable long-time spreading—unifying geometric, algebraic, and dynamical aspects of IR physics in a controlled, perturbative setting.

Abstract: We develop a manifestly gauge and Lorentz invariant perturbative calculus to study the finite time and distance evolution of the electrically charged states created by Wilson-line-dressed scalar field operators in scalar quantum electrodynamics. By an Osterwalder-Schrader reflection and Euclidean gluing, the overlap of two dressed states is expressed as a Euclidean 2 point function of dressed operators, which is then evaluated in renormalized perturbation theory with a Stueckelberg photon mass as a gauge, BRST and Lorentz invariant infrared cutoff. This renders explicit and computable in closed form several structural features of the charged sector that have so far been accessible mainly through non-perturbative or algebraic arguments. We find that states dressed by non-parallel Wilson lines are ``cloud orthogonal". Their overlap vanishes as a power of the infrared regulator, with an exponent that depends only on the angle between the two dressings and is an infrared analogue of the cusp anomalous dimension with the difference that it is one-loop exact whenever the charged matter is mass-gapped. Charged states are thereby superselected by the orientation of their dressing. Within a superselection sector the 2 point function is infrared finite but exhibits the infraparticle. The mass shell pole is replaced by the edge of a branch cut. As a result, the asymptotic spread of a dressed charge is a power law in proper time with a one-loop-exact computable exponent depending on the dressing and on the direction of motion. We show that although cloud orthogonality and the infraparticle are each extremely sensitive to fundamental infrared cutoffs, the infraparticle scaling law persists across a wide window of proper time.

</details>


### [117] [AMSB in Truly Confining Gauge Theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06093)
*Riku Ishikawa,Hitoshi Murayama,Shota Saito*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates deformations of supersymmetric t-confining gauge theories under small anomaly-mediated SUSY breaking and analyzes the resulting global symmetry breaking patterns, with potential comparability to lattice simulations of non-SUSY theories—except for a pseudoreal chiral case.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how global symmetries break under controlled SUSY-breaking deformations in t-confining theories, and to assess their relevance as theoretical proxies for non-supersymmetric strongly coupled dynamics via lattice comparison.

Method: Analytical field-theoretic analysis of symmetry breaking patterns in supersymmetric t-confining gauge theories deformed by small anomaly-mediated supersymmetry breaking.
