Result: Explicit identification of global symmetry breaking patterns across several t-confining models; one case is identified as pseudoreal and chiral, rendering direct lattice comparison infeasible.

Conclusion: The deformation preserves enough structure to allow meaningful comparison with non-SUSY lattice studies in most cases, offering a bridge between SUSY model building and non-perturbative QCD-like dynamics.

Abstract: We study deformation of supersymmetric $t$-confining (truly confininig) gauge theories with small anomaly mediation of supersymmetry breaking. We identify breaking pattern of global symmetries in the theories. These results can be compared in priciple to lattice simulation of non-supersymmetric theories (except for one of the cases where the theory is pseudoreal and chiral).

</details>


### [118] [Finite stress-tensor moment tomography of boundary-graviton coherences](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06363)
*Dyuman Bhattacharya,Robert B. Mann*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies stress moment tomography for boundary gravitons in AdS₃ gravity, showing that order-N stress moment measurements are informationally incomplete for distinguishing level-N descendant states, while order-(N+1) measurements are complete—establishing precise informational thresholds for state distinguishability in both chiral and full left-right Virasoro modules.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine the minimal number of stress tensor moment insertions required to fully reconstruct (i.e., perform quantum tomography on) finite-support Virasoro descendant states of boundary gravitons in AdS₃ gravity under Brown-Henneaux boundary conditions.

Method: Analyzes the Shapovalov form on highest-weight Virasoro modules under nondegeneracy assumptions; computes information content of n-point functions of the boundary stress tensor T(z); establishes completeness/incompleteness of tomography via linear independence of moment expectation values across descendant subspaces; extends analysis to the full left-right (chiral × antichiral) theory and heavy semiclassical sectors.

Result: For a state supported up to level N ≥ 2, stress moment tomography using N insertions is informationally incomplete (i.e., cannot distinguish some orthogonal pure states), whereas (N+1)-insertion tomography is informationally complete (fully reconstructs all density matrix blocks). Analogous thresholds hold for left-right theory and semiclassical heavy states.

Conclusion: The informational completeness threshold for boundary-graviton state tomography in AdS₃ is sharp and governed by descendant level: order-(L+1) stress moments are necessary and sufficient to uniquely identify states with descendant support up to level L — revealing a direct link between Virasoro representation theory and quantum measurement limits in holography.

Abstract: Einstein gravity in 2+1 dimensions is a topological theory that does not admit the existence of local bulk gravitons. However, $\mathrm{AdS}_3$ gravity with Brown-Henneaux boundary conditions does admit the existence of boundary gravitons, which are described by Virasoro descendants. An observer living on the asymptotic boundary can use finite order moments of the boundary stress tensor as a probe of these states. We fix one highest-weight module and assume that the Shapovalov forms are nondegenerate. We also assume that the unknown state the observer is probing has a known finite descendant support and that interlevel coherence is part of the unknown data. We find that, given a descendant at level $N\geq2$, stress moment tomography at order $N$ (meaning $N$ stress moment insertions) is informationally incomplete, while tomography with stress moments of order $N+1$ is informationally complete. This is to say that, on the unitary regular locus, orthogonal pure states can have the same complete order-$N$ record. Likewise, for exact finite-window result for levels through $L\geq2$, tomography of order $L$ is incomplete while tomography of order $L+1$ recovers every density-matrix block. Corresponding exact thresholds hold in the full left-right theory. In heavy sectors for which a semiclassical bulk interpretation exists, these thresholds say when finite-support boundary-graviton states first become distinguishable.

</details>


### [119] [Fermionic hidden zeros](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06552)
*Yong Zhang,Pengfei Zhu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper identifies and proves a universal class of 'hidden zeros' in tree-level amplitudes involving massless fermions — vanishing occurs on rectangular kinematic loci with species-specific bridge conditions — and extends this to gravitino and Einstein-Yang-Mills amplitudes, revealing a finite local structure common to gauge and gravity theories.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To uncover universal kinematic vanishing structures ('hidden zeros') in fermionic amplitudes that persist across dimensions (4,6,10) and extend to gravitational contexts, motivated by the need to understand underlying simplicity and constraints in scattering amplitudes beyond standard helicity or supersymmetry arguments.

Method: Uses worldsheet analysis (inspired by string theory and CHY formalism) to prove vanishing on rectangular kinematic loci; derives species-dependent bridge conditions via gauge-covariant contractions and vector current constraints; leverages local fusion channels (ff→g, fg/gf→f, gg→g) to establish universality at arbitrary multiplicity.

Result: Color-ordered gauge-theory amplitudes with arbitrary even numbers of massless adjoint fermions and gluons vanish on rectangular kinematic loci in D=4,6,10 when supplemented by fermion–fermion, fermion–gluon, and gluon–gluon bridge conditions; extension confirmed for 10D graviton–gravitino and single-trace EYM amplitudes with gluinos and gravitons.

Conclusion: A finite, local structural principle governs fermionic hidden zeros across both gauge and gravitational tree amplitudes — independent of multiplicity and rooted in universal worldsheet fusion rules — suggesting deeper organizing principles in quantum field theory amplitudes.

Abstract: We uncover and prove a general class of hidden zeros in tree-level amplitudes with massless fermions. For arbitrary even numbers and arbitrary arrangements of massless real adjoint fermions and gluons, color-ordered gauge-theory amplitudes in $D=4,6,10$ vanish on general rectangular kinematic loci supplemented by species-dependent bridge conditions. The fermion--fermion bridge is governed simply by the vector current $χ_iγ^μχ_j$, while mixed and gluonic bridges are fixed by the corresponding gauge-covariant contractions. A worldsheet analysis proves the result at arbitrary multiplicity and traces its universality to the local fusion channels $ff\to g$, $fg/gf\to f$, and $gg\to g$, with no new primitive bridge structures appearing at higher fermion multiplicity. The same mechanism extends to ten-dimensional amplitudes with gravitons and arbitrary even numbers of gravitinos in factorized gamma-traceless polarizations, and to single-trace Einstein-Yang-Mills amplitudes with adjoint gluinos and gravitons. These results expose a finite local structure underlying fermionic hidden zeros across gauge and gravitational amplitudes.

</details>


### [120] [Constructing the Monopole Formula for $A$-Type Good Quivers via Quiver Yangians](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06711)
*Tiantai Chen*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes a quiver Yangian interpretation of monopole formulas for 3D N=4 T_ρ(SU(N)) theories, reorganizing generators into boundary-adapted ones and recovering classical Casimir relations on the Slodowy slice; it confirms consistency with Hall-Littlewood Hilbert series predictions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To interpret monopole formulas for 3D N=4 good A-type quiver gauge theories (T_ρ(SU(N))) via the conjectured quiver Yangian / Coulomb branch algebra correspondence, especially leveraging its action on 1/2-BPS vortices.

Method: Uses the quiver Yangian action on 1/2-BPS vortices to define boundary-adapted generators of the truncated shifted quiver Yangian; derives classical relations in terms of U(N) Casimirs on the Slodowy slice S^{gl_N}_ρ; matches fugacities with the Hall-Littlewood expansion of the monopole formula.

Result: Boundary-adapted generators and their Casimir relations are explicitly constructed and shown to reproduce the correct Hilbert series—namely, the monopole formula for T_ρ(SU(N))—as the Hilbert series of the truncated shifted quiver Yangian.

Conclusion: The monopole formula for T_ρ(SU(N)) theories is fully reconstructed as the Hilbert series of the truncated shifted quiver Yangian, confirming the quiver Yangian/Coulomb branch correspondence at the level of chiral rings and providing an algebraic foundation for the geometric data encoded in the Slodowy slice.

Abstract: Based on the conjectured (and checked for tree-type quivers) quiver Yangian/Coulomb branch algebra correspondence, we give a quiver Yangian interpretation of the monopole formulas for 3D $\mathcal N=4$ good $A$-type quiver gauge theories, also known as the $T_ρ(SU(N))$ theories. Using the algebra action on the $\frac12$-BPS vortices, we reorganize the generators of the truncated shifted quiver Yangian into boundary-adapted generators, and obtain the classical relations in terms of $U(N)$ Casimirs on the Slodowy slice $\mathcal S^{\mathfrak{gl}_N}_ρ$, whose coordinates are given by these boundary-adapted generators. We also show that the boundary-adapted generators and the Casimir relations have the correct fugacities as predicted by the Hall-Littlewood expression of the monopole formula for $T_ρ(SU(N))$, hence reconstructing it as the Hilbert series of the truncated shifted quiver Yangian.

</details>


### [121] [Dual N=1 Lagrangians for Argyres-Douglas theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06995)
*Minseok Cho,Jaewon Song*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper discovers new 4D N=1 Lagrangian gauge theories—SU(2)^n quivers with flip fields—that flow in the IR to N=2 Argyres-Douglas theories of types (A₁,A₂ₙ), (A₁,A₂ₙ₊₁), (A₁,D₂ₙ₊₁), and (A₁,D₂ₙ₊₂), providing dual descriptions to earlier Sp(n)/SU(n+1) realizations by swapping rank and quiver length.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct explicit N=1 Lagrangian descriptions for N=2 Argyres-Douglas SCFTs, which are strongly coupled and lack conventional Lagrangians, thereby enabling controlled study via RG flows and duality.

Method: Constructing SU(2)^n linear quiver gauge theories augmented with gauge-singlet 'flip' fields; analyzing their infrared fixed points using supersymmetric RG flow arguments, matching central charges, chiral ring generators, and moduli space geometry to known (A₁, X) AD theories.

Result: Explicit N=1 Lagrangians for all (A₁, Aₖ) and (A₁, Dₖ) Argyres-Douglas theories; demonstration of duality between SU(2)^n quivers and previously known Sp(n) or SU(n+1) theories, where quiver length n maps to gauge group rank.

Conclusion: The SU(2)^n + flip construction provides a uniform, quiver-based N=1 framework for (A₁,X) AD theories, revealing a rank–node duality and enriching the landscape of Lagrangian embeddings of non-Lagrangian SCFTs.

Abstract: We find a new set of four-dimensional $\mathcal{N}=1$ Lagrangian gauge theories that flow in the infrared to $\mathcal{N}=2$ Argyres-Douglas theories of type $(A_1, A_{2n})$, $(A_1, A_{2n+1})$, $(A_1, D_{2n+1})$, and $(A_1, D_{2n+2})$. The new dual theories are given by $SU(2)^n$ quiver gauge theories with a number of gauge-singlet flip fields. This description provides dual theories for the previous $\mathcal{N}=1$ Lagrangian realizations given by $Sp(n)$ or $SU(n+1)$ gauge theories, exchanging the rank and the number of nodes in a quiver diagram.

</details>


### [122] [Tachyon-massless couplings at order $α'$ in bosonic string theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07221)
*Mohammad R. Garousi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper uses T-duality to constrain the bosonic string effective action at α′ order, showing that only even-tachyon couplings survive; it constructs a minimal basis of 14 four-derivative terms, reduces it to 6 via T-duality (parameterized by two constants), fixes them using zero-tachyon limit and 4-tachyon S-matrix, and finds the tachyon potential is consistent with (2/α′)(−1 + cos T), implying condensation to AdS with Λ = −4/α′.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine the structure of the classical effective action of bosonic string theory—specifically, whether it contains only even powers of the closed string tachyon—and to constrain its form at leading α′ order using fundamental symmetries (T-duality) and scattering amplitudes.

Method: Applies T-duality transformations to the effective action, employs higher-derivative field redefinitions to isolate covariant derivative couplings, constructs a minimal basis of independent four-derivative terms involving tachyon and massless fields, imposes T-duality invariance to eliminate redundant couplings, and fixes remaining parameters by matching to the known massless-sector action and the sphere-level 4-tachyon S-matrix element.

Result: The four-derivative effective action contains exactly six nonzero T-duality-invariant couplings parameterized by two constants; one is fixed by the zero-tachyon limit (recovering standard NS–NS sector action), the other by the 4-tachyon amplitude, yielding a positive T⁴ coefficient and identifying the tachyon potential as (2/α′)(−1 + cos T).

Conclusion: The closed string tachyon potential is fully determined at O(α′) up to T-duality and S-matrix consistency, supporting tachyon condensation to a stable vacuum at T = π, which dynamically generates an Anti-de Sitter spacetime with cosmological constant Λ = −4/α′.

Abstract: Recently, it has been proposed that the classical effective action of bosonic string theory should contain only couplings with an even number of tachyon fields. We employ the T-duality procedure to derive such couplings at order \(α'\) (four-derivative order) for the tachyon and massless fields. We first observe that, through higher-derivative field redefinitions of the tachyon potential term, one can choose a scheme in which, apart from the tachyon potential itself, all remaining couplings involve only covariant derivatives of the tachyon and the massless fields. We then construct a minimal basis of such couplings at four-derivative order, consisting of 14 independent terms. Imposing T-duality reduces this set to 6 nonzero couplings, expressed in terms of two unfixed parameters. One of these parameters is determined by matching to the known effective action in the zero-tachyon limit, while the other is fixed by comparison with the sphere-level S-matrix element of four tachyon vertex operators. This latter comparison also establishes that the coefficient of the \(T^4\) term in the tachyon potential is positive. Remarkably, the tachyon mass term and this quartic term are consistent with a potential of the form \((2/α')(-1+\cos T)\), which implies that the closed string tachyon condenses to the minimum of the potential at \(T=π\), thereby generating an Anti-de Sitter spacetime with cosmological constant \(Λ=-4/α'\).

</details>


### [123] [Can one hear the shape of a black hole singularity?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07514)
*Sean A. Hartnoll,Alexander Zhiboedov*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes large-overtone quasinormal frequencies of asymptotically AdS black holes with Kasner-type near-singularity geometry, showing that Kasner exponents and BKL transitions are encoded in the dispersion relation ∂ωₙ/∂k² at k=0, and establishes a mapping between overtone number n and proper time to the singularity via WKB analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe the near-singularity spacetime structure of black holes—particularly Kasner epochs and BKL chaos—using boundary-observable quasinormal modes in asymptotically AdS spacetimes.

Method: WKB analysis of quasinormal mode equations tailored to isolate non-analytic, singularity-determined exponents; analytical derivation of large-n asymptotics of ωₙ(k), focusing on the k→0 dispersion; numerical verification across multiple black hole models.

Result: The Kasner exponents are cleanly extractable from lim_{k→0} ∂ωₙ/∂k² in the large-n limit; Kasner transitions manifest as discrete changes in the n-dependence of ωₙ, enabling reconstruction of the BKL sequence; a universal map n ↔ τ (proper time to singularity) emerges.

Conclusion: Quasinormal mode overtones serve as a precise holographic probe of Planck-scale near-singularity dynamics, encoding Kasner geometry and chaotic BKL transitions in analytically accessible spectral data.

Abstract: We compute the large overtone quasinormal frequencies $ω_n$ of asymptotically AdS black holes with interior near-singularity Kasner epochs. We show that the Kasner exponents can be extracted cleanly from the large $n$ behaviour of the dispersion $\partial ω_n/\partial k^2$ at $k=0$, where $k$ is a boundary momentum. We show that Kasner transitions, as occur in BKL near-singularity chaos, are audible in the quasinormal frequencies and reveal a map between overtone number $n$ and proper time to the singularity. Our results are obtained using a WKB analysis that isolates non-analytic exponents, determined by the near-singularity spacetime, from non-universal data such as the optical distance from the boundary to the singularity. We check our formulae in several numerical models.

</details>


### [124] [Exact spin form factors and correlations at a massive Kramers-Wannier interface](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07612)
*Zoltan Bajnok,Yizhuang Liu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes exact finite-volume spin form factors and two-point correlations for a massive Kramer-Wannier interface in the scaling Ising model, using dual lattice realizations (self-dual inhomogeneous chain and mixed thermal average), yielding exact NSR spin matrix elements and a factorized Fredholm determinant for the interface correlator—validated by defect TCSA and fermionic spin-chain numerics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To exactly characterize the quantum impurity/interface physics of the massive Kramer-Wannier domain wall separating ordered and disordered phases in the scaling Ising model, where standard bulk CFT tools fail due to massiveness and boundary/interface effects.

Method: Combines two complementary lattice representations: (i) a self-dual inhomogeneous Ising chain whose continuum limit yields a sign-changing Majorana mass profile; (ii) a Euclidean space–time exchanged picture leading to a mixed thermal average solvable via Toeplitz/Fredholm determinant techniques. Exact conformal bootstrap-like constraints from NSR sector structure are imposed, and results are cross-validated using defect Truncated Conformal Space Approach (TCSA) and exact diagonalization of fermionic spin chains.

Result: Exact finite-volume spin form factors and two-point correlation functions for the massive Kramer-Wannier interface; explicit Neveu-Schwarz-Ramond spin matrix elements; a factorized Fredholm determinant representation of the interface correlator; numerical confirmation of spectrum and matrix elements via defect TCSA and fermionic spin-chain calculations.

Conclusion: The Kramer-Wannier interface in the massive scaling Ising model admits an exactly solvable description through dual lattice formulations and Fredholm theory, establishing a rare example of exact interface observables beyond perturbation or thermodynamic limits, with implications for boundary CFT, topological defects, and integrable impurity problems.

Abstract: We determine exact finite-volume spin form factors and two-point correlations for a massive Kramer--Wannier interface in the scaling Ising model, separating ordered and disordered phases. The interface admits two complementary lattice realizations: a self-dual inhomogeneous Ising chain whose continuum limit has a sign-changing Majorana mass profile, and, after exchanging Euclidean space and time, a mixed thermal average amenable to Toeplitz/Fredholm methods. Combining these descriptions, we obtain the exact Neveu-Schwarz-Ramond spin matrix elements and a factorized Fredholm determinant for the interface correlator. Defect TCSA and independent fermionic spin-chain calculations confirm the predicted spectrum and matrix elements.

</details>


### [125] [(Anti-)De Sitter null strings and Carroll-Weyl symmetry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07765)
*Ulf Lindström,Bo Sundborg*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a tensionless string in de Sitter space $dS_d$ by lifting the standard tensionless string to embedding space $R^{1,d}$, gauging worldsheet scale symmetry to break translation invariance while preserving $SO(1,d)$, and identifying the resulting gauge-fixed action as the Carroll–Weyl symmetric string—now interpreted intrinsically in $d$-dimensional $dS_d$, resolving prior ambiguities about its target space. The framework extends to Anti-de Sitter and suggests algebraic generation of curved backgrounds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the ambiguous target-space interpretation of the Carroll–Weyl symmetric string action and provide a consistent, geometrically grounded formulation of tensionless strings in de Sitter spacetime—where conventional worldsheet approaches face obstructions due to background curvature.

Method: Lifts the tensionless string to the $(d+1)$-dimensional embedding space $R^{1,d}$; introduces worldsheet scale symmetry gauging to break translational invariance while preserving $SO(1,d)$ isometry; performs De Sitter-symmetric gauge fixing to descend to $d$-dimensional $dS_d$; identifies the resulting action as the Carroll–Weyl string with intrinsic $dS_d$ interpretation; extends construction to AdS via sign flip in embedding metric.

Result: A fully $dS_d$-covariant tensionless string action derived purely algebraically from embedding space; unambiguous identification of the Carroll–Weyl action as describing $dS_d$ (not $R^{1,d}$) strings; resolution of prior confusion regarding its dimensionality and background; straightforward generalization to AdS tensionless strings.

Conclusion: Tensionless strings in (A)dS can be consistently formulated via scale-symmetry gauging in embedding space, yielding geometrically faithful, lower-dimensional actions—demonstrating that curved spacetime geometry can emerge algebraically from flat ambient symmetries, with implications beyond string theory.

Abstract: We construct a $dS_d$ null string in a novel way by lifting the standard tensionless string to $R^{1,d}$. Gauging worldsheet scale symmetry introduces terms in the action breaking translation invariance, but preserving the $dS_d$ symmetry $SO(1,d)$. Choosing a De Sitter symmetric gauge fixing of the scale symmetry yields a $d$ dimensional theory which we show has all the expected properties of $dS_d$ tensionless strings. The action happens to be the intriguing Carrol-Weyl symmetric string action in $R^{1,d}$, now with a clear target space interpretation in $d$ dimensions. That it does not, as recently claimed, represent a ($d+1$)-dimensional string in flat ($d+1$)-dimensional Minkowski space settles an apparent issue with the Carrol-Weyl string model. Furthermore, while there are clear obstacles for similar formulations of tensile strings in (A)dS backgrounds, our new insights are easily modified to the formulation of Anti-De Sitter tensionless strings. That our construction generates a curved geometry purely algebraically may have even wider applications.

</details>


### [126] [Eikonal Scattering of Two D0-branes with Graviton Emission](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07788)
*Paolo Di Vecchia,Dawid Maskalaniec*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes five-point amplitudes for graviton and dilaton emission in D0-brane scattering using boundary-state formalism in bosonic and type II superstring theories, and confirms consistency with worldline-based low-energy field-theory calculations at nonzero impact parameter.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how massless closed-string states (e.g., graviton, dilaton) are emitted during D0-brane scattering, bridging string-theoretic amplitudes with their low-energy effective field-theory descriptions.

Method: Boundary-state formalism to compute five-point string amplitudes for graviton/dilaton emission; comparison with worldline quantum field theory in the field-theory limit.

Result: Computed amplitudes for graviton and dilaton emission in both bosonic and type II superstring theories; shown to match worldline-based field-theory predictions for nonzero impact parameter.

Conclusion: The boundary-state approach correctly reproduces low-energy gravitational and dilatonic radiation from D0-brane scattering, supporting the consistency of string theory with its effective field-theory limit in this dynamical regime.

Abstract: We study the emission of a massless closed-string state in the scattering of two D0-branes using the boundary-state formalism. We compute the corresponding five-point amplitudes for graviton and dilaton emission in bosonic string theory and in type II superstring theory, and analyze their field-theory limit. For nonzero impact parameter, the resulting amplitudes are reproduced by an independent calculation in the worldline formulation of the corresponding low-energy theory.

</details>


### [127] [Logarithmic correction to the entropy of near-extremal higher-curvature black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07801)
*Pablo Bueno,Pablo A. Cano,Robie A. Hennigar,Javier Moreno,Ángel J. Murcia,Guido van der Velde*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes a universal near-horizon JT gravity description for near-extremal black holes in broad D-dimensional gravitational theories (including Lovelock and Quasitopological gravities) via their spherically symmetric reduction to 2D Horndeski theory, proving universality of the logarithmic quantum entropy correction and computing its breakdown temperature scale.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify universal low-temperature quantum features of near-extremal black holes across broad classes of higher-dimensional gravitational theories beyond Einstein gravity.

Method: Leverages the dimensional reduction of spherically symmetric D-dimensional gravity to 2D Horndeski theory; constructs the universal near-horizon JT gravity effective action; computes the one-loop exact JT partition function to extract the logarithmic entropy correction; expresses the breakdown temperature T_breakdown in terms of D-dimensional theory data.

Result: Demonstrates universality of the logarithmic quantum entropy correction S_JT = (3/2) log(T / T_breakdown) for all D-dimensional theories admitting a 2D Horndeski description; derives the general functional form of T_breakdown from D-dimensional parameters; verifies results with explicit examples: charged black holes in Lovelock gravity and regular black holes in Quasitopological gravity.

Conclusion: The logarithmic quantum correction to near-extremal black hole entropy is a universal feature encoded in the near-horizon JT gravity description, independent of the specific higher-dimensional UV completion, as long as the theory admits a spherically symmetric reduction to Horndeski gravity.

Abstract: The spherically symmetric sector of broad classes of $D$-dimensional gravitational theories can be effectively described by general two-dimensional Horndeski theories. Exploiting this correspondence, we study the low-temperature dynamics of near-extremal black hole solutions of the parent theories by deriving a universal near-horizon effective description in terms of Jackiw--Teitelboim (JT) gravity. We explicitly show that the logarithmic quantum correction to the entropy, $S_{\rm JT}=\frac{3}{2}\log \left(T/T_{\rm breakdown} \right)$, previously derived for near-extremal black holes in Einstein gravity coupled to matter from the one-loop exact JT partition function, is universal across all models admitting an effective two-dimensional Horndeski description. We determine the general form of the scale at which this correction becomes dominant, $T_{\rm breakdown}$, in terms of the data of the $D$-dimensional theories. We illustrate our general results with charged black holes in Lovelock gravities and regular black holes in Quasitopological gravities.

</details>


### [128] [SolutionsX: supergravity theories/solutions as code, with an agentic workflow for machine-verifiable physics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07826)
*Vasil Dimitrov*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: SolutionsX is a Mathematica package built on xAct that enables machine-verifiable, reproducible encoding and verification of supergravity (and other xAct-compatible) solutions; it supports automated codification by AI agents, provides internal kernel-based verification during reasoning, and facilitates a reusable, searchable database of verified physics solutions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the lack of verifiability, reproducibility, and reusability of published supergravity solutions—due to ambiguous conventions, typos, and tedious algebra—by creating a machine-checkable, self-contained computational record.

Method: Development of SolutionsX: a Mathematica package layered on xAct that generates kernel-replayable, self-verifying code for tensorial, form-based, or spinorial physics models; integration with AI coding agents for automated 'blind' codification; benchmarking against native xAct; demonstration via full workflow on a published solution.

Result: SolutionsX successfully encodes and verifies supergravity solutions in a replayable, self-checking format; benchmarks show competitive performance vs. xAct; three blind AI agent runs correctly codified recent papers within <3 hours; establishes foundation for a verified solution database and agent training corpus.

Conclusion: SolutionsX bridges formal theoretical physics and computational reproducibility, enabling both human researchers and AI agents to build upon rigorously verified, modular, and extensible solution records—thereby transforming how analytic solutions are shared, validated, and extended.

Abstract: A supergravity solution in the literature is an artifact that cannot be verified without re-deriving it: conventions might not be clearly stated, papers have typos, algebra is tedious. SolutionsX is a Mathematica package that turns an xAct environment, whatever physics it may describe, into a stored, machine-verifiable record: code that replays in a fresh kernel, carries its own verification, and can later be fetched for re-use or extension. The shipped examples are supergravity theories and their solutions, but the scope is anything xAct reaches: abstract tensors, differential forms, spinors in any dimension. We follow one published solution through the entire workflow, benchmark the package's component engine against xAct's, and report on three blind runs of an agentic workflow in which an AI coding agent, given the package and a small toolkit, correctly codified a recent paper in under three hours. A database of such records is useful to researchers today and gives agents worked examples to learn from. The package acts as an internal verifier during the thought process of an agent: a claim is checked in the kernel before it is made.

</details>


### [129] [Unitary quantum conformal Bondi-Metzner-Sachs field theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07828)
*Daniel Grumiller,Iva Lovrekovic*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper applies unitarity constraints to 2D field theories with quantum conformal BMS symmetry, deriving bounds on the central charge c and identifying only four admissible values (c = 1, 3/2, 13/7, 2) compatible with positivity of the vertex algebra; their primary spectra satisfy BPS-like and holographic inequalities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify consistent, unitary two-dimensional field theories invariant under the quantum conformal Bondi-Metzner-Sachs (BMS) algebra — a symmetry combining 2D conformal invariance and asymptotic symmetries of flat spacetime — motivated by holography and flat-space quantum gravity.

Method: Imposing unitarity and positivity conditions on the representation theory of the quantum conformal BMS algebra; analyzing constraints on the central charge via Virasoro subalgebra embedding and vertex operator algebra (VOA) positivity; computing spectra of primary operators for allowed central charges.

Result: Derivation of strict bound 0 < c < 2.053 from unitarity; further restriction to exactly four discrete central charges — c = 1, 3/2, 13/7 ≈ 1.857, and 2 — required for VOA positivity; explicit computation of primary spectra for each case, all satisfying BPS-like saturation bounds and holographic entropy inequalities.

Conclusion: Only four unitary, consistent 2D CFTs (or BMS-invariant analogues) exist under quantum conformal BMS symmetry; these constitute viable candidates for holographic duals of 3D asymptotically flat gravity with cosmological constant tuned to zero or related limits.

Abstract: We impose unitarity constraints on two-dimensional field theories whose symmetries form the quantum conformal Bondi-Metzner-Sachs algebra, which combines conformal symmetries with asymptotic symmetries of asymptotically flat spacetimes. Positivity constraints put bounds on the central charge, 0<c<2.053. Positivity of the associated vertex algebra allows only four central charges, c = 1, 3/2, 13/7, 2. Thus, only four candidates for unitary field theories remain. We calculate their spectra of primaries and show that they are compatible with BPS-like and holographic inequalities.

</details>


### [130] [Diffeomorphism-invariant Approach to Asymptotically Safe Quantum Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07829)
*Friederike Ihssen,Benjamin Knorr,Silas Mezger,Jan M. Pawlowski,Paul P. Sprenger*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces a diffeomorphism-invariant renormalisation group framework for asymptotically safe quantum gravity, enabling background-independent, physics-informed flows and a robust computation of the Reuter fixed point with quantified approximation errors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the tension between maintaining diffeomorphism invariance and performing practical renormalisation group computations in metric quantum gravity—particularly in asymptotic safety—where standard background-field methods break invariance or obscure operator relevance counting.

Method: Constructs a physics-informed renormalisation group flow using a diffeomorphism-invariant kernel that preserves quantum diffeomorphism invariance at every RG step; implements background-independent coarse-graining; applies the formalism to compute the Reuter fixed point within truncations while systematically analysing regularisation dependence and approximation errors.

Result: A manifestly diffeomorphism-invariant and background-independent effective action; successful computation of the Reuter fixed point without background-field artifacts; quantification of systematic errors and regularisation sensitivity.

Conclusion: Diffeomorphism invariance can be preserved throughout the RG flow without sacrificing computational control; the approach strengthens the conceptual and technical foundations of asymptotically safe quantum gravity and enables more reliable extraction of physical predictions.

Abstract: We provide a novel diffeomorphism-invariant approach to asymptotically safe metric quantum gravity. It is based on physics-informed renormalisation group flows with a renormalisation group kernel that guarantees diffeomorphism invariance at each renormalisation group step. Importantly, it also allows us to control and maintain the relevance counting of operators in metric quantum gravity. The resulting effective action is quantum diffeomorphism-invariant and background-independent. As a first non-trivial application, we compute the Reuter fixed point in a manifestly diffeomorphism-invariant way. This computation is augmented with a detailed discussion of regularisation dependence and a systematic estimate of the errors arising from approximations.

</details>


### [131] [Truncated Polyakov bootstrap for BCFTs: Neumann-Dirichlet flow and Ising special transition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07841)
*Kaushik Kangsabanik,Apratim Kaviraj,Astha Tiwari*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Extends the truncated Polyakov bootstrap to boundary CFTs using analytic functionals; identifies nonperturbative BCFT solutions via deformations from generalized free fields (GFF) with Neumann to Dirichlet boundary conditions; applies method to 3d Ising special transition, yielding BCFT data consistent with lattice results.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a nonperturbative bootstrap framework for boundary conformal field theories (BCFTs), bridging the gap between analytic bootstrap methods and boundary physics, especially where lattice or perturbative data exist but exact solutions are lacking.

Method: Applies the truncated Polyakov bootstrap within the analytic functional framework to solve BCFT crossing equations; constructs continuous deformations of BCFT data starting from generalized free fields (GFF) with Neumann boundary condition, flowing to Dirichlet; uses Wilson–Fisher perturbation theory as the deformation path for the 3d Ising special transition.

Result: Obtains high-precision nonperturbative BCFT spectra and OPE coefficients for the Neumann→Dirichlet flow and the 3d Ising special transition; quantitative agreement with existing lattice Monte Carlo results for the latter; provides first-principles predictions where lattice data are absent.

Conclusion: The analytic functional + truncated Polyakov approach is a viable, systematic nonperturbative tool for BCFTs; GFF-based deformations provide an effective organizing principle for BCFT solution space; the method successfully reproduces and extends known results for physically relevant boundaries like the 3d Ising special transition.

Abstract: We extend the recently proposed truncated Polyakov bootstrap to boundary conformal field theories (BCFTs). The formalism uses the analytic functional framework to find approximate solutions of BCFT crossing. We show that a generic nonperturbative solution can be identified by relating it to a family of deformations around generalized free fields (GFF) with a fixed boundary condition. We show this by tracking the flow of BCFT data from GFF with Neumann boundary condition to that with Dirichlet. The same method is then used for the 3d Ising special transition, using the perturbative Wilson-Fisher description as the deformation to identify it. The BCFT data obtained are in remarkable agreement with existing lattice results where available, and are otherwise new.

</details>


### [132] [Scattering of Null strings - Flipped Vacuum & CHY](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07845)
*Arjun Bagchi,Sachin Grover,Sharang Rajesh Iyer,Amartya Saha,Stephan Stieberger*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs scattering amplitudes for quantum null tensionless strings using the 'flipped' vacuum and highest-weight representation of the 2D Conformal Carroll Algebra (CCA), recovering the CHY formulae for massless states.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a consistent quantum theory of null tensionless strings and understand its scattering amplitudes, leveraging the residual 2D Conformal Carroll symmetry on the null worldsheet.

Method: Uses the ILST action for classical null strings; selects the 'flipped' vacuum realizing the highest-weight representation of the 2D CCA; introduces novel vertex operators tied to compactified null strings to construct scattering amplitudes.

Result: Scattering amplitudes for massless states in the quantum null 'flipped' string theory are shown to reproduce the Cachazo-He-Yuan (CHY) formulae.

Conclusion: The CHY formalism emerges naturally from the quantum theory of null tensionless strings quantized in the flipped vacuum, establishing a concrete link between Carrollian symmetry, tensionless limits, and modern amplitude methods.

Abstract: We investigate the scattering of null tensionless strings. Classical null strings, given by the ILST action, give rise to three distinct quantum theories built on different vacua and representations of the underlying 2d Conformal Carroll algebra (CCA) which form the residual gauge symmetries on the null worldsheet. In this paper, we are interested in the quantum theory built out of the so-called ``flipped'' vacuum which realises the highest weight representation of the 2d CCA. By considering a novel class of vertex operators, intimately connected to the compactified null string, we build scattering amplitudes of the theory. We show that one naturally obtains the Cachazo-He-Yuan (CHY) formulae when one considers scattering of massless states in the null ``flipped'' string.

</details>


### [133] [Double Copy from the Flipped Null String](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07846)
*Arjun Bagchi,Sachin Grover,Sharang Rajesh Iyer,Amartya Saha,Stephan Stieberger*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes a Carrollian world-sheet origin for the double-copy structure in string theory, linking bosonic null string correlators to CHY half-integrands and KLT representations, and connecting lattice momentum-winding sectors to BCJ relations and $O(d,d)$-compatible color currents.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To uncover a world-sheet origin of the double copy—particularly how kinematic numerators and color-kinematics duality emerge from string-theoretic structures—and to reconcile it with $O(d,d)$ T-duality and Carrollian symmetry.

Method: Analysis of tree-level amplitudes of the bosonic null string in the flipped vacuum; identification of level-one and level-two correlators with CHY kinematic half-integrands and Parke-Taylor factors; examination of momentum-winding lattice sectors and their factorization properties; derivation of BCJ ordering and KLT representation from common-momentum lattice sectors.

Result: Level-one vector correlator yields the $(DF)^2$ gauge-theory CHY half-integrand; specific winding sectors produce Parke-Taylor factors; factorized level-two states generate two half-integrands enabling Weyl-cubed graviton amplitudes via symmetric double copy; common-momentum lattice sectors reproduce BCJ relations and field-theory KLT kernel; emergent color sector is compatible with $O(d,d)$ structure.

Conclusion: The double copy has a direct Carrollian world-sheet realization in the bosonic null string; its correlators and lattice sectors encode both kinematic numerators and color-like structures, suggesting a current-algebra origin for color compatible with double-field-theory geometry.

Abstract: We study the double-copy structure of tree amplitudes of the bosonic null string in the flipped vacuum. We show that its level-one vector correlator supplies the Cachazo-He-Yuan (CHY) kinematic half-integrand of the higher-derivative $(DF)^2$ gauge theory, while distinguished compact momentum-winding sectors generate the complementary Parke-Taylor factor. For factorized level-two states, the same Carrollian world-sheet produces two kinematic half-integrands and hence the symmetric double copy to the six-derivative Weyl-cubed graviton sector. Lattice sectors with common external momenta reproduce the Bern-Carrasco-Johansson (BCJ) ordering relation and the corresponding field-theory Kawai-Lewellen-Tye (KLT) representation. These results provide a direct Carrollian world-sheet origin of the double copy and point toward a current-algebra realization of the emergent color sector compatible with the $O(d,d)$ structure of the compact lattice.

</details>


### [134] [Entanglement Wedge Reconstruction Beyond the Large N Limit via the Twirled Petz Map](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07866)
*Arash Alipour Shahmiri,Maryam Sharifian,Niloofar Vardian*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes quantum error correction as the natural framework for bulk reconstruction in AdS/CFT beyond the semiclassical (large N) regime, using the twirled Petz map to systematically incorporate 1/N corrections and deriving an explicit bi-local subleading correction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend entanglement wedge reconstruction beyond leading-order large N (semiclassical) regime, where modular flow and standard Petz map reconstructions are insufficient.

Method: Leverages quantum error correction formalism; employs the twirled Petz map to construct a systematic expansion in 1/N; derives subleading corrections via a refined Petz kernel yielding a bi-local bulk operator reconstruction.

Result: Reproduces known leading-order reconstruction; obtains explicit, controlled 1/N-corrected reconstruction formula with a bi-local correction term governed by a subleading Petz kernel.

Conclusion: Quantum error correction provides a rigorous, systematic, and controllable framework for bulk reconstruction beyond large N, sharpening its role in holography and enabling progress toward fully quantum gravitational settings.

Abstract: Entanglement wedge reconstruction provides the sharpest formulation of bulk locality in AdS/CFT, identifying the entanglement wedge as the largest bulk region reconstructible from a boundary subregion. While this statement is well understood at leading order in the large N expansion through both modular flow and Petz map reconstruction, its extension beyond the semiclassical regime has remained unclear. In this work, we show that quantum error correction provides the natural framework for addressing this problem. At leading order, we reproduce the known leading-order reconstruction formulas, and by employing the twirled Petz map, we obtain a systematic and explicit method for incorporating subleading 1/N corrections. Beyond the leading approximation, we have obtained an explicit bi-local correction controlled by a subleading Petz kernel. Our results establish a controlled approach to bulk reconstruction beyond large N and sharpening the role of quantum error correction in holography. This framework provides a controlled path toward understanding bulk reconstruction in fully quantum gravitational settings.

</details>


### [135] [ABJM/BMN Index Matching in the Plane-Wave Limit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07872)
*Chi-Ming Chang,Kangning Liu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proves coefficientwise equality between refined supersymmetric indices of ABJM theory and the BMN matrix model in the plane-wave limit at any fixed positive Chern–Simons level k, extending prior single-membrane results to multi-membrane configurations via monopole-harmonic/BMN-matrix-harmonic correspondence and residue-based Higgs branch analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the known index equivalence between ABJM theory and the BMN matrix model from single membranes to arbitrary finite multi-membrane families, while clarifying the role of Chern–Simons level k, longitudinal momenta, monopole fluxes, and harmonic cutoffs in the plane-wave limit.

Method: Uses refined supersymmetric index computations; maps scalar-dressed equal-flux ABJM monopole sectors to BMN fuzzy-sphere vacua; evaluates Higgs-branch residues to obtain diagonal-holonomy integrals; compares monopole-harmonic and BMN matrix-harmonic kernels with finite spin cutoff; analyzes fugacity-grade dependence on k and momentum scaling.

Result: Coefficientwise equality of refined indices holds for all fixed k > 0 and finite multi-membrane families in the plane-wave limit (infinite longitudinal momenta with fixed pairwise differences); the harmonic kernel agreement is exact up to the BMN upper-spin cutoff; the index difference decomposes into a non-plane-wave remainder (k-shifted in fugacity grade) and a cutoff-dependent tail; no change in surviving kernel or tail contribution under k variation.

Conclusion: The ABJM–BMN index duality is robust beyond the single-membrane case: it holds coefficientwise for arbitrary finite membrane multiplicities, with k controlling only the grading shift of subleading corrections—not the leading plane-wave kernel—thereby solidifying the M-theoretic interpretation of ABJM monopoles as fuzzy-sphere membranes in the plane-wave background.

Abstract: We establish coefficientwise equality between the refined supersymmetric indices of ABJM theory and the BMN matrix model in the plane-wave limit at every fixed positive Chern-Simons level k. For multi-membrane families with fixed multiplicities, and hence fixed finite ABJM gauge rank, the equality holds as all longitudinal momenta tend to infinity with their pairwise differences fixed. Scalar-dressed equal-flux ABJM monopoles correspond to BMN vacua of fuzzy-sphere membranes: multiplicities count coincident membranes, whereas flux magnitudes give the momentum per membrane along the M-theory circle. At the index level, the associated Higgs residues yield a diagonal-holonomy integral for the ABJM plane-wave contribution, whose monopole-harmonic kernel agrees with the BMN matrix-harmonic kernel up to the finite BMN upper-spin cutoff. At finite momentum, the full index difference splits into the non-plane-wave remainder and the harmonic tail above this cutoff. Increasing k shifts the former to higher fugacity grade without changing the surviving kernel or the cutoff contribution. This gives a coefficientwise, multi-membrane extension of the earlier single-membrane index relation.

</details>


### [136] [Spherical DAHA as an algebra of framed BPS states](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07911)
*Kunal Gupta,Pietro Longhi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends the physical realization of skein algebras from SL_N to GL_N in 4d N=2 class S theories, proving that the GL_2 skein algebra of the punctured torus is isomorphic to the spherical double affine Hecke algebra SH^{q,t}_2; it uses q-nonabelianization for SU(2) N=2^* theory, linking skein relations to quantum torus algebras on the Seiberg–Witten curve, and shows how distinct Coulomb branch regions yield different presentations (Macdonald vs. cluster-type), unified by framed wall-crossing and the BPS spectrum.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the known correspondence between line operators in 4d N=2 class S theories and SL_N skein algebras to GL_N skein algebras, and to physically realize and unify various presentations of the spherical double affine Hecke algebra (DAHA) via gauge theory and geometric structures.

Method: Uses q-nonabelianization for the SU(2) N=2^* theory in a half-Omega background to map line operators to quantum torus algebra elements associated with the Seiberg–Witten curve; analyzes dependence on Coulomb branch moduli to obtain distinct algebraic presentations (Fenchel–Nielsen/Macdonald vs. Fock–Goncharov/cluster); employs framed wall-crossing and the vanilla BPS spectrum to relate these presentations.

Result: Proves isomorphism between the GL_2 skein algebra of the punctured torus and the spherical double affine Hecke algebra SH^{q,t}_2; derives multiple equivalent presentations of SH^{q,t}_2 — Macdonald q-difference modules in weak coupling and cluster-type algebras in strong coupling — linked by BPS-induced wall-crossing transformations.

Conclusion: The GL_N skein algebra admits a unified physical realization in 4d N=2 class S theories, with the N=2^* example providing a concrete, geometric, and wall-crossing–controlled framework for spherical DAHA representations, bridging quantum topology, integrable systems, and supersymmetric gauge theory.

Abstract: Line operators of 4d $\mathcal{N}=2$ theories of class $\mathcal{S}$ of type $A_{N-1}$ in a half Omega background provide a physical model for $SL_N$ skein algebras. In this paper we extend this correspondence to $GL_N$ skein algebras and prove that the $GL_2$ skein algebra of the punctured torus is isomorphic to the $GL_2$ spherical double affine Hecke algebra ${{\mathbf{S}\ddot{\mathbf{H}}}}^{q,t}_2$. The construction is based on $\mathfrak{q}$-nonabelianization for the $SU(2)$ $\mathcal{N}=2^*$ gauge theory, which realizes the skein algebra as a quantum torus algebra associated with the Seiberg-Witten curve. Different regions of the Coulomb branch yield distinct presentations of ${{\mathbf{S}\ddot{\mathbf{H}}}}^{q,t}_2$. These range from the Macdonald $q$-difference module in Fenchel-Nielsen (weak coupling) charts to cluster-type realizations in Fock-Goncharov (strong coupling) charts. Transitions between these descriptions are governed by the vanilla BPS spectrum of the $\mathcal{N}=2^*$ theory via framed wall-crossing, which provides a unified physical framework for several representations of spherical DAHA.

</details>


### [137] [Wilson loops, M2-branes and strings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07928)
*Alexia Nix*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper develops a universal semiclassical quantization framework for probe M2-branes and fundamental strings in asymptotically AdS₄ backgrounds, dual to half-BPS Wilson loops in 3D N≥2 Chern-Simons-matter theories; it yields novel semiclassical predictions, matches prior localization results, and identifies the holographic ensemble where M2-brane quantization reproduces the full 1/N perturbative expansion of the Wilson loop.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify and generalize semiclassical quantization of probe branes/strings in AdS₄, enabling precise dual field-theoretic predictions for half-BPS Wilson loops in 3D N≥2 Chern-Simons-matter theories beyond existing case-specific methods.

Method: Applies universal semiclassical quantization techniques to probe M2-branes and fundamental strings in asymptotically AdS₄ geometries; leverages AdS/CFT duality to relate their partition functions to vacuum expectation values of half-BPS Wilson loops; compares with supersymmetric localization results and analyzes holographic ensemble dependence.

Result: Derives novel semiclassical predictions for Wilson loop VEVs; achieves consistency with known localization results where available; identifies that semiclassical quantization of probe M2-branes—under the correct holographic ensemble—reproduces the complete perturbative 1/N expansion (in gauge group rank N) of the dual Wilson loop.

Conclusion: A universal semiclassical framework successfully bridges bulk probe dynamics in AdS₄ and boundary Wilson loop observables in 3D Chern-Simons-matter theories, with M2-branes providing the full perturbative 1/N series when the appropriate holographic ensemble is selected.

Abstract: We establish a universal approach for studying the semiclassical quantization of both probe M2-branes and fundamental strings in asymptotically AdS$_{4}$ backgrounds. On the field theory side, their partition functions are dual to the vacuum expectation value of half-BPS Wilson loops of a class of three-dimensional $\mathcal{N}\geq 2$ Chern-Simons-matter theories. Utilizing this universal approach we arrive at novel predictions for the semiclassical behaviour of the Wilson loop or upon availability match our results with the previously obtained expressions via supersymmetric localization. We also show that the correct choice of holographic ensemble suggests that the semiclassical quantization of probe M2-branes yields the full perturbative answer in the rank of the gauge group $N$ for the dual Wilson loop. This is a proceedings contribution to the ''Athens Workshop in Theoretical Physics: 10th Anniversary'', held at the National and Kapodistrian University of Athens on December 17-19 2025.

</details>


### [138] [Fortuitous ABJM Operators as Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08052)
*Connor Behan,Leonardo Pipolo de Gioia*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies BPS states in ABJM theory by diagonalizing the anomalous dimension matrix, identifying 'fortuitous' operator trajectories that are BPS only at finitely many N; it finds 16 such trajectories (vs. only one accessible in N=4 SYM), confirms large-N agreement with integrability, links some to black hole duals via restricted graviton dressing, and observes a qualitatively distinct BPS gap compared to N=4 SYM.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To distinguish holographically between operators dual to horizonless geometries versus black holes by analyzing how BPS conditions depend on N, leveraging ABJM's computational tractability relative to N=4 SYM.

Method: Diagonalization of the anomalous dimension matrix in U(N)_k × U(N)_{-k} ABJM theory treating N as a formal parameter; identification and analysis of 'fortuitous' operator trajectories (BPS only at finitely many N); large-N asymptotics; comparison with integrability predictions and cohomological classification.

Result: 16 fortuitous BPS trajectories identified (with descendants); their large-N behavior matches integrability predictions; several exhibit black-hole-like restrictions on allowed graviton dressings; the BPS gap shows qualitatively different scaling from N=4 SYM.

Conclusion: ABJM theory provides a richer, analytically accessible setting than N=4 SYM for probing the holographic operator-black hole correspondence via N-dependent BPS structure, revealing new classes of fortuitous operators and distinct spectral features.

Abstract: Given a superconformal gauge theory at a fixed loop order, BPS states can be studied in two ways --- by enumerating cohomology classes of a nilpotent supercharge or by diagonalizing the anomalous dimension matrix. The second one allows for a deeper look at how holographic theories distinguish between operators based on whether they are dual to horizonless geometries or black holes. To this end, we carry out the diagonalization in $\mathrm{U}(N)_k \times \mathrm{U}(N)_{-k}$ ABJM theory with $N$ as a formal parameter. This makes it possible to define trajectories of operators and focus on the fortuitous ones which are characterized by being BPS at only finitely many values of $N$. In contrast to $\mathcal{N} = 4$ Super Yang-Mills, where it has been numerically prohibitive to study more than one of these, ABJM gives us access to 16 such trajectories along with their descendants. We show that they take an especially simple form at large $N$ where they agree with the predictions of integrability. We also show that many of them, as with black holes, can only be dressed by certain types of gravitons and comment on how this exercise in cohomology also has implications for properties of the full operators. Finally, we show that the gap to the first non-BPS state in ABJM theory qualitatively differs from that of $\mathcal{N} = 4$ Super Yang-Mills in the expected way.

</details>


### [139] [Bogomol'nyi equations for Kruglov strings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08111)
*I. Praseyto,U. Ubaydillah,H. S. Ramadhan*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs Bogomol'nyi (BPS) equations for Abelian gauge–Higgs vortices with Kruglov nonlinear electrodynamics—parameterized by exponent σ—and derives them via the stressless method without pre-specifying the Higgs potential; it introduces a constitutive map Φ(Y;σ), analyzes its monotonicity and boundedness across σ regimes, obtains closed-form BPS potentials and field equations for six σ values, computes vortex profiles numerically, and shows the BPS string tension is universally topological: μ_BPS = 2πn.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize vortex BPS structure beyond Maxwell electrodynamics by embedding Kruglov nonlinear electrodynamics—a power-law family interpolating Maxwell, Born–Infeld, and exponential electrodynamics—into the Abelian Higgs model, and to derive BPS equations in a systematic, stress-tensor-based way without assuming the Higgs potential a priori.

Method: Stressless method: deriving first-order Bogomol'nyi equations from the vanishing of the spatial stress tensor; introduction and analysis of a constitutive map Φ(Y;σ) linking gauge and Higgs fields; classification of σ regimes (σ < 1/2, σ = 1/2, σ > 1/2) based on monotonicity and boundedness; explicit construction of BPS potentials and gauge-field equations for six representative σ values (including linear, quadratic, cubic algebraic cases); numerical computation of vortex profiles.

Result: For σ > 1/2, Φ(Y;σ) is strictly monotonic and unbounded; for 0 < σ < 1/2, it has a finite maximum, imposing explicit upper bounds on the nonlinear parameter β; at σ = 1/2, Φ is bounded but not unbounded. Closed-form BPS potentials and gauge equations are obtained for six σ values. Numerical vortex solutions confirm finite-energy, topologically quantized configurations. Crucially, the BPS string tension is universally μ_BPS = 2πn, independent of σ and β.

Conclusion: The BPS structure of Abelian vortices persists under Kruglov nonlinear electrodynamics, but the coupling between gauge and Higgs sectors becomes implicit and σ-dependent via the constitutive map Φ(Y;σ). The topological nature of the BPS tension is robust across the entire family, underscoring universality of the Bogomol'nyi bound in this generalized electromagnetic framework.

Abstract: We construct the Bogomol'nyi equations for Abelian gauge--Higgs vortices in which the Maxwell gauge sector is replaced by Kruglov nonlinear electrodynamics, a power-law family that interpolates between Maxwell theory, Born--Infeld electrodynamics, and exponential electrodynamics, characterized by a dimensionless exponent $σ$. Using the stressless method, we derive a pair of first-order equations directly from the vanishing of the spatial stress tensor, without assuming the Higgs potential \textit{a priori}. For generic $σ$, the gauge and Higgs sectors are coupled through an implicit algebraic relation. We therefore introduce a constitutive map $Φ(Y;σ)$ and analyze its monotonicity and range to determine the conditions for a smooth admissible Bogomol'nyi branch. For $σ>1/2$, the constitutive map is strictly monotonic and unbounded, whereas for $0<σ<1/2$ it possesses a finite maximum; the marginal case $σ=1/2$ is bounded. These properties yield explicit bounds on the nonlinear parameter $β$ for the latter cases. We further obtain closed-form constitutive relations, BPS potentials, and gauge-field equations for six representative values of $σ$, spanning linear, quadratic, and cubic algebraic structures. The corresponding vortex profiles are then computed numerically. The resulting BPS string tension is purely topological, $μ_{\rm BPS}=2πn$, independent of both $σ$ and $β$.

</details>


### [140] [Scalar Casimir Effect on a Two-Dimensional Sphere with a Wu--Yang Magnetic Monopole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08378)
*Jun-Cheng Zhang,Xiao-Yin Pan,Juhao Wu,Qing-hai Wang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies the Casimir effect of a charged scalar field on a 2D sphere with a central Wu–Yang magnetic monopole, showing that sufficiently strong monopole strength can reverse Casimir pressure from attractive to repulsive, due to topological and curvature effects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how nontrivial topology—specifically the Wu–Yang magnetic monopole—alters vacuum quantum fluctuations (Casimir effect) of a charged scalar field on a curved background (S²), potentially yielding repulsive vacuum stress relevant for gravity and cosmology.

Method: Analytic solution of the Klein–Gordon equation for a complex scalar field on S² in the presence of a Wu–Yang monopole; use of a generalized Abel–Plana formula for multivalued functions to compute the renormalized Casimir energy and pressure.

Result: The Casimir pressure changes sign—from attractive to repulsive—as the monopole strength increases beyond a critical value; this reversal is robust across a wide range of curvature coupling parameters (ξ).

Conclusion: Wu–Yang monopoles induce topologically driven modifications to vacuum energy that can generate repulsive Casimir pressure, suggesting a mechanism for exotic vacuum stresses with potential relevance in gravitational physics and early-universe cosmology.

Abstract: We investigate the Casimir effect of a complex scalar field on a two-dimensional sphere threaded by a fixed Wu--Yang magnetic monopole at the center. In this background the charged scalar field is a section of a nontrivial complex line bundle over $S^2$, reflecting the monopole's nontrivial topology. We solve the Klein--Gordon equation analytically and compute the Casimir energy using a generalized Abel--Plana formula for multivalued functions. We find that the monopole can reverse the sign of the Casimir pressure, turning an attractive force into a repulsive one when the monopole strength is sufficiently large. This behavior persists across a broad range of curvature couplings and suggests that Wu--Yang monopoles may provide a source of repulsive vacuum stress, with possible implications for gravitational and cosmological settings.

</details>


### [141] [Covariant Phase Space and Carroll-Weyl $χ$ Symmetry of Carroll Non-BPS D$_p$-branes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08874)
*Limin Zeng*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies the covariant phase space of Carroll non-BPS Dp-branes, analyzing constraint structure, degrees of freedom, and symmetry properties under the Carroll limit; it distinguishes generic and tachyon-vacuum sectors, identifies rank conditions for constraints, and shows that Carroll–Weyl χ-symmetry fails to become first-class except in a highly restricted global vacuum.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the canonical structure and gauge symmetries of Carroll-limit non-BPS Dp-branes—particularly how the Carroll contraction affects constraint algebra closure, phase-space degrees of freedom, and the status of Carroll–Weyl χ-symmetry—relative to null string theory.

Method: Covariant phase-space analysis of Klusoň's canonical Carroll non-BPS Dp-brane actions; systematic application of the Carroll contraction to the canonical one-form and Hamiltonian constraints; classification into electric-like and magnetic-like sectors; identification of first- and second-class constraints; computation of local physical degrees of freedom; analysis of χ-symmetry via its action on the symplectic form and compatibility with constraints.

Result: The Carroll limit yields sector-dependent constraint algebra closure: weak closure for electric-like, strong closure for magnetic-like; generic non-BPS sector has D−1 local phase-space d.o.f., tachyon-vacuum sector has D−2 only after imposing second-class background conditions; rank condition for electric-like constraints is strengthened in the vacuum sector; Carroll–Weyl χ-transformation preserves symplectic form and admits a charge but cannot be promoted to a first-class generator except in a highly restricted global vacuum sector.

Conclusion: Carroll non-BPS Dp-branes exhibit rich constraint structure sensitive to sector choice and background conditions, with fundamental differences from null string theory in the realizability of χ-symmetry as a gauge generator—highlighting the rigidity of the Carroll limit in non-BPS settings.

Abstract: We analyze the covariant phase space of Klusoň's canonical Carroll non-BPS D\(_p\)-brane actions. The Carroll limit is formulated as a contraction of the canonical phase space: the canonical one-form is invariant under the scaling of conjugate pairs, while the leading Hamiltonian constraint differs between the electric-like and magnetic-like sectors. This difference controls the weak closure of the electric-like constraint algebra and the stronger closure of the magnetic-like Hamiltonian brackets. We separate the generic non-BPS sector, which carries \(D-1\) local phase-space degrees of freedom, from the tachyon-vacuum sector, which carries \(D-2\) only after imposing second-class background conditions. We also identify the rank condition for the generic electric-like sector and its strengthening in the vacuum sector. Finally, we show that the Carroll--Weyl \(χ\) transformation preserves the symplectic form and admits a charge, but is obstructed by the constraints and cannot be promoted to a first-class gauge generator except in a highly restricted global vacuum sector. This contrasts with null string theory, where a restricted \(χ\)-symmetry can be completed to an additional first-class constraint.

</details>


### [142] [Gravitational decoherence from soft-graviton $S$-matrix](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08584)
*Hiroki Matsui*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives a gauge-invariant, nonnegative leading-soft decoherence exponent for matter superpositions using Weinberg's soft graviton theorem and coherent radiation state overlaps; it shows vacuum decoherence vanishes as IR cutoff → 0, identifies the dominant nonrelativistic term as quadrupolar due to energy-momentum conservation, and analyzes how single- and two-mode squeezed graviton states (including inflation-motivated ones) modulate decoherence—phase averaging yields enhancement scaling with GW energy density and t⁴.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously quantify gravitational decoherence of matter superpositions using fundamental infrared (soft) structure of quantum gravity—specifically, the soft graviton sector of the S-matrix—beyond phenomenological models, and to extend analysis to cosmologically relevant nonvacuum backgrounds like squeezed states from inflation.

Method: Starts from Weinberg's soft graviton theorem and overlap of coherent radiation states; computes gauge-invariant decoherence exponent for branches sharing total four-momentum; performs exact polarization sum and angular integration at leading-soft order; extends to single- and two-mode squeezed graviton states; evaluates phase-averaged decoherence for relic backgrounds with flat energy-density spectrum and sharp IR cutoff identified as inverse observation time.

Result: Derives exact, nonnegative, gauge-invariant leading-soft decoherence exponent; confirms vacuum coherence loss only in IR limit; identifies purely quadrupolar nonrelativistic limit enforced by energy-momentum conservation; shows squeezing can enhance or suppress decoherence depending on relative phase; finds phase-averaged enhancement ∝ Ω_GW × t⁴ for flat-spectrum relic background with IR scale Λ ~ 1/t.

Conclusion: Gravitational decoherence of matter superpositions is fundamentally governed by soft-graviton dynamics encoded in the S-matrix; vacuum decoherence is IR-sensitive but controllable, while nonvacuum backgrounds—especially squeezed states—can dramatically alter decoherence rates, linking infrared quantum information, soft scattering, and observational gravitational-wave physics.

Abstract: We revisit gravitational decoherence of matter superpositions using the leading soft sector of the graviton $S$-matrix. Starting from Weinberg's soft theorem and the overlap of coherent radiation states, we derive, for branch configurations with the same total four-momentum, a gauge-invariant, nonnegative leading-soft decoherence exponent and evaluate the polarization sum and angular integrals exactly at this order. In the vacuum, coherence between branches with distinguishable soft radiation vanishes as the infrared cutoff is removed. The leading nonrelativistic term is purely quadrupolar as a consequence of energy--momentum conservation. We extend the analysis to single- and two-mode squeezed graviton states, including the two-mode case motivated by inflation. Squeezing can enhance or suppress decoherence depending on the relative phase, while phase averaging gives an enhancement governed by the graviton occupation. For a phase-averaged relic background with an approximately flat energy-density spectrum over the relevant soft frequency band, the sharp-cutoff identification of the infrared scale with the inverse observation time gives a contribution that grows with the gravitational-wave energy density and with the fourth power of the observation time. These results connect soft-graviton scattering, infrared quantum information, and decoherence in nonvacuum gravitational backgrounds.

</details>


### [143] [Symplectic Chern-Simons theories dual to Abelian rank-$0$ theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08648)
*Tadashi Okazaki,Douglas J. Smith*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Proposes a duality between 3d N=4 USp(2n) symplectic Chern-Simons theory with a fundamental chiral multiplet and an Abelian rank-0 theory T_{ℓ,n}; verifies via vacuum counting and index matching, with detailed analysis for ℓ=1.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish and test a proposed duality between a non-Abelian half-integral-level symplectic Chern-Simons theory and an Abelian rank-0 theory in 3d N=4 supersymmetry, leveraging pseudo-reality of the fundamental representation and absence of non-Abelian flavor symmetry.

Method: Uses supersymmetric localization: computes and matches real-mass-deformed vacuum counts and supersymmetric indices; analyzes the ℓ=1 case explicitly via its dual U(1)^n description with tadpole level matrix and bare monopole superpotential.

Result: Confirms duality for all (n,ℓ) via exact match of supersymmetric vacua under real mass deformations; verifies supersymmetric index agreement in multiple cases; fully characterizes the ℓ=1 dual as a U(1)^n theory with specific topological and superpotential data.

Conclusion: The proposed duality holds robustly across parameter space; the absence of Higgs/Coulomb branches and N=4 enhancement are essential structural constraints; the ℓ=1 case provides an explicit, solvable realization of the duality in terms of Abelian gauge theory with monopole superpotential.

Abstract: We propose that the 3d symplectic Chern-Simons theory $USp(2n)$ at level $k=n+\frac12+\ell$ with a single chiral multiplet in the fundamental representation is dual, for every pair of positive integers $(n,\ell)$, to the Abelian rank-$0$ theory $\mathcal{T}_{\ell,n}$ of Creutzig, Garner and Kim. The fundamental is pseudo-real, so the level is half-integral, there is no non-Abelian flavor symmetry, and the supersymmetry is enhanced to $\mathcal{N}=4$. Both sides have vanishing Higgs and Coulomb branches. We match the counting of supersymmetric vacua under real mass deformations for all $(n,\ell)$, and the supersymmetric indices in a number of cases. We then study in detail the case $\ell=1$, the minimal $USp(2n)$ Chern-Simons theory at level $n+\frac32$, whose dual is a $U(1)^{n}$ theory with a tadpole level matrix and a bare monopole superpotential.

</details>


### [144] [Mass for Particles from Extra Dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08697)
*F. E. A. de Souza,M. O. Tahim,R. I. de Oliveira Junior,I. M. Macêdo*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates how a massless particle in a codimension-two hedgehog braneworld acquires an effective 4D mass via motion in a compact angular extra dimension; spin-curvature coupling in spinning-particle models induces radial localization and level-dependent Kaluza-Klein mode spectra.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand geometric mass generation in flux-supported codimension-two braneworlds, particularly how bulk geometry and spin influence the effective 4D mass spectrum of localized fields.

Method: Canonical quantization of a massless particle in a codimension-two hedgehog background; analysis of bosonic and N = 1, 2 spinning-particle models with inclusion of spin-curvature coupling; derivation of effective potentials and Kaluza-Klein spectra.

Result: Bosonic sector shows no radial confinement; spin-curvature coupling generates level-dependent radial localization and modifies the effective potential, leading to a dynamically determined, spin-sensitive Kaluza-Klein mass spectrum.

Conclusion: Geometric mass generation in such braneworlds admits a consistent particle-mechanics description, where bulk geometry, spin, and extra-dimensional topology jointly determine the observable 4D mass spectrum.

Abstract: We investigate a geometric mechanism for mass generation in a codimension-two hedgehog braneworld. A massless particle propagating in the bulk acquires an effective four-dimensional mass through its motion along the compact angular extra dimension, yielding a dynamically determined Kaluza-Klein spectrum after canonical quantization. While the bosonic sector exhibits no radial confinement, extending the analysis to N = 1, 2 spinning-particle models shows that the spin-curvature coupling modifies the effective potential and introduces a level-dependent localization pattern for the Kaluza-Klein modes. The results establish a particle-mechanics realization of geometric mass generation in flux-supported codimension-two braneworlds and highlight the interplay between bulk geometry, spin, and the effective four-dimensional mass spectrum.

</details>


### [145] [Splitting Dynamics of Multiply Quantized Vortices in Holographic Superfluid of Finite Temperature](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08831)
*Shanquan Lan,Xin Li,Jiexiong Mo,Yu Tian,Yu-Kun Yan,Hongbao Zhang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates the splitting dynamics of high-winding-number vortices (n = 5–8) in a 2D holographic superfluid at finite temperature, revealing three novel phenomena: deviation from the 2n−3 unstable mode count, temperature-driven jump-like transitions in dominant instability modes, and multi-scale sub-splitting patterns within a single topological configuration.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the stability and dynamical evolution of multiply quantized vortices beyond low-n regimes in strongly coupled superfluids, where conventional mean-field theories may break down and holographic duality offers a non-perturbative framework.

Method: Combined linear quasinormal mode analysis (to identify unstable azimuthal modes p) with fully nonlinear real-time numerical simulations of the holographic superfluid model at finite temperature.

Result: 1) For n=8, only 2n−4 unstable modes exist—violating the 2n−3 rule due to absence of the p=2(n−1) mode; 2) Dominant unstable mode transitions shift from sequential (n≤6) to jump-like (n≥7) with temperature; 3) Single splitting channel (e.g., l=4 for n=8) hosts three thermally distinct sub-patterns with different topological structures.

Conclusion: High-winding vortices exhibit rich, non-monotonic instability landscapes not captured by extrapolations from low-n cases; holographic methods uncover universal features relevant to quantum turbulence and vortex decay in cold-atom superfluids.

Abstract: We study the splitting dynamics of multiply quantized vortices with winding numbers $n=5,6,7$ and $8$ in a two-dimensional holographic superfluid at finite temperature, by combining linear perturbation analysis of quasinormal modes with fully nonlinear real-time numerical simulations. Three new physical phenomena are revealed. First, the number of unstable modes no longer strictly follows the $2n-3$ formula as $n$ increases. For the vortex with $n=8$, the unstable mode with $p=2(n-1)$ is absent throughout the entire temperature range, so that only $2n-4$ unstable modes exist.
