Second, the transition of the dominant unstable mode with increasing temperature exhibits new characteristics. For vortices with $n\le 6$, the dominant mode changes sequentially as $p=2,3,\dots,n$, whereas for $n\ge 7$ jump-like transitions occur-for instance, for $n=7$ the dominant mode jumps from $p=2$ to $p=4$ at $T=0.325T_c$ and then directly to $p=7$ at $T=0.359T_c$, and for $n=8$ it jumps directly from $p=2$ to $p=8$ at $T=0.302T_c$. Third, a single splitting pattern of high-winding-number vortices can contain multiple sub-splitting patterns with distinct topological structures, as exemplified by the $l=4$ pattern of the $n=8$ vortex, which exhibits three sub-patterns at low, intermediate and high temperatures. The nonlinear simulations confirm the predictions of the linear stability analysis, and the implications of our results for cold-atom experiments are discussed.

</details>


### [146] [Quantum spacetime and gravity from the IKKT matrix model: an invitation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08955)
*Harold C. Steinacker*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces a framework for spacetime, gravity, and fundamental physics based on the IKKT matrix model, using noncommutative 3+1D backgrounds to realize FLRW cosmology; perturbations yield weakly-coupled gauge fields and a novel quantum gravity description with IR modifications, reliant on maximal supersymmetry for effective locality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a unified framework for spacetime, gravity, and fundamental physics from first principles using the IKKT matrix model, avoiding ad hoc geometric inputs and addressing quantum gravity in a background-independent yet phenomenologically viable way.

Method: Analyzes nontrivial vacua (matrix backgrounds) of the IKKT model as emergent 3+1-dimensional noncommutative spacetimes; focuses on homogeneous, isotropic FLRW-type backgrounds solving the one-loop effective action; studies linear perturbations to derive effective field dynamics and gravitational behavior.

Result: Identifies FLRW-like expanding backgrounds that solve the one-loop effective action with finite degrees of freedom per volume; shows perturbations yield weakly-coupled gauge theory fields and a nonstandard, UV-finite quantum gravity sector exhibiting IR modifications; confirms maximal supersymmetry is crucial for emergent locality.

Conclusion: The IKKT matrix model supports emergent classical spacetime and gravity without assuming geometry a priori; its noncommutative structure and maximal supersymmetry enable a consistent, potentially predictive quantum theory of gravity with testable low-energy deviations.

Abstract: This is a concise and compact introduction to a framework for spacetime, gravity, and fundamental physics based on the IKKT matrix model, focusing on conceptual and structural aspects. The approach rests on nontrivial vacua or matrix backgrounds, which serve as 3+1-dimensional noncommutative spacetime. We discuss a specific class of backgrounds describing expanding homogeneous and isotropic FLRW spacetime, which provide a solution of the one-loop effective action with finitely many dof per volume. Perturbations of the background give rise to fields propagating on spacetime governed by a weakly-coupled gauge theory, as well as a nonstandard description of (quantum) gravity leading to some IR modifications. Maximal supersymmetry of the model is essential to ensure effective locality.

</details>


### [147] [5d SCFT fixtures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09007)
*Michele Del Zotto,Andrea Sangiovanni*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces a new class of 5D SCFTs—'fixtures'—to complete the atomic classification of 5D SCFT building blocks with non-simply laced global symmetries, realized via Higgs branch RG flows and M-theory on explicit singular threefolds; it also constructs non-simply laced conformal matter and discusses circle reduction to class-S-like 4D theories.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The atomic classification of 5d SCFTs lacks building blocks with non-simply laced global symmetries; this gap motivates the introduction of new UV fixed points ('fixtures').

Method: Constructs 5d SCFT fixtures via Higgs branch renormalization group (RG) flows, geometrically realized as M-theory compactified on explicitly identified singular threefolds; employs circle reduction to relate to 4d class-S theories.

Result: Provides the first systematic construction of 5d SCFTs with non-simply laced flavor symmetries, including conformal matter; establishes their geometric realization in M-theory and connects them to 4d class-S via dimensional reduction.

Conclusion: The 'fixture' construction completes the landscape of elementary 5d SCFT building blocks for non-simply laced symmetries, unifying geometric, field-theoretic, and dimensional-reduction perspectives.

Abstract: In the context of the atomic classification of 5d SCFTs building blocks with non-simply laced global symmetries are lacking. In this work we fill this gap by introducing a new class of 5d UV fixed points (the 5d SCFT "fixtures"). Physically, 5d SCFTs fixtures are the result of a Higgs branch RG flow, realized geometrically by M-theory on singular threefolds which we identify explicitly. In particular, this enables the construction of 5d conformal matter SCFTs with non-simply laced flavor symmetry. Along the way, we discuss their circle reduction in relation to 4d theories of class-S type.

</details>


### [148] [M5 Instantons in ABJM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09041)
*Jieming Lin,Jesse van Muiden,Zihan Wang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates supersymmetric M5-brane instantons in AdS₄ × S⁷/ℤₖ backgrounds dual to ABJM theory; it finds that while classical embeddings are fixed by κ-symmetry to equivariant points and specific cycles, their one-loop partition function vanishes quantum mechanically—implying they do not contribute to the bulk path integral, consistent with ABJM’s S³ partition function being governed by supergravity modes and M2-instantons.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the role of M5-brane instantons in the AdS₄/CFT₃ correspondence for ABJM theory, particularly whether they contribute to the bulk partition function and how their quantum behavior aligns with known dual field-theoretic results (e.g., S³ partition function).

Method: Analysis of M5-brane κ-symmetry constraints to determine supersymmetric embeddings in asymptotically AdS₄ × S⁷/ℤₖ geometry; computation of the one-loop partition function using standard quantum effective action techniques for brane instantons in curved supergravity backgrounds.

Result: Supersymmetric M5-brane instantons are localised to equivariant fixed points in AdS₄ and wrap (S³ × S³)/ℤₖ; their one-loop partition function identically vanishes, indicating no quantum contribution to the bulk partition function.

Conclusion: M5-brane instantons do not contribute perturbatively or non-perturbatively (at one-loop) to the bulk partition function in this setup; the partition function is instead dominated by supergravity fluctuations and M2-brane instantons—matching expectations from the dual ABJM theory on S³.

Abstract: We study the existence of supersymmetric M5-brane instantons in asymptotically AdS$_4 \times S^7/\mathbf{Z}_k$ geometries of M-theory, dual to the ABJM theory. The supersymmetric embeddings of these instantons are determined by the M5-brane $κ$-symmetry, which forces them to localise to the equivariant fixed points in the asymptotically AdS$_4$ geometry, and wrap $(S^3\times S^3)/\mathbf{Z}_k $. At the quantum level we find that the one-loop partition function of these instantons vanishes, suggesting that the bulk partition function is instead to be expanded in terms of supergravity modes and M2-brane instantons, in line with known results for the $S^3$ partition function of the dual ABJM theory.

</details>


<div id='gr-qc'></div>

# gr-qc [[Back]](#toc)

### [149] [Coherent multipath wave response on Reissner-Nordström analogue surface](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05534)
*Peng-Yu Chen,Sen Guo,Qing-Quan Jiang,Yu Liang,Kai Lin*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops a theoretical framework linking the geometry of a charged Reissner-Nordström black hole to wave phenomena on an analogue curved surface, using geodesic path spectra and Huygens-Fresnel principles to unify interference, resonance, and echo structures under charge-controlled geometric deformation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To uncover how the intrinsic metric of a relativistic compact object governs macroscopic wave phenomena, and to bridge strong-field gravitational lensing with tabletop transformation optics via analogue gravity.

Method: Mapping RN black hole spatial geometry onto an analogue curved surface; solving exact spatial geodesic boundary value problem on isometrically embedded equatorial slice; extracting discrete multi-loop path-length spectrum; constructing physical wave field via finite-path surface Huygens-Fresnel principle; analytically computing arbitrary-order winding trajectories and their high-winding accumulation limits.

Result: Demonstration that analogue charge acts as a precise dial reconfiguring the event horizon throat and reorganizing the discrete path sequence; discovery that geometric deformation uniquely dictates macroscopic interference, unifying steady-state fringes, spectral resonance combs, and transient echo ladders as projections of a single charge-controlled path spectrum.

Conclusion: The proposed parameter-to-response methodology establishes a rigorous theoretical bridge between strong-field gravity and analogue experiments, offering a tunable blueprint for multi-domain analogue gravity platforms.

Abstract: To uncover how the intrinsic metric of a relativistic compact object governs macroscopic wave phenomena, we establish a theoretical framework mapping the charge dependent spatial geometry of a Reissner-Nordström (RN) black hole onto the coherent response of an analogue curved surface. By solving an exact spatial geodesic boundary value problem on an isometrically embedded equatorial slice, we extract the discrete multi-loop path-length spectrum and convert this geometric backbone into a physical wave field via a finite-path surface Huygens-Fresnel construction. We analytically compute the arbitrary order winding trajectories alongside their high winding accumulation limits, demonstrating that the analogue charge acts as a precise physical dial that reconfigures the event horizon throat and fundamentally reorganizes the discrete path sequence. Furthermore, we find that this underlying geometric deformation uniquely dictates the macroscopic interference, revealing that steady state spatial fringes, spectral resonance combs, and transient temporal echo ladders are intrinsically unified physical projections of a single charge controlled path spectrum. This systematic parameter to response methodology establishes a rigorous theoretical bridge between strong field gravitational lensing and tabletop transformation optics, providing a highly tunable blueprint for future multi domain analogue gravity experiments.

</details>


### [150] [Quantum-gravity-inspired Alcubierre warp-drive geometries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05554)
*Kimet Jusufi,Francisco S. N. Lobo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes a T-duality-inspired modification of the Alcubierre warp drive geometry, replacing the sharp warp profile with a smooth, string-motivated smearing characterized by a minimal length scale $l_0 = 2\pi\sqrt{\alpha'}$. The resulting metric is $C^2$-smooth, yields finite, closed-form expressions for energy density and total energy, and avoids the divergent energy cost of the profile-contraction limit ($R \to 0$) by enforcing $l \geq l_0$. However, it remains an effective ansatz—not a derivation from string-corrected gravity—and still requires exotic matter without proven semiclassical stability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a physically more viable Alcubierre-type warp geometry by incorporating string-theoretic short-distance regularization—specifically, the zero-point length $l_0$ from string T-duality—to eliminate the unphysical divergences in energy density and total energy that plague the original thin-wall or sharp-profile limits.

Method: The authors replace the standard Alcubierre 'top-hat' warp function $f(r)$ with a smooth, T-duality-motivated profile derived from the complementary cumulative mass fraction of a smeared gravitational source. They adopt a one-scale ansatz $f(r_s) = 1 - r_s^3/(r_s^2 + l^2)^{3/2}$, where $l^2 = R^2 + l_0^2$, ensuring $C^2$ differentiability at the bubble center and analytic tractability; they compute the energy density $\rho_E$, its volume integral (total energy $E$), and the York expansion explicitly.

Result: The constructed metric is globally $C^2$, yields closed-form expressions: $E = -(15\pi/1024)v_s^2 l$ (geometric units) and $|\rho_E| \leq C v_s^2 / l^2$; crucially, the divergence as $R \to 0$ is removed because $l \geq l_0$, bounding energy cost from below. The model does *not* recover the distributional Alcubierre limit as $l_0 \to 0$, but instead approaches a smooth thick-walled configuration.

Conclusion: The T-duality-inspired smearing provides a finite-energy, smooth warp geometry that cures the profile-contraction singularity, offering a more robust effective framework than the classical Alcubierre metric. However, it remains phenomenological—an ansatz lacking derivation from fundamental equations—and does not resolve the core issues of exotic matter requirement or semiclassical instability without further quantum stress-tensor analysis.

Abstract: String T-duality, through its correspondence with path-integral duality, endows the low-energy propagator with a zero-point length $l_0=2π\sqrt{α'}$ that softens the short-distance behavior of gravitational fields. Motivated by the regular black-hole construction obtained from the corresponding smeared source, we formulate a T-duality-inspired Alcubierre geometry by identifying the warp profile with the complementary cumulative mass fraction of that source. For the effective one-scale choice $f(r_s)=1-r_s^3/(r_s^2+l^2)^{3/2}$, with $l^2=R^2+l_0^2$, the metric is $C^2$ at the bubble center and smooth elsewhere, while the energy density, its volume integral, and the York expansion are available in closed form. In particular, $E=-(15π/1024)v_s^2 l$ in geometric units and $|ρ_E|$ is bounded by a constant times $v_s^2/l^2$. Within this one-scale family, the model therefore removes the divergence associated with the profile-contraction limit $R\to 0$ at fixed velocity, because $l$ cannot fall below $l_0$. This should not be confused with a regularization of the conventional Alcubierre thin-wall limit at fixed macroscopic bubble radius, in which an independent wall thickness is taken to zero. We emphasize, however, that the construction is an effective ansatz rather than a derivation from string-corrected field equations: $R$ is a macroscopic profile scale, the choice $l^2=R^2+l_0^2$ is an interpolation, and the classical $l_0\to 0$ limit remains a smooth thick-walled profile rather than the distributional Alcubierre top hat. Exotic matter remains necessary, and neither semiclassical stability nor a modified quantum energy inequality is claimed without an explicit renormalized stress-tensor calculation.

</details>


### [151] [The Einstein Tensor from UV-regulated Field Correlations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05558)
*Matheus H. Zambianco,Achim Kempf*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper shows that spacetime curvature tensors—including the Einstein and Ricci tensors—can be directly expressed in terms of UV-regulated quantum field correlators, and that the renormalized stress-energy tensor fits naturally into this correlation-based framework, advancing the reconstruction of semiclassical gravity from quantum field data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the programme of recovering spacetime geometry from quantum-field correlators by directly encoding gravitational curvature tensors—and ultimately the semiclassical Einstein equations—in terms of field correlations, without presupposing a classical metric background.

Method: Analyzing coincidence limits and higher-order derivatives of UV-regulated two-point (and possibly higher) quantum field correlators on curved spacetime; employing Hadamard regularization and geometric expansion techniques to isolate local curvature tensors from correlation structure.

Result: The Einstein tensor, Ricci tensor, Levi-Civita connection, and Riemann tensor are all explicitly recoverable from specific algebraic and differential operations on regulated field correlators; the renormalized stress-energy tensor is formulated within the same framework, modulo standard finite ambiguities.

Conclusion: Spacetime geometry and semiclassical gravity can be organized entirely around quantum field correlations, supporting a deeper operational and background-independent understanding of gravity in quantum field theory.

Abstract: It is known that the spacetime metric can be recovered from quantum-field correlators. We extend this programme by showing that the Einstein tensor can be directly written in terms of UV-regulated field correlations. The same correlation structure also directly determines the Ricci tensor, while higher coincidence derivatives recover the connection and Riemann tensor. We further show that the renormalized stress-energy tensor can be formulated within this framework, up to the standard finite renormalization freedom. These results provide a correlation-based organization of the semiclassical Einstein equations and advance the programme of formulating spacetime geometry in terms of quantum field correlations.

</details>


### [152] [End state of the experimental black-hole bomb](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05595)
*Patrik Švančara,Leonardo Solidoro,Pietro Smaniotto,Sam Patrick,Silvia Schiattarella,Maciej T. Jarema,Sean M. D. Gregory,Vitor S. Barroso,Maurício Richartz,Anastasios Avgoustidis,Carlo F. Barenghi,Ruth Gregory,Silke Weinfurtner*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper experimentally realizes the black-hole bomb instability in a superfluid helium gravity simulator, observing exponential growth of a resonant mode followed by nonlinear saturation into a long-lived non-equilibrium steady state mediated by wave mixing.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe the poorly understood nonlinear evolution of the black-hole bomb instability—a key mechanism for bosonic cloud formation around astrophysical black holes—using an accessible laboratory analog.

Method: A draining vortex in superfluid helium serves as a gravity simulator for a rotating black hole; surface waves on the superfluid interface experience effective superradiance within a cylindrical cavity; temperature and flow parameters are tuned to trigger and control the instability; high-resolution spatiotemporal measurements capture linear growth and subsequent nonlinear dynamics including frequency shifts, harmonic generation, and three-/four-wave mixing.

Result: Exponential growth of a low-frequency resonant mode is observed, followed by arrest of the instability and emergence of a long-lived non-equilibrium steady state sustained by coherent nonlinear wave interactions (e.g., three- and four-wave mixing).

Conclusion: Nonlinear wave interactions—not present in linear theory—quench runaway superradiant growth and govern late-time dynamics of the black-hole bomb, establishing superfluid helium vortices as a valid laboratory platform for studying nonlinear strong-field gravitational phenomena.

Abstract: Rotating black holes can amplify incident waves through superradiant scattering. When these waves are confined, repeated amplification gives rise to the black-hole bomb instability, whose nonlinear evolution remains poorly understood despite its central role in models of bosonic clouds around astrophysical black holes. Here, we reproduce the black-hole bomb mechanism in a laboratory setting using a gravity simulator based on a draining vortex in superfluid helium. Surface waves propagating on the superfluid interface experience an effective rotating spacetime and undergo repeated superradiant amplification within a cylindrical cavity. By tuning the temperature and flow parameters, we achieve exponential growth of a low-frequency resonant mode, followed by the arrest of the instability and the formation of a long-lived non-equilibrium steady state. Using spatially and temporally resolved measurements, we identify nonlinear frequency shifts, harmonic generation, and coherent three- and four-wave mixing that redistribute energy among interacting modes. This novel end state of the experimental black-hole bomb highlights the role of nonlinear wave interactions in quenching the runaway growth expected from linear theory and governing the system's late-time dynamics. Our results establish a laboratory framework for investigating the nonlinear evolution of black-hole bombs, with implications for analogous phenomena involving ultralight bosonic fields around rotating black holes.

</details>


### [153] [A compact time-frequency representation for gravitational-wave data analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05601)
*Noah Pearson,Mesut Çalışkan,Sophie Bini,Neil J. Cornish*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes a new Gaussian-based window function for the Wilson-Daubechies-Meyer (WDM) wavelet transform, designed to be more time-frequency symmetric and compact than the standard WDM window; it analytically and numerically demonstrates near-optimal time-frequency localization—within ~2.4% of the Heisenberg-Gabor uncertainty limit—and conjectures it minimizes time-frequency area among WD wavelet windows.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve time-frequency localization in gravitational-wave data analysis by addressing the temporal spread of the standard Wilson-Daubechies-Meyer (WDM) wavelet window, which is compact in frequency but overly broad in time.

Method: Designs a novel window function composed of phase-shifted Gaussians; derives and computes its time-frequency variance product analytically and verifies numerically; compares symmetry and compactness against the WDM window; evaluates proximity to the Heisenberg-Gabor uncertainty limit.

Result: The proposed Gaussian window achieves near-minimal time-frequency uncertainty—within ~2.4% of the theoretical Heisenberg-Gabor limit for the symmetric case—and exhibits superior time-frequency compactness and symmetry compared to the standard WDM window.

Conclusion: The Gaussian window offers a theoretically and practically improved alternative for WD wavelet transforms in gravitational-wave analysis; its near-uncertainty-limited performance supports the conjecture that it minimizes time-frequency area among all WD wavelet windows.

Abstract: Time-frequency (wavelet) domain analyses are seeing greater use for gravitational-wave data analysis due to the advantages they have in handling non-stationary noise. A popular choice is the Wilson-Daubechies-Meyer (WDM) wavelet transform, which uses a window function that is very compact in frequency, but more spread out in time. In this work, we consider an alternative window function that is built from a sum of phase-shifted Gaussians. This "Gaussian" window is more symmetric in time-frequency, and consequently more compact. We examine the properties of this window function and the implications it has for gravitational-wave analyses. We calculate the window's time-frequency variance product analytically and verify it numerically. For the symmetric case, where the window has the same form in time and frequency, the construction comes within $\sim2.4\%$ of saturating the Heisenberg-Gabor uncertainty limit. We conjecture that this Gaussian window achieves the minimum time-frequency area of any WD wavelet window.

</details>


### [154] [Effective Field Theory of Gravity in Relativistic Media](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05639)
*Beka Modrekiladze*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops an effective field theory for gravity in relativistic media, showing how environmental stress-energy correlators modify vacuum gravity's Feynman rules—yielding new physical effects like running Love numbers, graviton decay, gravitational opacity, and flow-sensitive memory—while preserving diagram topology across media types.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically incorporate the influence of relativistic media (e.g., fluids, wave dark matter) on gravitational dynamics beyond standard vacuum GR, enabling consistent treatment of medium-induced corrections to gravitational wave propagation, tidal response, and memory effects.

Method: Constructs a Wilsonian effective field theory by integrating out the medium degrees of freedom; computes matched Wilson coefficients encoding medium-induced modifications to the graviton propagator, bulk vertices, and worldline couplings; applies the formalism to relativistic fluids to derive 1PN potentials, self-energies, and memory; analyzes dispersion relations and decay channels using in-medium correlators.

Result: Derives full 1PN Einstein-Infeld-Hoffmann potential and Stokes drag; obtains generalized Christodoulou memory with flow-direction-dependent tensor structure ('astrophysical weathervane'); shows sound pole induces resonant, running black-hole Love numbers; discovers allowed h→hh graviton decay in moving media with closed-form rate; identifies birefringent gravitational opacity governed by local flow geometry; estimates detectability with Einstein Telescope and LISA.

Conclusion: Gravity in relativistic media is not a new theory but vacuum GR dressed by medium correlators; this unified framework predicts novel, observable phenomena—running Love numbers, gravitational opacity, flow-sensitive memory—that probe astrophysical environments via gravitational waves.

Abstract: We develop an effective field theory of gravity in relativistic media. Integrating out the medium leaves vacuum gravity with updated Feynman rules: a graviton propagator dressed by the stress-energy two-point function of the environment, medium-induced bulk graviton vertices from higher correlators, and generalized worldline couplings encoded in matched Wilson coefficients. The diagram topologies are unchanged from vacuum, so different media (perfect fluids, collisionless matter, coherent scalar fields such as wave dark matter) are not different theories but different correlators inserted into the same diagrams. We derive the in-medium rules for a relativistic fluid and obtain the full 1PN Einstein-Infeld-Hoffmann potential, the 1PN Stokes drag, the gravitational self-energy and a generalized Christodoulou memory whose new tensor structure records the direction of the surrounding flow, turning the permanent strain into an $\textit{astrophysical weathervane}$. The same rules activate phenomena forbidden in vacuum: the sound pole converts the symmetry-protected, non-running black-hole Love number into a resonant, running one, and opens the channel $h\to hh$ in a moving medium, yielding a closed-form decay rate and a birefringent $\textit{gravitational opacity}$ set by the local flow geometry. We assess observational prospects, from dephasing and tidal resonances within reach of the Einstein Telescope and LISA to proof-of-principle memory and opacity signatures.

</details>


### [155] [Fermionic Casimir memory from an anisotropic gravitational pulse](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05666)
*Celio R. Muniz,Herondy F. Santana Mota*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies how a weak, anisotropic gravitational pulse deposits energy in a massless Dirac field confined between two parallel plates with MIT bag boundary conditions; it finds a finite, boundary-dependent fermionic memory effect—distinct from bosonic vacuum-polarization memory—arising from gravitationally produced fermion–antifermion pairs, leading to a nonmonotonic, sign-changing correction to the Casimir pressure.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand dissipative quantum memory effects in fermionic fields under gravitational perturbations, specifically how boundaries modify late-time energy deposition and stress tensor responses beyond vacuum polarization.

Method: Tetrad formalism with linear spin-connection analysis; Bogoliubov transformation technique to compute pair production; subtraction of unbounded-space contribution to isolate boundary-dependent energy deposition; asymptotic analysis in adiabatic and impulsive limits.

Result: A finite, negative (adiabatic) to vanishing positive (impulsive) boundary-dependent energy deposition; nonmonotonic pressure correction with two sign changes relative to the Casimir force; emergence of a dissipative fermionic memory encoded in real particle pairs.

Conclusion: Gravitational pulses induce a genuine dissipative fermionic memory in confined Dirac fields—distinct from conservative bosonic memory—manifested as boundary-modified pair production and nontrivial Casimir pressure corrections.

Abstract: We investigate the late-time energy deposited by a weak, spatially homogeneous anisotropic gravitational pulse in a massless Dirac field confined between two static parallel plates. The field satisfies MIT bag boundary conditions, while the prescribed geometry approaches Minkowski spacetime in both asymptotic time regions. Using a tetrad formulation, we show that the linear spin-connection contribution cancels for the traceless anisotropy, leaving a coupling to the shear component of the Dirac stress tensor. The pulse produces real fermion--antifermion pairs, whose deposited energy is evaluated through the corresponding Bogoliubov coefficients. Subtracting the production in unbounded space isolates a finite boundary-dependent contribution governed by the pulse duration relative to the characteristic spectral timescale set by the MIT gap. This contribution is negative in the adiabatic regime, where confinement suppresses excitations relative to the continuous free spectrum, and approaches zero from above in the impulsive limit. The resulting pressure correction is nonmonotonic and exhibits two sign changes, alternately reinforcing and reducing the ordinary attractive Casimir force. Unlike the bosonic vacuum-polarization memory previously obtained for the same class of backgrounds, the present effect is a dissipative fermionic memory carried by gravitationally created pairs. Here, memory denotes the persistent late-time modification of the quantum state after the pulse has vanished.

</details>


### [156] [Self-supervised reconstruction of transients in data from space-borne gravitational-wave detectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05890)
*Yuxiang Xu,Minghui Du,Bo Liang,Zihao Xiao,Tianyu Zhao,Peng Xu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a self-supervised Noise2Noise-inspired deep learning framework for extracting gravitational-wave transients with unknown morphologies from space-based data, without requiring clean training targets or waveform-specific templates; it demonstrates high-fidelity recovery of MBHB signals, instrumental glitches, and cosmic string bursts, and enables template-free subtraction of anomalies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing DNN-based GW transient extraction methods require clean training targets and waveform-class-specific templates, limiting applicability to previously uncharacterized or morphologically ambiguous transients (e.g., novel astrophysical sources or unidentified instrumental glitches).

Method: A self-supervised Noise2Noise (N2N)-inspired framework is developed, trained solely on noisy LISA-like observations of massive black-hole binaries (MBHBs) without clean targets or transient-specific templates; inference is performed on source-confused test data containing glitches and cosmic string bursts; extracted waveforms are used for conditional, template-free subtraction in simulated continuous streams.

Result: The single N2N model achieves high-overlap recovery of MBHB waveforms, successfully captures dominant morphologies of instrumental glitches and cosmic string bursts in source-confused data, and—when guided by auxiliary anomaly identification—suppresses injected-glitch power significantly in glitch-dominated frequency bands via template-free subtraction.

Conclusion: Self-supervised waveform extraction enables robust initial estimation of transients with unknown morphology, facilitating downstream characterization and template-free mitigation of instrumental anomalies in space-based GW data.

Abstract: Space-based gravitational-wave (GW) data may contain transient signals whose waveform morphologies are not known in advance. Extracting these signals is important for characterizing new sources and mitigating instrumental anomalies. However, existing deep neural network (DNN)-based extraction approaches rely on clean training targets and waveform-class-specific examples, which may limit their applicability when the transient morphology is not specified in advance. This work develops a Noise2Noise (N2N)-inspired self-supervised framework that learns from noisy observations without clean training targets and requires no transient-specific waveform templates at inference. A single model trained on noisy massive black-hole binary (MBHB) observations provides high-overlap MBHB recovery and recovers the dominant morphologies of instrumental glitches and other GW transient signals such as cosmic string bursts in source-confused test data. Beyond waveform recovery, when independent information identifies a candidate transient as instrumental, its extracted waveform can be subtracted from the data without an anomaly-specific template. In a simulated continuous data stream, this procedure substantially suppresses the injected-anomaly power within the glitch-dominated frequency band. These results support the use of self-supervised extraction for initial waveform estimation of candidate transients with unknown morphologies, enabling subsequent characterization and, where appropriate, conditional subtraction of instrumental anomalies.

</details>


### [157] [Thin accretion disk and gravitational capture cross sections of a quantum Oppenheimer--Snyder black hole immersed in an external magnetic field](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05906)
*Anuar Idrissov,Hernando Quevedo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates how quantum gravity effects (via a quantum parameter in an Oppenheimer–Snyder black hole geometry) and an external magnetic field jointly influence accretion disk structure and particle capture in a static, asymptotically uniform magnetic field. It computes key observables—including ISCO, radiative flux, spectral luminosity, efficiency, and capture cross sections—for massless, massive, and charged particles—finding that quantum corrections weakly modify disk emission, while magnetic coupling strongly enhances efficiency and spectral peak frequency; crucially, shadow observables depend only on the quantum parameter, not the magnetic field.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe quantum-gravitational signatures in astrophysical black hole environments by modeling accretion disks and particle capture around a quantum-corrected Oppenheimer–Snyder black hole immersed in a Wald-type uniform magnetic field—specifically assessing separability of quantum vs. electromagnetic effects on observables such as spectra and shadows.

Method: Analytic and semi-analytic computation in a static, spherically symmetric quantum black hole spacetime with one quantum parameter spanning two-horizon, extremal, and horizonless regimes; uses Wald’s formalism for magnetic field embedding; derives geodesics and charged-particle orbits; computes Novikov–Thorne disk structure (flux, temperature, redshift, luminosity, efficiency); evaluates capture cross sections for photons, massive neutral particles, and charged particles; validates against Schwarzschild limit and bolometric identity.

Result: Quantum parameter weakly suppresses disk observables (e.g., flux, efficiency) and shrinks all capture cross sections; magnetic coupling strongly increases radiative efficiency, peak flux, and spectral peak frequency, while leaving photon capture cross section invariant; charged-particle capture is halted beyond a magnetic shielding radius; shadow size depends solely on quantum parameter.

Conclusion: In this test-field model, black hole shadows are clean probes of quantum geometry, decoupled from magnetic field strength; conversely, spectral and efficiency observables are highly sensitive to magnetic coupling—enabling joint constraints on quantum parameters and magnetization from multi-messenger accretion data.

Abstract: We study a geometrically thin, optically thick Novikov--Thorne accretion disk and the gravitational capture cross sections of a quantum Oppenheimer--Snyder black hole immersed in an external, asymptotically uniform magnetic field. The exterior geometry carries a single quantum parameter, and we work over its entire admissible range, which contains a two-horizon black hole, an extremal configuration and a horizonless compact object, and which ends where the photon sphere disappears. Since the spacetime is static, the Wald potential is purely axial, so the field acts on the disk only through the Lorentz force on weakly charged accreting matter and all of its effects are controlled by a single magnetic coupling. We compute the charged circular orbits, the innermost stable circular orbit, the radiative flux, the effective temperature, the redshift factor, the differential and spectral luminosity and the radiative efficiency, together with the marginally bound orbit and the capture cross sections of massless, massive and charged particles. The construction is checked against the exact Schwarzschild limit and against the identity that relates the bolometric luminosity to the efficiency. The quantum parameter changes the disk observables only weakly, whereas the magnetic coupling raises the efficiency, the peak flux and the height of the spectral peak by large factors and shifts that peak to higher frequency. The two parameters act with opposite signs on absorption. The quantum parameter shrinks every capture cross section, while the magnetic coupling enlarges the one for massive particles and leaves the photon cross section unchanged, so that within this test-field model shadow observables respond to the quantum parameter alone. For charged particles the uniform field confines the motion, and no particle reaches the hole from beyond a magnetic shielding radius.

</details>


### [158] [Slow rotation of a localized Kalb-Ramond wormhole: Wald charges and fractional quadrupolar hair](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05921)
*Sardor Murodov*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs a slow-rotation wormhole solution up to second order in angular velocity, supported by a phantom scalar, localized radial strings, and a nonminimally coupled Kalb-Ramond 2-form; it finds vanishing ADM mass, finite angular momentum, oblate throat geometry, and—most notably—a fractional-power metric tail from Kalb-Ramond coupling that breaks smooth multipolar asymptotics while preserving well-defined global charges.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how nonminimal couplings (especially of the Kalb-Ramond field to curvature) affect rotational structure and asymptotic properties of exotic compact objects, testing the robustness of standard multipole formalisms and charge definitions in beyond-GR settings.

Method: Covariant asymptotic analysis and perturbative construction of a slow-rotation (spin-1 and spin-2) expansion for a traversable wormhole spacetime, incorporating phantom scalar, string-localized radial sector, and nonminimally coupled Kalb-Ramond two-form; employs Iyer-Wald formalism for conserved charges and Geroch-Hansen/ACMC quadrupole definitions to assess asymptotic differentiability.

Result: Vanishing ADM mass at all computed orders; finite first-order axial angular momentum agreeing between covariant and metric definitions; second-order throat area increase and oblateness; emergence of a long-range, noninteger-decaying (fractional-power) metric tail in the polar quadrupole sector due to Kalb-Ramond–background coupling; consequent failure of standard smooth multipole constructions (Geroch-Hansen, ACMC) at spatial infinity despite well-defined global mass and angular momentum.

Conclusion: Fractional Kalb-Ramond hair induces anomalous quadrupolar asymptotics—breaking smooth conformal completion and standard multipole definitions—thereby decoupling charge-level asymptotic flatness from smooth multipolar structure, and identifying such hair as the leading rotational signature of the solution.

Abstract: We construct an action-consistent slow-rotation branch, through second order, for a traversable wormhole supported by a phantom scalar, a scalar-localized radial string sector, and a Kalb-Ramond two-form coupled nonminimally to both the Ricci scalar and Ricci tensor. For the regular benchmark branch, covariant asymptotic analysis shows a vanishing ADM mass, while the first-order axial mode carries finite Iyer-Wald angular momentum. The nonminimal contributions to the angular charge decay at infinity, so the covariant and metric definitions of angular momentum agree. At second order, no nonzero mass correction is resolved; at fixed asymptotic scalar charge, rotation increases the throat area and makes the throat oblate. The most distinctive result arises in the quadrupolar sector. A long-range polar Kalb-Ramond response couples to the localized background and generates a matter-supported metric tail that decays more slowly than the ordinary vacuum quadrupole. The same noninteger hierarchy appears in curvature and reduces the differentiability of the conformal completion at spatial infinity. Consequently, the Geroch-Hansen and asymptotically Cartesian mass-centered quadrupole constructions are not applicable in their standard smooth form to this branch, even though its global mass and angular-momentum charges remain well defined. This separates charge-level asymptotic flatness from smooth multipolar asymptotics and identifies fractional Kalb-Ramond hair as the leading rotational quadrupolar signature of the benchmark solution.

</details>


### [159] [Physics-based phenomenological modeling of binary black hole hierarchical formation 1: Synthetic universes from globular cluster simulations for GWTC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05996)
*R. O'Shaughnessy,R. Mechum,M. Qazalbash,Z. Rosenberg,M. Zeeshan*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper models the GWTC-5.0 binary black hole population using three astrophysical components—globular cluster (GC), field (isolated binary), and an intermediate-mass isotropic component—to jointly explain mass, spin, and merger rate observations; it yields physically normalized local rates and makes testable, mass-resolved predictions on spectral breaks, mass ratio features, and spin distribution.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve residual tensions in the observed binary black hole mass and spin distributions—particularly at 15–30 M⊙ and in effective-spin dispersion—by jointly modeling contributions from globular clusters, isolated field binaries, and a hypothesized reprocessed intermediate-mass component.

Method: Hierarchical Bayesian population modeling of GWTC-5.0 data, incorporating: (1) physically normalized GC simulations (varying cluster mass, metallicity, redshift, compactness, natal spin); (2) phenomenological field channel with aligned, low-mass binaries; and (3) an isotropic intermediate-mass component to address 15–30 M⊙ tension. Physical normalization anchors high-mass event rates to astrophysical priors.

Result: The best-fit model yields local merger rates R_cl ≈ 9.1 and R_field ≈ 16.0 Gpc⁻³ yr⁻¹, GC contribution fraction f̂_GC ≈ 0.39%, and predicts correlated features: mass-spectrum breaks near 35 and 70 M⊙, a q ≈ 0.5 enhancement from first+second-generation pairings, and a symmetric but sharply broadening effective-spin distribution above 45 M⊙.

Conclusion: A multi-channel astrophysical model—combining GC, field, and reprocessed components—provides a self-consistent, physically grounded explanation for current GWTC-5.0 census features; its mass-resolved predictions are falsifiable with upcoming gravitational-wave detections.

Abstract: We iteratively model the GWTC-5.0 binary-black-hole census with three components. First, a physically normalized population from Rapster globular-cluster simulations spans cluster mass, metallicity, formation redshift, compactness, and natal black-hole spin. Hierarchical mergers reproduce higher-mass events and their larger effective-spin dispersion, provided black holes are born with zero natal spin. Second, a phenomenological field (isolated-binary) channel supplies the low-mass, preferentially aligned population. Its inclusion flattens the cluster compactness likelihood, permitting ordinary dense globular-cluster birth radii without requiring nuclear-cluster-like conditions. Finally, residual tension at $15$--$30 M_\odot$ motivates an intermediate-mass isotropic component representing field remnants reprocessed in clusters. This is a hypothesis, not a detection: current numerical support precludes a reliable evidence comparison. Physical normalization converts the high-mass rate into $\hat{f}_{\rm GC}\simeq0.39\%$, with local rates $R_{\rm cl}\simeq9.1$ and $R_{\rm field}\simeq16.0$ Gpc$^{-3}$ yr$^{-1}$. The model predicts linked mass-spectrum breaks near $35$ and $70 M_\odot$, a $q\simeq0.5$ feature from first-plus-second-generation pairings, and a symmetric effective-spin distribution that broadens sharply above $45 M_\odot$. These correlated, mass-resolved predictions can be tested as the gravitational-wave census grows.

</details>


### [160] [A principal-symbol obstruction in metric-affine Gauss--Bonnet gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06084)
*Hamed Bouzari Nezhad*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper compares two affine continuations of the 4D Gauss-Bonnet term—metric-contracted and double-epsilon—in metric-affine gravity with scalar coupling f(φ), analyzing their principal symbols for the independent connection. The metric-contracted version yields a nonvanishing, indefinite, rank-18 principal form (on torsion-free subspace), obstructing algebraic elimination of the connection; the double-epsilon version yields identically vanishing principal symbol, removing that obstruction—but not guaranteeing eliminability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether different affine continuations of the 4D Riemannian Gauss-Bonnet term yield the same leading differential structure for the independent affine connection in metric-affine gravity, especially near the Levi-Civita limit.

Method: Linearized analysis of the connection field equations about the Levi-Civita locus in a metric-affine scalar-Gauss-Bonnet model; computation and comparison of quadratic two-derivative principal forms (principal symbols) for both continuations, evaluated on torsion-free subspaces and at timelike principal covectors.

Result: The metric-contracted continuation yields a non-zero, indefinite principal form of rank 18 (nullity 6) on a 24D torsion-free subspace — implying nonzero principal symbol and obstruction to algebraic local elimination of the connection where f(φ) ≠ 0. The double-epsilon continuation yields identically vanishing principal form and symbol — eliminating that obstruction, though algebraic eliminability is not proven.

Conclusion: The leading differential structure (i.e., hyperbolicity, constraint structure, and algebraic eliminability potential) of the independent connection is not unique but depends critically on the choice of affine continuation of the Gauss-Bonnet term.

Abstract: The four-dimensional Riemannian Gauss--Bonnet combination admits more than one continuation to a geometry with an independent affine connection. Two natural candidates, the metric-contracted representative and the double-epsilon representative, agree in their Levi-Civita limit, and we ask whether they also give the independent connection the same leading differential structure. In a metric-affine model with a scalar coupling $f(φ)$ to the Gauss--Bonnet term, we compare their quadratic two-derivative connection principal forms about the Levi-Civita locus. For the metric-contracted continuation, the principal form, evaluated on a twenty-four-dimensional hook-free torsion-free witness subspace at a timelike principal covector, has rank $18$ and nullity $6$ and is indefinite; the full torsion-free form at the same covector is therefore indefinite with rank at least $18$. Locally where $f(φ)\neq0$, the two-derivative principal symbol of the linearized connection equations is nonzero, which obstructs purely algebraic local elimination of the connection in the activated directions. For the double-epsilon continuation, the corresponding quadratic two-derivative form vanishes identically for an arbitrary perturbation of the connection about that locus, so its principal symbol vanishes with it, and that particular obstruction is absent, though its absence does not by itself establish algebraic eliminability. Thus the leading differential structure of the independent connection depends on the affine continuation of the Riemannian Gauss--Bonnet combination.

</details>


### [161] [Regular black holes from dynamical-tension string hedgehogs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06097)
*Sebastian Bahamonde,Eduardo I. Guendelman*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes a modified-measure string theory framework to construct regular, asymptotically flat black holes from radial string clouds, overcoming conical singularities by engineering the radial tension profile—requiring T(r) ∼ r² near the origin (yielding a de Sitter core) and screening ∼ r⁻² at infinity; an exact cubic tension profile yields geometrically regular black holes with first deviation from Schwarzschild at O(r⁻⁴).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the conical singularity inherent in spherical clouds of constant-tension radial strings—which yield r⁻² energy density and non-asymptotically-flat, singular geometries—by constructing regular, asymptotically flat black hole solutions within a consistent string-theoretic framework.

Method: Employs the modified-measure formulation of string theory, introducing a composite bulk tension scalar that generates a continuum of radial worldsheets; imposes an SO(3)-hedgehog constraint to reduce to Einstein gravity coupled to a nonlinear kinetic term K(Y) and an auxiliary three-form; derives model-independent conditions on the radial tension T(r) from curvature finiteness, energy conditions (NEC, DEC), and ADM mass requirements.

Result: Derives necessary and sufficient conditions on T(r): finite central curvature ⇒ T(r) = O(r²); NEC ⇒ no faster leading power ⇒ T(r) ∼ αr² (α > 0) ⇒ de Sitter core; finite ADM mass ⇒ T(r) must be screened as r → ∞, but DEC cannot hold globally if screening is faster than r⁻²; constructs an exact cubic tension profile T(r) = αr²(1 + βr)⁻¹ satisfying all conditions and yielding asymptotically flat, geometrically regular black holes with metric deviations from Schwarzschild beginning at O(r⁻⁴).

Conclusion: Geometrically regular, asymptotically flat black holes from radial string clouds are possible only in modified-measure string theory with carefully tuned, screened tension profiles; the minimal physically consistent central behavior is a de Sitter core, and the cubic profile provides an explicit, exact solution family interpolating between Schwarzschild at large r and de Sitter at small r.

Abstract: A spherical cloud of radial strings with non-zero constant tension has an energy density proportional to $r^{-2}$ and therefore produces a singular conical geometry rather than an asymptotically flat regular black hole. We show that this obstruction can be removed within the modified-measure formulation of strings. A continuum of radial worldsheets whose tensions are generated by a composite bulk tension scalar reduces, on a constrained $\mathrm{SO}(3)$ hedgehog branch, to Einstein gravity coupled to a nonlinear function $K(Y)$ and an auxiliary three-form. This construction gives model-independent conditions directly in terms of the radial tension $\mathcal{T}(r)$. Finite curvature at the centre requires $\mathcal{T}=\mathcal{O}(r^2)$, while the null energy condition forbids a faster leading power. Thus a positive regular centre satisfying the null energy condition is necessarily de Sitter. Finite ADM mass requires the tension to be screened at infinity, whereas the dominant energy condition cannot hold globally for a non-trivial positive profile screened faster than $r^{-2}$. An exact cubic profile realises these properties and gives a continuous family of asymptotically flat geometrically regular black holes whose first correction to Schwarzschild appears at order $r^{-4}$.

</details>


### [162] [Heat distribution of quantum fields interacting with Unruh-DeWitt detectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06185)
*Marcos L. W. Basso,Alberto Saa*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops a relativistically causal, operational framework for heat-exchange statistics between an Unruh-DeWitt detector and a quantum field in Minkowski spacetime, using interferometric protocols to define the heat characteristic function without projective measurements; it derives perturbative and exact (in gapless/delta-switching limits) expressions showing Poissonian heat statistics (unidirectional in vacuum, bidirectional for KMS states), recovers fluctuation relations and the entropic Landauer inequality from algebraic entropy-balance theorems, and formulates consistent first/second laws including switching work.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a relativistically consistent, measurement-free operational definition of heat exchange in quantum field theory—beyond standard non-relativistic open quantum systems—addressing causality, detector-field backreaction, and emergence of thermodynamic laws and fluctuation theorems from QFT structure.

Method: Interferometric protocol to define heat characteristic function without projective measurements; perturbative Dyson-series expansion of detector-field interaction; exact analysis in gapless and delta-switching limits; application of Jakšić–Pillet algebraic entropy-balance theorem; derivation of first/second laws via explicitly time-switched interaction Hamiltonian.

Result: Heat statistics is unidirectional Poissonian in vacuum and bidirectional Poissonian (to second order) for KMS states; exact fluctuation theorem emerges in gapless/delta-switching regimes governed by KMS condition; entropic Landauer inequality is verified perturbatively and compared with a complementary bound from full counting statistics; first/second laws are formulated with explicit switching work reconciling energy balance.

Conclusion: A fully relativistic, operational, and causally consistent framework for heat statistics in QFT is established, clarifying how thermodynamic laws—including fluctuation relations and Landauer bounds—emerge from fundamental QFT structures (e.g., KMS condition, algebraic entropy balance) and detector dynamics.

Abstract: We introduce an operational framework for heat-exchange statistics between a quantum field and an Unruh-DeWitt detector in Minkowski spacetime. Using an interferometric protocol, we define the characteristic function of heat without projective measurements, consistently with relativistic causality. We derive perturbative expressions for this function and the heat distribution. In the vacuum, the statistics coincides with the low-intensity expansion of a unidirectional Poisson law, while for Kubo-Martin-Schwinger (KMS) states it has a bidirectional Poisson structure up to second order in perturbation theory. We study how fluctuation relations emerge from detector properties and field correlations, identifying regimes where they reduce to known heat-exchange fluctuation relations. We derive the entropic Landauer inequality as a specialization of the algebraic entropy-balance theorem of Jakšić and Pillet and verify it numerically within the perturbative detector-field interaction. We compare its tightness with a complementary Landauer bound derived from the full counting statistics of heat, analyzing their dependence on the detector's initial state and interaction time. We also formulate the first and second laws for the explicitly switched detector-field dynamics, identifying the external switching work that reconciles the field-energy gain with the detector-energy change. In the gapless and delta-switching regimes, where the dynamics admits a non-perturbative treatment, the characteristic function coincides with that of a bidirectional Poisson process, yielding an exact fluctuation theorem determined by the KMS condition. These results provide a consistent framework for heat statistics in relativistic quantum field theory and clarify the emergence of fluctuation relations.

</details>


### [163] [Alternative neural-network follow-up for all-sky FrequencyHough in continuous gravitational-wave searches](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06211)
*Martina Di Cesare,Pia Astone,Rosario De Rosa,David Keitel,Cristiano Palomba,Marco Serra*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a neural network-enhanced all-sky search for continuous gravitational waves from isolated neutron stars, improving sensitivity and false-alarm rejection over the FrequencyHough pipeline using O3 LIGO/Virgo data in [129, 229] Hz without added computational cost.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The vast majority of Milky Way neutron stars (~10^8–10^9) remain undetected electromagnetically; continuous gravitational wave (CW) all-sky searches offer a pathway to discover these silent sources, but current pipelines suffer from limited sensitivity and high false-alarm rates.

Method: A neural network classifier is integrated post-processing into the FrequencyHough all-sky CW search pipeline; trained on statistically realistic simulated noise mimicking LIGO/Virgo O3 data, and tested on real O3 data; applied to frequency [129, 229] Hz and spin-down [-2.5e-9, 1.0e-9] Hz/s; scalable across multiple detectors via parallelization.

Result: The NN achieves robust signal-noise discrimination at low strain amplitudes; successfully identifies hardware injections even outside the training spin-down range; improves detection sensitivity without increasing computational cost; reduces false alarms.

Conclusion: Neural networks can effectively augment traditional CW search pipelines, enabling more sensitive, scalable, and computationally efficient all-sky searches for electromagnetically invisible neutron stars—paving the way for population-level CW astrophysics.

Abstract: Continuous gravitational waves are long-lived signals emitted by spinning neutron stars (NSs) or boson clouds around black holes. While the Milky Way is expected to host $\mathrm{O}(10^{8}-10^{9})$ NSs, only $\mathrm{O}(10^{3})$ are currently identified through electromagnetic observations. This large observational gap motivates searches based on alternative messengers. In particular, all-sky searches for continuous gravitational waves (GWs) offer a unique opportunity to detect NSs that are electromagnetically silent or otherwise undiscovered. By exploring a broad region of parameter space without any a priori source information, these searches can probe the vast hidden NS population of our Galaxy.
  In this work, we present a novel way to include a Neural Network (NN) classifier into an all-sky search strategy for isolated NSs. Starting from candidates produced by the FrequencyHough pipeline running on data from ground-based detectors such as LIGO and Virgo, the proposed method improves sensitivity without increasing computational cost and can be parallelized across multiple detectors to enhance detection probability and reduce false alarms.
  We analyze data from the third observing run (O3) --- April 1, 2019 to March 27, 2020 --- and focus on the frequency range [129, 229] Hz and spin-down interval [$-2.5\cdot10^{-9}, 1.0\cdot10^{-9}$] Hz/s. The model is trained on noise constructed to be statistically consistent with real O3 data and tested on real O3 data. Results show robust performance in distinguishing signal from noise, even at low strain, and successful identification of hardware injections outside the training spin-down range.

</details>


### [164] [Distinguishing lensing and precessional modulation in binary black-hole inspiral waveforms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06347)
*Tien N. Nguyen-Vo,Tamanjyot Singh,Benjamin McKallip,Michael Kesden,Lindsay King*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates whether gravitational-wave (GW) modulations from binary black hole (BBH) spin precession can be confused with those from strong gravitational lensing, finding that precessional modulation can mimic two-image lensing when time delays are short (<3 interference fringes in detector band); thus, disentangling precession is critical for robust lensing identification.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Strong lensing of GWs from BBHs is rare but astrophysically valuable; however, spin-induced precessional modulation may produce similar frequency-domain interference patterns, risking false lensing claims. Distinguishing them is essential for reliable lensing detection.

Method: Analytical and phenomenological modeling of GW phase modulations from both strong lensing (two-image interference with fixed time delay) and spin-orbit precession (L-precession about J causing amplitude/phase modulations), followed by comparative analysis of their observable signatures in a single L-shaped interferometer's sensitivity band.

Result: Precessional modulation can closely mimic the interference pattern of two-lensed images when the lensing time delay yields fewer than three fringes across the detector’s frequency band; confusion is most likely for moderate-redshift sources where lensing is rare but misaligned spins are common.

Conclusion: Robust identification of strongly lensed GW events requires explicit modeling and exclusion of spin precession as an alternative explanation—especially for short-delay, low-fringe lensing candidates—otherwise lensing claims risk high contamination.

Abstract: Binary black holes (BBHs) emit gravitational waves (GWs) as they inspiral towards merger. These GWs can be gravitationally lensed by large-scale structure along the line of sight, potentially creating multiple images of the same source with fixed time delays determined by the lensing geometry. As the BBHs inspiral, the GW frequency increases, leading to successive constructive and destructive interference between the multiple images. BBHs also have spins $\mathbf{S}_i$ that may be misaligned with their orbital angular momentum $\mathbf{L}$. As the BBHs inspiral, these misaligned spins cause $\mathbf{L}$ to precess about the total angular momentum $\mathbf{J}$, modulating the GW emission similar to pulsar emission resulting from a misaligned jet rotating in and out of the line of sight. We investigate the ability of a single L-shaped GW detector to distinguish between these two sources of modulation. We find that precessional modulation can mimic the lensing modulation between two images with comparable magnifications when the time delay between the images is short enough that fewer than three interference fringes occur during the time the GW signal spends in the sensitivity band of the detector. As strong lensing is rare for GW sources at moderate redshift while misaligned spins are common for BBHs produced in certain formation channels, ruling out precessional modulation is essential to identifying genuinely lensed systems.

</details>


### [165] [Cycles Without End: An Ekpyrotic Braneworld Bounce with a Kinetically Coupled Entropic Mechanism](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06485)
*Rikpratik Sengupta*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes a cyclic cosmological model that resolves three longstanding problems—singular bounces, blue spectral tilt in ekpyrotic contraction, and unbounded cycle growth due to entropy—using a Randall-Sundrum brane with timelike extra dimension, a single scalar field for smooth turnaround and BKL-safe contraction, and a kinetically coupled entropy field to achieve near-scale-invariant perturbations (ns ≈ 0.965), negligible non-Gaussianity (fNL ≈ −0.02), and ultra-suppressed tensor modes (r ∼ 10⁻²⁶); spatial flatness evades Tolman’s entropy bound, requiring ~75 e-folds of dark-energy-driven expansion per cycle to reset entropy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a viable, non-inflationary cyclic cosmology that simultaneously resolves (i) the singularity problem at the bounce, (ii) the generic blue tilt (ns ≈ 3) of single-field ekpyrotic models, and (iii) Tolman’s entropy-bound argument predicting divergent cycle durations—thereby restoring predictive power and observational consistency.

Method: The model combines: (1) a Randall-Sundrum braneworld with timelike extra dimension to regularize the bounce without violating the null energy condition; (2) a single canonical scalar field with a tailored potential enabling smooth transition from thawing dark energy → sign-changing turnaround → BKL-safe ekpyrotic contraction → re-expansion; (3) a minimally extended two-field sector introducing a kinetically coupled entropy field (à la Ijjas-Lehners-Steinhardt) whose coupling strength is analytically tuned to yield exact scale invariance for the given ekpyrotic potential, then slightly adjusted to match ns = 0.9649; (4) Friedmann equation analysis on the flat brane to show entropy dilution via ~70–80 e-folds of dark-energy expansion resets entropy density each cycle.

Result: The model achieves: ns = 0.9649 (via percent-level tuning of kinetic coupling), fNL ≈ −0.02, r ≈ 7.6×10⁻²⁶, brane tension ρc¹/⁴ = 10¹³ GeV, dimensionless coupling λ ≈ 5.7×10⁻³, and self-consistent cyclic evolution with no singularities, no BKL instability, and bounded entropy per cycle due to spatial flatness and sufficient dark-energy e-folds.

Conclusion: A fully explicit, observationally viable cyclic cosmology is constructed—free of singularities, compatible with CMB spectral tilt and non-Gaussianity constraints, and immune to Tolman’s entropy divergence—demonstrating that inflation is not uniquely capable of explaining cosmic structure and large-scale homogeneity.

Abstract: Cyclic cosmologies replace inflation's single creation event with an eternal sequence of smooth turnarounds, but three problems have historically stood in their way: singular bounces, the blue tilt generic to single-field ekpyrotic contraction, and Tolman's observation that entropy production should make successive cycles longer and larger without bound. We resolve all three within one explicit model. A Randall-Sundrum brane with a timelike extra dimension regularizes the bounce without violating the null energy condition, and a single scalar field's potential carries the universe from a thawing dark-energy plateau through a sign-changing turnaround into a BKL-safe ekpyrotic contraction and back. We show that no correction internal to the brane's Friedmann equation, and no straight two-field trajectory, can rescue the spectral index from the generic ekpyrotic result $n_s\approx3$. We then build the minimal completion that can: a kinetically coupled entropy field, following the general mechanism of Ijjas, Lehners, and Steinhardt, whose coupling steepness has a closed-form, exact-scale-invariance value for this model's ekpyrotic potential, with only a percent-level offset needed to match the measured tilt $n_s=0.9649$. This fixes the local non-Gaussianity ($f_{\rm NL}\approx-0.02$) and tensor-to-scalar ratio ($r\approx7.6\times10^{-26}$). Matching the observed amplitude fixes the conversion coupling and brane tension to unremarkable values ($λ\approx5.7\times10^{-3}$, $ρ_c^{1/4}=10^{13}\,$GeV). Finally, because the brane construction is spatially flat, Tolman's entropy argument does not bound the number of cycles here: we derive $N_{\rm DE}\sim70$-80 e-folds of accelerated expansion needed each cycle to dilute the entropy produced back to a negligible density before the next contraction.

</details>


### [166] [Semilinear wave equations on the Witten bubble spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06609)
*Onyx Gautam*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper establishes small-data global existence and improved decay for nonlinear wave equations on the Witten bubble spacetime — a 5D vacuum solution modeling semiclassical instability of Kaluza–Klein spacetime — using novel linear wave estimates and a U(1)-adapted null condition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To study nonlinear wave dynamics on the Witten bubble spacetime, motivated by its role in modeling the semiclassical instability of the classically stable Kaluza–Klein vacuum R^{3+1} × S^1.

Method: Develops novel dispersive and energy estimates for the linear wave equation on the Witten bubble background; applies them to prove small-data global existence (without symmetry) and enhanced decay for U(1)-symmetric semilinear equations satisfying a modified null condition.

Result: Small-data global existence for general (non-symmetric) solutions to a class of semilinear wave equations; improved pointwise decay rates for U(1)-symmetric solutions under a geometrically adapted null condition.

Conclusion: The Witten bubble spacetime admits robust global nonlinear wave dynamics, with symmetry enhancing decay — confirming its viability as a geometric setting for studying gravitational instabilities and nonlinear dispersive phenomena in higher dimensions.

Abstract: We initiate the study of nonlinear wave equations on the Witten bubble spacetime (also known as the "bubble of nothing"), which is an $\mathrm{SO}(3,1)\times \mathrm{U}(1)$-symmetric solution to the Einstein vacuum equations in $(4 + 1)$ dimensions. This spacetime was introduced by Witten to model the semiclassical instability of the Kaluza--Klein spacetime $\mathbf{R}^{3+1}\times S^1$ (which is classically stable by work of Huneau--Stingo--Wyatt (arXiv:2307.15267)). We prove a small-data global existence result without symmetry assumptions and an improved decay result for $\mathrm{U}(1)$-symmetric solutions to a class of semilinear equations satisfying a version of the null condition. Key to the proof are novel estimates for the linear wave equation, which has been studied in the physics literature by Bhawal and Viveshwara and in the mathematics literature by Bachelot (arXiv:1601.03682).

</details>


### [167] [Shadows and Thin-Disk Images of Kerr-Newman Black Holes in a Bertotti-Robinson Magnetic Field](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06648)
*Xi Wan,Haiyu Wang,Zhenyu Zhang,Zelin Zhang,Bin Chen*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies the optical properties of Kerr-Newman-Bertotti-Robinson (KN-BR) black holes using null geodesic analysis and backward ray tracing of a thin accretion disk, revealing how electric charge and external magnetic fields affect image size, critical curves, and inner shadows—particularly highlighting contrasting charge effects in Kerr-Newman vs. magnetized limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To characterize the distinctive optical signatures of exotic magnetized KN-BR black holes for potential identification in future observations, especially given their non-asymptotically-flat spacetime and relevance to strong-field gravity tests.

Method: Analytical separation of null geodesics to determine unstable spherical photon orbits and photon shell extent; construction of critical curves for a finite-distance, zero-angular-momentum observer; backward ray tracing of a geometrically thin, optically thin accretion disk extending from outer region to event horizon; quantitative analysis of images, intensity profiles, critical-curve areas, and inner-shadow areas.

Result: Neutral Kerr-BR₀ and specially charged Kerr-BRₛ configurations exhibit nearly identical optical appearances; increasing electric charge shrinks image size in the Kerr-Newman limit but enlarges it in magnetized KN-BR configurations; external magnetic field strongly amplifies apparent image scale; observer inclination influences inner-shadow area more than critical-curve area.

Conclusion: The optical behavior of KN-BR black holes is highly sensitive to magnetic field strength and observer geometry, with charge effects reversing between unmagnetized and magnetized regimes—providing discriminative theoretical signatures for detecting such exotic black holes observationally.

Abstract: In this paper, we investigate the optical properties of Kerr-Newman-Bertotti-Robinson (KN-BR) black holes. We use the separability of null geodesics to analyze unstable spherical photon orbits and determine the radial extent of the photon shell. Because the spacetime is not asymptotically flat, we construct the critical curve on the screen of a finite-distance zero-angular-momentum observer. We then perform backward ray tracing for a geometrically thin and optically thin disk that extends from the outer region to the event horizon, and examine the resulting images, intensity profiles, critical-curve areas, and inner-shadow areas. We find that the genuine neutral Kerr-BR$_0$ and specially charged Kerr-BR$_s$ configurations have nearly identical optical appearances. It is remarkable that for the KN-BR black holes increasing the electric charge reduces the characteristic image size in the Kerr-Newman limit but enlarges it in the magnetized configurations considered here. We also find that the external magnetic field strongly increases the apparent image scale, while the observer inclination affects the inner-shadow area more significantly than the critical-curve area. These results may provide useful theoretical insight for future observations aimed at identifying such exotic magnetized black holes.

</details>


### [168] [Physics-based phenomenological modeling of binary black hole hierarchical formation 2: Autodifferentiable functional inference of hierarchical compact-binary populations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06728)
*R. O'Shaughnessy,M. Zeeshan,M. Qazalbash*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces an autodifferentiable, physics-based phenomenological model—integrated into the gwkokab Poisson-likelihood framework—to jointly infer natal black hole population and dynamical interaction parameters from gravitational-wave merger data (e.g., GWTC-5.0), revealing limitations of simple pairwise coagulation models in explaining high-mass, comparable-mass mergers.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To connect observed mass-spin structure in the gravitational-wave census to underlying formation physics—particularly repeated mergers in dense environments—via models that are both physically interpretable and computationally tractable for population inference.

Method: Constructs an autodifferentiable, phenomenological model where each dense environment is characterized by a 'coagulation response'; the superposition of such environments yields an observable merger-rate density; embedded in the gwkokab Poisson-likelihood framework for hierarchical Bayesian inference.

Result: Application to GWTC-5.0 quantifies why simple pairwise coagulation models fail to reproduce the high-mass, comparable-mass merger subpopulation, and enables statistical comparison of alternative interaction topologies while preserving explicit modeling of the natal black hole population.

Conclusion: The proposed framework bridges interpretable astrophysics and scalable inference, enabling robust discrimination among dense-environment interaction models using current GW data.

Abstract: The gravitational-wave (GW) census contains mass and spin structure consistent with contributions from black holes assembled through repeated mergers in dense environments. Connecting that structure to formation physics requires models that are both physically interpretable and tractable within population inference. We construct an autodifferentiable, physics-based phenomenological model in which each dense environment is represented by a coagulation response and a population of such environments produces an observable merger-rate density. Embedded in the gwkokab Poisson-likelihood framework, this model enables joint inference of natal-population and interaction parameters from the GW census. Applied to GWTC-5.0, the framework shows why simple pairwise coagulation models struggle to reproduce the observed high-mass, comparable-mass population and tests alternative interaction structures against the data, while retaining an explicitly modeled natal component.

</details>


### [169] [Dissection of a merger-ringdown waveform in the small-mass-ratio limit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06861)
*Patrick Bourg,Lorenzo Küchler,Benjamin Leather,Kevin Cunningham,Jack Lewis,Adam Pound,Béatrice Bonga,Marc Casals,Rodrigo Panosso Macedo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops a framework to analyze nonlinear features in black hole binary merger-ringdown waveforms in the small-mass-ratio limit, identifying distinct inspiral, merger, and ringdown phases; it shows quadratic quasinormal modes dominate over linear overtones in ringdown for comparable-to-intermediate mass ratios and reveals cubic memory effects analogous to horizon absorption.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To isolate and understand individual nonlinear effects—such as quasinormal modes, tails, transients, memory—in black hole binary mergers and ringdowns, especially where standard perturbative approaches break down.

Method: Analytical framework in the small-mass-ratio limit, specializing to quasicircular, nonspinning black hole binaries; decomposition of the waveform into phase-separated components and identification of nonlinear mode couplings (e.g., quadratic and cubic QNMs) and memory contributions.

Result: Waveform cleanly separates into pre-merger inspiral, ~0.5-cycle merger, and QNM-dominated ringdown; quadratic QNMs dominate linear overtones in ringdown for comparable-to-intermediate mass ratios; gravitational-wave memory exhibits cubic wave-zone effects analogous to horizon absorption.

Conclusion: Nonlinearities—especially quadratic and cubic QNM couplings and memory—are essential and quantifiable features of merger-ringdown waveforms, even in regimes where mass ratios are not extremely small; this framework enables systematic dissection of waveform physics beyond linearized gravity.

Abstract: Work over the past two decades has unveiled the rich phenomenology of black hole binary mergers and subsequent ringdowns, involving a tapestry of quasinormal modes (QNMs), nonlinearities, tails, transients, and secular effects including gravitational memory.
