Here we develop a framework for analyzing nonlinear merger-ringdown features in the small-mass-ratio limit, where individual effects can be cleanly isolated. Specializing to the case of a quasicircular, nonspinning black hole binary, we find the waveform sharply divides into a pre-merger extended inspiral phase, a merger phase lasting roughly half a cycle, and a post-merger ringdown dominated by QNMs. We show quadratic QNMs dominate over linear overtones in the ringdown phase for comparable-to-intermediate mass ratios, and we highlight nonlinear effects of gravitational-wave memory, including cubic wave-zone phenomena analogous to horizon absorption effects.

</details>


### [170] [The Dynamical Instability of Rotating Boson Stars](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06954)
*Kai-Dong Zhou,Shou-Shan Bao,Jian Deng,Hong Zhang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies dynamical instability in rotating boson stars using Gross–Pitaevskii–Poisson equations; identifies quasiperiodic ring/twin-star oscillations via 3D simulations; develops linear stability analysis and a three-mode Hamiltonian to explain the instability mechanism and quantify how repulsive interactions extend lifetime.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the nonlinear dynamical instability of rotating boson stars—astrophysical candidates for dark matter halos or compact objects—and clarify the role of self-interactions in their stability and evolution.

Method: Three-dimensional numerical simulations of the Gross–Pitaevskii–Poisson system; systematic linear stability analysis around rotating ground states; derivation and reduction to a three-mode truncated Hamiltonian capturing dominant unstable modes.

Result: Rotating boson stars exhibit early-stage quasiperiodic density oscillations between ring-like and twin-star-like configurations; repulsive contact interactions suppress instability growth, markedly increasing stellar lifetime; the three-mode Hamiltonian quantitatively reproduces simulation dynamics.

Conclusion: The quasiperiodic conversion is driven by resonant coupling among three low-lying unstable modes; repulsive self-interactions stabilize rotating boson stars nonlinearly, and the reduced Hamiltonian provides an accurate, interpretable dynamical framework beyond linear theory.

Abstract: We investigate the dynamical instability of rotating boson stars described by the Gross--Pitaevskii--Poisson equations with contact self-interactions. Through three-dimensional simulations, we confirm that the rotating boson star undergoes a quasiperiodic conversion between ring-like and twin-star-like density configurations in the early nonlinear stage. We develop a systematic linear stability analysis to identify the modes driving this instability and show that repulsive self-interactions could significantly increase the lifetime of the rotating boson stars. We further construct a three-mode Hamiltonian to describe the early nonlinear stage, which explains the quasiperiodic conversion. This analytical framework agrees reasonably well with the simulation results and provides a clear picture to understand the dynamics of rotating boson stars.

</details>


### [171] [A New Window for Testing the PPN in the Strong-Field Regime: Stellar Rotation of S301](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06977)
*Mohsen Khodadi,Salvatore Capozziello*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes using the newly discovered S301 star—whose highly eccentric orbit brings it extremely close to Sagittarius A*—to test strong-field gravity via both orbital and spin precession, breaking the PPN β–γ degeneracy and achieving unprecedented constraints on γ (~10%) and β (σ_β ≈ 2.3×10⁻⁴) in the φ/c² ∼ 10⁻⁴ regime.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the β–γ degeneracy in post-Newtonian (PPN) parameter estimation from stellar orbits around Sgr A*, and to probe gravity in a stronger-field regime (φ/c² ∼ 10⁻⁴, v/c ∼ 0.08) than current Solar System or S-star tests.

Method: Analytical modeling of relativistic spin precession for S301, combined with orbital pericenter advance; joint MCMC analysis of projected astrometric (S2, S301) and spectroscopic (S301 spin-induced velocity shifts) data to constrain PPN parameters β and γ.

Result: Spin precession of S301 constrains γ_PPN to ~10% precision (σ_γ ≈ 0.1); combined with orbital precession (~1.95°/orbit), it breaks the β–γ degeneracy, yielding σ_β ≈ 2.3×10⁻⁴; sensitivity reaches gravitational potentials four orders of magnitude stronger than Solar System tests.

Conclusion: S301’s spin precession provides a novel, independent probe of strong-field gravity that complements orbital dynamics, enabling degeneracy-breaking PPN tests at φ/c² ∼ 10⁻⁴ — a new frontier for empirical validation of GR in the Galactic Center.

Abstract: The recently discovered S301 star, with an orbital period of 8.7 years and eccentricity $e = 0.982$, reaches a pericenter of just $140\,R_{\mathrm{s}}$ --- approximately nine times closer than S2's $\sim 1200R_{\mathrm{s}}$ --- offering an unprecedented laboratory for testing strong-field gravity. We demonstrate that S301's stellar spin precession provides a novel probe of the PPN parameter $γ$, scaling as $(2γ_{\mathrm{PPN}} + 1)/3$ relative to general relativity (GR). For maximum projected velocity shifts of $46.1~\mathrm{km\,s^{-1}}$, next-generation spectrographs could constrain $γ_{\mathrm{PPN}}$ to $\sim 10\%$ precision. When combined with S301's orbital precession --- a substantial $\sim 1.95^\circ$ pericenter advance per orbit (GR prediction), approximately an order of magnitude larger than S2's $\sim 0.2^\circ$, constraining $(2β_{\mathrm{PPN}} + 2γ_{\mathrm{PPN}} - 1)/3$ --- the spin measurement breaks the $β$-$γ$ degeneracy. A MCMC analysis of projected S2 and S301 observations yields $σ_β\approx 2.3 \times 10^{-4}$, with the $γ$ precision limited by the spin precession measurement to $σ_γ\approx 0.1$. While the spin precession provides an independent consistency check, its true significance lies in probing gravity at $φ/c^2 \sim 10^{-4}$ and $v/c \sim 0.08$ --- four orders of magnitude stronger than Solar System tests and an order of magnitude stronger than current S-star constraints. This complementary approach opens a new window for testing the PPN framework in the strong-field regime and demonstrates the power of combining multiple stellar probes at the Galactic Center.

</details>


### [172] [The (in)stability on total transmission modes with small bumps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07167)
*Xi-Ning Dong,Liang-Bi Wu,Yu-Sen Zhou,Zong-Kuan Guo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies spectral stability of total transmission modes (TTMs) in Tangherlini black holes under localized Pöschl-Teller potential perturbations, using a modified Chebyshev-Lobatto grid; results show strong stability for purely imaginary TTMs but high sensitivity for complex TTMs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: TTMs are reflectionless solutions closely tied to QNMs and exhibit high sensitivity to weak environmental perturbations; understanding their spectral stability is crucial for assessing robustness in gravitational wave modeling and black hole spectroscopy.

Method: Introduces a localized Pöschl-Teller bump into the effective potential of Tangherlini black holes and computes TTM spectrum shifts using high-accuracy numerical eigenvalue methods based on a modified Chebyshev-Lobatto grid.

Result: For d=14, ℓ=2, s=2: purely imaginary TTM shows strong spectral stability; complex TTMs undergo large migrations even under small perturbations — consistent with prior pseudospectrum analyses.

Conclusion: TTM spectral stability is mode-dependent: purely imaginary TTMs are robust, while complex TTMs are highly unstable under localized potential deformations, implying caution in interpreting TTMs as physical observables in noisy or imperfectly modeled spacetimes.

Abstract: Total transmission modes (TTMs) are a class of reflectionless solutions to black hole perturbation equations, closely related to quasinormal modes (QNMs), and can exhibit significant sensitivity to weak environmental perturbations. In this work, we investigate the spectrum (in)stability of TTMs of Tangherlini black holes by introducing a localized Pöschl-Teller bump perturbation into the effective potential, and employ a modified Chebyshev-Lobatto grid to improve the numerical accuracy of the localized perturbation. For $d=14$, $\ell=2$, and $s=2$, the purely imaginary TTM exhibits relatively strong spectrum stability, whereas the genuine complex TTMs undergo significant migrations even for small perturbations, consistent with the spectrum stability revealed by previous pseudospectrum analyses.

</details>


### [173] [Thin accretion-disc transfer functions in the q-metric spacetime: quadrupolar lensing signatures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07259)
*Saken Toktarbay,Manas Khassanov,Aray Muratkhan,Zhantay Muratkhan,Aliya Taukenova,Hernando Quevedo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates how disc images in the q-metric spacetime—generalizing Schwarzschild geometry via a quadrupole deformation parameter q—deviate from Schwarzschild predictions using backward ray tracing; it computes equatorial transfer functions, quantifies shifts and broadening of brightness features with q, and finds modest (<2.4%) integrated flux changes under a power-law emissivity model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To characterize observational signatures of static, axisymmetric deviations from Schwarzschild geometry—specifically, how quadrupole deformations (q ≠ 0) in the q-metric affect thin accretion disc imaging, especially in absence of horizons (since r = 2M is a curvature singularity for q ≠ 0).

Method: Backward ray tracing in the static, axisymmetric q-metric spacetime; computation of first three equatorial transfer functions r_m(b; q); analysis of critical impact parameter shifts and contour distortions; modeling of direct disc images with prescribed power-law emissivity and relativistic Doppler/gravitational redshift weighting.

Result: The third-crossing branch center b_{3,c} shifts significantly with q in metric-parameter units (b/M), but much of the shift is absorbed when rescaled to physical monopole mass ℳ = (1+q)M; at fixed ℳ, disc contours expand with q—especially near the inner edge; integrated flux varies by ≤2.4% across q ∈ [−0.3, 0.3]; median-flux position shifts +8.8%, interquartile width broadens +14.5%; residual deformation effects manifest primarily as displacement and broadening—not shape distortion—of the brightness distribution.

Conclusion: For geometrically thin, equatorially emitting discs in the q-metric, moderate quadrupole deformations induce systematic, measurable changes in image morphology (position and width of brightness features) but only minor changes in total flux—suggesting that high-resolution imaging (e.g., EHT-like) may constrain q more sensitively than photometry alone, though higher-order radiative effects remain unaccounted for.

Abstract: We study geometrically thin equatorial disc images in the static, axisymmetric q-metric spacetime using backward ray tracing. The Schwarzschild solution is recovered at $q=0$; for the nonzero deformations considered here, $r=2M$ is a curvature singularity rather than an event horizon. For a distant nearly face-on observer, we construct the first three equatorial transfer functions $r_m(b;q)$. In the {Schwarzschild} limit, the resolved third-crossing interval contains the critical impact parameter $b=3\sqrt{3}M$. As $q$ increases from $-0.3$ to $0.3$, the branch center shifts from $b_{3,c}/M\simeq3.716$ to $6.702$. In units of the physical monopole mass $\mathcal M=(1+q)M$, it instead decreases from $b_{3,c}/\mathcal M\simeq5.309$ to $5.156$. Much of the displacement in metric-parameter units therefore reflects the changing monopole scale. At fixed $\mathcal M$, the inclined first-intersection maps show that the contour dimensions increase with $q$, with larger relative changes near the inner disc. A prescribed power-law emissivity and relativistic frequency-shift weighting yield direct images whose integrated flux differs from the Schwarzschild value by at most $2.4\%$. Between the endpoint models, the horizontal median-flux position increases by $8.8\%$ and the interquartile width by $14.5\%$. Within this emission prescription, the residual deformation dependence appears mainly as a displacement and broadening of the brightness distribution. Higher-order radiative contributions are not included.

</details>


### [174] [Darboux Isospectrality Constraints on Quasinormal Modes Deformed by Bumps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07323)
*Zhen-Xiao Zhang,Chen Lan,Yan-Gang Miao*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper identifies an inconsistency in using additive Gaussian or Pöschl-Teller bumps to probe Schwarzschild quasinormal modes across axial and polar sectors, due to violation of the Darboux transformation linking them; it classifies admissible bump pairs and proposes two consistent prescriptions—Darboux generator and Riccati completion—to eliminate spurious axial-polar mode splitting while preserving centroid shifts.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the inconsistency between commonly used additive localized bumps (e.g., Gaussian, Pöschl-Teller) and the fundamental Darboux duality between Regge-Wheeler (axial) and Zerilli (polar) potentials in Schwarzschild perturbation theory.

Method: Analytical derivation using Darboux transformation constraints and Riccati equation structure; classification of admissible bump pairs; frequency-domain numerical computation of quasinormal modes under various bump prescriptions.

Result: Additive bumps violate Darboux consistency unless they possess an unavoidable r^{-2} tail—excluding arbitrary Gaussians/Pöschl-Teller profiles; two consistent alternatives are formulated—(i) Darboux generator prescription (bump profile serves as Darboux seed, not direct potential addition), and (ii) Riccati completion (one bump is prescribed, the partner solved via Riccati equation); numerical results confirm that inconsistent prescriptions induce finite, unphysical axial-polar QNM splitting, while the fundamental mode's centroid shift remains robust.

Conclusion: Darboux consistency is essential for physically meaningful perturbative probes of Schwarzschild quasinormal spectra; ad hoc additive bumps must be replaced by structurally compatible prescriptions to avoid spurious symmetry-breaking effects.

Abstract: A localized Gaussian or Pöschl-Teller bump is widely used to probe the sensitivity of Schwarzschild quasinormal modes, where it is typically added to both the Regge-Wheeler and Zerilli potentials. We show that such an additive prescription is generically incompatible with the Darboux transformation connecting the two parity sectors. The reason is that such a transformation restricts a parity-blind bump to a fixed family of bumps with an unavoidable $r^{-2}$ tail, excluding any Gaussian or Pöschl-Teller profile with arbitrary amplitude, center, or width in the additive prescription. We give the complete classification of Darboux-admissible axial and polar pairs of bumps and propose two consistent scenarios: Darboux generator prescription in which a Gaussian or Pöschl-Teller profile is retained as a generator of the bump pair rather than as the bump itself, and Riccati completion prescription in which a Gaussian or Pöschl-Teller profile is regarded as one of the bump pair and its partner will be solved from the Riccati equation. Our frequency-domain calculations confirm that a finite and spurious axial-polar splitting of quasinormal modes will be produced if the Darboux-consistent scenarios are absent, while the centroid shift of the fundamental mode is nearly unchanged.

</details>


### [175] [Static spherically symmetric electroweak models with fermions and gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08667)
*Marko Sobak*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper systematically reduces the Einstein-Yang-Mills-Higgs-Dirac-Yukawa system with SU(2)×U(1) gauge group to static spherical symmetry, derives the most general ansatz, and provides an open-source C++ GUI solver for the resulting ODE system.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable rigorous study of gravitational effects on a Standard Model–like lepton sector (SU(2)×U(1)) by reducing complexity via symmetry while preserving physical relevance.

Method: Systematic Lie symmetry reduction to static spherical symmetry; derivation of the most general invariant ansatz for all fields (metric, Yang-Mills, Higgs, Dirac); formulation of the reduced field equations as a coupled ODE system.

Result: Complete explicit form of the static spherically symmetric Einstein-Yang-Mills-Higgs-Dirac-Yukawa equations; implementation of an interactive, open-source C++ GUI solver for numerical exploration of global solutions.

Conclusion: The reduction yields a tractable yet physically rich framework for investigating solitonic or black hole solutions in gravity-coupled electroweak-like models; the provided solver facilitates further numerical analysis and validation.

Abstract: In this article we provide a systematic reduction to static spherical symmetry for (classical) gauge theoretic models with structure group SU(2) x U(1), coupled to gravity. In particular, we derive the most general static spherically symmetric ansatz for the Einstein-Yang- Mills-Higgs-Dirac-Yukawa equation system. This model is reminiscent of the lepton sector of the Standard Model coupled to gravity. The resulting total system of ordinary differential equations is rather large, so that finding global solutions is non-trivial. For this reason, an open-source interactive solver GUI written in C++ is provided together with the article.

</details>


### [176] [Exact Cancellation of Horizon Modes in Kerr Ringdown](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07326)
*Kei-ichiro Kubota,Yusuke Manita,Hayato Motohashi,Daiki Watarai*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper refutes the interpretation of oscillatory features in QNM-filtered ringdown waveforms as near-horizon 'direct waves', showing via exact analysis and numerical confirmation that source-induced horizon-mode poles are fully canceled by Matsubara zeros, eliminating such signals at late times.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve conflicting interpretations of oscillatory features in quasinormal mode (QNM)-filtered gravitational wave ringdowns—specifically, whether they represent a near-horizon 'direct wave'—by rigorously examining the validity of the saddle-point approximation near the black hole horizon.

Method: Exact analytical treatment of a particle plunging into a Kerr black hole within the near-horizon perturbative framework; identification and cancellation mechanism between horizon-mode poles and Matsubara zeros; time-domain numerical integration to verify sequential pole-zero cancellations up to the fourth stage.

Result: Every source-induced horizon-mode pole is exactly canceled by an infinite tower of Matsubara zeros, irrespective of orbital or black-hole parameters; the first four stages of this cancellation are numerically confirmed; the saddle-point approximation breaks down near the horizon, invalidating prior 'direct wave' interpretations.

Conclusion: Within the near-horizon perturbative regime, source-induced horizon-mode contributions vanish at late times due to complete pole-zero cancellation; thus, observed oscillatory features in QNM-filtered ringdowns cannot be attributed to near-horizon direct waves.

Abstract: Ringdown gravitational waves probe black hole spacetimes via quasinormal modes (QNMs). Recent studies have reported oscillatory features in QNM-filtered waveforms, and have led to conflicting interpretations as to whether they represent a near-horizon "direct wave". Reexamining a particle plunging into a Kerr black hole, we show that the saddle-point approximation underlying the proposed interpretation fails near the horizon. Our exact analysis reveals that every source-induced horizon-mode pole is canceled by an infinite tower of Matsubara zeros, independently of orbital and black-hole parameters. The first four stages of this sequential screening are also confirmed by time-domain numerical integration. Thus, within the near-horizon perturbative framework, source-induced horizon-mode signals do not survive at late times.

</details>


### [177] [Black Holes Thermodynamic Topology in Sharma-Mittal Statistics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07354)
*Issam Najma,Yahya Ladghami,Amine Bouali,Taoufik Ouali*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates black hole thermodynamic topology using Sharma-Mittal entropy (two-parameter generalization of Boltzmann-Gibbs statistics), applies Duan's Φ-mapping theory to compute winding numbers W, and finds dimension-independent topological classification for d > 4, while highlighting distinct thermodynamic topological behavior in higher vs. four dimensions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how generalized statistical frameworks—specifically Sharma-Mittal entropy with parameters δ and R—affect the thermodynamic topology and stability of black holes beyond standard Boltzmann-Gibbs assumptions.

Method: Applies Sharma-Mittal entropy to various black hole solutions (Schwarzschild, Reissner-Nordström, d-dimensional charged/uncharged); employs Duan's Φ-mapping topological field theory to compute topological charge (winding number W) characterizing thermodynamic phase structure.

Result: Topological classification via winding number W is independent of spacetime dimension for d > 4; however, d = 4 Schwarzschild and Reissner-Nordström black holes exhibit qualitatively different thermodynamic topological behavior under Sharma-Mittal statistics compared to higher dimensions.

Conclusion: Sharma-Mittal entropy induces a robust topological taxonomy of black holes, where thermodynamic phase structure is encoded in winding numbers invariant under dimensional change above four, yet reveals critical dimension-dependent features at d = 4—suggesting a fundamental role of spacetime dimension in generalized black hole thermodynamics.

Abstract: In this study, we explore the thermodynamic topology of black holes within the Sharma-Mittal entropy framework. Our study covers several black hole solutions, including charged and uncharged black holes in $d$-dimensional, as well as Schwarzschild and Reissner-Nordström black holes. By introducing the Sharma-Mittal entropy characterized by two parameters $δ$ and $R$, we explore how deviations from the standard Boltzmann-Gibbs statistics modify the thermodynamic structure and stability properties of these systems. Using Duan's $Φ$-mapping theory, we compute the corresponding topological numbers and classify black holes into distinct topological categories according to the winding number $W$. Moreover, our analysis shows that, within the Sharma-Mittal formalism, the topological classification is independent of spacetime dimension in the case $d>4$, although dimensions higher than four exhibit distinct features compared to the four-dimensional Schwarzschild and Reissner-Nordström black holes, reveal different behavior of thermodynamic topology under generalized statistics.

</details>


### [178] [Binary Black Holes in Einstein-Maxwell-Dilaton Theory: Second Post-Newtonian Dynamics from Effective Field Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07413)
*Pawan Kumar Gupta*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper derives the conservative two-body Lagrangian for charged black-hole binaries in Einstein-Maxwell-Dilaton theory up to second post-Newtonian order using effective field theory and temporal Kaluza-Klein decomposition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test general relativity and alternative theories of gravity—specifically Einstein-Maxwell-Dilaton (EMd) theory—using gravitational-wave observations from compact binary coalescences, by providing high-accuracy conservative dynamics for waveform modeling.

Method: Effective field theory approach combined with temporal Kaluza-Klein decomposition of the metric into non-relativistic gravitational fields; derivation of the conservative two-body Lagrangian for EMd black-hole binaries through 2PN order, including gravitational, electromagnetic, and dilaton interactions.

Result: Complete conservative two-body Lagrangian in EMd theory at 2PN order; verified consistency with Einstein-Maxwell, scalar-tensor, and general relativistic limits; independently confirmed static 2PN sector via test-body-limit check.

Conclusion: The derived Lagrangian provides the necessary conservative dynamics for constructing higher-accuracy gravitational-wave templates and enables concrete tests of EMd theory using current and future LIGO/Virgo/KAGRA and LISA observations.

Abstract: The detection of gravitational waves from compact binary coalescences provides a powerful opportunity to test general relativity in the strong-field regime and to search for signatures of alternative theories of gravity. In this work, we consider Einstein-Maxwell-Dilaton (EMd) theory, in which black holes can carry both electric and scalar (dilatonic) charges. We employ the effective field theory approach, together with a temporal Kaluza-Klein decomposition of the metric in terms of non-relativistic gravitational fields, to derive the conservative two-body Lagrangian for charged black-hole binaries in EMd theory through second post-Newtonian (PN) order. Our calculation extends the previously known conservative dynamics at 1PN order and includes the gravitational, electromagnetic, and dilaton interactions at 2PN order. We verify the result in the appropriate Einstein-Maxwell, scalar-tensor, and general relativistic limits, and perform an independent test-body-limit check of the static 2PN sector. These results provide the conservative dynamics needed for developing higher-accuracy waveform models and testing EMd theory with gravitational-wave observations.

</details>


### [179] [Thermodynamics and phase transitions of spherically symmetric AdS regular black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07491)
*Qi-Quan Li,Yu Zhang,Hoernisa Iminniyaz*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper derives thermodynamic properties of a class of spherically symmetric AdS regular black holes—obtained from a nonlinear electromagnetic field coupled to Einstein gravity with Λ—by imposing a regularity condition (M = q³/α) on the 'mother' singular black hole solution. It clarifies that thermodynamic response functions (e.g., heat capacity) must be computed *after*, not before, imposing constraints; doing so reveals novel 8-shaped and C-shaped Gibbs free energy–temperature (G–T) diagrams for P < Pc, signaling first- and zeroth-order small/large black hole phase transitions—unlike the standard RN-AdS swallowtail.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct and thermodynamically characterize a physically motivated class of regular black holes in AdS space, avoiding curvature singularities via a nonlinear electromagnetic field, and to resolve conceptual ambiguities in constrained thermodynamic derivations—particularly the order of constraint imposition versus differentiation for response functions.

Method: Starts from a coupled Einstein–nonlinear-EM system with Λ to obtain a singular 'mother' black hole solution; derives thermodynamic quantities in the unconstrained phase space (S, q, α, P); then imposes the regularity condition M = q³/α to obtain consistent thermodynamics for the regular subclass; distinguishes fundamental conjugate variables (from first law) from response functions (second derivatives, e.g., heat capacity); rigorously compares constraint-before-differentiation vs. differentiation-before-constraint procedures.

Result: Identifies two new phase-transition signatures in the G–T diagram: an 8-shaped curve for P < Pz (first-order transition) and a C-shaped curve for Pz < P < Pc (zeroth-order transition), both absent in RN-AdS; confirms that correct thermodynamic response functions require constraint imposition *prior* to differentiation; establishes richer phase structure due to regularity.

Conclusion: The regularity condition M = q³/α not only removes the central singularity but also fundamentally alters the thermodynamic phase structure—introducing qualitatively new zeroth- and first-order phase transitions—and mandates careful ordering of constraint application in thermodynamic derivations.

Abstract: We obtain the singular ''mother'' black hole solution by solving the coupling between Einstein gravity with a cosmological constant and a nonlinear electromagnetic field. The thermodynamic quantities of the ''mother'' black hole are first derived in the unconstrained phase space $(S, q, α, P)$, after which the regularity condition $M = q^3/α$ is imposed to obtain the thermodynamic quantities of a class of spherically symmetric AdS regular black holes self-consistently. We distinguish two categories of thermodynamic quantities: fundamental conjugate variables defined by the first law, and thermodynamic response functions constructed from them. For response functions involving second-order derivatives -- in particular the heat capacity -- we show that the order of constraint imposition and derivative calculation cannot be interchanged, and the correct procedure is to first impose the constraint and then construct the response function. Within this framework, we find that when $P<P_c$, the $G$--$T$ diagram exhibits a behavior qualitatively different from the swallowtail behavior of the standard RN-AdS black hole. Instead, it exhibits an 8-shaped structure for $P<P_z$ and a C-shaped structure for $P_z<P<P_c$, corresponding to first-order and zeroth-order phase transitions between small and large black hole phases, respectively. Imposing the regularity condition thus leads to a richer phase structure for this class of black holes than the standard RN-AdS case.

</details>


### [180] [Globally regular charged black holes in non-polynomial quasi-topological gravity with Born-Infeld electrodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07576)
*Hong-Lin Liu,Zhong-Wen Feng,Qing-Quan Jiang,Xia Zhou,Xue-Ling Mu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs exact static, spherically symmetric charged black hole solutions in 4D non-polynomial quasi-topological gravity coupled to Born-Infeld electrodynamics; it shows that Born-Infeld nonlinearity can resolve a finite-radius curvature singularity present in the vacuum solution, yielding globally regular geometries—including regular black holes (RBHs) with novel horizon structure (e.g., triple-degenerate inner horizon and nonzero outer surface gravity).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the finite-radius curvature singularity appearing in the vacuum branch of a specific non-polynomial quasi-topological gravity model (h(p) = p/(1 + ℓ²p)), by coupling to nonlinear Born-Infeld electrodynamics and exploring whether charge can restore global regularity rather than disrupt it.

Method: Analytical construction of exact static, spherically symmetric solutions in four-dimensional non-polynomial quasi-topological gravity minimally coupled to Born-Infeld electrodynamics; systematic analysis of metric behavior, curvature invariants, horizon structure, and surface gravities (κ₊, κ₋) across parameter space.

Result: Born-Infeld electrodynamics eliminates the vacuum’s finite-radius singularity for a finite region of parameter space, producing globally regular spacetimes: both horizonless configurations and regular black holes (RBHs); among the latter, a continuous branch exhibits a triple-degenerate inner horizon (κ₋ = 0) and a simple outer event horizon (κ₊ ≠ 0).

Conclusion: Unlike many models where charge introduces singularities or destabilizes regularity, here Born-Infeld electrodynamics actively cures a vacuum gravitational singularity and supports new classes of globally regular charged geometries—including RBHs with exotic inner-horizon degeneracy—demonstrating a constructive role of nonlinear electrodynamics in quantum-gravity-motivated theories.

Abstract: We construct exact static, spherically symmetric charged solutions in four-dimensional non-polynomial quasi-topological gravity coupled to Born-Infeld electrodynamics. We focus on the model $h(p)=p/(1+\ell^{2}p)$, whose vacuum branch develops a curvature singularity at a finite radius. We show that Born-Infeld nonlinearities can remove this singularity within a finite region of parameter space, yielding globally regular geometries with an asymptotically flat exterior and a finite-curvature AdS-type core. The regular sector contains both horizonless configurations and RBHs, separated by a degenerate-horizon boundary. We further identify a continuous branch of regular black holes with a triple-degenerate inner horizon and a simple outer event horizon, satisfying $κ_-=0$ and $κ_+\neq0$. This provides a converse example to cases in which introducing charge spoils the regularity of a vacuum regular black hole. In the present model, Born-Infeld electrodynamics instead removes the finite-radius singularity of a vacuum-singular gravitational branch while supporting globally regular charged geometries, including RBHs with nontrivial inner-horizon structure.

</details>


### [181] [Global structure and stability of Kerr-Bertotti-Robinson spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07602)
*Yu-Sen Zhou,Liang-Bi Wu,Ming-Fei Ji,Wen-Tao Fu,Li-Ming Cao,Rong-Gen Cai*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the Kerr-Bertotti-Robinson (KBR) spacetime, showing it admits an analytic extension forming an infinite chain of universes connected by wormhole bridges; this exposes ring singularities without horizons—violating weak cosmic censorship. It further demonstrates instability via purely imaginary growing quasinormal modes of a scalar field, linked to chronology violation and superradiant amplification in a double-barrier cavity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate the global structure and stability of the Kerr-Bertotti-Robinson spacetime, particularly whether its asymptotic boundary is genuine, and to test implications for cosmic censorship and chronology protection via scalar field perturbations.

Method: Analytic continuation of the KBR metric to construct maximal extension; separation of variables and exact solution of the axisymmetric massless scalar wave equation on a two-universe scattering segment; quasinormal mode analysis including identification of purely imaginary modes, horizon-flux balance, and parametric dependence on rotation and magnetic field.

Result: 1) The r=∞ surface is not a boundary but connects smoothly to r=−∞ of an adjacent KBR region, yielding an infinite chain with naked ring singularities—violating weak cosmic censorship. 2) Axisymmetric scalar perturbations exhibit purely imaginary unstable (growing) quasinormal modes for all ℓ, rooted in chronology-violating regions. 3) For (ℓ,m)=(2,2), instability occurs at large rotation and small magnetic field; marginal modes obey exact horizon-flux balance, supporting a black-hole-bomb mechanism. 4) The double-barrier potential also supports weakly damped modes suggestive of echo-like signals.

Conclusion: The KBR spacetime is globally pathological—violating weak cosmic censorship—and dynamically unstable under scalar perturbations due to both chronology violation and superradiant cavity effects, challenging its physical viability as a realistic spacetime.

Abstract: The surface $r=\infty$ of the Kerr-Bertotti-Robinson spacetime is not a genuine boundary. We construct its natural analytic extension and show that $r=+\infty$ of one KBR region is smoothly connected to $r=-\infty$ of a neighboring one. Repeated continuation produces an infinite chain of regions connected by wormhole-like bridges and exposes the neighboring ring singularity without an intervening horizon, which violates the weak cosmic censorship conjecture. We then study a test massless scalar field on a two-universe scattering segment to probe the stability of the spacetime. For axisymmetric perturbations, we analytically establish purely imaginary growing quasinormal modes for every $\ell$ and trace their origin to the chronology-violating region. In the $(\ell,m)=(2,2)$ sector, unstable branches occur for sufficiently large rotation and sufficiently small magnetic field. Their marginal real modes obey an exact horizon-flux balance, supporting a black-hole-bomb interpretation in which superradiant extraction is amplified by trapping within the double-barrier potential. The same cavity also supports families of weakly damped modes and may produce echo-like responses.

</details>


### [182] [The generalized second law for Kerr-Taub-NUT black holes perturbed by test field](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07658)
*Koray Düztaş,Ahmet Cem Erdoğan*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes how bosonic and fermionic test fields perturb Kerr-Taub-NUT black holes, finding that area and entropy variations differ significantly: while both field types can reduce horizon area, only sub-superradiant fermions reduce entropy; violations of the Generalized Second Law (GSL) occur for fermionic perturbations violating the Null Energy Condition (NEC), whereas GSL holds for NEC-satisfying bosonic perturbations when von Neumann entropy is assigned to the field.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate whether the Generalized Second Law (GSL) of thermodynamics holds for Kerr-Taub-NUT black holes under perturbations by quantum test fields—specifically addressing discrepancies between area and entropy laws in non-Kerr geometries and clarifying the role of energy conditions (e.g., NEC) and field statistics (bosonic vs. fermionic).

Method: Analytical thermodynamic and scattering analysis of linear perturbations: computing horizon area and Bekenstein–Hawking entropy variations induced by bosonic (scalar) and fermionic (Dirac) test fields on Kerr-Taub-NUT spacetime; identifying critical frequencies where area variation vanishes; comparing with superradiance thresholds; evaluating entropy balance including von Neumann entropy of the field and checking NEC compliance.

Result: 1) Critical frequency for zero area variation > superradiance limit → area decrease possible for both bosons (in relevant range) and fermions (non-superradiant); 2) Entropy ∝ area fails for Kerr-Taub-NUT → only sub-superradiant fermions reduce entropy; 3) GSL violation occurs generically for fermionic perturbations violating NEC; 4) For NEC-satisfying bosonic perturbations near extremality, minimum black hole entropy increase compensates environmental entropy loss when field’s von Neumann entropy is included → GSL upheld.

Conclusion: The Generalized Second Law is preserved for NEC-compliant bosonic perturbations of Kerr-Taub-NUT black holes when quantum (von Neumann) entropy of the field is accounted for, but is generically violated for fermionic perturbations that violate the Null Energy Condition—highlighting the crucial interplay among spacetime geometry (NUT charge), field statistics, energy conditions, and entropy assignment in black hole thermodynamics.

Abstract: We evaluate the variations in the area and the entropy of Kerr-Taub-NUT black holes perturbed by bosonic and fermionic test fields. We demonstrate that the critical frequency for a test field at which the variation of the area vanishes is strictly larger than the superradiance limit for Kerr-Taub-NUT black holes. Therefore both bosonic fields in the relevant range and fermionic fields which do not exhibit superradiant scattering can induce a decrease in the area of a Kerr-Taub-NUT black hole. However, the entropy is not directly proportional to the area for Kerr-Taub-NUT black holes. Only fermionic fields with frequencies below the superradiance limit can induce an entropy decrease. The simultaneous loss of entropy by the black hole and the environment identifies a generic violation of the Generalized Second Law (GSL) for fermionic perturbations that fail to satisfy the Null Energy Condition (NEC). For bosonic perturbations that satisfy the NEC, we evaluate the limiting case, where a nearly extremal black hole is perturbed by a scalar field with frequency slightly above the superradiance limit. Our analysis reveals that the minimum increase in the black hole entropy compensates for the entropy loss of the environment, when one assigns Von Neumann entropy to the test field. The GSL remains valid for perturbations satisfying the NEC.

</details>


### [183] [Coherent Selection of Critical Kasner Response](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07848)
*Yi-kun Li*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper shows that a tiny coherent electromagnetic wave can significantly alter the initial dynamics of a contracting spacetime in Einstein–Maxwell–scalar theory with two spacelike Killing fields; using a critical family inspired by scalarized black-hole interiors, it demonstrates magnetic delay enabling longitudinal field redistribution, and reveals a reversal (enhancement vs. suppression) of scalar response depending on background geometry—accurately predicted by a perturbative quadratic-source calculation with <0.4% coherent profile error.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how minimal coherent electromagnetic perturbations influence early-time dynamics in highly anisotropic, contracting spacetimes—particularly near singularities—and whether such perturbations encode predictive information about departure from Kasner behavior.

Method: Analyzes Einstein–Maxwell–scalar theory with two commuting spacelike Killing fields; constructs a critical family of solutions motivated by scalarized black-hole interiors; employs a perturbative scheme propagating electromagnetic field and momentum, then their quadratic scalar and geometric source terms; quantifies coherence preservation and response reversal via controlled low-frequency modulation and error comparison (with/without cross terms).

Result: A subdominant coherent wave induces magnetic delay sufficient for longitudinal redistribution; scalar response reverses (enhances/suppresses) depending on background geometry; quadratic-source calculation predicts reversal with 0.38% coherent profile error (vs. 46.9% without cross terms); final response evolves into a magnetic pulse involving electric and scalar exchange; incident coherence is preserved as predictive information for first Kasner departure.

Conclusion: Coherent low-energy electromagnetic waves act as sensitive probes of spacetime geometry near singularities; their nonlinear backreaction—mediated through quadratic sources—is essential for accurate prediction of scalar dynamics; the critical geometry encodes initial coherence as structural information governing the onset of non-Kasner evolution.

Abstract: A coherent wave component with extremely small initial self-energy can substantially change the first response of a contracting spacetime. We demonstrate this effect in Einstein--Maxwell--scalar theory with two commuting spacelike Killing fields. Along a critical family motivated by scalarized black-hole interiors, the estimated magnetic delay leaves time for longitudinal propagation to redistribute the incident field. Increasing a low-frequency component at fixed initial electromagnetic energy enhances the scalar response on one background and suppresses it on another. A calculation that propagates the field and its momentum, then their quadratic scalar and geometric sources, predicts this reversal before nonlinear evolution. At the first suppression comparison, the coherent profile error is $0.38\%$, against $46.9\%$ when cross terms are omitted. The selected response develops into a magnetic pulse with electric and scalar exchange. The critical geometry thus preserves incident coherence as information that predicts its first departure from a Kasner epoch.

</details>


### [184] [Scalar field with nonminimal couplings to metric-affine geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07856)
*Ilaria Andrei,Damianos Iosifidis,Laur Järv,Margus Saal*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies a scalar field nonminimally coupled to metric-affine gravity with general quadratic torsion/nonmetricity and derivative couplings (e.g., Nieh-Yan-like), derives the fully reduced metric-scalar-tensor equivalent, classifies how different geometric sectors affect the Einstein-frame kinetic term, identifies projective consistency constraints, analyzes canonical field redefinitions and potential reshaping—including realization of natural inflation—and derives modified Friedmann equations under cosmological symmetry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically explore how generic metric-affine geometry—beyond Riemannian GR—alters scalar-tensor phenomenology, especially kinetic structure and inflationary potentials, while maintaining consistency (e.g., projective invariance) and connecting to observable cosmology.

Method: Variational derivation of field equations from a general linear-in-curvature, quadratic-in-torsion/nonmetricity action including scalar–hypermomentum derivative couplings; algebraic elimination of the independent affine connection on the nondegenerate branch; classification of kinetic-term modifications by sector; imposition of projective symmetry; canonical field redefinition and Einstein-frame potential mapping; restriction to FLRW symmetry for cosmological reduction.

Result: The pure quadratic torsion or pure quadratic nonmetricity sectors preserve the standard Einstein-frame kinetic function; their combination, derivative couplings, and mixed torsion–nonmetricity terms generically deform it; projective consistency fixes a coupling relation in the derivative sector; derivative + nonminimal couplings can map a Jordan-frame quadratic potential into a natural-inflation potential after canonical normalization; cosmological reduction yields modified Friedmann equations dependent on specific quadratic coupling combinations and scalar hypermomentum.

Conclusion: Metric-affine extensions enrich scalar-tensor theory not only via nonminimal curvature coupling but crucially through torsion–nonmetricity interplay and derivative interactions—enabling novel inflationary dynamics (e.g., natural inflation) and imposing tight consistency conditions like projective invariance; the resulting effective metric theory is highly constrained yet phenomenologically flexible.

Abstract: We study a scalar field nonminimally coupled to metric-affine gravity within actions linear in the affine curvature and containing all independent parity-even and parity-odd terms quadratic in torsion and nonmetricity, including mixed contractions. We also include Nieh-Yan-like derivative couplings between the scalar-field derivative and the four torsion and nonmetricity vectors. We derive the connection, metric, and scalar-field equations and, since the connection equation is algebraic, eliminate the independent connection on the generic nondegenerate branch to obtain an equivalent metric scalar-tensor theory in which the non-Riemannian interactions are encoded in an effective kinetic function. We classify several sectors and find that the pure quadratic nonmetricity and pure quadratic torsion sectors separately leave the Einstein-frame kinetic function unchanged relative to the simplest metric-affine scalar-tensor model, whereas their simultaneous presence, the derivative couplings, and generic mixed torsion-nonmetricity interactions can modify it. In the derivative-coupling sector, projective consistency imposes a relation among the couplings. We then consider polynomial coupling functions and study the resulting canonical field redefinition and Einstein-frame potentials. In particular, we illustrate how derivative and mixed torsion--nonmetricity couplings reshape quadratic and quartic potentials in canonical-field space, and show that a quadratic Jordan-frame potential can be mapped, for a suitable choice of derivative and nonminimal couplings, into a natural-inflation potential after canonical normalization. Finally, we separately impose the cosmological principle, determine the reduced combinations of quadratic couplings and the scalar field hypermomentum, and obtain the corresponding modified cosmological equations in the simplest sectors.

</details>


### [185] [NRSur7dq4v2: A multi-domain precessing surrogate model with improved accuracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07873)
*Abhishek Ravishankar,Vijay Varma,Scott E. Field,Keefe Mitman,Nils Deppe,Leo C. Stein,Michael Boyle,Mark A. Scheel*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: NRSur7dq4v2 is a multi-domain extension of the NRSur7dq4 surrogate model for binary black hole waveforms, designed to improve accuracy—especially in the merger-ringdown regime—via independent error control across temporal subdomains; it yields 3–10× better remnant mass/spin estimation, extends to ℓ=5 modes, refines subdominant mode modeling, introduces runtime complexity control, and is accelerated fourfold via optimized gwsurrogate.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: NRSur7dq4, while widely used in parameter estimation, exhibits insufficient accuracy in the merger-ringdown phase—critical for high-mass BBH events—motivating a more accurate and flexible surrogate.

Method: Constructed NRSur7dq4v2 as a multi-domain surrogate with overlapping temporal subdomains (inspiral vs. merger-ringdown), enabling independent, tighter error control per domain followed by smooth combination; incorporated higher modes (ℓ ≤ 5), refined subdominant mode physics, added user-controllable runtime complexity, and optimized gwsurrogate for speed.

Result: NRSur7dq4v2 achieves 3–10× improvement in remnant mass and spin estimation from ringdown QNM fits; supports ℓ=5 modes with enhanced subdominant mode fidelity; enables accuracy–cost tradeoff tuning; and achieves 4× faster evaluation—making precessing surrogates slightly faster than LALSimulation equivalents.

Conclusion: NRSur7dq4v2 significantly advances the state-of-the-art in precessing BBH waveform surrogates by improving physical accuracy where it matters most (merger-ringdown), expanding spectral content, and enhancing computational efficiency—enabling more robust and efficient gravitational-wave inference.

Abstract: Numerical relativity simulations provide the most accurate waveforms for binary black hole coalescences, but are prohibitively expensive for direct use in gravitational-wave data analysis. Surrogate models overcome this cost, and NRSur7dq4 is commonly used in parameter estimation for this reason. However, there is evidence in the literature that NRSur7dq4's accuracy could be improved in the merger-ringdown portion of the waveform, which is particularly important for the analysis of high-mass binary black hole events. Motivated by these observations, we construct NRSur7dq4v2, a multi-domain extension of NRSur7dq4 in which overlapping temporal subdomains allow tighter, independent error control over the inspiral and merger-ringdown portions of the waveform before they are smoothly combined. To assess the new model's performance in the merger-ringdown regime, we infer the mass and spin of the remnant black hole from quasi-normal fits to the ringdown portion of the surrogate waveform. We find that NRSur7dq4v2 produces significantly improved remnant mass and spin estimates, with gains of roughly factors of $3$ to $10$ over NRSur7dq4. Compared to NRSur7dq4, NRSur7dq4v2 also includes modes up to $\ell=5$, includes more accurate modeling of certain subdominant modes, and introduces a runtime model-complexity feature that gives users direct control over the tradeoff between evaluation cost and accuracy. Finally, alongside the model development, we have optimized the gwsurrogate package to achieve a fourfold speedup for precessing surrogates. The resulting precessing surrogates called through gwsurrogate are now slightly faster than their LALSimulation counterparts.

</details>


### [186] [A Generalized Class of Taub-NUT-Scalar Metrics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.25368)
*Fataneh Bakherad,Behrouz Mirza,Reza Bahani,Mahnaz Tavakoli Kachi,Ali Derekeh*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a new class of three-parameter Taub-NUT-scalar (TNS) metrics by applying the Ehlers transformation to a previously proposed metric family, incorporating a NUT charge to generate gravitomagnetic effects; it analyzes gravitational lensing, quasinormal modes, and test particle motion to study how mass, multipole moments, scalar fields, and NUT charge jointly influence spacetime structure and orbital stability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend known scalar–gravity solutions (ZV and FJNW) into axially symmetric, asymptotically flat, gravitomagnetic spacetimes and systematically explore how scalar fields and NUT charge affect physical observables and orbital dynamics.

Method: Ehlers transformation applied to a three-parameter seed metric to introduce NUT charge; subsequent analysis of geodesic motion, null geodesics for lensing, perturbative computation of quasinormal mode spectra, and stability analysis of circular orbits.

Result: A novel family of Taub-NUT-scalar (TNS) metrics is constructed; gravitational lensing deflection, QNM frequencies, and orbital stability are explicitly computed and shown to depend nontrivially on the scalar field coupling and NUT parameter; NUT charge modifies innermost stable circular orbit (ISCO) locations and can destabilize otherwise stable orbits.

Conclusion: The TNS metrics provide a versatile framework for probing interplay among scalar fields, gravitomagnetism, and multipole structure; the NUT charge significantly impacts spacetime phenomenology—especially orbital stability—highlighting its non-negligible role in scalar–tensor gravity.

Abstract: We consider a class of three-parameter metrics that was introduced in \cite{azizallahi2024three}. The class of metrics contain Zipoy-Voorhees (ZV) and Fisher-Janis-Newman- Winicour (FJNW) spacetimes at certain values of the parameters. We build an axially symmetric and asymptotically flat spacetime with gravitomagnetic effects by introducing a Newman- Unti-Tamburino (NUT) charge into this class of metrics via the Ehlers transformation. We investigate the physical features of this class of Taub-NUT-scalar (TNS) metrics including gravitational lensing, quasinormal mode (QNM) spectra, and test particle motion. Our results provide fresh insight on how mass, multipole moments, and gravitomagnetic fields behave in the presence of a scalar field and show the effect of the NUT charge on stability of orbits in this class of metrics.

</details>


### [187] [Nonradial oscillations of realistic anisotropic neutron stars: Polar modes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07878)
*L. M. Becerra,José F. Rodríguez-Ruiz,E. A. Becerra-Vergara,F. D. Lora-Clavijo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates polar perturbations of anisotropic neutron stars in full GR, computes f-mode frequencies and damping times with a consistent treatment of the radial vector perturbation Δk^α, finds monotonic mass dependence and anisotropy effects on f-mode properties, and establishes quasi-universal relations between f-mode quantities and compactness insensitive to EOS.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how pressure anisotropy affects neutron star oscillations (particularly f-modes) within full general relativity, enabling asteroseismological constraints on anisotropy from future observations.

Method: Full general relativistic linear perturbation theory for static, spherically symmetric anisotropic neutron stars; consistent treatment of metric and fluid perturbations; explicit derivation and constraint of the Lagrangian perturbation Δk^α to the (ũ,k̃) plane; numerical solution of perturbation equations for f-modes using three EOSs and two anisotropy prescriptions (Horvat and Bowers-Liang).

Result: f-mode frequency increases with mass (1–3 kHz), damping time decreases (0.5–1.25 s); increasing tangential pressure anisotropy lowers frequency; damping time decreases with anisotropy in Horvat model but increases in Bowers-Liang model; quasi-universal Mω_R–C and Mω_I–C relations found, fitted with <10% error.

Conclusion: Pressure anisotropy has distinct, prescription-dependent imprints on neutron star f-modes; quasi-universal relations with compactness provide a robust, EOS-insensitive framework for inferring anisotropy from asteroseismology.

Abstract: In this work, we study the polar perturbations of static, spherically symmetric neutron stars with anisotropic pressure in full general relativity, including linear-order perturbations of both the metric and the fluid. We calculate the $f$-mode frequencies and the corresponding damping times using a consistent treatment of the perturbation of the radial vector $k^α$. In particular, its Lagrangian perturbation $Δk^α$ is determined by the metric perturbations and the fluid Lagrangian displacement and is constrained to the $(\tilde{u},\tilde{k})$ plane, where $\tilde{u}^α$ is the normalized fluid four-velocity. This constraint introduces an additional dynamical degree of freedom into the perturbation equations. Considering three equations of state and the Horvat and Bowers-Liang prescriptions for pressure anisotropy, we find that the $f$-mode frequency increases with stellar mass, ranging from $1$ to $3$~kHz, while the damping time decreases, ranging from $0.5$ to $1.25$~s. Increasing anisotropy, in the sense of tangential pressure exceeding radial pressure, generally lowers the oscillation frequency, while its effect on the damping time depends on the anisotropy prescription: the damping time decreases with increasing anisotropy for the Horvat model but increases with increasing anisotropy for the Bowers-Liang model. We further find quasi-universal relations between the real and imaginary parts of the $f$-mode frequency, $Mω_R$ and $Mω_I$, and the stellar compactness $\mathcal{C}=M/R$, which are largely insensitive to the equation of state. Polynomial fits to these relations achieve an accuracy better than $10\%$, providing a simple phenomenological framework for constraining neutron-star pressure anisotropy through future asteroseismology observations.

</details>


### [188] [Scalarized Einstein-Euler-Heisenberg black holes at the approach to extremality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08128)
*Hong Guo,Xiao Yan Chew,Yun Soo Myung*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs scalarized extremal black holes in Einstein-Euler-Heisenberg-scalar theory with exponential and polynomial scalar–Maxwell couplings; it identifies critical scalar coupling α_c governed by the Breitenlohner-Freedman bound, shows entropy is an attractor invariant (confirming secondary hair), and maps the scalarized domain in the (q, α) plane bounded by sub- and super-extremal existence curves meeting at q = q_e.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how scalar hair arises on extremal black holes in a realistic effective theory (EEH-scalar) beyond standard GR, particularly whether scalarization persists at extremality and whether hair remains secondary (i.e., non-primary, undetermined by conserved charges) under full backreaction and near-horizon attractor mechanisms.

Method: Analytical and numerical construction of linearized and fully backreacted scalarized black hole solutions on cold EEH black hole backgrounds; derivation of existence curves α_n(q); asymptotic and near-horizon analysis approaching extremality (q → q_e); application of the entropy function formalism to the AdS₂×S² throat; classification of scalarized extremal black holes (SEBHs) by asymptotic scalar value φ_∞ and scalar charge Q_s.

Result: Existence curves α_n(q) accumulate at a universal critical coupling α_c as q → q_e, determined by the BF bound in the AdS₂×S² throat; fully backreacted SEBHs carry constant secondary scalar hair; entropy is an attractor invariant (φ_∞-independent); new SEBH families with Q_s ≠ 0 exist only for q ≥ q_e, completing the scalarized domain bounded by sub-extremal (q ≤ q_e) and super-extremal branches meeting precisely at q = q_e.

Conclusion: Scalar hair on extremal black holes in EEH-scalar theory is robust, secondary, and governed by near-horizon AdS₂ physics; the scalarized domain is topologically closed at extremality, with α_c acting as a universal threshold—highlighting the interplay between UV-motivated nonlinear electrodynamics, scalar couplings, and infrared attractor mechanisms.

Abstract: We investigate extremal black holes with scalar hair in the generic Einstein-Euler-Heisenberg (EEH)-scalar theory with two scalar couplings to the Maxwell term. One is an exponential coupling with coupling constant $α$ and the other is its polynomial coupling. We first construct scalarized black holes on the cold (C)-horizon of EEH black holes described by mass $M$ and magnetic charge $P$ both at the linear level, through the existence curves $α_n(q)$ with $q=P/M$, and as fully backreaction solutions. As extremality ($q=q_e$) is approached, all existence curves accumulate, following $α_n-α_c\sim 1/\ln^2(q_e-q)$, at the critical branch $α_c$ fixed by the Breitenlohner-Freedman bound for the near-horizon (AdS$_2\times S^2$) throat. We obtain scalarized extremal black hole (SEBH) with constant secondary hair and it is recovered exactly from the entropy function approach working on the near-horizon throat. Its entropy is an attractor invariant, being independent of the asymptotic modulus, so the scalar hair remains secondary. Exploiting this constant scalar, we seek further SEBHs with scalar hair keeping its charge $Q_s$ for the zero asymptotic scalar ($φ_\infty=0$). In the $(q,α)$ plane, we observe that these extremal solutions occupy $q\ge q_e$. Hence, the existence curves $α_n(q)$ existing for $q\le q_e$ and the extremal branches bound the scalarized domain from opposite sides and they meet only at $q=q_e$.

</details>


### [189] [Cosmological extra dimensions can mimic dark energy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08294)
*Mattia Villani*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes a higher-dimensional cosmological model with compactified extra dimensions (hypersphere) coupled to a 4D FLRW universe; it shows that dark energy-like acceleration can emerge geometrically from the Gaussian curvature and dynamics of contracting extra dimensions, without requiring a cosmological constant or quintessence, and fits SNe Ia data favoring 3–4 contracting extra dimensions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain cosmic acceleration (interpreted as dark energy) as a purely geometric effect arising from dynamical extra dimensions, avoiding ad hoc dark energy components while remaining consistent with supernova observations and CMB constraints.

Method: Constructs a (4+d)-dimensional spacetime with FLRW 4D submanifold and d-sphere compact extra dimensions; derives modified Friedmann equations via dimensional reduction and Einstein field equations; computes luminosity distance, deceleration parameter q, and Ωm effective parameters; performs phenomenological fitting to Type Ia supernova (SNe) data.

Result: The model yields an effective dark energy contribution from the Gaussian curvature and time-variation of the extra-dimensional scale factor; predicts q and Ωm 'dressed' by extra-dimensional terms; fits SNe data best with d = 3–4 contracting extra dimensions; leaves CMB angular power spectrum unchanged but does not resolve Hubble tension.

Conclusion: Dark energy may be a geometric artifact of dynamical compact extra dimensions—specifically their curvature and contraction—rather than a physical fluid or vacuum energy; this provides a testable, minimal alternative to ΛCDM with no new fields, though it does not alleviate the Hubble tension.

Abstract: We present a simple model of a higher dimensional spacetime in which the 4d submanifold is a FLRW metric, while the extra dimensions are compactified to a hypersphere. We calculate the Friedmann equations of this model finding that the extra dimensions interact in a non-trivial way with the 4d submanifold evolution. We prove that what is observed as Dark Energy could be explained only as an effect due to the Gaussian curvature and the expansion rate of the extra dimensions. We calculate the luminosity distance in this model and derive the acceleration parameter q. We find that Ωm and q are in some sense dressed by terms coming from the extra dimensions, thus a Universe filled only with matter (both luminous and dark) and with extra dimensions can explain the observational data. We find that the extra dimensions do not affect the CMB spectrum, but this is not a solution to the Hubble tension. We fit our model to SNe data and find evidence for the presence of 3-4 extra dimensions which are presently contracting.

</details>


### [190] [Kerr-referenced analytical parametrization of rotating black holes in dynamical Chern-Simons gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08302)
*Sardor Murodov,Bahodir Ahmedov,Bekzod Rahmatov,Javlon Rayimbaev,Bobomurat Ahmedov*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces an analytical parametrization of rotating black hole solutions in dynamical Chern-Simons gravity, built on Kerr geometry and validated against numerical solutions; it enables accurate strong-field calculations (e.g., light rings, ray tracing, shadows) without slow-rotation approximations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Numerical solutions for rotating black holes in dynamical Chern-Simons gravity are computationally expensive and not directly usable in strong-field astrophysical modeling; an efficient, accurate analytical metric is needed.

Method: Constructs a Kerr-referenced analytical metric using a compact radial coordinate, low-order angular multipoles, and continued fractions; calibrated to near-general-relativistic numerical solutions; validated via off-grid comparisons and geodesic observables (light rings, impact parameters, full 2D null ray tracing).

Result: A two-parameter family that accurately reproduces numerical metrics across weak-coupling and moderate-spin regimes; maintains fidelity for off-grid solutions; yields shadow observables matching numerical geometries within validation domain.

Conclusion: The proposed parametrization serves as a practical, non-perturbative, analytical metric for geodesic integration and strong-field tests in dynamical Chern-Simons gravity, bypassing limitations of slow-rotation expansions and numerical tabulations.

Abstract: Rotating black holes in dynamical Chern-Simons gravity are known nonperturbatively mainly through numerical solutions, which limits their direct use in strong-field applications. We construct a Kerr-referenced analytical parametrization of the near-general-relativistic numerical branch using a compact radial coordinate, low-order angular multipoles, and continued fractions. The resulting two-parameter family accurately reproduces the numerical metric throughout the validated weak-coupling and moderate-spin domain. Independent off-grid solutions confirm that the parametrization retains its accuracy away from the calibration set. We further assess the model through equatorial light rings, critical impact parameters, and full two-dimensional null-geodesic ray tracing without assuming separability. The resulting shadow observables closely match those obtained from the numerical geometries. This parametrization therefore provides a practical analytical metric for geodesic and strong-field calculations without relying on a slow-rotation expansion.

</details>


### [191] [Particle Dynamics and Thermodynamics of a Charged-Like Hairy Black Hole in Extended Gravitational Decoupling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08304)
*M. Zeeshan Gul,Ahmad Al-Badawi,J. Andrade,Sadia Zahid*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes particle dynamics and thermodynamics of a hairy black hole derived via extended geometric deformation gravitational decoupling from the Schwarzschild solution, satisfying the dominant energy condition and Q² = 2χℓM; it computes photon spheres, shadows, ISCOs, accretion efficiency, and thermodynamic quantities (Hawking temperature, Bekenstein-Hawking entropy, heat capacity) in the fixed-(Q,ℓ) ensemble.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To study how hair—introduced via extended geometric deformation gravitational decoupling—affects particle motion and thermodynamic behavior in black hole spacetimes, particularly testing consistency with energy conditions and astrophysically relevant observables (e.g., shadows, ISCO, accretion efficiency).

Method: Constructs a hairy black hole solution by applying extended geometric deformation gravitational decoupling to the Schwarzschild metric; imposes physical constraints (dominant energy condition, Q² = 2χℓM); analytically and numerically studies geodesic motion (massive and massless particles), effective potentials, circular orbit conditions, ISCO, radiative efficiency, and thermodynamic variables (Hawking temperature, Bekenstein-Hawking entropy, heat capacity) in the fixed-charge-and-length-scale (Q,ℓ) ensemble.

Result: The hairy black hole satisfies the dominant energy condition and admits well-defined photon spheres and black hole shadows; circular orbits for massive particles exist with calculable specific energy, angular momentum, ISCO radius, and radiative efficiency up to ~15%; thermodynamic analysis reveals Hawking temperature dependence on M, Q, ℓ, non-extensive Bekenstein-Hawking entropy, and a sign-changing heat capacity indicating phase transitions.

Conclusion: The decoupled hairy black hole is physically viable—supporting stable particle orbits and consistent thermodynamics—and offers a controlled framework to probe hair-induced deviations from Schwarzschild predictions in strong-field gravity, with implications for black hole imaging and accretion physics.

Abstract: This work focuses on the analysis of particle dynamics and the thermodynamic properties associated with a particular branch of hairy black hole solutions. These solutions are obtained by using extended geometric deformation gravitational decoupling on the seed solution of the Schwarzschild solution. This resulting geometry satisfies the dominant energy condition and the condition $Q^{2}=2χ\ell M$. We analyze the motion of massive particles and photons in this geometry and the thermodynamics of this solution. In particular, for massless particles, we study the effective potential and determine the radii of the photon sphere and the shadow of the black hole. For massive particles, we calculate the specific energy and angular momentum of circular orbits, the ISCO, the radiative efficiency of an accretion disk, and representative particle trajectories. Finally, we investigate the thermodynamic properties of this hairy black hole geometry, including the Hawking temperature, the Bekenstein-Hawking entropy, and the heat capacity in the fixed-$(Q,\ell)$ ensemble.

</details>


### [192] [Dynamical systems in quadratic teleparallel cosmology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08369)
*Manuel Hohmann*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes cosmological dynamics in general teleparallel quadratic gravity, reformulating field equations as a homogeneous dynamical system and identifying projective fixed points, finite-time singularities, and asymptotically vanishing expansion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the cosmological implications and dynamical behavior of general teleparallel quadratic gravity, a modified gravity theory beyond General Relativity.

Method: Reformulates the cosmological field equations using a suitable variable set to obtain a homogeneous dynamical system; applies dynamical systems analysis to identify fixed points, singularities, and asymptotic behavior.

Result: Identifies generic projective fixed points admitting analytical solutions; reveals presence of finite-time singularities and asymptotically vanishing Hubble expansion.

Conclusion: General teleparallel quadratic gravity exhibits rich cosmological dynamics—including analytically tractable fixed points, singularities, and late-time suppression of expansion—highlighting its distinct phenomenology compared to standard GR-based models.

Abstract: We discuss the cosmological dynamics of general teleparallel quadratic gravity. Using a suitable set of variables, we write the cosmological field equations as a homogeneous dynamical system. We find that this system generically features projective fixed points, where the dynamics can be solved analytically, and exhibits finite time singularities as well as an asymptotically vanishing cosmological expansion.

</details>


### [193] [Picard Iteration for the Characteristic Initial Value Problem in Einstein Equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08421)
*Shengrong Wu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces an iterative algorithm in double-null gauge to solve vacuum and Einstein scalar-field equations by converting non-linear PDEs into ODE systems, implemented numerically using characteristic constraints, spectral elements, spherical operators, Galerkin projection, and rigorous residual/consistency verification.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a robust, high-accuracy numerical framework for solving the Einstein equations coupled to a scalar field in vacuum, overcoming challenges from non-linearity and coordinate singularities in spherical symmetry.

Method: Iteration algorithm in double-null gauge; numerical implementation via characteristic constraint solves, Legendre–Gauss–Lobatto (LGL) spectral elements, pole-free spherical differential operators, Galerkin projection, and dual first-order residual and consistency checks.

Result: Successful transformation of non-linear PDEs into tractable ODE systems with verified numerical stability, accuracy, and absence of coordinate-related artifacts (e.g., poles).

Conclusion: The method provides a spectrally accurate, geometrically faithful, and computationally reliable approach for simulating dynamical spacetimes with scalar fields in double-null coordinates.

Abstract: We present an iteration algorithm for vacuum and Einstein scalar-field equations in double-null gauge, which transform the non-linear PDE into systems of ODE. The numerical realization combines characteristic constraint solves, LGL spectral elements, pole-free spherical operators, Galerkin projection, and independent first-order residual and consistency checks.

</details>


### [194] [Black holes with torsion hair in cubic Holst-type Poincaré gauge gravity: from singular to regular geometries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08641)
*Sebastian Bahamonde,Jorge Gigante Valcarcel,Matteo Magi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper extends the Holst quadratic model in Poincaré Gauge (PG) theory by adding cubic curvature–torsion invariants, yielding new black hole solutions—both singular (e.g., Kiselev- and Boulware-Deser-like) and regular—with dynamical torsion that violates causal convergence conditions, thereby evading PG singularity theorems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the singularity theorems of Poincaré Gauge theory by constructing extended gravitational actions that allow evasion via torsion-driven geometric modifications.

Method: Introducing cubic-order curvature–torsion invariants into the Holst action; analyzing irreducible torsion modes (including parity components); solving field equations for static, spherically symmetric configurations; classifying solutions by singularity structure and torsion hair.

Result: New exact black hole solutions: (i) singular ones with Kiselev-, Boulware-Deser-, and Lambert W-type metric corrections; (ii) regular black holes with primary and secondary torsion hair; all enabled by pseudoscalar-dominated kinetic structure and nonlinear torsion interactions violating causal convergence.

Conclusion: Holst-type PG models with cubic torsion–curvature couplings support a rich black hole phenomenology and provide concrete mechanisms to evade PG singularity theorems through torsion-induced violation of standard energy and causality conditions.

Abstract: Motivated by the singularity theorems of Poincaré Gauge (PG) theory, we investigate extensions of the Holst quadratic model by introducing cubic order invariants constructed from the curvature and torsion tensors into the gravitational action. Such models are characterised by a kinetic structure that is governed by a pseudoscalar mode, whereas the remaining irreducible modes of torsion contribute through nonlinear interactions that can have important implications for the space-time geometry. In particular, in line with other well-known models of PG theory, the Birkhoff theorem does not hold in general, allowing for new exact static and spherically symmetric black hole solutions with dynamical torsion. Across the different torsion sectors, corresponding to the irreducible modes and parity components of the torsion field involved in the analysis, we find both singular and regular configurations. Among the singular solutions, we obtain Kiselev-like and Boulware-Deser-like geometries, as well as new geometries with distinct algebraic and Lambert $W$ metric corrections. In addition, we find regular black holes with both primary and secondary torsion hair, which evade the singularity theorems through violations of the causal convergence conditions induced by the nonlinear torsion interactions. Therefore, we show that Holst-type PG models can support a rich variety of black hole geometries, while providing explicit mechanisms for evading the singularity theorems of PG theory.

</details>


### [195] [Persistence of measurement-induced nonlocality in uniformly accelerating Unruh-DeWitt detectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08653)
*Shi-Pu Gu,Ming-Ming Du,Yu-Bo Sheng,Lan Zhou*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates how measurement-induced nonlocality (MIN) responds to the Unruh effect in uniformly accelerating Unruh-DeWitt detectors coupled to a massless scalar field. Contrary to prior field-mode results showing monotonic suppression of MIN with acceleration, the authors find MIN exhibits rich, initial-state-dependent behavior—decreasing, vanishing then recovering, or increasing—with acceleration; notably, for many states, MIN asymptotes to a nonzero value at infinite acceleration (infinite Unruh temperature), demonstrating non-universality of MIN suppression.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether the previously observed vanishing of measurement-induced nonlocality (MIN) under infinite acceleration is a generic consequence of the Unruh effect or an artifact of the specific physical model (e.g., field-mode vs. detector-based description).

Method: Analytical and numerical analysis of two uniformly accelerating Unruh-DeWitt detectors interacting with a massless scalar quantum field in 3+1D Minkowski spacetime; computation of MIN as a function of Unruh temperature for various initial detector states (including Bell-diagonal and non-Bell-diagonal states); comparison with prior bosonic field-mode results.
