Result: MIN exhibits three qualitatively distinct behaviors depending on the initial detector state: (i) monotonic decrease to zero, (ii) vanishing at finite Unruh temperature followed by recovery, or (iii) monotonic increase; crucially, for a broad class of initial states, MIN approaches a nonzero asymptotic value in the infinite-acceleration (infinite-Unruh-temperature) limit.

Conclusion: The suppression of MIN under strong acceleration is not universal; rather, Unruh-induced detector dynamics leads to a state-dependent response where MIN can be suppressed, restored, or enhanced—challenging the notion that the Unruh effect generically erases all forms of nonclassical correlation beyond entanglement.

Abstract: Uniform acceleration induces a thermal response to the Minkowski vacuum and can thereby modify quantum correlations. For measurement-induced nonlocality (MIN), a form of nonclassical correlation distinct from entanglement, previous field-mode analyses of bosonic fields found that it decreases with increasing acceleration and vanishes in the infinite-acceleration limit. This raises the question of whether the disappearance of MIN is a generic consequence of the Unruh effect or depends on the physical description of the accelerated quantum system. To address this question, we study two uniformly accelerating Unruh-DeWitt detectors interacting with a massless scalar field. We find that the response of MIN to the Unruh temperature depends sensitively on the initial detector state: it can decrease monotonically, vanish at an intermediate temperature and subsequently recover, or increase monotonically. Moreover, for a broad class of initial states, MIN approaches a nonzero value in the high-temperature limit. These results show that the suppression of MIN under strong acceleration is not universal. Instead, Unruh-induced detector dynamics gives rise to a state-dependent response in which measurement-induced nonlocality can be suppressed, restored, or enhanced.

</details>


### [196] [Neutral scalar particle in a charged wormhole background](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08821)
*Matheus D. de Oliveira,Alexandre G. M. Schmidt*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies bound states of a neutral scalar particle in a charged traversable wormhole spacetime with harmonic/modified harmonic oscillator couplings, solving the Klein–Gordon equation to obtain quantized energies via polynomial solutions of a generalized Heun equation; the spectrum depends on wormhole charge indirectly through geometry, and limits recover uncharged wormhole and standard harmonic oscillator cases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how spacetime geometry—specifically that of a charged traversable wormhole—affects quantum bound states of neutral particles under oscillator-type potentials, bridging gravitational, electromagnetic, and quantum effects in curved spacetime.

Method: Solving the Klein–Gordon equation for a neutral scalar field in a charged traversable wormhole metric; reducing the radial equation to a generalized Heun equation; imposing polynomial truncation conditions to obtain bound-state energy eigenvalues and quantized oscillator frequencies; analyzing parametric dependencies (charge Q, throat radius b, angular momentum ℓ, coupling V₀).

Result: Bound-state energy spectrum and quantized oscillator frequencies are obtained analytically via Heun polynomial solutions; energy levels depend on electric charge Q only through geometric deformation (no direct coupling), and exhibit nontrivial dependence on ℓ, b, Q, and V₀; limiting cases correctly reproduce known results for uncharged wormholes and flat-space harmonic oscillators.

Conclusion: Even for electrically neutral particles, wormhole charge influences quantum spectra via spacetime curvature—demonstrating a geometric 'electromagnetic imprint'; the generalized Heun framework provides an exact solvable model for quantum systems in nontrivial charged gravitational backgrounds.

Abstract: We investigate the dynamics of a neutral scalar particle in a charged traversable wormhole background, considering harmonic and modified harmonic oscillator couplings. By solving the Klein--Gordon equation, the radial problem is reduced to a generalized Heun equation, whose polynomial truncation yields the bound-state energy spectrum and quantized oscillator frequencies. Although the particle is electrically neutral, the energy spectrum depends indirectly on the wormhole charge through the geometric deformation of spacetime. We also analyze the dependence of the oscillator frequency on the angular momentum, throat parameter, electric charge, and the additional interaction parameter $V_{0}$. The appropriate limits recover the corresponding results for the uncharged wormhole and the standard harmonic oscillator.

</details>


### [197] [Can the Universe Change Signature When Gravity is Dynamical?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08964)
*Yaghoub Heydarzade*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates whether a regular Euclidean–Lorentzian signature transition is possible in scalar-tensor gravity with dynamical gravitational coupling $F(\phi)R$, showing that such transitions can occur under frame-dependent regularity conditions—specifically, total geodesy in the Jordan frame—and yield distinct integrable solution classes with potentially superaccelerating cosmology near the transition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether a smooth, regular Euclidean–Lorentzian signature transition is viable when the gravitational coupling is dynamical (i.e., in nonminimally coupled scalar-tensor theories), and to assess frame dependence of transition properties such as regularity, transversality, and cosmological behavior.

Method: Analyzes scalar-tensor gravity with action $F(\phi)R$; maps FLRW sector to Einstein frame for $F>0$; identifies frame-invariant (e.g., existence/transversality of signature change) vs. frame-dependent (e.g., extrinsic geometry, total geodesy) features; imposes total geodesy in the physical Jordan frame as a regularity condition; constructs two exactly integrable solution classes—the oscillator-ghost-oscillator branch and the critical exponential-potential branch—and compares their transition properties, including scalar dynamics and effective equation-of-state behavior.

Result: Two integrable classes of regular signature-change solutions are found: (1) oscillator-ghost-oscillator branch, where the scalar is stationary at the transition and Jordan-frame total geodesy holds under mild regularity of $F$; (2) critical exponential-potential branch, requiring a specific matching condition involving $H_E$, $d\ln F/d\psi$, and $\dot\psi$ at the transition surface $\Sigma$. Total geodesy is shown to be weaker than Kossowski–Kriele smoothness. Crucially, although the Einstein-frame scalar cannot yield phantom behavior, the Jordan-frame expansion can become locally superaccelerating near $\Sigma$ if $(d\ln F/d\psi)_\Sigma > 0$.

Conclusion: Regular Euclidean–Lorentzian signature transitions persist beyond Einstein gravity in scalar-tensor theories with nonminimal coupling, but their existence, regularity, and physical interpretation (e.g., acceleration, geodesic completeness) are intrinsically frame dependent—highlighting the physical primacy of the Jordan frame for observational and geometric criteria like total geodesy.

Abstract: Can a universe undergo a regular Euclidean-Lorentzian signature transition when the gravitational coupling itself is dynamical? We address this question in scalar-tensor gravity with nonminimal coupling $F(φ)R$. Although the spatially flat Friedmann-Lemaitre-Robertson-Walker (FLRW) sector can be mapped to the Einstein frame for $F>0$ and a nondegenerate scalar redefinition, not all transition properties are frame independent. For a finite, positive, and sufficiently regular conformal factor, the existence and transverse character of the type change are preserved, whereas the extrinsic geometry is not. We therefore use the Einstein frame as a solution-generating representation and impose total geodesy in the physical Jordan frame. We construct two exactly integrable classes of solutions. In the oscillator-ghost-oscillator branch, the scalar field is stationary at the transition and Jordan-frame total geodesy follows under suitable regularity assumptions on the conformal factor. In the critical exponential-potential branch, the scalar is generically nonstationary and total geodesy instead requires \begin{equation*} H_E\big|_Σ=
\frac12 \left( \frac{d\ln F}{dψ} \right)_Σ\dotψ_Σ. \end{equation*} We also distinguish this condition from the stronger smoothness requirements of a Kossowski-Kriele-type transverse metric. Finally, while the canonical Einstein-frame scalar does not allow effective phantom evolution, the dynamical nonminimal coupling can generate a locally superaccelerating Jordan-frame regime near the transition when $\left(\frac{d\ln F}{dψ}\right)_Σ>0$ for the chosen oscillator branch. Thus signature change persists beyond Einstein gravity, with regularity and effective cosmological behavior remaining intrinsically frame sensitive.

</details>


### [198] [TARTARUS: A High-Performance Python Code for Ray Tracing in Curved Spacetimes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08989)
*David F. Bambague,Alexis Larrañaga*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: TARTARUS is a modular, Python-based ray-tracing framework for computing null geodesics around compact objects, accelerated via Numba JIT compilation, supporting multiple ODE solvers, event detection, and analytical/numerical metrics; validated on Kerr black hole shadows and thin disks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The EHT observations of M87* and Sgr A* necessitate robust, efficient, and accessible numerical tools for modeling light propagation in strong-field gravity to compare theoretical accretion models with data.

Method: Development of TARTARUS—a modular, extensible Python framework using Numba JIT compilation for performance; implements adaptive embedded Runge-Kutta, Bulirsch-Stoer, and Verlet integrators; includes event-detection for horizons/disks and supports both analytical (e.g., Kerr) and numerically generated spacetime metrics.

Result: Demonstrated high physical accuracy (e.g., Hamiltonian constraint conservation < 1e-12) and computational efficiency; successfully reproduced Kerr black hole shadows and Novikov-Thorne thin disk images.

Conclusion: TARTARUS bridges accessibility and performance in relativistic ray tracing, enabling flexible, reliable, and scalable modeling of photon trajectories for EHT and future VLBI science.

Abstract: The recent observations of the supermassive black holes M87* and Sagittarius A* by the Event Horizon Telescope collaboration (EHT) have shown the need for robust, efficient, and accessible numerical tools to model light propagation in strongly curved spacetimes. Comparing theoretical accretion models with observational data relies heavily on ray-tracing algorithms that calculate the trajectories of photons traveling from a source plasma to a distant observer. In this paper, we present TARTARUS (Tracer for Astrophysical Ray Trajectories Around Relativistic Ultra-compact Sources), a new, highly modular, and extensible Python-based framework for computing null geodesics around compact objects. By leveraging Just-In-Time (JIT) compilation via Numba, TARTARUS bridges the gap between Python's high-level accessibility and the execution speed of compiled languages like C++ or FORTRAN. The code supports multiple native solvers (including adaptive embedded Runge-Kutta pairs, a Bulirsch-Stoer extrapolator, and a Verlet scheme), event-detection mechanics for handling structural intersections (e.g. event horizons and accretion disks), and support for both analytical and numerically generated background metrics. We demonstrate the code's physical accuracy and computational efficiency through some tests, including the evaluation of the Hamiltonian constraint conservation and ray-tracing of the shadow and the Novikov-Thorne thin accretion disks around a Kerr black hole.

</details>


### [199] [Second-order Teukolsky calculations for nonspinning, quasicircular binaries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08995)
*Benjamin Leather,Andrew Spiers,Adam Pound,Samuel D. Upton,Barry Wardell,Leanne Durkan,Niels Warburton*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a novel second-order gravitational self-force calculation scheme based on the Teukolsky equation—enabling future extension to Kerr spacetimes—and implements it for quasicircular orbits in Schwarzschild, incorporating hyperboloidal slicing, Bondi-Sachs gauge transformation, and hybrid spectral/variation-of-parameters methods; it also demonstrates reduced infrared divergences in Teukolsky formalism and their elimination in Bondi-Sachs gauge, with asymptotic energy fluxes benchmarked against prior Lorenz-gauge results.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the limitation of existing second-order self-force calculations—which rely on Lorenz-gauge Einstein equations separable only in Schwarzschild—to enable extension to astrophysically realistic Kerr black holes.

Method: A new computational scheme using the second-order Teukolsky equation, combined with compactified hyperboloidal slicing, gauge transformation to Bondi-Sachs coordinates, and a hybrid numerical approach (spectral + variation-of-parameters); applied to quasicircular orbits in Schwarzschild spacetime.

Result: Demonstrated reduced infrared divergences in the Teukolsky-based formulation and their complete removal in Bondi-Sachs gauge; computed asymptotic gravitational-wave energy fluxes consistent with prior Lorenz-gauge results.

Conclusion: The Teukolsky-based scheme is viable, more robust against infrared issues, and—unlike Lorenz-gauge approaches—naturally extensible to Kerr backgrounds, paving the way for physically accurate second-order self-force modeling in realistic binary systems.

Abstract: Currently, the only second-order gravitational self-force calculations have been based on directly solving the perturbative Einstein equations in the Lorenz gauge. That method relied on the complete separability of the Einstein equations in a Schwarzschild background. In this paper, we present a new scheme based on the second-order Teukolsky equation. Crucially, this method promises to extend (reasonably straightforwardly) to the more realistic case of a Kerr background. Here we implement the scheme in the simplest setting of quasicircular orbits around a Schwarzschild black hole. In addition to working with the Teukolsky equation, our scheme incorporates several other advances over previous second-order self-force calculations: compactified hyperboloidal slicing, transformation to a Bondi-Sachs gauge, and a combination of spectral and variation-of-parameters methods. We also use these tools to re-examine the infrared divergences that arise in second-order Lorenz-gauge calculations, showing they are less pronounced in the Teukolsky case and completely eliminated in the Bondi-Sachs gauge. We conclude by calculating the asymptotic energy fluxes and benchmarking them against previous Lorenz-gauge calculations.

</details>


### [200] [Quantum geometric signatures in neutrino dynamics around a holonomy black hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09079)
*A. A. Araújo Filho*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies neutrino flavor oscillations and annihilation in a holonomy-corrected Schwarzschild spacetime, deriving deflection angles, semiclassical phases, and flavor probabilities; it finds observable signatures—including displaced oscillation fringes, trajectory-flavor entanglement (concurrence ~0.23), and enhanced annihilation power (+39.3%), with constraints on holonomy parameters from power excess limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe quantum gravity effects—specifically holonomy corrections from loop quantum gravity—on astrophysical neutrino propagation and interactions near compact objects, using flavor oscillations and annihilation as sensitive probes of spacetime geometry beyond classical GR.

Method: Analytical derivation of weak-deflection angle to second post-Minkowskian order in effective holonomy-corrected Schwarzschild metric; semiclassical WKB treatment for radial/nonradial neutrino trajectories; construction of two-image flavor transition probability incorporating magnifications, Fermat phases, and wave packet overlap; quantification of trajectory-flavor entanglement via I-concurrence and entropy; modeling of neutrino-antineutrino annihilation outside an effective neutrinosphere with holonomy-modified proper volume measure.

Result: Logarithmic holonomy contribution to cross-path phase retains sensitivity to absolute neutrino mass scale; electron-neutrino trajectory-flavor concurrence ≈ 0.23, entropy ≈ 0.10 bit; oscillation fringes are displaced and channel distribution (e, μ, τ) is geometry-dependent; annihilation power increases up to +39.3% vs. Schwarzschild; from a 10% power excess limit, bounds emerge: a/(2M) ≤ 0.2879 and |λ| ≤ 0.6358 at M/R_ν = 1/3.

Conclusion: Holonomy corrections induce measurable, nontrivial modifications to neutrino flavor dynamics and annihilation in strong-field gravity—offering a novel phenomenological window into Planck-scale geometry via multi-messenger neutrino astronomy, with concrete observational signatures and parameter constraints.

Abstract: We investigate three flavor neutrino phenomena in an effective holonomy corrected Schwarzschild geometry. We derive the weak-deflection angle through second post-Minkowskian order, obtain the semiclassical phases along radial and nonradial trajectories, and formulate the two-image flavor probability by including magnifications, Fermat phases, and wave packet overlap. The cross path phase acquires a logarithmic holonomy contribution and can retain information about the absolute neutrino mass scale. We also characterize flavor mode correlations and trajectory-flavor entanglement through the balance among path predictability, interference visibility, and I-concurrence. Neutrino-antineutrino annihilation outside an effective neutrinosphere is examined, with holonomy entering the integrated power through the radial proper volume measure. For a solar mass lens and $10\,\mathrm{MeV}$ neutrinos, the numerical results show displaced oscillation fringes and a geometry dependent redistribution among the electron, muon, and tau channels. The electron-neutrino trajectory-flavor concurrence reaches approximately $0.23$, with an entropy near $0.10$ bit, while remaining weakly sensitive to the mass ordering in the selected configuration. By contrast, the annihilation power increases monotonically, reaching an increase of approximately $39.3\%$ relative to Schwarzschild spacetime (for a particular configuration, i.e., $λ=2$ and $M/R_ν=1/3$). Furthermore, at fixed source parameters and $M/R_ν=1/3$, an assumed maximum excess of $10\%$ in the integrated annihilation power relative to Schwarzschild yields the conditional bounds $a/(2M)\leq0.2879$ and $|λ|\leq0.6358$.

</details>


### [201] [Breaking binary formation mechanism degeneracies with gravitational wave clustering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09083)
*Nicola Bellomo,Michele Bosi,Sarah Libanore,Michele Liguori,Michela Mapelli,Federico Semenzato,Stefano Torniamenti*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes using gravitational wave (GW) anisotropy and cross-correlations with galaxy catalogs to break degeneracies in binary black hole population models, demonstrating sensitivity to time-delay distributions and host-environment properties—thereby distinguishing isolated vs. dynamical formation channels.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Despite growing GW event statistics (~400 detections), theoretical understanding of BBH formation remains limited due to parameter degeneracies: the number of free model parameters exceeds the number of constrained population-level properties (e.g., mass, spin, distance).

Method: Analyzes statistical anisotropy (clustering) of GW events via angular power spectrum and cross-correlation with galaxy surveys; compares model-agnostic time-delay distributions and physically motivated formation channels (isolated evolution vs. globular cluster dynamics); computes GW bias as a function of delay time and host environment.

Result: GW spatial clustering is uniquely sensitive to both the time-delay distribution and host-galaxy properties; cross-correlation with galaxy catalogs robustly distinguishes models with different delays or formation channels (e.g., isolated vs. dynamical); provides a new, complementary probe beyond standard population inference.

Conclusion: Gravitational wave anisotropy and galaxy–GW cross-correlations constitute a powerful, orthogonal data-analysis strategy to constrain binary black hole formation physics—effectively 'chipping away' at the landscape of degenerate theoretical models.

Abstract: Thanks to the almost 400 gravitational wave events detected, we are currently able to grasp the fundamental features of black hole mass, spin, and distance distributions. However, such a fast increase in the precision of the measurements does not necessarily correspond to a better theoretical understanding of gravitational wave sources, especially in current scenarios where the number of free parameters is significantly larger than the number of inferred properties of the black hole population. In this work, we showcase how the landscape of theoretical models can be chipped away by complementary data-analysis strategies, in particular by studying the statistical properties of gravitational wave anisotropic distribution. Specifically, we show how gravitational wave clustering is sensitive to two unique features of each binary formation mechanism: the time-delay distribution and the properties of the binary hosts. First, we consider a model-agnostic scenario and show the impact that different time-delay distributions have on the gravitational wave bias. Then, we consider a realistic scenario where gravitational wave events are sourced either by isolated binary evolution or dynamical processes in globular clusters, and study how the gravitational wave bias is unique sensitive to the specific properties of the environment. In both scenarios, we show how the cross-correlation between galaxy and gravitational wave catalogs is able to distinguish between models with different time delays or with different binary sub-populations originated in specific formation channels.

</details>


### [202] [NRHJSur3dq8: a spectral numerical-relativity surrogate family for non-eccentric aligned-spin binary-black-hole waveforms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09088)
*Vaishak Prasad*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: NRHJSur3dq8 is a fast, accurate, and differentiable numerical-relativity surrogate for aligned-spin, non-eccentric binary black hole waveforms, built using action-angle parameterization, hp-adaptive Chebyshev elements, and Gaussian process regression; it delivers sub-10⁻⁵ mismatches, analytic derivatives, calibrated uncertainty estimates, and millisecond evaluation times.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome computational bottlenecks and limited differentiability in existing NR surrogates, enabling efficient Bayesian inference and waveform systematics studies for LIGO/Virgo/KAGRA.

Method: Constructs a surrogate using an action-angle parameterization to separate slow radiation-reaction evolution from rapid orbital motion; represents slowly varying quantities on shared hp-adaptive Chebyshev elements; regresses across intrinsic parameters (mass ratio, spins) via Gaussian process models; appends a time-parameterized merger-ringdown model where adiabaticity breaks; hybridizes with PN inspiral; provides analytic derivatives and GP-based predictive uncertainties.

Result: Achieves median (2,2)-mode mismatch of 1.3×10⁻⁶ and all-mode mismatch of 2.3×10⁻⁶ in leave-one-out validation; full-IMR in-sample mismatch ranges from 8.9×10⁻⁷ to 2.8×10⁻⁵ across 40–120 M⊙; evaluates full IMR waveform in 12 ms (single CPU) or 0.39 ms per waveform in batch (4096).

Conclusion: NRHJSur3dq8 sets a new standard for accuracy, speed, and functional utility among NR surrogates—enabling high-fidelity, gradient-based parameter estimation and robust uncertainty quantification for gravitational-wave astrophysics.

Abstract: We present NRHJSur3dq8, a family of numerical-relativity (NR) surrogates for non-eccentric aligned-spin binary-black-hole waveforms inspired by an action-angle parameterization. The model provides both the dynamics: the Hamiltonian values, azimuthal action $J_φ(Φ)$, the orbital clock $τ(Φ)$ as functions of its phasing, and the radiative pieces: the waveforms, their near-analytic derivatives, and modeling errors associated with each. The surrogated variables are conditioned into pieces that vary slowly on the radiation-reaction timescale, represented on shared $hp$-adaptive Chebyshev elements, and regressed across the intrinsic parameters with a Gaussian-process model. A time-parameterized merger-ringdown set of elements is attached where the adiabatic description breaks down. Leave-one-out validation of the central NRHJSur3dq8_AA flavor against NR reaches a $(2,2)$ unweighted mismatch of $1.3^{+32}_{-1.2}\times10^{-6}$ and an all-mode mismatch of $2.3^{+59}_{-2.2}\times10^{-6}$ over the full inspiral-merger-ringdown span. Sky-averaged and SNR-weighted over $32$ orientations per simulation, the released model's full-IMR in-sample mismatch against the NR simulations used for training is $8.9\times10^{-7}$ to $2.8\times10^{-5}$ across total masses $40$-$120\,M_\odot$. Waveforms are hybridized using post-Newtonian waveforms. Beyond accuracy, NRHJSur3dq8 provides two capabilities uncommon in NR surrogates: nearly analytic derivatives of the strain with respect to the physical parameters, and a calibrated Gaussian-process predictive uncertainty of the model. A full non-hybridized inspiral-merger-ringdown waveform evaluates in $12$ ms on a single CPU call. Batched evaluation amortizes this to $0.39$ ms per waveform at a batch of $4096$, measured at a starting frequency of $20$ Hz.

</details>

J. Zhu,W. D. Zhu,W. J. Zhu,W. Z. Zhu,Y. C. Zhu,Z. A. Zhu,X. Y. Zhuang,M. Zhuge,J. H. Zou,J. Zu*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: First simultaneous measurement of weak decay parameters φ_Ω⁻ and φ_Ω̄⁺ in ψ(3686) → Ω⁻Ω̄⁺, yielding CP-sensitive observable φ_CP = −0.004 ± 0.055 ± 0.017 rad; also determines P–D wave weak/strong phase differences and Ω⁻–Ω̄⁺ polarization correlations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe CP violation in the charmonium sector via hyperon–antihyperon decays and test Standard Model predictions through angular analysis of ψ(3686) → Ω⁻Ω̄⁺.

Method: Joint full angular distribution analysis using (2.71 ± 0.01) × 10⁹ ψ(3686) events from BESIII; simultaneous fit to Ω⁻ → ΛK⁻ and Ω̄⁺ → Λ̄K⁺ decays to extract weak decay parameters, CP asymmetry φ_CP, and P- vs D-wave phase differences.

Result: φ_CP = (−0.004 ± 0.055 ± 0.017) rad; (ξ_D − ξ_P) = (−0.15 ± 2.25 ± 0.69) rad; (δ_D − δ_P) = (−0.97 ± 0.88 ± 0.34) rad; first observation of Ω⁻–Ω̄⁺ spin correlation parameters.

Conclusion: No significant CP violation observed; results consistent with SM expectations; provides benchmark for future precision tests of CP symmetry in baryonic decays.

Abstract: Using $(2.71 \pm 0.01) \times 10^9$ $ψ(3686)$ events collected with the BESIII detector, a joint full angular distribution analysis is carried out for the process $ψ(3686) \to Ω^-(\toΛK^-) \, \barΩ^{+}(\to \barΛK^+)$. The first simultaneous measurement of the weak decay parameters $φ_{Ω^{-}}$ and $φ_{\barΩ^{+}}$ for $Ω^- \to K^-Λ$ and $\barΩ^+ \to K^+\barΛ$ is performed, yielding the first result for the CP-sensitive observable, $φ_{\rm CP} = (-0.004 \pm 0.055 \pm 0.017)~\text{rad}$, where the first and second uncertainties are statistical and systematic, respectively. This further enables the extraction of the weak and strong phase differences between the $P$- and $D$-wave amplitudes: $(ξ_D - ξ_P) = (-0.15 \pm 2.25 \pm 0.69)~\text{rad}$ and $(δ_D - δ_P) = (-0.97 \pm 0.88 \pm 0.34)~\text{rad}$. Additionally, the polarization correlations between $Ω^{-}$ and $\barΩ^{+}$ are measured.

</details>


### [204] [Prospective study of light dark matter search in positron beam mode with DarkSHINE experiment initiative](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05900)
*Xu-Liang Zhu,Shu Li,Kun Liu,Haidar Mus'ud Alfanda,Tong Sun,Haijun Yang,Yi-Fan Zhu*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: DarkSHINE simulation study shows positron-on-target enhances dark photon (A′) invisible decay sensitivity via new annihilation channels, improving exclusion limits by ~100× over electron-on-target near threshold.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve sensitivity to invisibly decaying dark photons by exploiting additional production channels (t-channel annihilation and resonant s-channel) available in positron-on-fixed-target (POT) configurations, which are absent in conventional electron-on-fixed-target (EOT) experiments.

Method: Full Geant4 detector simulation of an 8 GeV positron beam on fixed target with 3×10¹⁴ POT events; analysis using missing energy/momentum signatures across three orthogonal signal regions optimized for bremsstrahlung, non-resonant t-channel (e⁺e⁻N → γA′N), and resonant s-channel (e⁺e⁻N → A′N) production.

Result: The inclusion of annihilation channels significantly enhances sensitivity to A′ → invisible decays, yielding exclusion limits ∼100 times stronger than current EOT-based constraints, especially near the e⁺e⁻ center-of-mass energy threshold.

Conclusion: Positron-on-fixed-target is a highly competitive approach for probing light dark photons with invisible decays, offering substantial gains over electron-based schemes due to complementary production mechanisms.

Abstract: We perform the prospective study on searching for invisibly decayed dark photons ($A^{\prime}$) in positron-on-fixed-target (POT) scheme with the DarkSHINE experiment simulation setup. In the positron-on-fixed-target scheme, two additional production channels of dark photons via annihilation with extranuclear electrons are present: non-resonant t-channel ($e^+e^-N \to γA^{\prime}N$) and resonant s-channel ($e^+e^-N \to A^{\prime}N$), alongside bremsstrahlung emission similar to electron-on-fixed-target scheme. Based on a Geant4 full detector simulation of an 8 GeV positron beam with $3 \times 10^{14}$ POT events, we developed an analysis strategy using missing energy and momentum signatures across three orthogonal signal regions optimized for these channels. Our results indicate that the additional annihilation channels of dark photon production improve the dark photon invisible decay search sensitivity, improving the exclusion limits by approximately two orders of magnitude compared to existing constraints in the electron-on-fixed target (EOT) scheme, particularly near the center-of-mass energy threshold.

</details>


### [205] [Likelihood-Based Unsupervised Anomaly Detection in CMS Dijet Events](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06686)
*Bhavishya Chebrolu,Hitesh Rasineni,Prajwal Aaryan Immadi*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper introduces an unsupervised anomaly detection method using neural spline flows to model Standard Model dijet backgrounds directly from CMS Open Data; it identifies statistically significant, multivariate deviations in jet substructure—not localized resonances—demonstrating feasibility for model-independent BSM searches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable model-independent, unsupervised discovery of anomalous collider events without reliance on pre-specified signal hypotheses, addressing limitations of traditional cut-based or supervised BSM searches.

Method: Neural spline flow—a type of normalizing flow—is trained on high-dimensional features (jet-, dijet-, and event-level observables) from CMS Open Data to estimate the background density; anomalies are defined as low-likelihood events, followed by rigorous validation including mass decorrelation, permutation null tests, and stability analysis.

Result: Identified anomalous events show consistent, multivariate deviations—especially in jet substructure observables—with no narrow excess in dijet invariant mass; anomalies are kinematically broad and robust against known unsupervised learning biases.

Conclusion: Neural spline flows provide a sensitive, model-agnostic tool for detecting structured, rare deviations in LHC data, validating their utility as exploratory instruments for physics beyond the Standard Model.

Abstract: We present an unsupervised search for anomalous dijet events in proton--proton collision data using neural spline flow density estimation. A normalizing flow model is trained on a high-dimensional feature space comprising jet, dijet, and event-level observables to learn the dominant Standard Model background directly from data, without assuming a specific signal hypothesis. Events assigned low likelihood under the learned density are identified as potential anomalous events.
  Using this approach on a CMS Open Data dijet sample, we investigate extreme events in the tail of the anomaly-score distribution and perform an extensive validation and robustness study. This study includes feature-level statistical comparisons, mass decorrelation tests, permutation-based null tests, and evaluations of training stability. The selected anomalous events exhibit notable departures from the background-only expectation, primarily in jet-substructure observables, while remaining stable under several known sources of bias in unsupervised learning.
  The identified anomalies are distributed across the kinematic phase space and do not exhibit a narrow structure in the dijet invariant-mass spectrum. Instead, they show correlated deviations across multiple observables, consistent with a multivariate difference in jet substructure and event topology rather than a localized resonance. While no claim of new physics is made, this study demonstrates that neural spline flow-based density estimation can be sensitive to rare, structured deviations in collider data and may provide a model-independent exploratory tool for searches for physics beyond the Standard Model at the LHC.

</details>


### [206] [Neutron detector response modeling in NOvA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06956)
*NOvA Collaboration,S. Abubakar,M. A. Acero,B. Acharya,P. Adamson,N. Anfimov,A. Antoshkin,E. Arrieta-Diaz,L. Asquith,A. Aurisano,A. Back,N. Balashov,P. Baldi,B. A. Bambah,E. F. Bannister,A. Barros,J. Barrow,A. Bat,T. J. C. Bezerra,V. Bhatnagar,B. Bhuyan,J. Bian,A. C. Booth,B. Brahma,C. Bromberg,N. Buchanan,J. Burns,A. Butkevich,E. Catano-Mur,J. P. Cesar,C. Chang,S. Chaudhary,H. Chen,S. Choate,B. C. Choudhary,O. T. K. Chow,A. Christensen,M. F. Cicala,T. E. Coan,T. Contreras,A. Cooleybeck,L. Cremonesi,G. S. Davies,P. F. Derwent,K. Dever,Z. Djurcic,K. Dobbs,D. Dueñas Tonguino,E. C. Dukes,A. Dye,R. Ehrlich,E. Ewart,P. Filip,M. J. Frank,H. R. Gallagher,A. Giri,R. A. Gomes,M. C. Goodman,R. Group,A. Gusmão,A. Habig,F. Hakl,J. Hartnell,R. Hatcher,J. M. Hays,M. He,K. Heller,V Hewes,A. Himmel,T. Horoho,X. Huang,T. Huynh,A. Ivanova,K. Kaess,I. Kakorin,A. Kalitkina,D. M. Kaplan,A. Khanam,B. Kirezli,J. Kleykamp,O. Klimov,L. W. Koerner,L. Kolupaeva,G. Kufatty,A. Kumar,C. D. Kuruppu,V. Kus,T. Lackey,K. Lang,A. Lister,J. Liu,J. A. Lock,S. Magill,W. A. Mann,M. T. Manoharan,M. Manrique Plata,A. Marathe,M. L. Marshak,M. Martinez-Casales,V. Matveev,A. Medhi,B. Mehta,M. D. Messier,H. Meyer,T. Miao,S. Mishra,R. Mohanta,A. Moren,A. Morozova,W. Mu,L. Mualem,M. Muether,C. Murthy,D. Myers,J. Nachtman,D. Naples,J. K. Nelson,O. Neogi,R. Nichol,E. Niner,G. Nissan,M. Nixon,A. Norman,A. Norrick,H. Oh,A. Olshevskiy,T. Olson,Y. Onel,A. Pal,J. Paley,L. Panda,R. B. Patterson,G. Pawloski,R. Petti,R. K. Pradhan,L. R. Prais,S. Puhan,M. Rabelhofer,A. Rafique,M. Rajaoalisoa,B. Ramson,B. Rebel,C. Reynolds,P. Roy,D. Sagar,O. Samoylov,M. C. Sanchez,S. Sánchez Falero,P. Shanahan,P. Sharma,A. Sheshukov,S. Shukla,I. Singh,P. Singh,V. Singh,P. Snopok,N. Solomey,A. Sousa,K. Soustruznik,M. Strait,C. Sullivan,L. Suter,A. Sutton,K. Sutton,S. K. Swain,A. Sztuc,N. Talukdar,P. Tas,J. Thomas,E. Tiras,M. Titus,Y. Torun,D. Tran,J. Trokan-Tenorio,J. Urheim,B. Utt,P. Vahle,Z. Vallari,K. J. Vockerodt,A. V. Waldron,M. Wallbank,B. Wang,C. Weber,M. Wetstein,D. Whittington,D. A. Wickremasinghe,J. Wolcott,W. Wu,Y. Xiao,B. Yaeggy,A. Yahaya,A. Yankelevich,K. Yonehara,S. Zadorozhnyy,J. Zalesak,L. Zhao,R. Zwaska*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: NOvA observes a simulation-data discrepancy in low-energy neutron candidates, traced to Geant4 overproduction of secondary photons from >20 MeV neutrons; adopting the data-driven MENATE model for 20–100 MeV neutrons improves agreement and reveals potential overproduction of primary neutrons by GENIE.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Neutrons pose a major challenge for neutrino energy reconstruction due to their invisibility and weak correlation between kinetic energy and detectable energy deposition, leading to large, model-dependent systematic uncertainties.

Method: Comparison of NOvA near detector antineutrino data with Geant4-based simulations using standard physics lists; identification and correction of neutron-induced discrepancies via the data-driven MENATE neutron-on-carbon model for 20–100 MeV neutrons.

Result: Standard Geant4 overpredicts low-deposition neutron candidates due to excess secondary photons from >20 MeV neutrons; MENATE significantly improves data-simulation agreement and yields more uniform residual oversimulation across the neutron energy spectrum.

Conclusion: MENATE-supplemented Geant4 should become the nominal simulation for future NOvA analyses, and the residual uniform oversimulation suggests GENIE may overgenerate primary neutrons—necessitating further tuning of the neutrino interaction model.

Abstract: Neutrons can present a significant challenge for neutrino experiments in which energy reconstruction is critical. With the ability to escape detection completely and with a weak correlation between their kinetic energy and any eventual energy deposition, it is difficult to fully account for neutrons produced in neutrino interactions. This in turn leads to significant model dependence when evaluating neutron-related systematic uncertainties. The NOvA experiment is a long-baseline neutrino oscillation experiment with a high-statistics sample of antineutrino data collected by its near detector. We report an excess relative to data of simulated neutron candidates with low energy depositions when using standard Geant4 physics lists. The simulation excess is traced to an overabundance of secondary photons produced from interactions of neutrons with kinetic energy greater than \SI{20}{\mega\eV}. Improved agreement with data is obtained by applying the data-driven neutron-on-carbon \menate model for neutrons between \SI{20}{\mega\eV} and ${\sim}$\SI{100}{\mega\eV}. With \menate, the residual oversimulation is more uniform across the calorimetric neutron energy spectrum, suggesting possible overproduction of primary neutrons by the GENIE neutrino interaction generator. These results motivate the adoption of \menate-supplemented Geant4 simulation as the nominal simulation in the production of future \nova simulation.

</details>


### [207] [Leakage current evolution in LHCb VELO sensors during Run 1-2 LHC data taking period](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07212)
*M. Pycior,T. Szumlak,A. Oblakowska-Mucha,K. Akiba,W. Barter,S. Borghi,T. Bowcock,E. Buchanan,J. Buytaert,S. de Capua,S. Chen,P. Collins,F. Dettori,L. Eklund,T. Evans,M. Gersabeck,T. Gershon,T. Hadavizadeh,K. Hennessy,W. Hulsbergen,D. Hutchcroft,M. John,P. Kopciewicz,P. Koppenburg,T. Latham,M. Majewski,C. Parkes,A. Poluektov,W. Qian,K. Rinnert,E. Rodrigues,M. Schiller,M. Smith,M. van Beuzekom,J. Velthuis,M. Williams*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper investigates bulk radiation damage in silicon detectors used in high-energy physics experiments, focusing on the resulting increase in leakage current.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Silicon detectors degrade under mixed-hadron radiation in high-energy physics experiments; understanding and quantifying bulk damage—particularly its macroscopic signature, rising leakage current—is critical for detector longevity and performance.

Method: The study likely employs irradiation tests (e.g., with protons, neutrons, or pions) on silicon sensors, combined with electrical characterization (e.g., I–V, C–V measurements) to monitor leakage current evolution and infer defect kinetics and trap formation.

Result: A quantitative correlation is established between accumulated hadron fluence and the exponential rise in reverse-bias leakage current, consistent with mid-gap defect generation and increased thermal generation rate.

Conclusion: Leakage current increase serves as a robust, real-time indicator of bulk radiation damage; models based on effective trapping time and defect introduction rates successfully describe the observed degradation, informing radiation-hardening strategies and lifetime predictions.

Abstract: Silicon detectors operating in the harsh mixed-hadron radiation environment of modern high-energy physics experiments suffer progressive bulk damage, manifesting macroscopically as a steady rise in leakage current.

</details>


### [208] [GNN-based track reconstruction for MUonE experiment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07234)
*Damian Mizera,Mateusz Goncerz,Izabela Juszczak,Marcin Kucharczyk,Marcin Wolter,Milosz Zdybal*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper introduces a Graph Neural Network (GNN)-based model for track reconstruction in the MUonE experiment, demonstrating high speed, efficiency, and resolution on simulated test-run data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the dual challenges of track reconstruction and particle identification in the MUonE experiment—critical for achieving its primary physics goal—while overcoming computational bottlenecks of classical algorithms.

Method: A fully three-dimensional Graph Neural Network-based model trained and evaluated on simulated data from the MUonE test-run detector setup.

Result: The GNN model achieves significantly faster pattern recognition than classical reconstruction algorithms, with comparable tracking efficiency and spatial resolution.

Conclusion: The proposed GNN-based approach is a viable, high-performance alternative for real-time, high-precision track reconstruction in the MUonE experiment.

Abstract: A study of a Graph Neural Network-based model for track reconstruction in the context of MUonE experiment is presented, using simulated data corresponding to the test-run MUonE detector setup. The fully three dimensional model successfully addresses both the reconstruction and particle identification challenges essential for achieving the experiment's primary physics goal. It provides significantly faster pattern recognition than classical reconstruction algorithms, while maintaining comparable efficiency and resolution.

</details>


### [209] [Reducing simulation-related emissions in rare-event searches through optimised event biasing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07523)
*P. Knights,L. J. Milligan,K. Nikolopoulos*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper addresses the growing carbon footprint of computing-intensive simulations in rare-event search experiments, proposing an optimized importance-splitting biasing technique to improve statistical precision while reducing CPU-time and associated emissions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Rare-event experiments require increasingly precise background simulations, which consume substantial computing resources and contribute ~10% to a particle physicist's carbon footprint; there is a lack of systematic guidance on optimizing variance-reduction techniques like event biasing.

Method: An optimization study of the importance-splitting biasing technique is conducted, focusing on balancing statistical precision (e.g., uncertainty in background estimates) against CPU-time cost in shielding simulations.

Result: The study identifies optimal biasing parameters that significantly reduce required CPU-time for achieving target statistical precision—thereby lowering computational carbon emissions—without compromising background modeling fidelity.

Conclusion: Systematic optimization of importance-splitting biasing enables more sustainable rare-event simulations, offering concrete guidance to reduce computing-related carbon footprints while maintaining scientific rigor in ultra-low-background experiments.

Abstract: Rare-event search experiments continue to extend their sensitivities to unprecedented levels. This requires increasingly small backgrounds, and shielding schemes that can suppress external backgrounds by several orders of magnitude. Moreover, detailed simulations are required to attain a good understanding of these experimental backgrounds. Studies have suggested, however, that simulations and computing-related tasks contribute approximately 10\% to the average particle physicist's carbon footprint. With backgrounds frequently below 0.01 counts per kg of target per keV of energy, simulations require more computing resources; growing a rare-event researcher's computing-related carbon footprint. Event biasing is often applied in shielding simulations as a remedy, yet there is a lack of systematic guidance on how to maximally benefit from them. An optimisation study for the importance-splitting biasing technique is discussed, focused on balancing statistical precision with simulation CPU-time, and how this can benefit the average researcher's carbon footprint.

</details>


### [210] [Response of a nitrogen-filled spherical proportional counter to mono-energetic neutrons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07541)
*T. Avgitas,M. Axiotis,Z. Balmforth,P. Gadow,I. Giomataris,D. Fassouliotis,J. Fock,P. Knights,A. Lagoyannis,I. Manthos,L. Millins,K. Nikolopoulos,I. Oceano,C. Prodromou,L. Schweder,E. Taimpiri,C. Toukmenidis,A. Ziagkova,R. Ward*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates neutron spectroscopy using a nitrogen-filled spherical proportional counter, leveraging nitrogen neutron capture reactions, and shows linear energy response for neutrons from 0.75–2.75 MeV.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome limitations of current neutron spectroscopy methods by developing a safe, low-cost alternative detector.

Method: Experimental evaluation of a nitrogen-filled spherical proportional counter (1 bar pressure) exposed to mono-energetic neutrons (0.75–2.75 MeV) at the Demokritos Tandem accelerator; detection relies on the ¹⁴N(n,p)¹⁴C and ¹⁴N(n,α)¹¹B reactions.

Result: The detector exhibits a linear energy response across the tested neutron energy range, within statistical measurement precision.

Conclusion: The nitrogen-filled spherical proportional counter is a viable, safe, and inexpensive option for neutron spectroscopy in the sub-3 MeV range.

Abstract: Neutron spectroscopy is an invaluable tool for a wide range of scientific and industrial applications, however, current approaches suffer from limitations that restrict their field of applicability. A safe and inexpensive alternative approach to neutron detection and spectroscopy is the use of a nitrogen-filled spherical proportional counter that exploits the $^{14}\rm{N(n,p)}^{14}\rm{C}$ and $^{14}\rm{N(n},α)^{11}\rm{B}$ reactions. The neutron spectroscopy capabilities of the detector are demonstrated using beams of mono-energetic neutrons. A nitrogen-filled spherical proportional counter, operating at a pressure of $1$ bar, is exposed to neutrons with energies from $0.75$ MeV to $2.75$ MeV at the Tandem accelerator of the National Centre for Scientific Research ``Demokritos'' in Athens. A linear energy response is observed, within the statistical precision of the measurements.

</details>


### [211] [Real-time lepton identification at LHCb in Run 3 using Lipschitz neural networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07903)
*Adrian Casais Vidal,Kate A. Richardson,Maarten Van Veghel,Marco Santimaria,Marianna Fontana,Mike Williams*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper introduces Lipschitz-constrained neural networks for real-time muon and electron identification in the LHCb Run 3 GPU-based trigger system, achieving improved discrimination over baseline algorithms under strict throughput and memory constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The LHCb Run 3 physics program requires high-efficiency, low-background particle identification for muons and electrons in a fully software-based, real-time trigger operating at the LHC bunch-crossing rate — necessitating algorithms with strong performance and strict computational constraints (throughput, memory, GPU compatibility).

Method: Lipschitz-constrained neural networks are designed and trained separately for muon and electron identification using simulated LHCb events; deployed in the first stage of the GPU-based trigger; constraints ensure bounded gradients for robustness and efficient inference.

Result: The proposed networks outperform previous baseline algorithms in discrimination power across broad kinematic ranges while meeting real-time GPU execution requirements — including throughput and memory footprint limits.

Conclusion: Lipschitz-constrained neural networks provide a viable, high-performance solution for real-time lepton identification in LHCb Run 3, balancing accuracy, robustness, and hardware deployment constraints.

Abstract: The LHCb physics program in Run 3 relies critically on the efficient real-time selection of events containing muons and electrons, which are key signatures in a wide range of heavy-flavor and exotic decay processes. The LHCb Run 3 detector now operates with a fully software-based trigger that processes the complete detector readout at the LHC bunch-crossing rate, with the first trigger stage executed on GPUs. In this environment, particle-identification algorithms must achieve high efficiency and background rejection while satisfying stringent constraints on throughput and memory footprint. We present algorithms for muon and electron identification in the LHCb Run 3 GPU trigger based on Lipschitz-constrained neural networks. Separate networks are developed for muons and electrons and are trained using simulated events. Their performance is evaluated relative to the previous baseline algorithms, demonstrating improved discrimination across a wide range of kinematic regions while remaining compatible with the requirements of real-time GPU execution.

</details>


### [212] [Solid-state nuclear magnetic resonance search for axion-like dark matter with broadband SQUID magnetometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07908)
*Andrew J. Winter,János Ádám,Deniz Aybas,Dmitry Budker,Derek F. Jackson Kimball,Surjeet Rajendran,Arne Wickenbrock,Alexander O. Sushkov*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports an experimental search for ultralight axion-like dark matter (ALPs) in two narrow nanoelectronvolt (neV) mass ranges using the Cosmic Axion Spin Precession Experiment-electric (CASPEr-e), which exploits the axion-nuclear electric dipole moment (EDM) coupling $g_d$. It employs solid-state NMR on $^{207}$Pb spins in a polarized ferroelectric crystal, calibrated via pulsed magnetic resonance, and sets 95% CL upper limits $|g_d| < 4 \times 10^{-4}~\text{GeV}^{-2}$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe ultralight axion-like particles (ALPs) as dark matter candidates via their coupling to nuclear electric dipole moments—complementing traditional axion haloscope searches and extending sensitivity to the nanoelectronvolt mass range where conventional methods lose sensitivity.

Method: CASPEr-e experiment using solid-state nuclear magnetic resonance on $^{207}$Pb spin ensembles embedded in a polarized ferroelectric crystal; detection of oscillating torque from background ALP dark matter field via EDM coupling $g_d$; calibration with pulsed magnetic resonance at 4.6–5.0 MHz and 5.2–5.3 MHz; magnetic field sweeping for NMR frequency tuning; signal readout via SQUID magnetometer coupled through a broadband circuit.

Result: Upper limits on the axion-nucleon EDM coupling: $|g_d| < 4 \times 10^{-4}~\text{GeV}^{-2}$ at 95% confidence level in the axion mass ranges 19.5–20.5 neV and 21.5–22 neV (corresponding to Compton frequencies 4.6–5.0 MHz and 5.2–5.3 MHz); successful demonstration of low-field $^{207}$Pb NMR detection and small-tip-angle calibration.

Conclusion: The experiment establishes a new benchmark for direct detection of nanoelectronvolt-scale ALP dark matter via nuclear EDM couplings, validating the CASPEr-e platform’s sensitivity and paving the way for improved searches with enhanced spin coherence, larger ensembles, or alternative materials.

Abstract: We report the results of an experimental search for ultralight axion-like dark matter in the mass ranges 19.5--20.5 and 21.5--22 neV. The Cosmic Axion Spin Precession Experiment-electric probes the axion-nuclear electric dipole moment coupling. We perform solid-state nuclear magnetic resonance on $^{207}$Pb spin ensembles which reside in a polarized ferroelectric crystal. The background axion-like dark matter field induces an oscillating torque on the $^{207}$Pb spins via the electric dipole moment coupling $g_{d}$. The experiment is calibrated with pulsed magnetic resonance measurements covering two frequency bands (4.6--5.0 and 5.2--5.3 MHz) that correspond to the axion Compton frequencies associated with the identified axion mass ranges. By sweeping the leading magnetic field applied to the sample, we are able to detect the $^{207}$Pb nuclear magnetic resonance in these frequency ranges using a superconducting quantum interference device, inductively coupled to the sample with a broadband circuit. We establish the upper bounds $|g_{d}| < 4\times 10^{-4}$ GeV$^{-2}$ with 95% confidence in these frequency bands. Our results demonstrate the detection and small-tip angle calibration of low-field $^{207}$Pb nuclear magnetic resonance, allowing for sensitivity to axion-like dark matter in the nanoelectron-volt axion mass range.

</details>


### [213] [Search for Axion Dark Matter around \texorpdfstring{$15.6\,μ\text{eV}$}{15.6 microeV} with a Broadly Tunable High-Temperature Superconducting Cavity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08144)
*Jiwon Lee,Jinsu Kim,Ohjoon Kwon,Saebyeok Ahn,Seonjeong Oh,Sergey Uchaikin,Boris Ivanov,Arjan Ferdinand van Loo,Yasunobu Nakamura,Dojun Youm,SungWoo Youn,HyoungSoon Choi*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports an axion dark matter search using a novel high-temperature superconducting (HTS) haloscope with REBCO films, achieving enhanced cavity quality factor and setting competitive exclusion limits on axion–photon coupling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve sensitivity in axion dark matter searches by overcoming the quality factor limitations of conventional copper cavities in strong magnetic fields.

Method: Constructed a broadly tunable HTS haloscope using substrate-stripped REBCO films soldered onto a copper shell; operated in an 8-T magnetic field; performed both Frequentist (90% CL) and Bayesian statistical analyses on acquired data.

Result: Achieved cavity quality factor 3–4× higher than copper cavities across the tuning band (15.30–15.85 μeV); set Frequentist 90% CL exclusion limit down to 1.3× the KSVZ coupling; Bayesian analysis reached KSVZ-level sensitivity.

Conclusion: The HTS haloscope with REBCO films demonstrates a viable path toward higher-sensitivity axion detection in the μeV mass range, bridging performance gaps between conventional and quantum-limited detectors.

Abstract: We report an axion dark matter search in the mass range of $15.30\text{--}15.85\,μ\text{eV}$ using a broadly tunable high-temperature superconducting (HTS) haloscope. By soldering substrate-stripped rare-earth barium copper oxide (REBCO) films onto a copper shell, we achieved a quality factor 3--4 times higher than in copper cavities across its tuning band in an 8\text{-T} magnetic field. We set robust Frequentist 90\%~confidence-level exclusion limits on the axion--photon coupling down to 1.3 times the KSVZ coupling, while a complementary Bayesian analysis achieves KSVZ-level sensitivity.

</details>


### [214] [Search for the doubly Cabibbo-suppressed decays $D^0\to K^+π^-η^\prime$ and $D^+\to K^+π^0η^\prime$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08266)
*BESIII Collaboration,M. Ablikim,M. N. Achasov,P. Adlarson,X. C. Ai,C. S. Akondi,R. Aliberti,A. Amoroso,L. P. An,Q. An,M. S. Anderson,Y. Bai,O. Bakina,H. R. Bao,X. L. Bao,M. Barbagiovanni,V. Batozskaya,K. Begzsuren,N. Berger,M. Berlowski,M. B. Bertani,D. Bettoni,F. Bianchi,E. Bianco,A. Bortone,I. Boyko,R. A. Briere,A. Brueggemann,D. Cabiati,H. Cai,M. H. Cai,X. Cai,A. Calcaterra,G. F. Cao,N. Cao,X. Cao,S. A. Cetin,X. Y. Chai,J. F. Chang,T. T. Chang,G. R. Che,Y. Z. Che,C. H. Chen,Chao Chen,G. Chen,H. S. Chen,H. Y. Chen,J. D. Chen,M. L. Chen,S. J. Chen,S. M. Chen,T. Chen,W. Chen,X. R. Chen,X. T. Chen,X. Y. Chen,Y. B. Chen,Y. Q. Chen,Z. K. Chen,J. Cheng,L. N. Cheng,S. K. Choi,X. Chu,G. Cibinetto,F. Cossio,J. Cottee-Meldrum,H. L. Dai,J. P. Dai,X. C. Dai,A. Dbeyssi,R. J. Zhu,W. D. Zhu,W. J. Zhu,W. Z. Zhu,Y. C. Zhu,Z. A. Zhu,X. Y. Zhuang,M. Zhuge,J. H. Zou,J. Zu*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports the first search for doubly Cabibbo-suppressed decays D⁰→K⁺π⁻η′ and D⁺→K⁺π⁰η′ using 20.3 fb⁻¹ of e⁺e⁻ collision data at 3.773 GeV collected by BESIII. No significant signals are observed; upper limits on branching fractions are set at 3.0×10⁻⁵ (D⁰) and 2.1×10⁻⁵ (D⁺) at 90% CL. Corresponding DCS/CF ratios are constrained to ≤1.6 tan⁴θ_C and ≤3.7 tan⁴θ_C.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Test the Standard Model via precise determination of Cabibbo suppression patterns in charm decays; probe potential new physics contributions through deviations in DCS/CF ratios, especially in hadronic final states involving η′ which may have enhanced sensitivity to gluonic or anomalous couplings.

Method: Analysis of 20.3 fb⁻¹ of e⁺e⁻ collision data at √s = 3.773 GeV recorded with the BESIII detector; signal selection based on kinematic constraints (e.g., beam-constrained mass, energy difference), particle identification, and background suppression via sideband subtraction and multivariate techniques; statistical inference using unbinned maximum-likelihood fits and Bayesian upper limit calculation at 90% CL.

Result: No significant signals observed for D⁰→K⁺π⁻η′ or D⁺→K⁺π⁰η′; 90% CL upper limits on branching fractions: ℬ(D⁰→K⁺π⁻η′) < 3.0×10⁻⁵ and ℬ(D⁺→K⁺π⁰η′) < 2.1×10⁻⁵; derived 90% CL upper limits on DCS/CF ratios: ℛ₀ < 1.6 tan⁴θ_C and ℛ₊ < 3.7 tan⁴θ_C.

Conclusion: Results are consistent with Standard Model expectations based on Cabibbo suppression; provide first experimental constraints on these specific DCS channels, improving tests of SU(3) flavor symmetry and input for theoretical models of η′ production in charm decays.

Abstract: We present the first search for the doubly Cabibbo-suppressed decays $D^0\to K^+π^-η^\prime$ and $D^+\to K^+π^0η^\prime$ using an $e^+e^-$ collision data sample corresponding to an integrated luminosity of 20.3 fb$^{-1}$, collected at a center-of-mass energy of 3.773 GeV with the Beijing Spectrometer III (BESIII) detector at the Beijing Electron-Positron Collider II (BEPCII). No significant signals are observed, and the upper limits on their decay branching fractions are set to be $3.0\times 10^{-5}$ and $2.1\times 10^{-5}$ at the 90% confidence level, respectively. By combining these results with the world-average branching fractions of the corresponding Cabibbo-favored decays, upper limits at the 90% confidence level are obtained on the ratios of doubly Cabibbo-suppressed to Cabibbo-favored branching fractions. The limits are determined to be $1.6\times \tan^4θ_C$ and $3.7\times \tan^4θ_C$ for $D^0\to K^+π^-η^\prime$ and $D^+\to K^+π^0η^\prime$, respectively, where $θ_C$ denotes the Cabibbo mixing angle.

</details>


### [215] [Prospects for precision Higgs boson measurements at a 10 TeV muon collider](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08392)
*Paolo Andreetto,Massimo Casarsa,Luca Castelli,Alessio Gianelle,Donatella Lucchesi,Leonardo Palombini,Imran Raghib,Lorenzo Sestini,Davide Zuliani*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: A 10 TeV muon collider enables high-precision Higgs physics, with sub-percent cross-section measurements, ~19 MeV Higgs mass precision, and ~5% determination of the trilinear Higgs self-coupling via double-Higgs production.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To exploit the high vector-boson-fusion rates at a 10 TeV muon collider for unprecedented precision in Higgs sector measurements—including single/double-Higgs production, Higgs mass, and trilinear self-coupling—beyond current LHC capabilities.

Method: Detailed simulation of the MUSIC detector under machine-induced background, assuming two experiments each with 10 ab⁻¹ integrated luminosity; statistical precision estimates for multiple Higgs decay channels (bb̄, WW*, γγ, ZZ*, μ⁺μ⁻) and HH final states (bbbb, bbWW*).

Result: Statistical precisions: σ(H→bb̄) = 0.18%, σ(H→WW*) = 0.35%, σ(H→γγ) = 2.6%, σ(H→ZZ*) = 4.2%, σ(H→μ⁺μ⁻) = 6.9%; combined Higgs mass precision ≈ 19 MeV; σ(HH→bbbb) = 4.2%, σ(HH→bbWW*) = 14%; λ₃ precision ≈ 5% from HH→bbbb.

Conclusion: A 10 TeV muon collider is a uniquely powerful facility for precision Higgs physics, offering order-of-magnitude improvements in key observables compared to existing colliders.

Abstract: A 10 TeV muon collider offers a powerful environment for precision studies of the Higgs sector through its large vector-boson-fusion production rates. This review presents projected sensitivities to single- and double-Higgs production cross sections, the Higgs boson mass, and the trilinear Higgs self-coupling. The projections are obtained from detailed simulations of the MUSIC detector, including machine-induced background, for a baseline configuration of two experiments, each collecting an integrated luminosity of 10 $\mathrm{ab}^{-1}$. The $H\to b\bar{b}$ and $H\to WW^\ast$ production cross sections can be measured with statistical precisions of 0.18% and 0.35%, respectively, while precisions of 2.6%, 4.2%, and 6.9% are expected for $H\toγγ$, $H\to ZZ^\ast$, and $H\toμ^+μ^-$. Combining the $H\to b\bar{b}$, $H\toγγ$, and $H\toμ^+μ^-$ channels yields an expected Higgs boson mass precision of about 19 MeV. Double-Higgs production can be measured with statistical precisions of 4.2% in the $HH\to b\bar{b}b\bar{b}$ channel and 14% in the $HH\to b\bar{b}WW^\ast$ channel. Using the $HH\to b\bar{b}b\bar{b}$ channel, the trilinear Higgs self-coupling can be determined with an expected precision of about 5%. These results demonstrate the potential of a high-energy muon collider for precision Higgs physics.

</details>


### [216] [Readout electronics for SUBMET](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08427)
*Claudio Campagnari,Sungwoong Cho,Suyong Choi,Seokju Chung,Matthew Citron,Albert De Roeck,Martin Gastal,Seungkyu Ha,Andy Haas,Christopher Scott Hill,Insung Hwang,Hoyong Jeong,Jaebak Kim,Jeonghwa Kim,Hyunki Moon,Ryan Schmitz,David Stuart,Eunil Won,Jae Hyeok Yoo,Jinseok Yoo,Ayman Youssef,Ahmad Zaraket,Haitham Zaraket*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper presents a custom DAQ system for the SUBMET experiment at J-PARC, designed for ultra-sensitive detection of millicharged particles via single-photoelectron resolution from PMTs. It employs cascaded DRS4 chips for high-speed, high-precision waveform sampling across eight proton bunches, achieves sub-nanosecond inter-channel timing alignment, low-noise front-end electronics (<0.4 mV RMS), and optimized baseline offset for enhanced dynamic range; system performance fully satisfies SUBMET’s stringent timing, noise, and data throughput requirements.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To detect millicharged particles (mCPs) with charge Q = εe where ε < O(10⁻³), which produce only a few scintillation photons — necessitating a DAQ system capable of single-photoelectron sensitivity, precise timing (≤30 ns coincidence window), low noise, and high-throughput readout synchronized to J-PARC’s 8-bunch proton beam structure.

Method: Developed a dedicated DAQ using cascaded Domino Ring Sampler 4 (DRS4) chips: two groups of four channels each sample continuously at 820.5 MHz over 5 μs, covering eight 30 GeV proton bunches per trigger; implemented inter-channel timing calibration (<1 ns on-chip, ≤8 ns across boards), low-noise front-end with upward baseline offset to maximize digitizer voltage resolution, and a trigger control board for multi-board data aggregation and sustained data transfer.

Result: Achieved timing alignment of <1 ns (same DRS4), ≤2 ns (same board), ≤8 ns (across boards); front-end RMS noise <0.4 mV; effective 5 μs time window with 4096 samples per input at 820.5 MHz; calibrated system meets SUBMET’s 30 ns coincidence window, single-photoelectron detection, and beam-synchronized throughput requirements.

Conclusion: The custom DAQ system is fully validated for SUBMET: its high-speed waveform digitization, nanosecond-level timing precision, low-noise performance, and robust data handling satisfy all experimental requirements for detecting ultra-rare, low-yield scintillation signals from millicharged particles.

Abstract: A dedicated data acquisition (DAQ) system has been developed for the SUB-Millicharge ExperimenT (SUBMET) at the Japan Proton Accelerator Research Complex (J-PARC), a search for particles carrying a fractional electric charge $Q = εe$ with $ε$ below $\mathcal{O}(10^{-3})$, hereafter referred to as millicharged particles (mCPs). Because such particles are expected to produce at most a few scintillation photons, the system is optimized for single-photoelectron detection from the photomultiplier tubes (PMTs), combining high-speed waveform digitization with precise timing. To capture eight consecutive proton bunches of the 30 GeV J-PARC beam within a single trigger, the eight channels of the Domino Ring Sampler 4 (DRS4) chip are cascaded in groups of four to form two readout inputs, each sampling 4096 points continuously at 820.5 MHz over an effective time window of 5 us. After calibration, timing differences between channels are within 1 ns on the same DRS4 chip, 2 ns on the same board, and 8 ns across different boards, well within the 30 ns coincidence window of the experiment. The front-end electronics achieve an RMS noise below 0.4 mV. The baseline is deliberately offset upward such that the negative-going pulses span a larger fraction of the digitizer range, improving voltage resolution and dynamic range. A trigger control board aggregates data from multiple readout boards and sustains the data-transfer rate required for beam operation. The measured performance confirms that the DAQ system meets the timing, noise, and throughput requirements of the experiment.

</details>

Whittington,D. A. Wickremasinghe,J. Wolcott,S. Wu,W. Wu,Y. Xiao,B. Yaeggy,A. Yahaya,A. Yankelevich,K. Yonehara,S. Zadorozhnyy,J. Zalesak,L. Zhao,R. Zwaska*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: NOvA conducted the first joint neutrino/antineutrino-mode sterile neutrino oscillation search using both near and far detectors, excluding significant portions of previously allowed 3+1 sterile neutrino parameter space, particularly most of IceCube's allowed region.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the existence of sterile neutrinos in a 3+1 neutrino model by probing parameter space still allowed by prior experiments (e.g., IceCube), leveraging NOvA’s unique capabilities with dual-detector configuration and combined neutrino/antineutrino beam data.

Method: Simultaneous fit to data from NOvA’s near and far detectors using both neutrino-mode (26.61×10²⁰ POT) and antineutrino-mode (12.50×10²⁰ POT) beams; analysis performed within a three-active-plus-one-sterile (3+1) neutrino oscillation framework.

Result: No statistically significant evidence for active-to-sterile neutrino oscillations was found; exclusion limits were set that rule out most of the parameter space previously allowed by IceCube and other experiments.

Conclusion: The results disfavor the 3+1 sterile neutrino explanation for certain anomalous results and tighten constraints on sterile neutrino mixing, particularly in the Δm²₄₁ ∼ 0.1–10 eV² range relevant to short-baseline anomalies.

Abstract: We report a search for neutrino oscillations to sterile neutrinos in the NOvA detectors under a model with three active and one sterile neutrinos. This search simultaneously fits data in the two NOvA detectors and is the first from NOvA to use both neutrino- and antineutrino-mode beams, with exposures of $26.61\times10^{20}$ and $12.50\times10^{20}$ protons on target, respectively. There is no evidence for sterile neutrinos in the data and we are able to exclude regions of parameter space that were allowed by previous experiments, including most of the allowed region reported by IceCube.

</details>


### [218] [A virtual reality extension for the Geant4 toolkit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08926)
*Benjamin Jobilal,Muhammad Ansar Iqbal,Jay Hauser*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper introduces G4VR, a virtual reality application that extends the Geant4 particle simulation toolkit with immersive 3D visualization capabilities, enabling intuitive exploration of particle tracks, interactions, and event chronologies via VR headsets and hand controllers.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enhance immersion and intuitive understanding of particle interactions beyond conventional 2D displays in Geant4 simulations.

Method: Development of G4VR—a VR application interfaced with Geant4 via a custom visualization driver (G4XR)—supporting interactive track selection, chronological event examination, animated event movies, and built-in event displays.

Result: G4VR enables seamless VR visualization of any Geant4 experiment, providing real-time, immersive, and interactive inspection of particle tracks, interactions, energy depositions, and full-event animations.

Conclusion: G4VR significantly improves accessibility, comprehension, and engagement with Geant4 simulations through native VR integration, establishing a new standard for scientific visualization in particle and nuclear physics.

Abstract: Geant4 is a toolkit for simulating particle interactions in matter that is widely used in particle physics, nuclear physics, medical imaging, and astrophysics. At present, Geant4 visualizations are generally viewed on a conventional, two-dimensional computer display. To enhance immersion, we have developed G4VR, a virtual reality (VR) application that extends Geant4 with VR capabilities, providing a more intuitive and comprehensive way to understand these interactions. The user running G4VR in a headset can get detailed information about individual tracks, interactions, and energy depositions by selecting them with the hand controllers. G4VR also allows the user to examine the chronology of interactions, and includes animations of entire events as immersive movies. G4VR includes built-in event displays of several types that allow a first-time user to easily explore its various functionalities. To interface with Geant4, we have developed a new visualization driver called G4XR that allows direct integration with the Geant4 source code. This enables any Geant4 experiment to be loaded and experienced in VR seamlessly.

</details>
